Not only that, you have presented Lord Caitanya to be an ordinary soul, giving life to matter, but He is actually the fountainhead of all living entities.” Being very, very angry, Svarūpa Dāmodara chastised this *brāhmaṇa* and went on to explain his misconceptions, establishing the truth very firmly on the basis of *śāstra.*

When all the Vaiṣṇavas heard Svarūpa Dāmodara’s expert explanation, they could understand that they had made a very great mistake. This person was speaking in a way that was comparable to Māyāvāda philosophy, but they had not been able to detect it. Only Svarūpa Dāmodara was able to detect it. The *brāhmaṇa* was very humiliated by the statements of Svarūpa Dāmodara. He immediately felt like a duck in a society of swans. However, being merciful, Svarūpa Dāmodara was very kind to this poet and tried to pacify him. He said, “Although you have made a grievous mistake with your poetry, Goddess Sarasvatī has taken what you have written and has presented it in a way that properly glorifies the Lord. Sometimes the demons, and even the demigods, speak about the Lord in a critical way, but Goddess Sarasvatī explains their statements as glorification of the Lord.”

Svarūpa Dāmodara gave various examples, such as when Indra became very angry with the Lord and called Him a “naughty, talkative, young child.” He also gave the example of Jarāsandha, who spoke of the Lord as *puruṣādhama,* which means the lowest of mankind. Goddess Sarasvatī took the word *puruṣādhama* as *puruṣottama,* meaning that He was the highest person, who nobody else could reach. He gave many other examples of how that which is intended as criticism can be seen as glorification of the Lord.

Having given these examples, Svarūpa Dāmodara said, “Similarly, what you have written, although it is criticism of the Lord, can be taken in a positive way.” He went on to explain how the writings could be seen in a positive light according to the blessings of Goddess Sarasvatī. Thus, the *brāhmaṇa* became very humble. He completely surrendered himself to all the Vaiṣṇavas, and from that day on, he became their associate while in Jagannātha Purī.

Prabhupāda explains that by the mercy of the Vaiṣṇavas, a poet who is not qualified can become qualified. Therefore, this is an example of how the poet previously had no qualification, because he had not taken shelter of a superior authority. His words and descriptions of the Lord were imperfect and could not be accepted as authoritative statements about the Lord. Prabhupāda says, “Unless one is empowered by the higher authorities or advanced devotees, one cannot write transcendental literature, for all such literature must be above suspicion.” (*Cc. Ādi* 8.72, Purport) Therefore, when those empowered by Vaiṣṇavas speak, it is as good as *śāstra*. In fact, sometimes the Lord prefers His devotee to speak rather than Himself, as in the case of Rāmānanda Rāya.

*Caitanya-caritāmṛta* describes the pastime of Lord Caitanya discussing transcendental topics with Rāmānanda Rāya. The Lord asked Rāmānanda Rāya to speak about the highest goal of life according to *śāstra.* Rāmānanda Rāya quoted *śāstra* about *varṇāśrama* and proceeded to describe higher and higher levels of spiritual realization. Each time he came to a higher level, he would quote references from *śāstra,* and the Lord would say, “Yes, but there is something higher.” Thus, Rāmānanda Rāya would speak about something higher, and quote more verses from *śāstra*. He eventually spoke about the different *rasas* of servitorship, fraternal affection, parental affection, and conjugal love. Lord Caitanya was very pleased with his statements and said, “Yes, but I think there is something more.”

Rāmānanda Rāya began speaking about the glories of Śrīmati Rādhārāṇī, quoting different references from the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* about Her glories. Lord Caitanya was moved to tears by hearing these descriptions and said, “Yes, now you have come to the highest level; but I think there is even more that you can say.”

Rāmānanda Rāya replied, “Lord, how can I say anything higher? My intelligence is bewildered. I can only say something higher if You speak through me. Otherwise, there is nothing higher that I can possibly say.” Rāmānanda Rāya finally said, “There is more that I can say, but nothing further from the scriptures. I can only say what is in my heart.”

Lord Caitanya became very eager to hear it. Rāmānanda Rāya began composing verses in glorification of the Lord from his heart. He gradually revealed that the Supreme Personality of Godhead had manifest before him as Caitanya Mahāprabhu. He described how Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa had combined into the transcendental form present before him. It thus came to the point where *śāstra* was no longer quoted; the words of the realized Vaiṣṇava were the highest truth.

When the Lord enters the heart of such empowered Vaiṣṇavas, and speaks to them, their statements are as good as *śāstra*. The Lord is pleased by their statements because their words are perfect. Their words are completely transcendental, and there is no material defect in their speaking. This is described in the verse in the *Mahābhārata* that says that the solid truth of religious principles is found in the heart of an unadulterated Vaiṣṇava (*Mahābhārata, Vana-parva* 313.117):

*dharmasya tattvaṁ nihitaṁ guhāyāṁ*  
*mahā-jano yena gataḥ sa panthāḥ*

One should therefore follow in the footsteps of such empowered Vaiṣṇavas. Empowerment is the qualification to write about and glorify the Lord properly. Where does empowerment come from? One has to receive it from higher authorities or advanced devotees. One must be very, very careful; one should not be very eager to write about the pastimes of the Lord by one’s own initiative. One must always write, recite, and speak only on the basis of empowerment. Prabhupāda speaks a little more about that in the next purport.

**Text 73**

*vaiṣṇavera ājñā pāñā cintita-antare*  
*madana-gopāle gelāṅ ājñā māgibāre*

**Having received the order of the Vaiṣṇavas but being anxious within my heart, I went to the temple of Madanamohana in Vṛndāvana to ask His permission also.**

**Purport**

A Vaiṣṇava always follows the order of *guru* and Kṛṣṇa. *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* was written by Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī by their mercy. Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī considered all the devotees that have been mentioned to be his preceptor *gurus*, or spiritual masters, and Madana-gopāla (Śrī Madana-mohana *vigraha*) is Kṛṣṇa Himself. Thus he took permission from both of them, and when he received the mercy of both *guru* and Kṛṣṇa, he was able to write this great literature, *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta*. This example should be followed. Anyone who attempts to write about Kṛṣṇa must first take permission from the spiritual master and Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa is situated in everyone’s heart, and the spiritual master is His direct external representative. Thus Kṛṣṇa is situated *antar-bahiḥ*, within and without. One must first become a pure devotee by following the strict regulative principles and chanting sixteen rounds daily, and when one thinks that he is actually on the Vaiṣṇava platform, he must then take permission from the spiritual master, and that permission must also be confirmed by Kṛṣṇa from within his heart. Then, if one is very sincere and pure, he can write transcendental literature, either prose or poetry.

Here, the point of taking permission from both *guru* and Kṛṣṇa is further elaborated. Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī considered all the devotees as his preceptor *gurus*. He approached Madana-mohana, who was Kṛṣṇa Himself, in order to receive His permission as well. Thus, this point is again emphasized that writing about the Supreme Lord is not something that can be done whimsically.

In another place, Prabhupāda explains that a Vaiṣṇava can write about the Lord if he has been given proper permission. He says, “However, he should not be so eager to publish what he writes for name or fame.” He should write for his own purification; by glorifying the Lord, one becomes purified. One can only glorify the Lord according to his realization and capacity.

There is a verse in the First Canto of the *ŚrīmadBhāgavatam* that describes that each bird flies in the sky according to its own capacity. Therefore, in the same way, saintly persons must glorify the Lord according to their realization. Just as some birds can only fly so high, we can only speak in glorification of the Lord according to our level of realization. If the devotee speaks according to his realization and purely tries to glorify the Lord, the Lord will accept his intents. That devotee will benefit and become purified by his attempt to glorify the Lord. Nevertheless, we should not be eager to publish books; one should only do so with the blessings of the Vaiṣṇavas. Otherwise, there are dangers of publishing something that is not authoritative (as was already explained).

Thus, we see in this example of Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī that he was not very eager to write. However, he was eager to please the Vaiṣṇavas and especially to satisfy the Lord after He gave an indication that He was giving His blessings.

Śrīla Prabhupāda similarly was very eager to publish books because Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta wanted him to do so; otherwise, he was not so eager to publish books. Every time Śrīla Prabhupāda completed a book—especially when he completed *Caitanya-caritāmṛta—*he would give us an explanation of the authority behind his translations of the verses. He always presented his writings in light of the higher authority. When the higher order and purity are present, empowerment is also present. Those who come in contact with the literature of an empowered Vaiṣṇava come in contact with Kṛṣṇa. *Kali-kālera dharma—kṛṣṇa-nāma-saṅkīrtana / kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana* (*Cc. Antya* 7.11):

Unless one is empowered by the Supreme Personality of Godhead, one cannot distribute the glories of the Lord. Therefore, one always must seek to be empowered. Prabhupāda says (*Cc. Ādi* 8.73, Purport):

One must first become a pure devotee by following the strict regulative principles and chanting sixteen rounds daily, and when one thinks that he is actually on the Vaiṣṇava platform, he must then take permission from the spiritual master. (…)

One might think that a Vaiṣṇava is never supposed to think that he is on the Vaiṣṇava platform. That point will come when the indication is given by the Lord within the heart. Prabhupāda says, “It has to be confirmed by Kṛṣṇa in the heart.”

Again, we have to be very careful to not misinterpret this. As with anything, there is always some room for misinterpretation. We must be very careful about thinking we are qualified to communicate with Kṛṣṇa in the heart.

Once, a devotee in New Vṛndāvana was telling Śrīla Prabhupāda about persons who think that they can speak with Kṛṣṇa in the heart, and Prabhupāda asked, “Did you defeat them? Did you tell them who Kṛṣṇa speaks to in the heart?” Since the devotee was not giving a direct answer, Prabhupāda repeated, “Did you tell them!?” Prabhupāda started to get angry and chastise him: “Who does Kṛṣṇa speak to? Kṛṣṇa does not speak to just anybody in the heart! He speaks to those who are constantly devoted to Him and worship Him with love (*Bg.*

10.10): *teṣāṁ satata-yuktānāṁ / bhajatāṁ prīti-pūrvakam.* ‘To those who are constantly devoted to serving Me with love, I give the understanding by which they can come to Me.’”

Prabhupāda continued, “Actually, how can one serve Kṛṣṇa with love without the order of *guru*?” First, we have to accept the instructions of *guru* and then dedicate our lives to following those instructions. In such a way, we can become qualified to have Kṛṣṇa speak to us in our hearts. Prabhupāda was very, very anxious to clarify the point that Kṛṣṇa does not speak to just anybody in their heart. One must take shelter of the Vaiṣṇavas, take their permission, take permission from the Lord, and then, if one is very sincere and pure, he can write transcendental literature.

In these different examples, there is very clear evidence that writing transcendental literature is not something that can be done whimsically. Of course, Śrīla Prabhupāda wanted his disciples to write, and in many places, he instructed that it is the duty of disciples to write books. Thus, the order is there, but the motivation should always be pure. We should not take the order and use it for our own purposes.

Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī had the order from superior Vaiṣṇavas, and he also had the desire to please them. They were all very eager to hear about the Lord. He was motivated exclusively by the desire to please the Vaiṣṇavas; he had no other motivation for writing. He wanted to satisfy the devotees by his transcendental writing. Therefore Prabhupāda says, “If the motivation is pure and one is sincere, one can write transcendental literature. One should never write books for name and fame. Those who do so are not qualified to write. They will not be empowered, and they will not inspire devotional service in the hearts of their readers.”

*\* Devotees are writing and reciting poetry. How should we accept it?*

Sometimes devotees will write something in a sincere attempt to glorify the Lord or His devotees. We just read that if we are interested in doing something in a sincere and pure attempt, we may write some prose or poetry. However, we have given enough indication that we should not be eager to propagate it unless the permission and the desire of the Vaiṣṇavas are present. Therefore, we can write for our purification. Especially if we are inclined, there is nothing wrong with that; it is the propagation that can be problematic.

*\* Sometimes we have to listen, so how should we behave?*

*Caitanya-caritāmṛta* gives different examples of how the listeners should behave. If the person is being asked by authorities to speak, the listener should hear. However, if the person wants to propagate without the blessings of the Vaiṣṇavas, we can follow the example of the followers of Lord Caitanya, who did not want hear Vallabha Bhaṭṭa.

In that particular pastime, Vallabha Bhaṭṭa once spoke to Gadādhara Paṇḍita with such aggression that Gadādhara Paṇḍita could not leave. He felt trapped and had to listen although he did not want to. He therefore prayed in his heart, “Lord Caitanya, please protect me! You know my heart. You know the circumstances by which I am standing here listening to these descriptions.” Therefore, sometimes there may be a situation where you can only pray, “Protect me.”

Of course, we are not intending to disqualify anybody who writes. We are simply repeating what is in the purport. We are not trying to present anything that is different from the conclusions that have already been given. Thus, our intention is not to say that nobody is qualified. We should try to extract the essence of what has been said, which is that each and every one of us should strive for purity. If we write with purity, our writing will be effective. That is the essence. We should never try to do anything for our own sense gratification. We should try to do that which is pleasing to the Vaiṣṇavas and Kṛṣṇa, thereby always striving for the highest standard. Furthermore, we should know what the highest standard is, so that we can strive for it. We are not saying that nobody should write, but we should not be eager to write for name and fame. If we look within our heart and see that we are motivated by name and fame, we should try to write for our own purification and become qualified. That is the essence.

*\* Lord Caitanya says in His Śikṣāṣṭaka that one has to chant the holy name with a state of mind of being ready to offer respect to others. However, sometimes it is rather difficult. How important is it to apply this instruction in our chanting?*

Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī says we should take this verse and wear it as a garland around our neck for constant remembrance. We cannot diminish the importance. This is the essence of Vaiṣṇava character. The ornaments of a Vaiṣṇava are humility and respect for others. Without that consciousness, we will not be able to chant the holy name of the Lord constantly. We will commit offenses and lose our taste for chanting the holy name.

## THE DESIRE TREE OF DEVOTIONAL SERVICE

**Ādi-līlā: 9.1–11**

**Kiev—May 2–3, 2000**

**Moscow—May 22, 2000**

**Let me offer my respectful obeisances unto the spiritual master of the entire world, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu, by whose mercy even a dog can swim across a great ocean. All glories to Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya, who is known as Gaurahari! All glories to Advaita Ācārya and Nityānanda Prabhu! All glories to the devotees of Lord Caitanya, headed by Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura! In order to fulfill all my desires, I remember their lotus feet. I also remember the six Gosvāmīs—Rūpa, Sanātana, Bhaṭṭa Raghunātha, Śrī Jīva, Gopāla Bhaṭṭa and Dāsa Raghunātha. It is by the mercy of all these Vaiṣṇavas and** *gurus* **that I attempt to write about the pastimes and qualities of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Whether I know it or know not, it is for self-purification that I write this book. (***Cc. Ādi* **9.1–5)**

**Purport**

This is the sum and substance of transcendental writing. One must be an authorized Vaiṣṇava, humble and pure. One should write transcendental literature to purify oneself, not for credit. By writing about the pastimes of the Lord, one associates with the Lord directly. One should not ambitiously think, “I shall become a great author. I shall be celebrated as a writer.” These are material desires. One should attempt to write for self-purification. It may be published or it may not be published, but that does not matter. If one is actually sincere in writing, all his ambitions will be fulfilled. Whether one is known as a great author is incidental. One should not attempt to write transcendental literature for material name and fame.

**I take shelter of the Supreme Personality of Godhead Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, who Himself is the tree of transcendental love of Kṛṣṇa, its gardener and also the bestower and enjoyer of its fruits. Lord Caitanya thought, “My name is Viśvambhara, ‘one who maintains the entire universe.’ Its meaning will be actualized if I can fill the whole universe with love of Godhead.” (***Cc. Ādi* **9.6–7)**

This is the real and complete meaning of the name Viśvambhara. The name Viśvambhara means one who maintains the universe. The Lord is ultimately maintaining everything in both the material and spiritual worlds. Everything is resting upon His energies: *nityo nityānāṁ cetanaś cetanānām* (*Kaṭha Upaniṣad* 2.2.13). The Supreme Lord is the supreme maintainer of all living entities.

Here, Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja is giving a much more complete meaning to the name Viśvambhara—the maintainer of the universe. It means more than simply maintaining this inert matter and the living entities entangled within it. The whole universe is filled with love of Godhead. Thus, the Lord desires to maintain the universe, filled with love of Godhead. The Lord’s desires are completely spiritual, and the desire of Lord Caitanya is to distribute love of Godhead to everyone.

This figurative example of Lord Caitanya being the gardener is being used. The gardener plants the original seed, waters it, protects it, nourishes it, and when the seed grows and produces fruit, the gardener enjoys the fruit. This analogy describes Lord Caitanya’s planting and nourishing the seed and then enjoying the fruit, having given love of Godhead to everybody.

As described in the beginning of the *Ādi-līlā,* one of the purposes for the Lord’s descent was His eagerness to distribute the pure love of Godhead that is experienced by the residents of the eternal abode of Vraja-dhāma.

**Thinking in this way, He accepted the duty of a planter and began to grow a garden in Navadvīpa. Thus the Lord brought the desire tree of devotional service to this earth and became its gardener. He sowed the seed and sprinkled upon it the water of His will. (***Cc. Ādi* **9.8–9)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda explains in many places that devotional service is compared to a creeper. The seed, *bhakti-latā-bīja,* sprouts into a devotional plant that gradually grows to the outer coverings of the universe, crossing over the Virajā River, entering into the region of Vaikuṇṭha and then Goloka Vṛndāvana, and finally comes to the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa, where it bears fruits of love of God. This is also an analogy, which the Lord has given to help us understand how to cultivate love of Godhead through the process of hearing and chanting.

Prabhupāda explains that when one waters the seed, it grows into the fruit of devotional service. The gardener, or *mālā-kāraḥ,* can then enjoy these fruits without impediments. Lord Caitanya, the gardener (based on this analogy), wants to enjoy the fruits of love of Godhead manifested in others. The obstacles for His enjoyment of the fruits are the weeds, which are manifested in the hearts of living entities.

Lord Caitanya describes the different obstacles that obstruct the growth of the devotional plant. *Kuṭīnāṭī,* in Lord Caitanya’s instructions, refers to diplomatic behavior. Elsewhere, it is referred to as faultfinding. *Jīva-hiṁsā* is envy of other living beings. There are also *lābha, pūjā,* and *pratiṣṭhāśā*. *Lābha* is the desire for mundane profit; *pūjā* is the desire for mundane adoration; and *pratiṣṭhāśā* is the desire for mundane importance. The Lord describes these as the different obstacles to developing love for God.

Ultimately, the Lord wants to enjoy the fruit of distributing love of Godhead to all living beings. All living entities exist exclusively for the Lord’s enjoyment. The living entities are meant to give pleasure to the Lord, but the seeds of material desires are obstacles to this pure consciousness. There are two kinds of seeds: the seed of devotion (*bhakti-latā-bīja*) and the seeds of material desires. Lord Caitanya describes that when watering the seed of devotion, one has to be very careful or one can also water the seeds of material desires.

In verse nine, Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī is speaking about the seed of love of Godhead when he says, “He sowed the seed and sprinkled upon it the water of His will.”

**Text 10**

*jaya śrī mādhavapurī kṛṣṇa-prema-pūra*  
*bhakti-kalpatarura teṅho prathama aṅkura*

**All glories to Śrī Mādhavendra Purī, the storehouse of all devotional service unto Kṛṣṇa! He is a desire tree of devotional service, and it is in him that the seed of devotional service first fructified.**

**Purport**

(…) The process of worship in the disciplic succession of Madhvācārya was full of ritualistic ceremonies, with hardly a sign of love of Godhead. Śrī Mādhavendra Purī was the first person in that disciplic succession to exhibit the symptoms of love of Godhead and the first to write a poem beginning with the words *ayi dīna-dayārdra nātha*, “O supremely merciful Personality of Godhead.” In that poem is the seed of Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s cultivation of love of Godhead. (*Cc. Ādi* 9.10)

Lord Caitanya first planted the seed in Mādhavendra Purī, and it fructified in him. He was the first *ācārya* in our *sampradāya* to manifest the full characteristics of one who has love of Godhead in feelings of separation. Up until that point, there was no full display of symptoms of love of Godhead. Although Madhvācārya was a great *ācārya,* his followers did not study or describe the characteristics of the residents of Vṛndāvana. Rather, they performed many ritualistic ceremonies with hardly a sign of love of Godhead. By strictly following the rules and regulations, scriptures, and ritualistic performances, one can achieve the realm of Vaikuṇṭha. This is also described in the *Ādi-līlā* of the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta.*

However, if one wants to enter into the transcendental realm of Goloka Vṛndāvana, he has to follow in the footsteps of Mādhavendra Purī, Īśvara Purī, Lord Caitanya, and Rūpa Gosvāmī. Mādhavendra Purī was the first in our disciplic succession to manifest symptoms of love in the mood of separation. There are many descriptions of Mādhavendra Purī’s ecstasies of love of Godhead in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta.*

Mādhavendra Purī is speaking in the mood of the *gopīs’* feelings of separation: *ayi dīna-dayārdra nātha.* The verse means, “O my master, O master of Mathurā, when will I see You again? Because of not seeing You, my heart has become unsteady. O my beloved Lord, what should I do now?” He quoted this verse at the time of his departure from this world.

Rāmacandra Purī heard him saying this verse and criticized Mādhavendra Purī. He asked, “Why are you lamenting? You should be thinking of Brahman. You should not be lamenting.” Rāmacandra Purī was thinking of Brahman in terms of a verse from the *Bhagavad-gītā* (18.54)*:*

*brahma-bhūtaḥ prasannātmā*  
*na śocati na kāṅkṣati*  
*samaḥ sarveṣu bhūteṣu*  
*mad-bhaktiṁ labhate parām*

“One who is thus transcendentally situated at once realizes the Supreme Brahman and becomes fully joyful. He never laments or desires to have anything. He is equally disposed toward every living entity. In that state he attains pure devotional service unto Me.”

The verse by Mādhavendra Purī could only be understood by himself, Lord Caitanya, and Rādhārāṇī. The verse describes the feelings that are manifested in Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī. After Kṛṣṇa left Vṛndāvana and went to Mathurā, Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī spoke to Uddhava in this mood of separation. One of the characteristics of this mood of separation is the feeling of being very poor and neglected. These feelings are in the highest ecstasy.

Mādhavendra Purī was the spiritual master of three prominent disciples: Advaita Ācārya, Lord Nityānanda, and Īśvara Purī. Nityānanda would accept only Mādhavendra Purī as His spiritual master. It is described in *Caitanya-bhāgavata* that when Nityānanda met Mādhavendra Purī while traveling to places of pilgrimage, He saw a manifestation of ecstasy of love of Godhead. The two of them embraced for a long time, being unable to stop. They began crying and rolling on the ground in ecstasy. Nityānanda could then understand that only Mādhavendra Purī had ecstatic love of Godhead. He therefore considered Mādhavendra Purī His spiritual master.

Advaita Ācārya also first met Mādhavendra Purī when He was traveling to places of pilgrimage. At that time, Advaita Ācārya was known as Kamalākṣa. He went to the village of Uḍupī. The followers of Madhvācārya resided in Uḍupī. Advaita Ācārya saw the followers of Madhvācārya, and He also saw Mādhavendra Purī. When He approached Madhva’s followers and heard the topics they were speaking, Advaita Ācārya manifested ecstatic symptoms. When the followers of Madhvācārya saw the ecstatic symptoms of Advaita Ācārya, they began to understand that He was highly advanced.

However, when Advaita Ācārya saw Mādhavendra Purī, He said, “I have been traveling throughout all the places of pilgrimage, waiting to see a person who has love of Godhead like you.” Advaita Ācārya thus submitted himself before Mādhavendra Purī and said, “I want to take instructions from you.” Mādhavendra Purī was very pleased by Advaita Ācārya. He taught Him Madhvācārya’s commentaries on the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. Advaita Ācārya memorized the entire discourses by hearing them, and people were overwhelmed by his powerful memory.

One day, after receiving these instructions from Mādhavendra Purī, Advaita Ācārya approached him and said, “Everywhere I look in this world, I see that there are no symptoms or manifestations of love of Godhead. Can you please tell me how it is possible that these living entities can manifest symptoms of love of Godhead? I see only the symptoms of Kali-yuga. No one is chanting the names of Kṛṣṇa. What needs to be done for these people to obtain love of Godhead?”

Mādhavendra Purī said to Advaita Ācārya, “I can understand that You are a very exalted soul. Only empowered souls can think of the welfare of all living beings.” He told Him that the Lord was going to appear as Caitanya Mahāprabhu because only the Lord Himself could distribute devotional service to everyone. He then told Him that there was a verse in the *Ananta-saṁhitā* that described the appearance of the Lord. He cited this verse as evidence of the appearance of Lord Gaurāṅga.

When Advaita Ācārya heard this from Mādhavendra Purī, He became very excited and said, “Can you please show Me this verse?” Thus, Mādhavendra Purī read Him the verse, which describes that the Lord would appear in Navadvīpa and distribute love of Godhead to all the fallen souls. When Advaita Ācārya heard this verse, He chanted the name of Gaurāṅga continuously for three hours. He then stated that He would do anything to get the association and the shelter of the lotus feet of Lord Caitanya, even if it meant breaking Vedic injunctions. That is another manifestation of those who are on the spontaneous platform of devotional service— they appear to act contrary to the Vedic injunctions.

Later, when Mādhavendra Purī was traveling to get sandalwood for Gopāla, he stopped at the house of Advaita Ācārya in Śāntipura. Advaita Ācārya was very happy to receive him. Advaita Ācārya knew well the glories of Mādhavendra Purī. At His home, Advaita Ācārya requested instructions. Mādhavendra Purī instructed Him to paint a picture of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī and to worship that picture so that He could render service in the mood of the residents of Vṛndāvana. He also instructed Advaita Ācārya to become a householder. He said, “You should become householder. You should marry because your progeny will expand the glories of devotional service.”

Advaita Ācārya replied, “But in the engagement of worshiping the Deity at home, it is possible to commit many offenses.”

Mādhavendra Purī said, “Do not fear. Engage in the worship of the Deity as I have instructed, and there will be no offenses committed by any of your progeny for fourteen generations.”

At that meeting Advaita Ācārya submitted Himself to receive initiation from Mādhavendra Purī. Mādhavendra Purī therefore gave Advaita Ācārya the Kṛṣṇa *mantra,* and shortly thereafter, Mādhavendra Purī left Śāntipura and continued his trip to get sandalwood. Advaita Ācārya did not want him to leave. He fell at his feet begging him, “Please, do not leave My home.” Mādhavendra Purī explained to Him that he was on a mission for the service of the Lord, and he could not remain any longer. That was Advaita Ācārya’s second meeting with Mādhavendra Purī.

**Text 11**

*śrī-īśvarapurī-rūpe aṅkura puṣṭa haila*  
*āpane caitanya-mālī skandha upajila*

**The seed of devotional service next fructified in the form of Śrī Īśvara Purī, and then the gardener Himself, Caitanya Mahāprabhu, became the main trunk of the tree of devotional service. (*****Cc. Ādi*** **9.11)**

Śrīla Īśvara Purī was the spiritual master of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, but before initiating Lord Caitanya he went to Navadvīpa and lived for a few months in the house of Gopīnātha Ācārya. At that time Lord Caitanya became acquainted with him, and it is understood that he served Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu by reciting his book, *Kṛṣṇa-līlāmṛta*. (*Cc. Ādi* 9.11, Purport)

Previously in our *sampradāya*, Lakṣmīpati (the spiritual master of Mādhavendra Purī) and others were followers of Madhvācārya and worshipped the Lord in awe and veneration. Mādhavendra Purī was the first one to manifest these symptoms of separation with the feelings of the *gopīs* of Vraja.

Lord Caitanya distributed the seed and it first fructified in Mādhavendra Purī. The fruits of love of Godhead became fully manifest in Mādhavendra Purī. It is described in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* that the next place the seed fructified was in Īśvara Purī, who was a disciple of Mādhavendra Purī. The seed of devotional service fructified in Īśvara Purī because of the service he rendered to his spiritual master, Mādhavendra Purī. When Mādhavendra Purī was elderly and invalid, Īśvara Purī would clean up his stool. His body was so invalid that he could not move, but yet he was always absorbed in feelings of ecstatic love of Godhead and was manifesting such feelings of separation. Īśvara Purī rendered this very meek and humble service very devotedly, every single day. Because of this service, Mādhavendra Purī bestowed his benediction and impregnated Īśvara Purī with the seed of love of Godhead. The fruit of love of Godhead then manifested in Īśvara Purī.

Lord Caitanya then received that seed back when He took initiation from Īśvara Purī and could then enjoy the fruit of the same seed that He planted.

When Mādhavendra Purī was preparing to depart from this world, he was offering prayers to the Lord in a mood of separation with the feelings of the *gopīs* of Vraja: “O my Lord, master of Mathurā, my agitated heart is feeling much separation from You; therefore, please tell me what I should do now.” Rāmacandra Purī understood his prayer in one way and Īśvara Purī understood it in another way. Rāmacandra Purī was infected by Māyāvāda philosophy, and when he heard Mādhavendra Purī speaking this prayer, he said to him, “Why are you lamenting? If you have realized Brahman, then there should be no need for lamentation whatsoever.” Kṛṣṇa says in *Bhagavad-gītā* (*Bg.* 18.54):

*brahma-bhūtaḥ prasannātmā*  
*na śocati na kāṅkṣati*  
*samaḥ sarveṣu bhūteṣu*  
*mad-bhaktiṁ labhate parām*

Kṛṣṇa says that one who achieves the stage of *brahmabhūtaḥ* does not hanker or lament. Thus, thinking in this way, Rāmacandra Purī could not recognize the ecstatic symptoms of love of Godhead that were manifested in his spiritual master, and therefore he criticized him.

Mādhavendra Purī said to him, “Get out of my sight! You are such an offender. If I leave my body seeing your face, then certainly I will not attain my destination.” He was very heavy with Rāmacandra Purī.

However, Īśvara Purī was just the opposite of Rāmacandra Purī. Īśvara Purī could understand what Mādhavendra Purī was feeling. He was intimately serving his spiritual master by reciting various verses to accompany Mādhavendra Purī’s particular ecstasies. He therefore rendered very personal service to Mādhavendra Purī by serving in this way and also by personally serving his body.

There is a very nice description in *Caitanya-bhāgavata* about Īśvara Purī. Before meeting Īśvara Purī, Lord Caitanya was a householder, living and teaching in Navadvīpa. Īśvara Purī would wander about in disguise, wearing the dress of an *ekadaṇḍi-sannyāsī.* He did not want to be recognized, wanting to hide himself from offenders. At that time, there were many people who were offensive towards Vaiṣṇavas, and although he was situated on the platform of ecstatic love for the Lord, he never manifested these symptoms before anybody except devotees. When hearing about Kṛṣṇa in the association of Vaiṣṇavas, he would not be able to control his emotions and ecstasies.

He then came to Navadvīpa, as all of the Lord’s associates who had taken birth in other places gradually did. The first place that Īśvara Purī wanted to go was to the house of Advaita Ācārya, who was also a disciple of Mādhavendra Purī. Mādhavendra Purī had many exalted disciples. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta describes how Īśvara Purī was naturally attracted to associate with Advaita Ācārya because it enabled him to remember his spiritual master, Mādhavendra Purī.

When Īśvara Purī went to Advaita Ācārya’s house, he found Him engaged in worshipping the Deity. Īśvara Purī immediately sat down next to Advaita Ācārya where He was doing His *pūjā*. By nature, a Vaiṣṇava may try to remain hidden in the presence of other Vaiṣṇavas, but it is not possible. While Advaita Ācārya was doing His *pūjā,* He looked over and saw this *ekadaṇḍi-sannyāsī* sitting next to Him. He looked at him again, and again, and again. He said, “Who are you? You do not look like an *ekadaṇḍi-sannyāsī*. I think you are a Vaiṣṇava *sannyāsī*.”

Īśvara Purī said very humbly with folded hands, “Actually, I am lower than a *śūdra*. I have simply come here to see Your lotus feet.” This is another example of the qualities of a Vaiṣṇava—the genuine feeling of being very low and fallen.

Standing nearby was Mukunda. It is described that Mukunda Datta used to sing wonderful songs about the Lord. Mukunda, understanding the whole situation, thus began singing. As soon as Īśvara Purī heard these songs about Kṛṣṇa, he began manifesting ecstatic symptoms of love for the Lord, and tears flowed from his eyes. He fell on the ground in uncontrollable ecstasy, and the more Mukunda continued to sing, the more Īśvara Purī’s ecstasy increased. Advaita Ācārya saw the ecstasy manifesting in Īśvara Purī, and He embraced him and placed him on His lap. Gradually, Advaita Ācārya’s whole body became drenched in Īśvara Purī’s tears. All the devotees who were present at the time could understand that such symptoms of love of Godhead could only be manifested by one who was an associate of Mādhavendra Purī. They came to learn that he was Īśvara Purī and they immediately remembered Kṛṣṇa by seeing and watching him. This is how Īśvara Purī first appeared in Navadvīpa at the house of Advaita Ācārya. He stayed in Navadvīpa for some time, but generally, he wandered around in disguise.

After visiting the home of Advaita Ācārya, while wandering in Navadvīpa, he happened to meet Lord Caitanya on the road. At this particular time, Lord Caitanya was a student and a teacher, as well as a householder. He was also in disguise; nobody recognized that He was the Supreme Personality of Godhead. When Īśvara Purī saw Lord Caitanya, he was greatly attracted by His features. When Lord Caitanya saw Īśvara Purī, recognizing His servant, He immediately got down on the ground and offered obeisances to Īśvara Purī. Lord Caitanya was in the dress of a householder, and Īśvara Purī was in the dress of a *sannyāsī,* so this was the proper etiquette. Lord Caitanya was always showing proper respect by offering obeisances to *sannyāsīs.*

After Nimāi Paṇḍita offered obeisances to him, Īśvara Purī was still very much attracted to the beautiful form of the Lord, and immediately asked, “Who are You? Where do You live? What are You studying, and what are You teaching?”

Lord Caitanya was surrounded by His students, who immediately spoke out, “He is Nimāi Paṇḍita!”

Īśvara Purī said, “So You are Nimāi Paṇḍita!” Īśvara Purī had heard much about the great scholarship of Nimāi Paṇḍita. He was very pleased to finally get the opportunity to meet Nimāi Paṇḍita, who had become quite famous. Lord Caitanya was a householder at the time of His first meeting with Īśvara Purī, and was therefore very eager to invite Īśvara Purī to His home for *prasāda.* Īśvara Purī thus came and entered the temple of Lord Viṣṇu at the house of Nimāi and then accepted some very wonderful *prasādam* that Śacīmātā had prepared for him.

After finishing *prasāda*, Īśvara Purī talked about Kṛṣṇa for the rest of the night. Nimāi Paṇḍita was very satisfied by seeing the symptoms of attachment to Lord Kṛṣṇa manifested in the body of Īśvara Purī. Īśvara Purī then left and continued wandering about Navadvīpa.

Īśvara Purī went to the home of Gopīnātha Ācārya, the brother-in-law of Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya, who had invited him to stay at his home. He stayed there for about three months. While there, he would regularly read from his book *Kṛṣṇa-līlāmṛta,* in which he had described many very intimate characteristics of Lord Kṛṣṇa. He read from *Kṛṣṇalīlāmṛta* every night.

At that time, Gadādhara Paṇḍita, who was a very young boy and who manifested qualities of renunciation in his childhood, evoked very strong affection for Īśvara Purī. Gadādhara Paṇḍita would go every evening and listen to *Kṛṣṇa-līlāmṛta* from Īśvara Purī. After His teachings and studies at school, Nimāi Paṇḍita would also go in the evening and spend time with Īśvara Purī. When He would come, He would first offer His obeisances to Īśvara Purī. He would then sit and listen to Īśvara Purī’s recitation of *Kṛṣṇa-līlāmṛta.*

Because of Nimāi’s scholarly abilities, Īśvara Purī asked Him, “Can You please tell me if there are some faults in my book, *Kṛṣṇa-līlāmṛta*? You are a very learned scholar. Certainly You must see some faults in the works that I have written.” At this time, Īśvara Purī did not know Lord Caitanya’s real identity as the Supreme Lord. He was also not aware that he was to be the spiritual master of Lord Caitanya, but Lord Caitanya was about to reveal this relationship, which would soon be manifest.

Īśvara Purī knew Lord Caitanya as the great logician and scholar who had defeated the great Keśava Kāśmīrī and had found faults in the famous poet’s one hundred verses glorifying the Ganges. Lord Caitanya, at that time, was extremely expert in argument. Therefore, Īśvara Purī was saying, “Please tell me, is there some fault in my writing?”

When Īśvara Purī made his request, Nimāi Paṇḍita said to him, “Whoever finds faults in a Vaiṣṇava’s descriptions of Lord Kṛṣṇa is certainly a sinner. Lord Kṛṣṇa is certainly pleased with His devotee’s poetry, even if it has some mistakes in it.”

Lord Caitanya then quoted one verse (*CB Ādi-khaṇḍa* 11.108)

*mūrkho vadati viṣṇāya*  
*dhīro vadati viṣṇave*  
*ubhayos tu samaṁ puṇyaṁ*  
*bhāva-grāhī janārdanaḥ*

This is a very famous verse describing that at the time of offering obeisances to Lord Viṣṇu, a foolish person chants “*viṣṇāya namaḥ*,” which is grammatically incorrect. However, a learned person chants “*viṣṇave namaḥ,*” which is the grammatically correct way of offering obeisances to Lord Viṣṇu. Nevertheless, both persons achieve equal piety by their offering of obeisances because the Lord is *bhāva-grāhī janārdana*. Lord Janārdana simply sees the sentiment of the living being. He sees the degree of devotion that is there in the heart and extracts only the essence of a person’s devotional attitude. Therefore, even if a fool says *viṣṇāya namaḥ*, yet says it with devotion, he achieves the same benefit as a learned person who says *viṣṇave namaḥ.*

Thus, Lord Caitanya used this verse in argument to say that He could not find fault in anything that Īśvara Purī had written: “Anyone who finds fault with a Vaiṣṇava is himself at fault, because a devotee’s descriptions of Lord Kṛṣṇa are always offered for the pleasure of the Lord. Therefore, who would dare find faults with your perfect devotional descriptions of Lord Kṛṣṇa?” Lord Caitanya would not do this. He said, “I will not go anywhere near this mentality.”

Īśvara Purī insisted, “No, it is okay. It is not finding fault. If You see some fault, please do not neglect it, rather, tell me.” Lord Caitanya said nothing. He continued to come every evening and listened to *Kṛṣṇa-līlāmṛta* for one to two hours. After some time, Lord Caitanya said to Īśvara Purī, “There is a mistake in the verbal root that you used in this phrase. You have used the word *ātmanepadī*, but this is not the proper form of the verb to use.” Lord Caitanya told him to think about this, and went home.

That night, after the Lord had returned to His home, Īśvara Purī, who was a very learned scholar, looked very carefully, from different angles of vision, at how this verb was being used. After a very thorough analysis, he came to the conclusion that the way it was used was correct.

When Lord Caitanya returned the following day, Īśvara Purī gave his explanation as to how it was the correct use of the verb. Lord Caitanya listened very, very carefully to his explanation and then said, “I have been defeated. You are right.” From that day on, He never found fault with anything of Īśvara Purī.

Vṛndāvana dāsa Ṭhākura describes that such is the nature of the Supreme Lord. The Lord did this because He wanted to establish that His devotees are victorious over Him.

Vṛndāvana dāsa Ṭhākura says, “The *Vedas* glorify the Lord, who is very eager to expand the glories of His devotees by allowing them to be victorious over Him.” Therefore, the Lord allowed Īśvara Purī to defeat Him. Nobody before had ever defeated Nimāi Paṇḍita.

The Lord was revealing the the relationship that would soon be established between Himself and Īśvara Purī.

Later, when Lord Caitanya went to Gayā and met Īśvara Purī again, they embraced each other, and the Lord fell at Īśvara Purī’s feet and asked him to please give Him Kṛṣṇa *mantra.* Thus, He took initiation from Īśvara Purī.

*Caitanya-bhāgavata* says that whoever hears these auspicious topics of Īśvara Purī lives at the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

## THE TRUNK, BRANCHES, AND SUBBRANCHES OF THE CAITANYA TREE

**Ādilīlā: 10.56–59**

**Riga—July 10, 2000**

Lord Caitanya distributes love of Godhead in three forms: *sākṣāt*, *āveśa*, and *āvirbhāva*. The first form, *sākṣāt,* is by His personal presence, which was how the spreading of love of Godhead was mostly done. Whenever anyone saw Lord Caitanya’s ecstatic manifestations of love, they also became influenced.

In the *āveśa* form, the Lord actually appears within another devotee who is empowered with His presence. *(*Referring to *Cc. Ādi* 10.56*)*

The example that is given in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* is that of Nakula Brahmacārī. He actually manifested all the symptoms of Lord Caitanya. Whenever anyone saw Nakula Brahmacārī, they would immediately feel the presence of Lord Caitanya. Just as Lord Caitanya had many hundreds of thousands of people gathered around Him, wanting to get His association, Nakula Brahmacārī similarly also attracted many hundreds of thousands of people. Externally, he appeared to be like a madman; he would sometimes fall on the ground in ecstasy, and tears would flow from his eyes. Anyone who saw him would also experience similar feelings of ecstasy and would also begin chanting.

There is a pastime in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* related to Nakula Brahmacārī, which describes how Śivānanda Sena had doubts about Lord Caitanya’s presence in the body of Nakula Brahmacārī. He heard from everybody that seeing Nakula Brahmacārī was as good as seeing Lord Caitanya. However, Śivānanda Sena did not want to go to him because he doubted this. He thus decided to go to the outskirts of the city where Nakula Brahmacārī was present. He said, “If Nakula Brahmacārī calls me and tells me what *mantra* I am chanting, I will accept what they say about him.”

Nakula Brahmacārī was surrounded by many followers, and he stopped at one moment and said to a few of them, “On the outskirts of this city is a devotee named Śivānanda Sena. He is waiting to be called by me. You should go find him and bring him here.” The devotees went to the outskirts of the city and began calling for Śivānanda Sena. When they found him, they told him that Nakula Brahmacārī was calling for him.

Śivānanda Sena was immediately thinking, “What is he calling me about?” He went to that place where Nakula Brahmacārī was sitting, and Nakula Brahmacārī said to him, “Oh, you have doubts?” Śivānanda Sena was startled and astonished. Immediately, Nakula Brahmacārī said, “You are chanting the four syllables of the Gopāla *mantra*.”

Śivānanda Sena could then understand that Lord Caitanya was present in Nakula Brahmacārī and immediately accepted him. He rendered service to him, just as he would render service to Lord Caitanya. This is one example of how Lord Caitanya manifests Himself in the form of *āveśa.*

The other form is called the *āvirbhāva* form. In this form, although the Lord manifests Himself before another devotee, not everybody can see Him.

It is described that Lord Caitanya appeared in the *āvirbhāva* form specifically in four places all the time: 1) where Lord Nityānanda was dancing, 2) in the altar room of Śacīmātā, 3) in the house of Śrīvāsa Paṇḍita during *kīrtana*, and 4) in the home of Rāghava Paṇḍita.

Śacīmātā would often prepare an offering to her Deities, and when she returned to get the plate, she would see that there was nothing on it. She would become a little bewildered. Externally she was bewildered, but internally she was very satisfied, though she did not know why she was so satisfied. Externally she would wonder, “Why is the plate empty?” She would go back and check the pots, and the pots would still be full. She would be thinking that maybe she forgot to make the offering, and so she would bring the plates back and put food on them again. This was going on regularly, but it was actually Lord Caitanya who was coming personally to eat the food that Śacīmātā was offering.

One time, Lord Caitanya told Dāmodara Paṇḍita, “You should tell My mother that when she goes to the altar and sees the food missing, it is because I am coming and eating it.” In this way, Śacīmātā was invoking the presence of the Lord, and Lord Caitanya would personally come and take the food that was offered by her.

Lord Caitanya would also appear before Lord Nityānanda. Nityānanda accepted the order that Lord Caitanya had given to Him to go to Bengal. When Lord Caitanya told Lord Nityānanda, “I will go wherever You are dancing, and I will appear in intervals,” He meant that He would appear in His *āvirbhāva* form*.*

The first place where Lord Nityānanda stopped was at the house of Rāghava Paṇḍita. Rāghava Paṇḍita received Lord Nityānanda and all His associates that were traveling with Him. This particular place is where Lord Nityānanda began His complete divine dispensation of ecstatic love of God (having received Lord Caitanya’s order to do so).

They would have *kīrtanas* at the home of Rāghava Paṇḍita. Lord Nityānanda, simply by glancing upon others, immediately would put them in ecstatic love of God. They became so ecstatic that they would forget to eat and sleep. The devotees went continuously for three months without eating. They were completely sustained on the spiritual platform. They lost all external consciousness.

The devotees celebrated Lord Nityānanda’s Vyāsa-pūjā at the home of Rāghava Paṇḍita. Everyone brought various offerings to Lord Nityānanda. They brought many pots of water from the Ganges for bathing Him. Lord Nityānanda then wanted to accept a garland. He told Rāghava Paṇḍita that He wanted a garland made specifically from the *kadamba* flowers, which were a type of very fragrant flowers that did not grow that time of the year. Rāghava Paṇḍita said, “How is this possible? These flowers do not grow this time of year.” Lord Nityānanda said, “Just go into your garden and look.

You may find these flowers somewhere.” Rāghava Paṇḍita went out, and growing on a lemon tree was a full blossom of these *kadamba* flowers. He immediately gathered the flowers from the tree, made a very nice garland, and came back and offered it to Lord Nityānanda. All the devotees began experiencing this wonderful fragrance that was emanating from the garland, and they were trying to find out where this fragrance was coming from. Suddenly, they also began to smell the enchanting fragrance of another flower. Lord Nityānanda asked, “You smell something, do you not?”

They replied, “We smell the very fragrant *damanaka* flowers, but we cannot see them.”

Lord Nityānanda told them, “You cannot see, but Lord Caitanya has appeared here because of our *kīrtana*. He has appeared here, assuming the form of a tree. He is present right now, and you smell the garland of *damanaka* flowers that He is wearing.”

Thus, Lord Nityānanda experienced that Lord Caitanya had come to participate in the *kīrtana,* but the other devotees could not see Him. Yet, wherever Lord Nityānanda was dancing, Lord Caitanya would go and participate. This is a manifestation of His *āvirbhāva* form.

Similarly, there was also the time when Lord Nityānanda saw Raghunātha Dāsa offering his obeisances from a distance. He called Raghunātha Dāsa before Him and said, “You are a thief because you are staying at a distance. You need to be punished. The punishment I am giving you is that you must prepare a festival for everyone that is present here and prepare chipped rice for all of them.”

Raghunātha Dāsa was the son of a wealthy landowner; thus he was also very wealthy. He was very ecstatic to receive this order from the Lord. He immediately went out and purchased ingredients for preparing chipped rice. They made hundreds of pots of chipped rice with condensed milk, and chipped rice with yogurt. When the local *brāhmaṇas* and priests heard about this festival that Raghunātha Dāsa was preparing, hundreds and hundreds of them came to take part in the festival.

When Raghunātha Dāsa saw all of them coming, he immediately went out and purchased more and more chipped rice, condensed milk, and yogurt. He fed all the *brāhmaṇas* and priests. He then went to some other merchants and bought all their ingredients in bulk. He fed chipped rice to the same merchants who had sold him ingredients. Everyone was lining up all along the banks of the Ganges River.

On a raised platform sat Lord Nityānanda and many of His confidential associates. They were all taking the chipped rice that was prepared by Raghunātha Dāsa. Below the platform, along the banks of the river, many hundreds of people had gathered. Everyone had one pot of chipped rice in yogurt and another pot of chipped rice in condensed milk. The whole bank was so full that some of the devotees could not even sit on the bank; they had to sit in the water. They were sitting in the water and had two pots of chipped rice. Everybody was having a wonderful time.

Rāghava Paṇḍita came to that place, and when he saw all the fun that everyone was having, he began laughing. Rāghava Paṇḍita brought some food that he had prepared for Lord Nityānanda. Rāghava Paṇḍita was very well known for his very devoted offerings to the Lord. He had such great devotion and cooked such wonderful preparations that Kṛṣṇa would personally come and eat his offerings.

In fact, Rāghava Paṇḍita, in *Kṛṣṇa-līlā*, is the *gopī* Dhaniṣṭhā, who used to cook for Kṛṣṇa. Thus, in Lord Caitanya’s pastimes, Rāghava Paṇḍita always cooked very wonderful preparations for the Lord, and his sister, Damayantī, also used to cook for Him. Damayantī was the one who cooked the bitter food for Lord Caitanya’s indigestion, which is described in *Antya-līlā*. She is also a *gopī* in *Kṛṣṇa*-*līlā* that cooks for Kṛṣṇa.

Thus, Rāghava Paṇḍita came to this festival, and he brought some *prasādam* for Lord Nityananda that he had offered to his Deity at home. He said to Lord Nityānanda, “I see that You are eating so much food, and I have brought this food for You. But it is just sitting here; You are not eating it.” Lord Nityānanda said, “I will come to your home tonight and eat. Today, I will eat with all of My friends. I am just a cowherd boy, and these are all my cowherd boyfriends. We are sitting on the banks of the Yamunā, enjoying our lunch together. I will eat here today, and tonight I will come and eat at your home.” Lord Nityānanda then said, “You should sit, and you should also take some chipped rice.” Rāghava Paṇḍita then also had two pots of chipped rice.

Lord Nityānanda then decided to bring Lord Caitanya to the festival to enjoy all of the fun. He began meditating on Lord Caitanya to invoke His presence at the festival. Lord Caitanya came, but not everyone could see Him. Only a few fortunate devotees could see Him.

Lord Nityānanda wanted to feed Lord Caitanya some chipped rice. He began reaching into the pots and taking handfuls of chipped rice. He was putting it in Lord Caitanya’s mouth, and the Lord was relishing this chipped rice. To participate in the same fun, Lord Caitanya also reached down, took a handful of rice, and put it in Lord Nityānanda’s mouth. They would go into each and every devotee’s pot, one at a time, and would take out one handful of rice and put it into each other’s mouth. All the devotees were watching Lord Nityānanda, thinking, “What is He doing?” They could not see Lord Caitanya. They just saw Lord Nityānanda taking from everybody’s pot, but they did not see what, rather whom, he was putting the rice into. In this way, Lord Nityānanda again invoked the presence of Lord Caitanya in His *āvirbhāva* form.

That night, Lord Nityānanda went together with Lord Caitanya to Rāghava Paṇḍita’s home. Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda sat down to take Their meal. At this particular time, Rāghava Paṇḍita could see Lord Caitanya, so he made an extra plate to offer Him. Lord Caitanya sat and ate the offering from Rāghava Paṇḍita. Every night thereafter, whenever Rāghava Paṇḍita made an offering, he would always make a plate for Lord Caitanya. Sometimes Lord Caitanya could be seen, but sometimes He could not be seen. Nevertheless, every night the plate was empty. Thus, Lord Caitanya appeared in the house of Rāghava Paṇḍita in His *āvirbhāva* form.

Another one of the four places where Lord Caitanya would appear was at the home of Śrīvāsa Paṇḍita. Sometimes He would also appear before a devotee. The example that is given in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* is that of Nṛsiṁhānanda Brahmacārī. Nṛsiṁhānanda Brahmacārī was a very great devotee of Lord Nṛsiṁhadeva. Actually, his name was Pradyumna Brahmacārī, but when Lord Caitanya saw his devotion to Nṛsiṁhadeva, He called him Nṛsiṁhānanda Brahmacārī.

Every year, all the devotees would go to Jagannātha Purī to see Lord Caitanya. The devotees from Navadvīpa and Śāntipura would all get together on the order of Lord Caitanya. Śivānanda Sena would make all the arrangements for the devotees for their journey to Purī. He would arrange for places for them to stay and for their food, personally taking the responsibility to see that all the needs of the party members were taken care of during the trip, including the needs of a dog, which he considered a member. Because of his kindness to the dog, the dog later went back to the spiritual world.

Every year, the devotees would go to see Lord Caitanya. They would take part in the Ratha-yātrā festival, stay for four months, and then return to Śāntipura and Navadvipa. Śrīkānta Sena, the nephew of Śivānanda Sena, came early one year and spent two months with Lord Caitanya. When it was time for Śrīkānta Sena to leave, Lord Caitanya told him, “When you return to Navadvīpa, please tell all the devotees that they should not come to Jagannātha Purī this year. Tell them that I will personally go there this year to see them, and nobody should come here.”

When Śrīkānta Sena returned, the first devotee he saw was Advaita Ācārya, who was getting ready to leave for Jagannātha Purī. Śrīkānta Sena said, “I just came from Jagannātha Purī, and Caitanya Mahāprabhu said that nobody should go to Purī this year. Lord Caitanya is personally coming to Navadvīpa this year.” When Advaita Ācārya heard this, He immediately became very blissful. “Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu is going to come here to see us!”

Very quickly, the word began to spread to all the Vaiṣṇavas: “Do not go to Jagannātha Purī; Lord Caitanya is coming to Navadvīpa.” Śivānanda Sena also became very happy to hear this. Lord Caitanya told him He would come in the month of December. Thus, when the month of December arrived, Śivānanda Sena and Jagadānanda began gathering preparations to receive Lord Caitanya. Each day, they would be waiting and asking, “When is Lord Caitanya going to arrive?” Day after day would pass; practically the whole month of December had passed. They were becoming very morose. They were thinking, “Lord Caitanya said He was coming, but He is not coming.”

One day, Nṛsiṁhānanda Brahmacārī arrived in Navadvīpa and saw Śivānanda Sena and Jagadānanda, who were very morose. He asked, “Why is your heart so heavy? What is the matter?”

They began to explain, “Lord Caitanya said He was going to come, and we are preparing for His arrival, but He is not coming.”

Nṛsiṁhānanda Brahmacārī said, “Oh, you want Lord Caitanya to come? I will bring Him here. In three days, Lord Caitanya will come here.”

Nṛsiṁhānanda Brahmacārī then sat down and began meditating on bringing Lord Caitanya to Navadvīpa. After two days, he said to Śivānanda Sena, “Quick, start gathering all the ingredients for cooking a feast. Get as much as you can possibly get. Today I will begin preparing a feast for the Lord. I have already brought Him as far as Pānihāṭi, and tomorrow, at noon, He will be here.”

When Śivānanda Sena and Jagadānanda heard this, they became very lively. They began getting ingredients. Nṛsiṁhānanda Brahmacārī began cooking a wonderful feast. The following day, he made three plates—one for Lord Jagannātha, one for Lord Nṛsiṁhadeva, and one for Lord Caitanya. He placed these three plates of food down and began meditating on offering them to the Lord. While he was meditating, Lord Caitanya appeared and saw the three plates; therefore, He sat down and ate from all three.

When Nṛsiṁhānanda Brahmacārī returned and saw the Lord eating from all three plates, internally he was very happy and satisfied. At the same time, he was also very disturbed because his worshipful Deity was Lord Nṛsiṁhadeva. He was thinking, “I can understand if Lord Caitanya eats from His plate and Lord Jagannātha’s plate, because Lord Caitanya and Lord Jagannātha are non-different. But why did He have to eat from Lord Nṛsiṁhadeva’s plate? Lord Nṛsiṁhadeva will have to fast today! If my master fasts, how can I have peace of mind?” Nṛsiṁhānanda Brahmacārī did not have complete realization and faith that Lord Caitanya and Lord Nṛsiṁhadeva were the same. Therefore, Lord Caitanya wanted to show him this by eating from all three plates. Lord Caitanya then left.

Nṛsiṁhānanda Brahmacārī turned to Śivānanda Sena and said, “See what your Caitanya Mahāprabhu has done?! He has eaten three plates! I can understand why He has eaten from two plates, but why has He eaten from the third plate? It is Lord Nṛsiṁhadeva’s!”

Śivānanda Sena was thinking, “What is he saying? Did it actually happen, or is he experiencing ecstatic love of Godhead and does not know what is happening?” He was asking this because he did not see anything. All Śivānanda Sena saw was Nṛsiṁhānanda Brahmacārī speaking this way and the empty plates. He did not see how the plates became empty. Therefore, they did not know that Lord Caitanya had appeared and had come to that place where Nṛsiṁhānanda Brahmacārī said he would bring Him.

The following year, all the devotees went to Purī and were sitting in the presence of the Lord, who immediately began speaking about Nṛsiṁhānanda Brahmacārī to Śivānanda Sena and all the other Vaiṣṇavas. He said, “Last year, the devotees thought that I did not come, but I came and ate all three plates that Nṛsiṁhānanda Brahmacārī offered.” When all the devotees heard the Lord speaking about His eating the offering of Nṛsiṁhānanda Brahmacārī, they could understand that Lord Caitanya actually did come to Navadvīpa and appeared before them. They all experienced great ecstasy. This is another example of how the Lord appeared in His *āvirbhāva* form, although it was one time in the presence of Nṛsiṁhānanda Brahmacārī.

These are different ways that Lord Caitanya appears— *sākṣāt*, when He personally appears; *āveśa*, when He personally appears in an empowered devotee; and *āvirbhāva,* when He personally appears before a particular devotee. In this way, the Lord is very much inclined to satisfy the desires of His devotees. On the strength of the devotee’s love, the devotee can actually invoke the presence of the Lord.

**Ādi-līlā: 10.134**

**Moscow—May 17, 1995**

**Text 134**

*ei pañca putra tomāra mora priyapātra*  
*rāmānanda saha mora deha-bheda mātra*

**Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu told Bhavānanda Rāya \[the father of Rāmānanda Rāya\], “Your five sons are all My dear devotees. Rāmānanda Rāya and I are one, although our bodies are different.”**

**Purport**

The *Gaura-gaṇoddeśa-dīpikā* (120–24) states that Rāmānanda Rāya was formerly Arjuna. He is also considered to have been an incarnation of the *gopī* Lalitā, although in the opinion of others he was an incarnation of Viśākhādevī. He was a most confidential devotee of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu said, “Although I am a *sannyāsī*, My mind is sometimes perturbed when I see a woman. But Rāmānanda Rāya is greater than Me, for he is always undisturbed, even when he touches a woman.” Only Rāmānanda Rāya was endowed with the prerogative to touch a woman in this way; no one should imitate him. Unfortunately, there are rascals who imitate the activities of Rāmānanda Rāya. We need not discuss them further. (…)

Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu advised Pradyumna Miśra to learn the science of Kṛṣṇa from Śrī Rāmānanda Rāya. As Subala always assisted Kṛṣṇa in His dealings with Rādhārāṇī in *kṛṣṇa-līlā*, so Rāmānanda Rāya assisted Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu in His feelings of separation from Kṛṣṇa. Śrī Rāmānanda Rāya was the author of the *Jagannātha-vallabha-nāṭaka*. (*Cc. Ādi* 10.134)

This verse and purport speaks a little bit about Rāmānanda Rāya. He was certainly one of the most confidential associates of Lord Caitanya. Lord Caitanya used Rāmānanda Rāya in many instructive ways. As we know, the Lord sometimes uses His very intimate associates to teach others. Śrīla Prabhupāda often quoted a very well-known verse that Lord Caitanya spoke to Rāmānanda Rāya (*Cc. Madhya* 8.128):

*kibā vipra, kibā nyāsī, śūdra kene naya*  
*yei kṛṣṇa-tattva-vettā, sei ‘guru’ haya*

It does not matter whether a person is a *vipra* (learned scholar in Vedic wisdom), born in a lower family, or in the renounced order of life, if he is a master in the science of Kṛṣṇa, he is the perfect and bona fide spiritual master. Lord Caitanya spoke this directly to Rāmānanda Rāya, who externally appeared to be a *śūdra.*

When Lord Caitanya wanted to learn the confidential topics of Lord Kṛṣṇa, He claimed His own inability to speak about such topics. He said, “I cannot possibly speak about such confidential topics, but Rāmānanda Rāya can.” Lord Caitanya personally heard the most confidential topics from the lips of Rāmānanda Rāya, rather than speaking them Himself.

In fact, when Pradyumna Miśra went to Lord Caitanya to hear about the confidential topics of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes with the *gopīs,* the Lord said, “I cannot speak about such subjects. If you want to hear about these subjects, you have to go to Rāmānanda Rāya.” When Pradyumna Miśra went to Rāmānanda Rāya, he saw that he was absorbed in preparing for a dramatic performance. He was training some *devī dāsīs* how to enact the confidential pastimes of Lord Kṛṣṇa in a dramatic performance, and every day he was massaging their bodies with oil. He was actually thinking he was a maidservant, rendering service to the *gopīs*. He was in no way whatsoever materially affected by doing this. It is described that his mind and senses were like a stone, completely unaffected by this behavior. Śrīla Prabhupāda said that only Rāmānanda Rāya could do this. Rāmānanda Rāya was always absorbed in this mood.

However, when Pradyumna Miśra went to hear from Rāmānanda Rāya, he saw his behavior and became a little bewildered. He again went back to Lord Caitanya and said, “I want to hear about the topics of Lord Kṛṣṇa,” and he described what he saw when he went to see Rāmānanda Rāya.

Lord Caitanya replied, “You must go back and hear from him, and you must tell him that I have sent you.” Thus, Pradyumna Miśra went back again and told Rāmānanda Rāya that Lord Caitanya had sent him to hear from him. As soon as Rāmānanda Rāya heard Lord Caitanya’s name, he became overwhelmed with ecstatic symptoms, explaining how the Lord is very kind to think of him in this way—capable of speaking about such confidential topics.

He responded, “Actually, I am not qualified to speak about it at all, but since He is ordering me to speak, I must. Nevertheless, you should know that my voice is simply like a string instrument, and He makes me vibrate according to His will. Therefore, although I will speak about such confidential topics, you should know that it is not me who is speaking; it is Lord Caitanya who is speaking through me.”

All day long, Rāmānanda Rāya described very, very confidential topics to Pradyumna Miśra, and Pradyumna Miśra could immediately realize what an exalted personality Rāmānanda Rāya was. He was overwhelmed with ecstasy after hearing such confidential topics. When he returned to Caitanya Mahāprabhu, he emphatically declared that he was very satisfied from hearing such topics from the mouth of Rāmānanda Rāya. He fell at the feet of the Lord and thanked Him for instructing him in this way.

This is how Lord Caitanya engaged His confidential devotee, Rāmānanda Rāya. The Lord personally heard the confidential topics from the mouth of Rāmānanda Rāya, and He also sent others to hear from him, even though he was externally a *śūdra*. Rāmānanda Rāya was actually from the *kāyastha* class, which, in the area of Orissa, was considered to be like *śūdras*. Although the *kāyastha*s were engaged in intellectual and political work, in Bengal they were considered to be like *kṣatriyas*, and in Orissa they were considered to be like *śūdras.*

Since Rāmānanda Rāya served in the ministry of Mahārāja Pratāparudra, he was considered a *śūdra*. He was also a governor for Mahārāja Pratāparudra; under his administration he served in South India. This was the work that Rāmānanda Rāya was engaged in prior to his meeting Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Just before Lord Caitanya began His journey to South India, He converted Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya to Vaiṣṇavism. When Sārvabhauma became a great Vaiṣṇava, he thought about his previous meeting with Rāmānanda Rāya. He was thinking, “Previously I met Rāmānanda Rāya, and I thought him to be a low-class *kāyastha* and a governor in the administration of Pratāparudra Mahārāja. In such a fallen condition I did not recognize his great saintly qualities.” Sārvabhauma said to Lord Caitanya, “Now, by Your mercy, I can understand that Rāmānanda Rāya is a very highly exalted personality.” He fell at the feet of the Lord and begged Him, “Now You are preparing to go to South India. When You go to the region where Rāmānanda Rāya is living, You will see that every day he goes to the banks of the Godāvarī River to take his bath. I beg You to please go to that place and meet with him.”

Thus, when Lord Caitanya traveled to South India, He remembered this request of Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya, and He went to meet Rāmānanda Rāya. *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* elaborately describes the first meeting between Lord Caitanya and Rāmānanda Rāya. During their first meeting, it was impossible for them to contain their love for one another, although they tried very hard due to the presence of some *brāhmaṇas* who could not understand the intimate exchanges that were being experienced by Lord Caitanya and Rāmānanda Rāya.

After their first meeting, they decided when they would next meet with each other. It was at that meeting that Rāmānanda Rāya spoke the confidential topics of Kṛṣṇa and Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī to Lord Caitanya. Thus, Lord Caitanya used Rāmānanda Rāya to teach the instruction, “Whether one is a *brāhmaṇa*, a *sannyāsī,* or a *śūdra*—regardless of what social or spiritual class one is in—he can become a spiritual master if he knows the science of Kṛṣṇa.” (*Cc. Madhya* 8.128) Rāmānanda Rāya acted as an instructor to Lord Caitanya and spoke the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

There are other examples of how Lord Caitanya used Rāmānanda Rāya, such as when Mahārāja Pratāparudra was very anxious to take the *darśana* of Lord Caitanya. Lord Caitanya was very strict and rigid. He would not grant permission for Mahārāja Pratāparudra to come see Him because Mahārāja Pratāparudra was a king. Meeting with a king could be very bad for a *sannyāsī’s* reputation.

Pratāparudra Mahārāja had many times begged the intimate devotees of Lord Caitanya to arrange a meeting for him; Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya and Rāmānanda Rāya were afraid to ask Lord Caitanya again. Lord Caitanya had even said, “If you ask Me one more time, I will leave Jagannātha Purī and never come back!” When Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya heard this, he was very anxious. He was thinking, “How could we possibly tolerate separation from Caitanya Mahāprabhu?” They made many different attempts, but Lord Caitanya refused each time.

It is described in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* that one of the reasons why Lord Caitanya finally agreed to meet with Pratāparudra Mahārāja was because of the service that the king had rendered to Rāmānanda Rāya. Rāmānanda Rāya, who was a governor for Mahārāja Pratāparudra, had approached him and said, “I want to renounce all of my duties; I want to give them up completely. I want to dedicate my whole life to the service of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. I beg you to please grant me permission and release me from these responsibilities as governor.”

Sanātana Gosvāmī also wanted to do the same thing. However, when he went to Nawab Hussain Shah and said, “I want to give up my duties,” he was immediately arrested.

When Rāmānanda Rāya went to Mahārāja Pratāparudra with his request, tears appeared in the eyes of the king as soon as he heard the name of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. He said, “Certainly, I will give you permission and release you from all your responsibilities.” He even granted him a full pension for the rest of his life. He did this so that Rāmānanda Rāya could take shelter of the lotus feet of Lord Caitanya.

When Rāmānanda Rāya told this to Lord Caitanya in Jagannātha Purī, the Lord’s heart immediately softened. He was thinking, “Because Pratāparudra Mahārāja has rendered service to you, I cannot forget his service.” Thus, the Lord’s heart softened to the point that He began to consider how He might agree to a meeting. He said, “Certainly, because of the service Pratāparudra Mahārāja has rendered, there is no stopping Kṛṣṇa’s mercy for him. Somehow or other his desires will be fulfilled.”

This is another example of how Lord Caitanya showed the importance of His confidential servant, Rāmānanda Rāya. Any service rendered to him immediately captured the attention of Caitanya Mahāprabhu. This was one of the events that led to Pratāparudra Mahārāja’s opportunity to finally get the *darśana* of Lord Caitanya.

These are some of the pastimes of Rāmānanda Rāya and Lord Caitanya. There are many more details given in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*. We have simply spoken about some of Rāmānanda Rāya’s general characteristics, which are very significant characteristics to remember. As explained in this purport, he was also a very confidential servant in *kṛṣṇa-līlā*, as were many of Lord Caitanya’s associates. It is described here that Rāmānanda Rāya is the *gopī* Viśākhā devī. It is also described here that Subala always assisted Kṛṣṇa in His dealings with Rādhārāṇī, and Rāmānanda Rāya assisted Lord Caitanya in His feelings of separation from Kṛṣṇa.

## THE EXPANSIONS OF ADVAITA ĀCĀRYA AND GADĀDHARA PAṆḌITA

**Ādi-līlā: 12.28–55**

**St. Petersburg—July 5, 1996**

**Advaita Ācārya’s very confidential servant named Kamalākānta Viśvāsa knew all the dealings of Advaita Ācārya. When Kamalākānta Viśvāsa was in Jagannātha Purī, he sent a note through someone to Mahārāja Pratāparudra. No one knew of that note, but somehow or other it reached the hands of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. That note established Advaita Ācārya as an incarnation of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. But it also mentioned that Advaita Ācārya had recently incurred a debt of about three hundred rupees that Kamalākānta Viśvāsa wanted to liquidate. Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu became unhappy upon reading the note, although His face still shone as brightly as the moon. Thus, smiling, He spoke as follows. “He has established Advaita Ācārya as an incarnation of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. There is nothing wrong in this, for He is indeed the Lord Himself. But he has made the incarnation of Godhead a povertystricken beggar. Therefore I shall punish him in order to correct him.” (***Cc. Ādi* **12.28–35)**

In the purport to verse thirty-five, Prabhupāda explains that presenting a poverty-stricken man as an incarnation of God is contradictory. It is the greatest offense. Prabhupāda explains that Māyāvādī philosophers spoil Vedic culture by preaching that everyone is God. They describe that a poverty-stricken man is *daridra-nārāyaṇa,* or poor Nārāyaṇa. Lord Caitanya never accepted such foolish, unauthorized ideas.

Māyāvādīs, who say that Lord Nārāyaṇa appears as an impoverished beggar, preach this philosophy as a device to get the people to engage in philanthropic work. They say that the Supreme Lord Nārāyaṇa is everywhere; we are all Nārāyaṇa, including poor beggars. Therefore, everybody should give charity to the poor Nārāyaṇa. In many places throughout *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, Śrīla Prabhupāda has written in his purports that describing the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the reservoir of all opulence, as poor is very inaccurate. Although the Supreme Lord may assume the position of being a beggar, He does not require anything.

In the purport, Śrīla Prabhupāda explains that the Lord appeared as Vāmanadeva to beg some charity from Bali Mahārāja, but it was a device to favor His devotee. By begging three steps of land from Bali Mahārāja, he caused Bali Mahārāja to surrender everything to Him. The Māyāvādīs use this philosophy of making the Lord a beggar to fulfill another purpose.

Lord Caitanya is stating here that He is going to punish Kamalākānta Viśvāsa (*Cc. Ādi* 12.36):

**The Lord ordered Govinda, “From today on, do not allow that** *bāuliyā* **Kamalākānta Viśvāsa to come here.”**

The *bāuliyā* sect is unauthorized; it is one of the different *apasampradāyas*. Because Kamalākānta Viśvāsa presented Advaita Ācārya as a poverty-stricken beggar, the Lord is referring to Kamalākānta Viśvāsa as a *bāuliyā.*

**When Kamalākānta Viśvāsa heard about this punishment by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, he was very unhappy, but when Advaita Prabhu heard about it, He was greatly pleased.**

**Purport**

In the *Bhagavad-gītā* (9.29) the Lord says, *samo* *’haṁ sarva-bhūteṣu na me dveṣyo ’sti na priyaḥ*: “I envy no one, nor am I partial to anyone. I am equal to all.” The Supreme Personality of Godhead being equal to everyone, no one can be His enemy, nor can anyone be His friend. Since everyone is a part or son of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the Lord cannot be partial and regard someone as a friend and someone as an enemy. Thus when Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu punished Kamalākānta Viśvāsa by no longer allowing him to come into His presence, although the punishment was actually very hard on him, Śrī Advaita Prabhu, understanding the inner meaning of such punishment, was happy because He appreciated that the Lord had actually favored Kamalākānta Viśvāsa. Therefore He was not at all unhappy. Devotees should always be happy with all the dealings of their master, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. A devotee may be put into difficulty or opulence, but he should accept both as gifts of the Supreme Personality of Godhead and jubilantly engage in the service of the Lord in all circumstances. (*Cc. Ādi* 12.37)

Here it is described that internally Advaita Ācārya was very happy to see how Lord Caitanya was dealing with Kamalākānta. Kamalākānta Viśvāsa, however, was very unhappy to hear about such a punishment. Nevertheless, as Śrīla Prabhupāda explains, when the Lord has dealings with His devotees—whether He is punishing or benedicting—it is all mercy for the devotee. He says that the devotee should always be happy with the dealings of his master. This means that however the Lord acts toward His devotee, the devotee should be happy that he has been dealt with at all. The mere fact that he has been dealt with is a benediction.
