Two other pastimes also convey this theme: one is when Mukunda was chastised, and the other is when Advaita Ācārya wanted to be chastised. Advaita Ācārya was preaching Māyāvādī philosophy so that he could evoke the punishment of the Lord. In another pastime, Mukunda Datta appeared to be the object of indifference by Lord Caitanya. When devotees saw that Lord Caitanya appeared to be indifferent toward Mukunda Datta, they approached the Lord and asked, “What has he possibly done to earn such indifference on Your part?” They begged the Lord, “If Mukunda Datta has committed an offense, then please punish him. But indifference? What could he possibly have done to receive indifference?”

The Lord, being very much inclined to give benedictions, gave boons to all the devotees. Sometimes He would invite all the devotees to come before Him and ask whatever benediction they wanted. At this particular time, one devotee asked Lord Caitanya, “Please forgive my father for his wrong actions.” Another devotee asked, “Please give me pure devotion to the lotus feet of my spiritual master.” The devotees thus asked for different benedictions from the Lord. One by one, the Lord fulfilled all their requests.

However, while this was going on, Mukunda was standing behind the curtain, and Lord Caitanya purposely did not call him, although He had called everyone else before Him and asked, “What benediction do you want?” When the devotees saw this behavior of the Lord, their minds were very agitated, and they said, “You like listening to Mukunda’s wonderful voice and *kīrtana* so much. Whenever the Vaiṣṇavas hear Mukunda chanting, their hearts melt. He displays great devotion. He is standing, waiting to be called by You, but You are being indifferent towards him.” They specifically asked, “Why indifference? If he has committed an offense, then punish him.”

Lord Caitanya then said, “Mukunda is two-faced.” He did not actually use this word, but He referred to him being like a chameleon, which is a type of lizard that changes color. He said, “All of you think that Mukunda is filled with devotion. He appears devotional when he is in our company, but I have heard that he goes to other places and associates with Māyāvādīs. He goes and hears topics from speakers in *apasampradāyas*, and he appears to be very devoted to them as well. Therefore, on one hand, Mukunda appears to be very humble with a blade of straw in his teeth, but on the other hand, he is holding an iron mace and always beating My body with it. Any philosophy or doctrine which preaches against the principles of devotional service to the Lord is like taking an iron mace and beating Me all over My body.” The Lord went on to describe how Mukunda Datta would associate in these different circles of people.

When Mukunda heard these statements from the Lord, he began crying, and soon streams of tears flowed from his eyes. Such statements were like thunderbolts to his heart. He then decided, “If the Lord is not going to call me before Him, maybe I should find out when I will be able to see Him.” He therefore asked Śrīvāsa Paṇḍita, “Please ask the Lord when I will see Him.” He was thinking that because of his past offenses, it might be a long time before the Lord would see him.

The Lord had previously told the devotees, “He will not see Me again.” When Mukunda heard the words, “He will not see Me,” he was thinking that he had to give up his life.

Then, when he asked Śrīvāsa Paṇḍita to speak to the Lord, Śrīvāsa asked, “Mukunda wants to know when he will be able to see You again.”

The Lord said, “He will see Me after ten million births.” As soon as Mukunda heard the words, “He will see Me,” he began jumping and dancing in great ecstasy. That was all he needed to hear. The ten million births were irrelevant to him. He became completely oblivious to what was going on around him. When the Lord saw how Mukunda Datta was behaving in response to His chastisement, He said, “Bring Mukunda Datta before Me immediately.”

However, Mukunda Datta was so much absorbed that he could not hear anything. All the devotees were calling him, “The Lord wants to see you, the Lord wants to see you,” but he could not hear. The devotees had to pick him up and carry him before the Lord. When he beheld the form of the Lord, he fainted in ecstasy.

The Lord told Mukunda, “Rise, Mukunda. Immediately you must rise. Because of your faith in My words when I said, ‘He will see Me,’ all your offenses have been completely eradicated. From this day on, even if you commit thousands of offenses, I will not take them as offenses. Because of your deep faith, you have captured Me and made Me a prisoner in your heart. I will never be able to leave your heart.”

There is much more to this pastime. Mukunda Datta spoke many wonderful prayers. This pastime displays how the Lord is always anxious to show mercy to His devotees. Thus, we should always be happy with whatever dealings the Lord has with us.

Mukunda Datta received the chastisement of the Lord as a great benediction. In fact, when the Lord heard the prayers offered by Mukunda Datta, He said, “Mukunda Datta, I will fulfill your desire. Every time that I appear in this world, you will take birth as a singer and will take part in *kīrtana* with Me.” In this way, the Lord bestowed His mercy upon Mukunda Datta.

Śrīla Vṛndāvana dāsa Ṭhākura describes, “Anyone who becomes happy upon hearing the recitation of this pastime and the prayers of Śrīla Mukunda will also take part in these *kīrtanas*.” Thus, certainly the Lord’s dealings towards his devotee are ultimately always a benediction. We see many examples of how devotees were ultimately favored when they accepted the chastisement of the Lord.

Another example is the *brahmacārī* who hid in the *kīrtana* in the house of Śrīvāsa Paṇḍita and was chastised. Lord Caitanya spoke out very openly against the behavior of this *brahmacārī*: “What does he think his qualifications are to see My *kīrtana* pastimes? Simply because he performed some austerities and drinks milk, he is not sufficiently qualified to see My *kīrtana* performance.” He spoke very strong words about this *brahmacārī*, and this *brahmacārī* was thinking, “What mercy!” When the Lord understood the heart of the *brahmacārī*, He immediately called him back, placed His lotus feet on his head, and gave him the benediction to take part in the *kīrtana*. *\[Details of this pastime are given in the next lecture.\]*

The following statement clarifies this point (*Cc. Ādi* 12.38):

**Seeing Kamalākānta Viśvāsa unhappy, Advaita Ācārya Prabhu told him, “You are greatly fortunate to have been punished by the Supreme Lord, the Personality of Godhead, Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu.”**

**Purport**

This is an authoritative judgment by Śrī Advaita Prabhu. He clearly advises that one should not be unhappy when reverses come upon him by the order of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. A devotee should always be happy to receive the fortune awarded him by the Supreme Lord, which seems pleasant or unpleasant according to one’s judgment.

The Lord’s dealings with His devotees, even chastisement, are ultimately always for the benefit of His devotee. The Lord knows how to bring His devotee closer to Him better than anybody possibly could. A devotee should simply see chastisement as the Lord’s mercy and be happy with any dealings from his master. There is another example here (*Cc. Ādi* 12.39):

**Formerly Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu always respected Me as His senior, but I did not like such respect. Therefore, My mind being afflicted by unhappiness, I made a plan.**

Here, Advaita Ācārya is speaking about the time when the Lord punished Him for preaching *Yoga-vāśiṣṭha* philosophy. Advaita Ācārya was a disciple of Mādhavendra Purī, who was also the spiritual master of Lord Caitanya’s spiritual master, Īśvara Purī. Lord Caitanya would always deal with Advaita Ācārya with great respect, but Advaita Ācārya thought Himself to be the servant of the Lord. He became disturbed due to always being respected by the Lord. He felt that He should always be giving respect to the Lord, rather than the Lord always respecting Him. He therefore devised a way to get punishment from Lord Caitanya. He decided to start preaching Māyāvādī philosophy: “Devotion to the Lord can only be achieved through impersonal philosophy.” He began regularly giving lectures on impersonal philosophy and thought, “Certainly if Lord Caitanya hears Me preaching this impersonal philosophy, He will punish Me.”

Once, Lord Caitanya was walking throughout the town of Navadvīpa with Lord Nityānanda, and They went to the house of Advaita Ācārya. Lord Caitanya immediately went inside and grabbed Him. Advaita Ācārya’s wife was there, and she was trying to understand what was happening. Lord Caitanya began slapping Advaita Ācārya. Acyutānanda, His son, said, “What are you doing to my father?”

The whole time, Advaita Ācārya had a big smile on His face. He was in complete ecstasy as He was being beaten by the Lord. The Lord threw Him on the ground. Advaita Ācārya was thinking, “This is the perfection of My life! This is exactly what I wanted—to be chastised.” He was considering it to be wonderful mercy and a great benediction: “To be chastised like this by the Lord is the greatest benediction.” Advaita Ācārya was saying that a similar punishment had also been awarded to Mother Śacī-devī. “Who could be more fortunate than she, to receive such a punishment?”

There was another time when Lord Caitanya was giving benedictions to all of His devotees. He would sometimes do this, adopting the attitude of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Sometimes in the middle of *kīrtana*, He would sit on the altar and say, “I am the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Ask any benediction that you would like from Me.” Of course, the next moment He could be crying in the mood of separation from Kṛṣṇa, demonstrating the mood of a devotee of the Lord.

At this particular time when Lord Caitanya was bestowing benedictions, Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura approached the Lord and said, “I ask for the benediction that Śacīmātā be bestowed with pure love of Godhead.”

When Lord Caitanya heard this request, He said, “Do not ask that from Me! She will not be able to get pure love of God for millions of births because she is an offender.”

When the devotees heard this statement from the Lord, they were shocked. They were thinking, “Śacīmātā is the mother of the Supreme Personality of Godhead; He appeared from her womb! How is it that she is being deprived of such a benediction?”

The Lord went on to explain that Śacīmātā would never receive pure love of Godhead because she had offended Advaita Ācārya. One time she called Advaita Ācārya “*dvaita.*” She said, “His name is Advaita, which means non-dual, but actually He is *dvaita* because He is so duplicitous. He presents Himself as a well-wisher, but actually, He is trying to steal my son who is my only source of life.” She did not even say this directly to Advaita Ācārya; she just said it to herself. Nevertheless, because she said it, the Lord said that she would never get love of Godhead.

The devotees asked, “How is it possible that she will never get love of Godhead?”

Lord Caitanya said, “The only way that she can get love of Godhead is if she goes to Advaita Ācārya and takes the dust from His lotus feet.”

The devotees therefore went to speak to Advaita Ācārya. They said, “Please, You have to be merciful to Śacīmātā. We are coming here on her behalf, begging You. The only way that she can be relieved of her offense is if she gets the dust from Your lotus feet.”

As soon as Advaita Ācārya heard the name “Śacīmātā,” ecstatic symptoms began manifesting in His body. He said, “Śacīmātā take the dust from My feet?! She is the embodiment of devotion. From her womb the Supreme Personality of Godhead has appeared in this world. How could I ever let her take the dust from My feet?! I will go to hell if she takes the dust from My feet!” Advaita Ācārya was not about to accept. While speaking the name and glories of Śacīmātā, He fell to the ground in ecstasy.

As soon as He fell to the ground, the devotees turned to Śacīmātā and said, “Look, there are His feet! Quick, go get the dust!” Thus, Śacīmātā immediately dove on the ground, grabbed the dust from the feet of Advaita Ācārya and began smearing it on her body. When she began smearing the dust from Advaita Ācārya’s feet on her body, she also began experiencing ecstatic symptoms, falling to the ground and losing consciousness.

The Lord then said, “Yes, now Śacīmātā has been relieved from the offense that she had committed. Now she has been bestowed with pure love of Godhead because she has taken the dust from the lotus feet of Advaita Ācārya.”

It seemed that the Lord’s dealings were very harsh. But Śacīmātā was thinking, “I must do something to clear myself from this offense. How will I ever get the dust from the lotus feet of Advaita Ācārya unless He allows me to take it?” So she also accepted this chastisement as the mercy of the Lord, even though she had committed a very, very slight offense. The omniscient Lord knew the comment she had made, and He wanted to establish that one has to be very careful about criticism of a Vaiṣṇava.

The reason why she made that statement is also described. Śacīmātā had two sons, Viśvambhara and Viśvarūpa. Viśvambhara was Lord Caitanya, and Viśvarūpa was His elder brother. Advaita Ācārya, at one time, used to deliver lectures from *Yoga-vāśiṣṭha*. However, He would take the impersonal teachings and describe them in terms of devotion to the Lord; they were very profound discourses. The only solace that Viśvarūpa had in His life was to go and hear these lectures from Advaita Ācārya. Viśvarūpa would speak to others, but He would see that nobody else had devotion in their hearts. Everywhere He looked, He saw that people were absorbed in mundane rituals, logic, and argument. He would always think of going to the forest because He could not tolerate the state of affairs of society. However, He found shelter in hearing the discourses of Advaita Ācārya. Everyday, He would go and listen to Him speak.

When Viśvarūpa would sit and listen to these discourses, Śacīmātā would send young Nimāi, who was just a little child, to get Viśvarūpa. She would tell Him, “Go get Your brother. He is listening to the lectures of Advaita Ācārya.” Little Nimāi would go to that place where all the sages had gathered.

Advaita Ācārya became very excited when He saw the beautiful form of this young child. He said, “I can understand that this is the glory of My Lord.” He was very much attracted to this child, and everybody else would also look at the beautiful features of Nimai. Advaita Ācārya would be struck with great wonder and reverence. Regularly, Nimāi would come and grab Viśvarūpa by the arm and say, “Brother, come. Mother is calling Us. It’s time to take *prasāda*.” So, Viśvarūpa would return home, but then He would always return the following day and listen.

One day, the desire to accept the renounced order of life awakened within His heart, and He decided to take *sannyāsa*. Jagannātha Miśra and Śacīmātā were very much lamenting over their son taking *sannyāsa*. Shortly afterwards, Jagannātha Miśra departed from this world. Now Śacīmātā had only little Nimāi. After Jagannātha Miśra departed, Śacīmātā could not live without constantly seeing the form of young Nimāi. If even one moment passed when He was not in her immediate vision, she would lose consciousness.

When Nimāi grew a little bit older, He also would go and listen to the lectures of Advaita Ācārya. Because He was attending those lectures, Śacīmātā immediately began relating it to what had happened with her eldest son, Viśvarūpa. She was thinking, “When Viśvarūpa went to hear these lectures from Advaita Ācārya, He took *sannyāsa*. Now, Nimāi is going.” She then made this one statement: “He calls Himself Advaita Ācārya, but actually He is *dvaita*. He is trying to steal my son.”

That was all she said. For that one statement, Lord Caitanya would not forgive her, and punished her by refusing to give her love of God. He made her take shelter of the lotus feet of Advaita Ācārya and take the dust from His feet.

Like Śacīmātā, Mukunda Datta, and Advaita Ācārya, Kamalākānta Viśvāsa was also being chastised.

**After pacifying Kamalākānta Viśvāsa in this way, Śrī** **Advaita Ācārya Prabhu went to see Caitanya** **Mahāprabhu. Śrī Advaita Ācārya told Lord Caitanya, “I cannot understand Your transcendental pastimes. You have shown more favor to Kamalākānta than You generally show to Me. The favor You have shown Kamalākānta is so great that even to Me You have never shown such favor. What offense have I committed at Your lotus feet so as not to be shown such favor?” Hearing this, Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu laughed with satisfaction and immediately called Kamalākānta Viśvāsa. Advaita Ācārya then said to Caitanya Mahāprabhu, “Why have You called back this man and allowed him to see You? He has cheated Me in two ways.” When Caitanya Mahāprabhu heard this, His mind was satisfied. Only They could understand each other’s minds.**

**Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu instructed Kamalākānta, “You are a** *bāuliyā,* **one who does not know things as they are. Why do you act in this way? Why do you invade the privacy of Advaita Ācārya and damage His religious principles? Advaita Ācārya, My spiritual master, should never accept charity from rich men or kings, because if a spiritual master accepts money or grains from such materialists his mind becomes polluted.” (***Cc. Ādi* **12.43–50)**

**Purport**

It is very risky to accept money or food from materialistic persons, for such acceptance pollutes the mind of the charity’s recipient. According to the Vedic system, one should give charity to *sannyāsīs* and *brāhmaṇas* because one who thus gives charity becomes free from sinful activities. Formerly, therefore, *brāhmaṇas* would not accept charity from a person unless he were very pious. Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu gave this instruction for all spiritual masters. Materialistic persons who are not inclined to give up their sinful activities like illicit sex, intoxication, gambling and meat-eating sometimes want to become our disciples, but, unlike professional spiritual masters who accept disciples regardless of their condition, Vaiṣṇavas do not accept such cheap disciples. One must at least agree to abide by the rules and regulations for a disciple before a Vaiṣṇava *ācārya* can accept him. In fact, a Vaiṣṇava should not even accept charity or food from persons who do not follow the rules and regulations of the Vaiṣṇava principles.

*\* Is there a risk when a spiritual master accepts many disciples and offerings?*

Śrīla Prabhupāda always gives the example that it is the duty of the spiritual master to change the character of the disciple, and therefore, sometimes the spiritual master must take a risk for the sake of preaching. In other words, if the spiritual master does not accept risk, what is our hope? What other means do we have for giving up the fruits of our work and becoming purified if the spiritual master will not accept? The point that Śrīla Prabhupāda is making is that the spiritual master can accept these things if he has the power to elevate his disciples. However, if he does not have the power to elevate them and becomes enamored by the gifts and charity that are offered to him, his mind becomes polluted by accepting the offerings. It is stated in *śāstra* that it is very risky to accept too many disciples because one’s mind can become polluted. One has to be powerful enough to be unaffected.

On one morning walk, devotees asked Śrīla Prabhupāda, “It says in the scriptures that the spiritual master should not accept too many disciples.”

Prabhupāda replied, “Yes, but I am willing to go to hell for Kṛṣṇa, and therefore, I am taking such risk.” Thus, the spiritual master accepts and uses everything for Kṛṣṇa. That is why Śrīla Prabhupāda preached *yukta-vairāgya*—using everything for the service of Kṛṣṇa. The person who is taking the money or offering has to actually be capable of using it only for Krsna. We always have to be very careful to make sure that what we are given is being used properly. If one has the power to always use everything for the service of Kṛṣṇa, he will not become polluted. However, if he is not capable of doing that, he can also become polluted. That is the risk.

**“When one’s mind is polluted, it is very difficult to remember Kṛṣṇa, and when remembrance of Lord Kṛṣṇa is hampered, one’s life is unproductive. Thus one becomes unpopular in the eyes of the people in general, for this damages his religiosity and fame. A Vaiṣṇava, especially one who acts as a spiritual master, must not act in such a way. One should always be conscious of this fact.” When Caitanya Mahāprabhu gave this instruction to Kamalākānta, all present considered it to be meant for everyone. Thus Advaita Ācārya was greatly pleased. Only Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu could understand the intentions of Advaita Ācārya, and Advaita Ācārya appreciated the grave instruction of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. In this statement there are many confidential considerations. I do not write of them all, fearing an unnecessary increase in the volume of the book. (***Cc. Ādi* **12.51–55)**

Thus, Kamalākānta Viśvāsa was given chastisement as well as instructions. Even though he was told that he could never come before the Lord again, by the desire of Advaita Ācārya, the Lord brought him back and instructed him in this way so that everyone else could also be instructed by this particular pastime.

From these different examples, we must understand that the Lord’s purpose is always perfect. He knows how to perfectly deal with His devotees. Therefore, any devotee who accepts the Lord’s dealings in the same spirit as the devotees in these examples can also have faith and confidence in the mercy of the Lord. We may not always understand the Lord’s plan. Sometimes it is very difficult to understand what plan the Lord has and the way that He deals with us. But one thing we must know is that the Lord’s plan is perfect. The Lord is always trying to establish many purposes, and He uses His devotees to establish these purposes. He used Mahārāja Parīkṣit to establish His purpose, as well as Arjuna for the purpose of speaking the *Bhagavad-gītā.*

Whatever the Lord’s plan may be for us, we must always accept His dealings as mercy: “By the mere fact that the Lord has dealt with me and has not been indifferent to me, this is mercy.” This is the greatest mercy. A devotee should always think in that way, and is therefore always ready to accept any suffering that may come to him as the Lord’s mercy. That is the meaning of the prayer of Lord Brahma: One who always tolerates all suffering, seeing it as mercy, and continuously glorifies the Lord, becomes a bona fide candidate for liberation. Such dealings of the Lord are perfect arrangements to bring the devotee closer and closer to Him.

*\* Are all the sufferings that devotees have, Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, or are some of them due to our previous* karma*?*

Our previous *karma* is also Kṛṣṇa’s mercy. It is all Kṛṣṇa’s mercy. If somebody is Kṛṣṇa conscious, it is all Kṛṣṇa’s mercy. He does not distinguish. It is mercy to be punished by the laws of *karma.* Kṛṣṇa says, “Material nature works under My direction.” That punishment is meant to bring one to take shelter of the Lord.

A devotee once asked Śrīla Prabhupāda, “In order to be benefited from suffering, do we have to see it as coming from the Lord?”

Prabhupāda said, “That is the only way that one can benefit from suffering.” He has to see the suffering in connection with the Lord. Thus, any suffering is ultimately seen as the Lord’s mercy if one is Kṛṣṇa conscious. That is what Kṛṣṇa consciousness means. We should not distinguish one type of suffering as Kṛṣṇa’s mercy and another type of suffering not to be Kṛṣṇa’s mercy. We accept all suffering as Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, and that is how we become Kṛṣṇa conscious.

## THE PASTIMES OF LORD CAITANYA MAHĀPRABHU IN HIS YOUTH

**Ādi-līlā: 17.20–54**

**Text 20**

*tabe śuklāmbarera kaila taṇḍula-bhakṣaṇa*  
*‘harer nāma’ ślokera kaila artha vivaraṇa*

**After this incident the Lord ate raw rice given by Śuklāmbara Brahmacārī and explained very elaborately the import of the** ***“harer nāma” śloka*** **mentioned in the** ***Bṛhan-nāradīya Purāṇa.***

**Text 21**

*harer nāma harer nāma*  
*harer nāmaiva kevalam*  
*kalau nāsty eva nāsty eva*  
*nāsty eva gatir anyathā*

**In this Age of Kali there is no other means, no other means, no other means for self-realization than chanting the holy name, chanting the holy name, chanting the holy name of Lord Hari.**

**Text 22**

*kali-kāle nāma-rūpe kṛṣṇa-avatāra*  
*nāma haite haya sarva-jagat-nistāra*

**In this Age of Kali, the holy name of the Lord, the Hare Kṛṣṇa** ***mahā-mantra,*** **is the incarnation of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Simply by chanting the holy name, one associates with the Lord directly. Anyone who does this is certainly delivered.**

**Text 23**

*dārḍhya lāgi’ ‘harer nāma’-ukti tina-vāra*  
*jaḍa loka bujhāite punaḥ ‘eva’-kāra*

**This verse repeats the word ‘*****eva’*** **\[‘certainly’\] three times for emphasis, and it also three times repeats ‘harer nāma’ \[‘the holy name of the Lord’\], just to make common people understand.**

**Purport**

To emphasize something to an ordinary person, one may repeat it three times, just as one might say, “You must do this! You must do this! You must do this!” Thus the *Bṛhan-nāradīya Purāṇa* repeatedly emphasizes the chanting of the holy name so that people may take it seriously and thus free themselves from the clutches of *māyā*. It is our practical experience in the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement all over the world that many millions of people are factually coming to the spiritual stage of life simply by chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* regularly, according to the prescribed principles. Therefore our request to all our students is that they daily chant at least sixteen rounds of this *harer nāma mahā-mantra* \[*Cc. Ādi* 17.21\] offenselessly, following the regulative principles. Thus their success will be assured without a doubt.

**The use of the word ‘kevala’ \[‘only’\] prohibits all other processes, such as the cultivation of knowledge, practice of mystic yoga, or performance of austerities and fruitive activities. This verse clearly states that anyone who accepts any other path cannot be delivered. This is the reason for the triple repetition ‘nothing else, nothing else, nothing else,’ which emphasizes the real process of self-realization. To chant the holy name always, one should be humbler than the grass in the street and devoid of all desire for personal honor, but one should offer others all respectful obeisances. A devotee engaged in chanting the holy name of the Lord should practice forbearance like that of a tree. Even if rebuked or chastised, he should not say anything to others to retaliate. For even if one cuts a tree, it never protests, and even if it is drying up and dying, it does not ask anyone for water. (***Cc. Ādi* **17.24–28)**

**Purport**

This practice of forbearance (*taror iva sahiṣṇunā*) is very difficult, but when one actually engages in chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra*, the quality of forbearance automatically develops. A person advanced in spiritual consciousness through the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* need not practice to develop it separately, for a devotee develops all good qualities simply by chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* regularly.

**Thus a Vaiṣṇava should not ask anything from anyone else. If someone gives him something without being asked, he should accept it, but if nothing comes, a Vaiṣṇava should be satisfied to eat whatever vegetables and fruits are easily available. One should strictly follow the principle of always chanting the holy name, and one should be satisfied with whatever he gets easily. Such devotional behavior solidly maintains one’s devotional service. (***Cc. Ādi* **17.29–30)**

**Text 31**

*tṛṇād api su-nīcena*  
*taror iva sahiṣṇunā*  
*amāninā māna-dena*  
*kīrtanīyaḥ sadā hariḥ*

**“One who thinks himself lower than the grass, who is more tolerant than a tree, and who does not expect personal honor yet is always prepared to give all respect to others can very easily always chant the holy name of the Lord.”**

**Raising my hands, I declare, “Everyone please hear me! String this verse on the thread of the holy name and wear it on your neck for continuous remembrance.” (***Cc. Ādi* **17.32)**

**Purport**

(…) One should note in this connection that chanting involves the activities of the upper and lower lips as well as the tongue. All three must be engaged in chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra.* The words “Hare Kṛṣṇa” should be very distinctly pronounced and heard. Sometimes one mechanically produces a hissing sound instead of chanting with the proper pronunciation with the help of the lips and tongue. Chanting is very simple, but one must practice it seriously. Therefore the author of *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī, advises everyone to keep this verse always strung about his neck.

Lord Caitanya is giving this explanation of the “*harer nāmaiva kevalam”* verse from the *Bṛhan-nāradīya Purāṇa.* In this verse, emphasis is made three times that the chanting of the holy name of the Lord is the means for self-realization. When one continues to chant the holy names of the Lord, there are certain qualities that must be developed. One should be humbler than a blade of grass and devoid of the desire for personal honor. By stringing this verse \[*tṛṇād api sunīcena*\] on the holy name, one should develop all of the qualities of humility (*dainya*). Similarly, when one puts beads on a string, both the beads and the string exist simultaneously.

*Nāma-aparādha* is the very first stage of chanting the holy name. When we begin the process of chanting the holy name, it is understood that we may be committing offenses. The offensive stage is called *nāma-aparādha*. The middle stage is called *nāmābhāsa*. The word *“ābhāsa”* means “a slight glimpse.” *Nāmābhāsa* is considered the clearing stage of chanting the holy name, which is the stage before one comes to complete offenseless chanting. In *nāmābhāsa,* there are still some offenses, but gradually they become cleared. The *nāmābhāsa* stage is sufficient for one to achieve liberation, as described by Śrīla Haridāsa Ṭhākura.

Once, a devotee named Balarāma Ācārya approached Haridāsa Ṭhākura and requested that they go together to the house of Govardhana and Hiraṇya Majumadāra. Govardhana and Hiraṇya Majumadāra would bring many different scholars to their home to discuss various spiritual topics. Balarāma Ācārya thought that it would be a very nice place for Haridāsa Ṭhākura to visit. Thus, together they went to their home, where Haridāsa Ṭhākura was very well received. In the discussion that took place, the topic of Haridāsa Ṭhākura came up. Someone said, “Haridāsa Ṭhākura is a very advanced Vaiṣṇava.” It was mentioned that Haridāsa Ṭhākura was chanting 300,000 names of the Lord daily.

Some of the scholars began to speak about the holy name of the Lord. One scholar said, “The holy name of the Lord is so glorious that it can give liberation from the material world.” Another scholar spoke up, saying, “No, actually, the holy name of the Lord can free one from all the reactions of sinfullife.”

Everyone was putting forward arguments and their conclusion of the goal of chanting the holy name. Haridāsa Ṭhākura protested: “No, you have not properly understood the glories of the holy name of the Lord. You are presenting liberation and freedom from all sinful reactions as the goal of chanting the holy name, but that is really not the goal. One can get liberation even from chanting in *nāmābhāsa*. Therefore, liberation and freedom from reaction are not the ultimate goal. The ultimate goal of chanting the holy name is to come to the stage of pure love of Godhead.”

The assembled scholars were very much impressed by this statement of Haridāsa Ṭhākura, and they asked him to explain the confidential meaning behind it. Haridāsa Ṭhākura quoted a verse comparing the chanting of the holy name to the sun. The first glimpse of sunlight from the rising sun immediately dissipates darkness; at this time, thieves, rogues, and ghosts disperse, and everybody begins conducting their religious affairs and duties very nicely. He said, “This is just like the *ābhāsa,* or slight glimpse of the holy name of the Lord. As soon as one gets a slight glimpse of the holy name of the Lord, one can immediately become freed from all the reactions of sinful life and becomes liberated from the material world. If one continues to chant the holy name of the Lord, he becomes situated on the platform of devotional service to the Lord in pure love of God. This is the ultimate goal of chanting the holy name of the Lord.” He thus established that even at the stage of *nāmābhāsa,* the benefit of liberation and freedom from sinful reaction can be produced.

At that time, a tax collector named Gopāla Cakravartī was also at the home of Govardhana and Hiraṇya Majumadāra. When he heard Haridāsa Ṭhākura make this statement, he was very astonished. He said, “If one attains the platform of absolute knowledge, even after millions and millions of births, he may not achieve liberation, but you are making the statement that after chanting even once the holy name on the platform of *nāmābhāsa,* one can immediately obtain liberation!?” Gopāla Cakravartī doubted the statement that was made by Haridāsa Ṭhākura and said, “If one does not get liberation from *nāmābhāsa*, I will cut off your nose.”

Haridāsa Ṭhākura replied to Gopāla Cakravartī, “This is the authorized statement of *śāstra*. Therefore, if one does not get liberation from *nāmābhāsa*, I will cut off my own nose!” All the assembled scholars, including Govardhana and Hiraṇya Majumadāra, were very offended to hear such statements by Gopāla Cakravartī; they knew that Haridāsa Ṭhākura was an extremely elevated soul. Haridāsa Ṭhākura continued to support the statements he had made on the authority of scripture. However, Gopāla Cakravartī would not listen. Hiraṇya and Govardhana Majumadāra were very angry and began to chastise Gopāla Cakravartī. Haridāsa Ṭhākura said, “I have not taken any insult whatsoever.” Haridāsa Ṭhākura was very humble, as well as learned and renounced.

Three days later, Gopāla Cakravartī contracted leprosy. It is described that he previously had a very handsome, wellbuilt body, but after he contracted leprosy, his nose rotted right on his face. All his fingers and the limbs of his body began rotting away. Everyone could understand what an elevated Vaiṣṇava Haridāsa Ṭhākura was, and that the Lord could not tolerate this offense committed to him. Haridāsa Ṭhākura became very sad when he heard what happened to Gopāla Cakravartī. He had great compassion for him, but he could see that the Lord could not tolerate the offense.

This pastime is given in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* to emphasize the power of the holy name of the Lord. When Haridāsa Ṭhākura said, “One can obtain liberation even from *nāmābhāsa*,” he was specifically describing the glories and the unlimited potency existing within the holy name.

*Nāmābhāsa* is also described elsewhere in the *Caitanyacaritāmṛta*. *Haram* was the statement made by Muslims who had been condemned. Even though it is not the actual chanting of the holy name of the Lord, one who chants it derives the benefit of chanting the holy name of the Lord.

Another example is Ajāmila, who chanted the holy name of Lord Nārāyaṇa without any offense. He was not chanting it for the purpose of relieving himself from sinful reaction; rather, he was chanting it in a helpless way. He was calling out, “My son, Nārāyaṇa, Nārāyaṇa, please come.” This chanting was considered to be another form of *nāmābhāsa*. Again, this establishes the exalted characteristics of the holy name of the Lord and the potency that is situated within it.

Prabhupāda says in the purport (*Cc. Ādi* 17.32): “Therefore one must chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* according to the principles of the above verse,” if he wants the full benefit of the holy name of the Lord. From *nāmābhāsa* one can obtain liberation and freedom from the reactions of past sinful life, but it does not situate one on the platform of pure love of Godhead. Liberation and freedom from reaction is only the by-product of chanting the holy name of the Lord, not the ultimate goal. The ultimate goal of chanting is the development of pure love of God.

*bahu janma kare yadi śravaṇa, kīrtana*  
*tabu ta’ nā pāya kṛṣṇa-pade prema-dhana*

“If one is infested with the ten offenses in the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa mahā-mantra, despite his endeavor to chant the holy name for many births, he will not get the love of Godhead that is the ultimate goal of this chanting.” (Cc. Ādi 8.16)

This is what we are actually striving for. We can chant for millions of births, but if we do not become free from our offenses, we cannot attain love of Godhead.

It is stated in the *Padma Purāṇa.*

*nāmāparādha-yuktānāṁ*  
*nāmāny eva haranty agham*  
*aviśrānti-prayuktāni*  
*tāny evārtha-karāṇi ca*

The chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* is recommended for those who are committing offenses, because if one continues to chant the holy name of the Lord, he will gradually become free from offenses. We must become free from offenses to achieve love of God. The stage beyond *nāmābhāsa* is the offenseless stage. At this stage, one experiences pure love of the Supreme Lord. At this stage, the devotee relishes the holy name of the Lord and experiences great pleasure.

Once, Prakāśānanda Sarasvatī was challenging Lord Caitanya: “Why are You chanting like a sentimentalist in the streets? You are a *sannyāsī*. You should be studying *Vedānta,* but instead You are acting just like a sentimentalist. Why are You doing this?”

Lord Caitanya took a humble position and described how His spiritual master considered Him to be a fool and unqualified for the study of *Vedānta* and therefore recommended that He chant the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. Lord Caitanya said, “Because I had firm faith in the order of My spiritual master, I accepted his instruction to chant the holy name of the Lord, and soon I found that while chanting the holy name of the Lord, I began behaving just like a madman. Sometimes I would laugh and sometimes I would cry. Tears would flow from My eyes. They were uncontrollable experiences. I went back to my spiritual master and asked him, ‘What kind of *mantra* have you given Me? I am experiencing all these symptoms from chanting.’ My spiritual master became very pleased and said, ‘Yes, this is the perfection.’” Lord Caitanya described all of this to Prakāśānanda Sarasvatī. He described the stage of chanting the holy name of the Lord in love.

When we achieve this stage of devotional service, we have actually achieved the stage of perfection. Therefore, if we want to come to the stage of pure chanting of the holyname, we must remember these verses. We must chant the holy name of the Lord with the attitude that is described here. We should wear this verse, *tṛṇād api su-nīcena*, on the string of the holy name of the Lord.

**One must strictly follow the principles given by Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu in this verse. If one simply follows in the footsteps of Lord Caitanya and the Gosvāmīs, certainly he will achieve the ultimate goal of life, the lotus feet of Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu regularly led congregational chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa** *mahā-mantra* **in the house of Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura every night for one full year. This ecstatic chanting was performed with the doors closed so that nonbelievers who came to make fun could not gain entrance. (***Cc. Ādi* **17.33–35)**

There was a period of about one year when Lord Caitanya would gather with His intimate associates. Lord Nityānanda, Haridāsa Ṭhākura, Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura, Murāri Gupta, and many other of the Lord’s intimate associates would gather every night in the house of Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura. These were called Lord Caitanya’s nocturnal *kīrtanas.*

At that particular time, many of the atheists in Navadvīpa were very critical of Lord Caitanya’s actions. Many of them would stand outside the house of Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura and criticize: “What is Nimāi Paṇḍita doing at this time?” It was observed that their behaviour was becoming very erratic, and they were making many threatening statements, such as, “Why do we not just take this house of Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura and throw it in the Gaṅgā and get rid of these sentimentalists once and for all?” Other people would say, “Why do they not go and get a job and make something useful with their life? They are simply howling all night long. They are not working. They should go out and work to get some food to feed their bellies!” Such atheistic statements were being made. Thus, Lord Caitanya and His associates engaged in these nocturnal *kīrtanas* behind closed doors.

Many pious people also resided in Navadvīpa, but they were not very advanced in devotional service. These pious people also used to wonder what was going on inside the house of Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura. They would stand around and criticize the atheists, saying, “Because all these atheists are making such offensive statements, Nimāi Paṇḍita will not open the door for us. Every night, they are in there chanting. We can only imagine how wonderful it is.” The pious people were praying for the opportunity to be able to see these wonderful pastimes. However, at this particular time, Lord Caitanya had not yet started the *saṅkīrtana* movement.

One very austere *brahmacārī* wanted to enter into the house of Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura to see the *kīrtana*. He approached Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura for permission. Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura said, “I think maybe you are qualified to see these *kīrtanas*. I will bring you in, but you will have to hide so that Lord Caitanya will not see you. If He knows that you are there, He will not allow you to stay.”

The brahmacārī was very pleased to hear this. He said, “Finally, I will get the opportunity to see these wonderful pastimes.” The whole town of Navadvīpa was talking about these kīrtanas, and the brahmacārī was waiting for the opportunity to see them. The following night, Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura brought him into his house. He brought him to a corner where he could hide and not be seen. Shortly after, the *kīrtana* began. Lord Caitanya, Lord Nityānanda, and Advaita Ācārya began dancing; the whole house was completely filled with the holy name—nothing else could be heard. Everyone began dancing ecstatically. The *brahmacārī* observed all this.

All of a sudden, Lord Caitanya, who was raising His hands, stopped and said, “Why am I not experiencing the usual ecstasy? Somebody is hiding in this house. I know there must be a non-devotee in here, and that is why I am not experiencing the usual ecstasy.”

As soon as Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura heard this, he became very nervous. He thought that he should say something fast. He immediately approached Lord Caitanya and said, “Yes, actually there is somebody here. I have allowed in one pious *brahmacārī*. He is very austere. He drinks only milk and eats only fruit. He sustains his life on these things. He has been practicing celibacy his whole life, and he was begging me to allow him to come in and see this ecstatic *kīrtana*. He is here in the room, right now.”

Upon hearing this, Lord Caitanya’s face became enraged. He said, “You have allowed a non-devotee in this *kīrtana*! Do you think that I can be obtained by some milk-drinker? Is that the method by which one can attain love for Me?! If one is not a devotee and does not take shelter of Me, even if he performs severe austerities and penances, he cannot attain Me. However, even the most low-class dog-eater can immediately obtain My shelter if he surrenders and takes shelter of My holy name.” Lord Caitanya began describing different devotees who came to the elevated stage of chanting the holy name. He continued in anger, “Get him out of here!”

The *brahmacārī* had been watching the whole thing and by now was very, very nervous and afraid. He was thinking, “I should get out of here quickly.” Nevertheless, he was appreciating how wonderful it was that he had gotten the opportunity to see the *kīrtana*. He was thinking that the Lord had very appropriately given him the proper chastisement, and he accepted that chastisement completely. He thought that he should leave because of the great fortune that he had of seeing the *kīrtana*. He therefore came out from hiding and started to walk to the door.

Lord Caitanya said, “Wait. Stop.” The Lord, being omniscient, could immediately understand the mind of the *brahmacārī*. The *brahmacārī* immediately responded to Lord Caitanya’s beckon and came before the Lord. Lord Caitanya said, “Never think that you can attain Me simply by performing austerities. I can only be approached by those who take shelter. Please always remember this.” The Lord then placed His lotus feet on the head of the *brahmacārī*, who began experiencing ecstatic symptoms. With tears flowing from his eyes, he experienced great love for the Lord. *Kīrtana* then resumed. The *brahmacārī* stayed for the *kīrtana*, but nobody else could come in.

Lord Caitanya had these kinds of pastimes in the association of intimate devotees. These nocturnal pastimes are being described in this verse (Cc. Ādi 17.35): “This ecstatic chanting was performed with the doors closed so that nonbelievers who came to make fun could not gain entrance.”

**Purport**

Chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* is open to everyone, but sometimes nonbelievers come to disturb the ceremony of chanting. It is indicated herein that under such circumstances the temple doors should be closed. Only bona fide chanters should be admitted; others should not. But when there is large-scale congregational chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahāmantra*, we keep our temples open for everyone to join, and by the grace of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu this policy has given good results.

**Thus the nonbelievers almost burned to ashes and died out of envy. To retaliate, they planned various ways to give trouble to Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura. One night while** *kīrtana* **was going on inside Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura’s house, a brāhmaṇa named Gopāla Cāpāla, the chief of the nonbelievers, who was talkative and very rough in his speech, placed all the paraphernalia for worshiping the goddess Durgā outside Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura’s door. (Cc. Ādi 17.38)**

Prabhupāda explains that in Bengal there was perpetual competition between the worshippers of Durgā and the worshippers of Kṛṣṇa. Generally, the Bengalis are very attached to worshipping Durgā Devī. Because many of them are drunkards and meat-eaters, they are envious of Vaiṣṇavas. Thus, Gopāla Cāpāla tried to defame Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura by trying to prove that he was actually a worshipper of goddess Durgā by putting all the paraphernalia for Durgā worship outside his door.

**On the upper portion of a plantain leaf he placed such paraphernalia for worship as** *oḍa-phula,* **turmeric, vermilion, red sandalwood and rice. He placed a pot of wine beside all this, and in the morning when Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura opened his door he saw this paraphernalia. Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura called for all the respectable gentlemen of the neighborhood and smilingly addressed them as follows. “Gentlemen, every night I worship the goddess Bhavānī. Since the paraphernalia for the worship is present here, now all you respectable** *brāhmaṇas* **and members of the higher castes can understand my position.”**

**Then all the assembled gentlemen exclaimed, “What is this? What is this? Who has performed such mischievous activities? Who is that sinful man?” They called for a sweeper \[***hāḍi***\], who threw all the items of worship far away and cleansed the place by mopping it with a mixture of water and cow dung. (***Cc. Ādi* **17.39–44)**

In the purport to this verse, Śrīla Prabhupāda speaks about the *hāḍis,* or street sweepers, who were called to clean up the entrance of Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura’s home by mopping it up with a mixture of water and cow manure. Such persons existed in Vedic society. Sometimes they are even considered untouchable, especially when engaged in their profession. However, Śrīla Prabhupāda is taking the opportunity of this verse to describe the influence that can take place by Lord Caitanya’s movement. Even a person of such a lower class can be elevated to the platform of devotee. Prabhupāda often quoted from the Third Canto of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (*SB 3.33.7*):

*aho bata śva-paco ’to garīyān*  
*yaj-jihvāgre vartate nāma tubhyam*  
*tepus tapas te juhuvuḥ sasnur āryā*  
*brahmānūcur nāma gṛṇanti ye te*

“Oh, how glorious are they whose tongues are chanting Your holy name! Even if born in the families of dog-eaters, such persons are worshipable. Persons who chant the holy name of Your Lordship must have executed all kinds of austerities and fire sacrifices and achieved all the good manners of the Āryans. To be chanting the holy name of Your Lordship, they must have bathed at holy places of pilgrimage, studied the Vedas and fulfilled everything required.” Thus, although having previously committed offenses, simply by chanting the holy name of Kṛṣṇa, a person is considered to have achieved the benefits of all the above-mentioned activities. This is the supreme potency of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. Simply one utterance of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa can free one from all reactions of one’s past sinful life. Thus, if one can be freed from all the reactions of his past sinful life, whatever condition of life he may presently be in no longer bears any restrictions whatsoever into his entrance into the realm of devotional service. This is true even for one who has taken his birth in a family of dog-eaters and is considered to be a social outcast.

Society consists of those who are *brāhmaṇa*, *kṣatriya*, *vaiśya,* and *śūdra*. Y*avanas*, *mlecchas*, and *caṇḍālas* have no place in society according to the Vedic standards. The *yavanas* are those who do not follow any principle of scripture; *mlecchas* are meat-eaters; and *caṇḍālas* are dog-eaters. In the Vedic times, these classes of people were called untouchables. If you touched them, you were considered to have been contaminated and tainted by that association.

The Mohammedans’ method of conversion of the Hindus was to sprinkle water on them. If a Hindu had water sprinkled on him by a Muslim, he was considered contaminated and rejected from the Hindu society. Even if one touched the shadow of an untouchable, he was considered to have been contaminated.

This is the *smārta* mentality that existed, but Lord Caitanya came to show the true qualities of all living beings. Many of the so-called caste *brāhmaṇas* had become very proud of their birth and were thinking themselves to be superior. However, Lord Caitanya established the quality of all living entities on the spiritual platform.

In the purport to text 44, Prabhupāda speaks about the *hari-jana* movement of Mahatma Gandhi. Mahatma Gandhi tried to create equality on a material and political basis. He tried to get equal rights for those who were degraded simply by labelling them *hari-jana,* although their behaviour was not as *hari-jana*. *Hari-jana* means “the follower of Lord Hari.” They continued to be just as degraded as they were before.

Lord Caitanya introduced the method by which those who were in the most degraded condition of life could become purified. He did not place a material designation on them. Lord Caitanya’s movement far surpassed the concept of simply giving a designation. He transformed the character of those in even the most degraded conditions of life. Lord Caitanya was therefore known as *patita-pāvana*—the uplifter of the most fallen. His upliftment of the most fallen was not by giving them equal rights on the basis of Gandhi’s vision. Rather, Lord Caitanya established equality in relation to the Lord.

*māṁ hi pārtha vyapāśritya ye ’pi syuḥ pāpa-yonayaḥ*  
*striyo vaiśyās tathā śūdrās te ’pi yānti parāṁ gatim*  
*(Bg. 9.32)*

Anyone who takes shelter of Kṛṣṇa, even if they are of lower birth—women, *vaiśyas* \[merchants\], and *śūdras* \[workers\]—can approach the supreme destination.

The Lord sees these qualities on the spiritual platform (*Bg.* 9.29):

*samo ’haṁ sarva-bhūteṣu*  
*na me dveṣyo ’sti na priyaḥ*  
*ye bhajanti tu māṁ bhaktyā*  
*mayi te teṣu cāpy aham*

“I envy no one, nor am I partial to anyone. I am equal to all. But whoever renders service unto Me in devotion is a friend, is in Me, and I am also a friend to him.”

Although the Lord may be particularly inclined to His devotees, the right to become a devotee is open to everyone. That is exactly what Lord Caitanya established by distributing the holy name of the Lord. If anyone associates with the holy name of the Lord, he will be delivered.

With reference to even a street sweeper becoming very elevated, Prabhupāda also explains in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (3.7, Purport): “A sincere sweeper in the street is far better than the charlatan meditator who meditates only for the sake of making a living.” Someone may pose himself as a renunciant and even adopt the dress of a *sannyāsī,* and because people are attracted to material designations, they offer respects to such a person who has simply changed his dress.

In the third chapter of the *Bhagavad-gītā,* Prabhupāda explains that one who withdraws his senses from sense objects but dwells upon sense objects within is deluding himself and is considered a pretender. On the other hand, one who engages the senses in works of devotion is considered to be far superior.

Getting back to this pastime with Gopāla Cāpāla, on seeing all the different articles for worshipping the goddess Durgā on the steps of Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura’s house, none of the neighbours believed that he was actually worshipping her and therefore called the street sweepers to clean it up.

**After three days, leprosy attacked Gopāla Cāpāla, and blood oozed from sores all over his body. Incessantly covered with germs and insects biting him all over his body, Gopāla Cāpāla felt unbearable pain. His entire body burned in distress. (***Cc. Ādi* **17.45–46)**

Previously, he was burning in envy, but now he is burning in pain from the insects and germs biting him all over his body.

**Since leprosy is an infectious disease, Gopāla Cāpāla left the village to sit down on the bank of the Ganges underneath a tree. One day, however, he saw Caitanya Mahāprabhu passing by and spoke to Him as follows. “My dear nephew, I am Your maternal uncle in our village relationship. Please see how greatly this attack of leprosy has afflicted me. As an incarnation of God, You are delivering so many fallen souls. I am also a greatly unhappy fallen soul. Kindly deliver me by Your mercy.” (***Cc. Ādi* **17.47-49)**

Prabhupāda brings out an important point in the purport to verse 49: Gopāla Cāpāla did not know “that the deliverance of all fallen souls does not consist of curing the bodily disease.” Many people are in distress due to some physical ailment. Kṛṣṇa says four types of persons will approach Him: those who are in distress, those who are in need of money, those who are inquisitive, and those who are seeking knowledge. Generally, those who are in distress are looking for relief, not knowing that simply by taking shelter of the Lord and engaging in His devotional service they can get permanent relief from distress. Although they approach the Lord, they are not considered to be at an advanced stage of devotional service.

Similarly, Gopāla Cāpāla approached Lord Caitanya, asking to be cured from his disease. The Lord was quite capable of delivering him. When a man is delivered from material clutches, his bodily diseases are also cured. To be delivered from the material clutches of life means to become liberated. Thus, one who seeks shelter at the lotus feet of the Lord simply prays to be engaged in His devotional service. For such a person, liberation automatically stands at his doorstep with folded hands, waiting to serve him. Certainly, such a person will be cured of material diseases. That is automatically included in deliverance.

Nevertheless, a devotee of the Lord is prepared to accept the sufferings of his disease as a result of his past sinful activities. It is stated in the Tenth Canto of the *ŚrīmadBhāgavatam* (10.14.8):

*tat te ’nukampāṁ su-samīkṣamāṇo*  
*bhuñjāna evātma-kṛtaṁ vipākam*  
*hṛd-vāg-vapurbhir vidadhan namas te*  
*jīveta yo mukti-pade sa dāya-bhāk*

“My dear Lord, one who earnestly waits for You to bestow Your causeless mercy upon him, all the while patiently suffering the reactions of his past misdeeds and offering You respectful obeisances with his heart, words and body, is surely eligible for liberation, for it has become his rightful claim.”

A devotee is prepared to accept whatever reactions the Lord gives him, and accepts it to be a small token of what he actually deserves. Accepting it, a devotee continues serving the Lord and becomes a bona fide candidate for liberation.

I knew a devotee who found out he had contracted terminal cancer. The doctors kept assuring him that they could save him: “Just continue taking these treatments.” He took the treatments for some time, but did not feel any confidence in them whatsoever. He felt like it was just artificially prolonging his life. He could feel his life wasting away, and he became very serious. Previously, he had not been very serious, but then he realized, “Now I have to accept this as the reactions to my past sinful life.” Therefore, he accepted it and decided, “I am going to devote the rest of my life to hearing and chanting about Kṛṣṇa.”

He then entered into the intimate association of devotees, continuously heard the pastimes of Kṛṣṇa, and always chanted on his beads. Devotees were constantly writing me letters, telling me how this devotee’s consciousness was very, very excellent—always thinking of Kṛṣṇa. He departed from this world in very auspicious consciousness. Thus, he accepted that this was the reaction from his past sinful life. He did not blame Kṛṣṇa for the suffering.

Although Gopāla Cāpāla is approaching Lord Caitanya saying, “Just cure me from my disease,” Lord Caitanya decides to take the opportunity to tell Gopāla Cāpāla the real reason why he is suffering from this disease.

**Hearing this, Caitanya Mahāprabhu appeared greatly angry, and in that angry mood He spoke some words chastising him. “O sinful person, envious of pure devotees, I shall not deliver you! Rather, I shall have you bitten by these germs for many millions of years. You have made Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura appear to have been worshiping the goddess Bhavānī. Simply for this offense, you will have to fall down into hellish life for ten million births. I have appeared in this incarnation to kill the demons \[***pāṣaṇḍīs***\] and, after killing them, to preach the cult of devotional service.” After saying this, the Lord left to take His bath in the Ganges, and that sinful man did not give up his life but continued to suffer. (***Cc. Ādi*

**17.50–54)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda says in the purport, “It appears that an offender to a Vaiṣṇava continues to suffer and does not give up his life. We have actually seen that a great *vaiṣṇavaaparādhī* continuously suffered so much that it was difficult for him to move, and yet he did not die.”

Previously, Lord Caitanya said that he would suffer for ten million births in hellish life, but as soon as he took shelter of the Lord’s lotus feet, the Lord said, “Alright, if you go back to that person and get his blessings, you will be freed from your sinful reactions.”

Gopāla Cāpāla therefore went to Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura and took shelter at his lotus feet. By Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura’s mercy, he was freed from all sinful reactions. Thus, one can actually be delivered by the mercy of a Vaiṣṇava even if he has been condemned to millions of births in hell.

Similarly, Prahlāda Mahārāja says (*SB* 7.5.32):

*naiṣāṁ matis tāvad urukramāṅghriṁ*  
*spṛśaty anarthāpagamo yad-arthaḥ*  
*mahīyasāṁ pāda-rajo-’bhiṣekaṁ*  
*niṣkiñcanānāṁ na vṛṇīta yāvat*

“Unless they smear upon their bodies the dust of the lotus feet of a Vaiṣṇava completely freed from material contamination, persons very much inclined toward materialistic life cannot be attached to the lotus feet of the Lord, who is glorified for His uncommon activities. Only by becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious and taking shelter at the lotus feet of the Lord in this way can one be freed from material contamination.”

In the same way, Śacīmātā was ordered by Lord Caitanya to take the dust from the feet of Advaita Ācārya for having committed the offense of calling Him “*dvaita,*” or duplicitous. (Refer to previous lecture for details of this pastime).

Vṛndāvana dāsa Ṭhākura describes that the Lord enters into pastimes just to teach us that if one commits an offense to a Vaiṣṇava, there is only one way that he can be relieved from the reactions of that offense. He must go back to the Vaiṣṇava whom he offended and beg for forgiveness and the dust from his lotus feet.

## Part 2 - Madhya-līlā

## THE LATER PASTIMES OF LORD ŚRĪ CAITANYA MAHĀPRABHU

**Madhya-līlā: 1.195–218**

**Kiev—June 12, 2000**

This chapter describes Lord Caitanya’s meeting with Rūpa and Sanātana Gosvāmī. Rūpa and Sanātana Gosvāmī were previously known as Dabira Khāsa and Sākara Mallika. These were Mohammedan names that were given to them due to their association with the Mohammedan government, as ministers for the Mohammedan King.

Rūpa and Sanātana Gosvāmī are giving their explanation to Lord Caitanya as to why they felt themselves to be far inferior to Jagāi and Mādhāi. They said:

**Text 195**

*tomāra nāma lañā tomāra karila nindana*  
*sei nāma ha-ila tāra muktira kāraṇa*

**Jagāi and Mādhāi uttered Your holy name by way of blaspheming You. Fortunately, that holy name became the cause of their deliverance.**

**Text 196**

*jagāi-mādhāi haite koṭī koṭī guṇa*  
*adhama patita pāpī āmi dui jana*

**We two are millions and millions of times inferior to Jagāi and Mādhāi. We are more degraded, fallen and sinful than they.**

**Text 197**

*mleccha-jāti, mleccha-sevī, kari mleccha-karma*  
*go-brāhmaṇa-drohi-saṅge āmāra saṅgama*

**Actually we belong to the caste of meat-eaters because we are servants of meat-eaters. Indeed, our activities are exactly like those of the meat-eaters. Because we always associate with such people, we are inimical toward the cows and** ***brāhmaṇas.***

**Text 198**

*mora karma, mora hāte-galāya bāndhiyā*  
*ku-viṣaya-viṣṭhā-garte diyāche phelāiyā*

**The two brothers, Sākara Mallika and Dabira Khāsa, very humbly submitted that due to their abominable activities they were** **now bound by the neck and hands and had been thrown into a ditch filled with abominable, stool-like objects of material sense enjoyment.**

In other words, Rūpa and Sanātana Gosvāmī are describing themselves to be *guilty by association*. As ministers for the Mohammedan king, they served him in conquering over neighboring territories. Sometimes when the king would go out to conquer, he would leave Sanātana Gosvāmī responsible for the kingdom. Thus, they considered themselves very, very sinful by that service. Their explanation as to why they thought themselves to be greatly inferior to Jagāi and Mādhāi was that they had caused so much pain and suffering by serving the wicked king. In this pastime, Rūpa and Sanātana Gosvāmī were absorbed in these genuine feelings of regret for their past sinful activities.

In the purport to this verse (*Cc. Madhya* 1.198), Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī explains that *ku-viṣaya* means abominable objects of sense gratification or sinful activities, whereas *su-viṣaya* means pious activities. It does not make any difference whether one is engaged in pious activities or sinful activities. Both are compared to be like stocks of poison. The aim of pious activity is to achieve a higher form of sense gratification or fruitive gain. Similarly, one may give in charity to get something in return. Sometimes charitable persons think, “I will give something in charity, and by so doing, I will get more back than what I gave.” Therefore, this is pious activity, because the aim or the motivation behind the charitable activity is to get something in return.

Of course, when one engages in *ku-viṣaya,* or sinful activity, he is also thinking of his own personal gain, but by the performance of sinful activities, he is committing many abominable activities and causing others to suffer.

Rūpa and Sanātana Gosvāmī are thus describing their activities as *ku-viṣaya,* because they have caused suffering to the innocent animals and to other people. “By dint of our sinful activities we caused much suffering to others.” However, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta is explaining that even though *kuviṣaya* activities are subject to punishment, the same is also there for *su-viṣaya*. If we get some pious result from our pious activities, it is called *bhogonmukhī sukṛti*. *Sukṛti* means pious acts, and *bhoga* means sense gratification. Therefore, when one performs pious activity, he performs activities for helping others—whether it is giving in charity, opening hospitals, or performing humanitarian activities. These are all under the category of pious activity, but if the aim is for one’s own personal gain, then he is subject to punishment.

This is also explained in the Sixth Canto of the *ŚrīmadBhāgavatam* in the pastime involving Ajāmila, where there is a discussion between Śukadeva Gosvāmī and Parīkṣit Mahārāja about the process of atonement. One cannot counteract the effects of a sinful activity by a pious activity. The conclusion is that the only way that one can uproot or counteract the effects of desires within the heart is by engaging in devotional service to the Lord (*SB* 6.1.15):

*kecit kevalayā bhaktyā*  
*vāsudeva-parāyaṇāḥ*  
*aghaṁ dhunvanti kārtsnyena*  
*nīhāram iva bhāskaraḥ*

Only a rare person who has attained unalloyed devotional service to the Supreme Lord Vāsudeva can uproot the weeds of sinful desires without the possibility of them ever being revived. We can do this only by discharging devotional service, just as the sun immediately dissipates the fog by its rays. Śukadeva Gosvāmī describes that the only way by which one can uproot these desires within the heart is by performing activities for the pleasure of the Lord. However, if one performs pious activities in an attempt to counteract his sinful activities, the desire remains within the heart.

This is compared to the elephant bathing. After the elephant goes into the water to take bath, it then comes onto the land and throws dirt on itself. Similarly, we may perform a pious activity in an effort to counteract our sinful activities, but because the desire for sense gratification remains within the heart, the inclination to commit sinful activities also remains. One therefore has to uproot this sinful desire seed.

In the *Nectar of Devotion,* Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī explains that one of the characteristics of pure devotional service is that it frees one from all material miseries. Freeing one from material miseries means not only freeing one from the sinful reactions of past sinful activities, but also from the inclination of committing sinful activities again. This is called *kūṭaṁ* and *bījaṁ*. *Bījaṁ* is the seed of sinful desire, and *kūṭaṁ* is a particular inclination that a person may have. Both *kūṭaṁ* and *bījaṁ* are considered results of past sinful activities.

For example, a person may be born with a particular inclination towards stealing; this is referred to as *kūṭaṁ*. However, if he contemplates stealing something in particular, then not only is the inclination there, but the act itself is there in the form of the seed, and that is called *bīja*—the sinful desire seed within the heart. Śukadeva Gosvāmī says, “One can uproot these seeds of sinful desires only by devotional service.” Not only does it burn up the reactions of past sinful life, but it also burns up the inclination to commit sinful acts.

There are several more verses where Rūpa and Sanātana Gosvāmī continue to offer prayers to Lord Caitanya.

**After hearing the prayer of Dabira Khāsa and Sākara** **Mallika, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu said, “My dear Dabira** **Khāsa, you two brothers are My old servants. My dear Sākara Mallika, from this day your names will be changed to Śrīla Rūpa and Śrīla Sanātana. Now please abandon your humility, for My heart is breaking to see you so humble. You have written several letters showing your humility. I can understand your behavior from those letters. By your letters, I could understand your heart. Therefore, in order to teach you, I sent you one verse, which reads as follows. ‘If a woman is attached to a man other than her husband, she will appear very busy in carrying out her household affairs, but within her heart she is always relishing feelings of association with her paramour.’” (***Cc. Madhya* **1.207-211)**

Lord Caitanya is saying that He had previously sent this verse to Rūpa and Sanātana Gosvāmī. They had written letters to Lord Caitanya, expressing their desire to surrender their lives to Him. They were very intent and were thinking, “How can we disentangle ourselves from these affairs? We are completely subservient to these wicked masters.”

*Caitanya-caritāmṛta* describes how Rūpa and Sanātana Gosvāmī extricated themselves from their positions. On the plea of sickness, Sanātana Gosvāmī told the king he could not perform his duties. He instead stayed at home and studied *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* with learned *brāhmaṇas*. The king was very disturbed by this and had Sanātana Gosvāmī arrested. While imprisoned, Sanātana Gosvāmī used a portion of the gold coins that Rūpa Gosvāmī had left behind to bribe the jailor to let him out of prison. This entire pastime is discussed in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta.*

Lord Caitanya had sent the verse to both Rūpa and Sanātana Gosvāmī to help them understand how they should carry out their duties. A woman who has a paramour appears to be very busily carrying out her household affairs, but within her heart she is always relishing the feelings of association with her paramour. Thus, He was telling Rūpa and Sanātana that they should be very busy in carrying out their duties, but within their hearts, they should meditate upon the Lord. By such meditation, the Lord would gradually remove all the obstacles from their lives so they could surrender to Him.

A similar instruction was given to Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmī, who was also subjected to various torments from family members, who did not allow him to renounce his work, wife, and family, to leave home and take shelter of Lord Caitanya. The Lord told Raghunātha dāsa not to become a *markaṭa-vairāgya,* a “monkey renunciant.” The monkey lives naked in the jungle; therefore he externally looks very renounced. However, he swings from one tree to the next, having sex with female monkeys. Lord Caitanya told Raghunātha dāsa, “Do not be a renunciant only externally.” He told him to return home to his family and work. Externally he should appear just like a “pounds and shillings man,” but within his heart he should take shelter of the Lord. Thus, the Lord would very soon deliver him from the stoollike ditch of materialistic life.

These are different examples that can help us to understand that renunciation must be internal, not just external. Because Rūpa and Sanātana Gosvāmī were meditating upon their fallen, degraded condition in a very humble way, they were attracted to receiving the mercy of the Lord. They had even said:

**We are very depressed at being unfit candidates for** **Your mercy. Yet since we have heard of Your transcendental qualities, we are very much attracted to You…\[and\] although we are completely unfit, a desire to receive Your mercy is awakening within our minds. (***Cc. Madhya* **1.204–5)**

They were revealing their internal thoughts of wanting to attain the mercy of the Lord; that was their meditation. For a devotee who wants to attain the mercy of the Lord, it is not the external activities that will enable one to capture the Lord’s attention.

Lord Kṛṣṇa explains in the *Bhagavad-gītā* that if one withdraws his senses, but his mind dwells upon sense objects, he deludes himself and is called a pretender. It is far better to engage the senses in acts of devotion. It is the meditation within the heart of the devotee that captures the Lord’s attention. The devotee is very eager to attain the mercy of the Lord, and therefore meditates on pleasing the Lord to obtain His favor. The Lord is pleased when the devotee meditates in this way. This devotional attitude induces the Lord to respond to and reciprocate with His devotee.

Because the Lord is *bhāva-grāhī janārdana,* He extracts the essence of a person’s attitude. The attitude is our internal meditation; not what we are doing externally. If the internal meditation is proper, then the external activities will naturally follow. However, if we engage in activities externally, but within our hearts we are always meditating on sense gratification, then we cannot attain the mercy of the Lord. To attain the mercy of the Lord, we have to feel ourselves to be very low, fallen, and unqualified, but at the same time, very eager to receive the mercy of the Lord. Therefore, even though we may chant improperly, if we chant with the motivation to please the Lord, the Lord accepts that just as much as someone who chants perfectly.

*bhagavad-bhakti-hīnasya*  
*jātiḥ śāstraṁ japas tapaḥ*

This verse from *Hari-bhakti-sudhodaya* (3.11) means that expert chanting of Vedic *mantras*, birth in a great family or nation, performance of austerities and penances, and knowledge of scriptures, if devoid of devotional service, are all compared to ornaments on a dead body.

Lord Caitanya explains a very nice point in the next verse (*Cc. Madhya* 1.212): “I really had no business in coming to Bengal, but I have come just to see you two brothers.” The Lord was revealing that His only business for coming to Bengal was because Rūpa’s and Sanātana’s hearts captured Him and lured Him there.

**Birth after birth you have been My eternal servants. I am sure that Kṛṣṇa will deliver you very soon.” The Lord then placed His two hands on the heads of both of them, and in return they immediately placed the lotus feet of the Lord on their heads. After this, the Lord embraced both of them and requested all of the devotees present to be merciful upon them and deliver them. When all of the devotees saw the mercy of the Lord upon the two brothers, they were very much gladdened, and they began to chant the holy name of the Lord, “Hari! Hari!” (***Cc. Madhya* **1.215–18)**

There is a wonderful purport to this verse. The essence of it is that a devotee should always be non-envious and very happy when he sees another Vaiṣṇava get the Lord’s mercy, just as it is described in this verse. Prabhupāda says that when a Vaiṣṇava sees another Vaiṣṇava as the recipient of the Lord’s mercy, he becomes very happy. Vaiṣṇava*s* are not envious. If a Vaiṣṇava, by the mercy of the Lord, is empowered by Him to distribute the Lord’s holy name all over the world, other Vaiṣṇava*s* become very joyful—that is, of course, if they are truly Vaiṣṇavas.

**Purport**

One who is envious of the success of a Vaiṣṇava is certainly not a Vaiṣṇava himself but is an ordinary, mundane man. Envy and jealousy are manifested by mundane people, not by Vaiṣṇavas. Why should a Vaiṣṇava be envious of another Vaiṣṇava who is successful in spreading the holy name of the Lord? An actual Vaiṣṇava is very pleased to accept another Vaiṣṇava who is bestowing the Lord’s mercy. A mundane person in the dress of a Vaiṣṇava should not be respected but rejected. This is enjoined in the *śāstra* (*upekṣā*). The word *upekṣā* means neglect. One should neglect an envious person. A preacher’s duty is to love the Supreme Personality of Godhead, make friendships with Vaiṣṇavas, show mercy to the innocent, and reject or neglect those who are envious or jealous. There are many jealous people in the dress of Vaiṣṇavas in this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, and they should be completely neglected. There is no need to serve a jealous person who is in the dress of a Vaiṣṇava. When Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura says *chāḍiyā vaiṣṇava sevā nistāra* peyeche *kebā*, he is indicating an actual Vaiṣṇava, not an envious or jealous person in the dress of a Vaiṣṇava. (*Cc. Madhya* 1.218, Purport)

That is Prabhupāda’s elaboration on this verse about how the Vaiṣṇavas were very glad to see Rūpa and Sanātana achieve the mercy of Lord Caitanya. All the Vaiṣṇava*s* present gave their mercy to Rūpa and Sanātana, and then Rūpa and Sanātana immediately touched their lotus feet. All the Vaiṣṇavas became very happy and congratulated the two brothers for receiving the mercy of the Lord.

## ŚRĪ MĀDHAVENDRA PURI’S DEVOTIONAL SERVICE

**Madhya-līlā: 4.107–128**

**Simferopol—2000**

**Text 112**

*remuṇāte kaila gopīnātha daraśana tāṅra rūpa dekhiñā haila vihvala-mana*

**Going into South India, Śrī Mādhavendra Purī visited Remuṇā, where Gopīnātha is situated. Upon seeing the beauty of the Deity, Mādhavendra Purī was overwhelmed.**

This section is where Mādhavendra Purī is on his way to Jagannātha Purī to get sandalwood for the Gopāla Deity. Mādhavendra Purī had received *darśana* from the Lord in a dream, who had requested him to go get sandalwood to bring down His body temperature. His body had been scorched for a long time under the heat of the blazing sun, and if sandalwood paste were placed upon His body, its temperature would decrease.

This chapter describes the glorious position of Mādhavendra Purī, whose devotion to Kṛṣṇa was pure and unalloyed. Mādhavendra Purī established the highest standard of devotional service. Lord Caitanya proclaimed that whatever standards Mādhavendra Purī established, He would personally follow. The Lord was so eager to accept service from him that He personally requested Mādhavendra Purī several times in his dreams to render some service to Him.

One of the primary purposes for Lord Caitanya’s descent was to distribute the seed of ecstatic love for Godhead. *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* describes that the Lord thought, “I will personally descend and inaugurate the congregational chanting of the holy names of the Lord. I will make everyone dance in ecstatic love of Godhead, realizing the four mellows of loving devotional service.”

Mādhavendra Purī experienced the highest symptoms of ecstatic love of Godhead. He experienced feelings of separation similar to those experienced by Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī when Kṛṣṇa left Vṛndāvana to go to Mathurā. In his poem, just prior to the time of his departure from this world, he prayed (*Cc. Madhya* 4.197): *ayi dīna-dayārdra nātha he, mathurānātha kadāvalokyase*. “O my Lord, O master of Mathurā, O merciful master, where are You now? Because of my not being able to see You, my agitated heart is very unsteady. O my most beloved, what should I do now?” A devotee feels very poor and neglected by the Lord, just like Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, who was feeling that way just after Kṛṣṇa left Vṛndāvana and went to Mathurā.

Because of this offense committed by Rāmacandra Purī to his *guru*, who had even directly offended Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the seed of material desires began to fructify in the heart of Rāmacandra Purī.
