Just as Kṛṣṇa in Gokula had many mischievous pastimes, young Nimai also had many of His own. It was from the time that Jagannātha Miśra told Him to stop studying that He and His friends started performing all types of mischief.

They would go into the gardens of the neighbors and pretend to be animals, covering themselves in cloth, and in the middle of the night, they would raid all of the banana trees. All the people would come out shouting, “Hey! What is happening out there? Hey, what are you doing?!” Nimai and His friends would run away, stealing bananas. Sometimes, they would sneak up to people’s homes and lock the doors from the outside, and when the people would have to go outside to take care of their bodily needs, they could not get out of the house. Nimai and His friends would stand outside laughing and laughing. In this way, their mischievous activities expanded.

Gradually, everyone began to learn that He was engaging in so much mischief because Jagannātha Miśra had stopped Him from studying.

One day, young Nimai was sitting on a large pile of pots that had been used for cooking. These pots were considered to be contaminated because they had already been used, and to touch these pots was considered to be unclean. Nimai’s friends ran to Śacīmātā and said, “Look at your son sitting on top of those rejected pots!”

Śacīmātā ran and found her son sitting on the pots. She said to Him, “What are You doing on these pots? Do You not know that these are rejected, contaminated pots, and You should not be sitting on them?”

Young Nimai began preaching to her: “How can these pots be considered contaminated if I am sitting on them?” However, because Śacīmātā was so absorbed in love for her son, she could not understand what He was speaking about. He said, “How do you expect Me to know that these are rejected pots? I am a fool. You will not let Me study. How can I know the difference between clean pots and unclean pots? You are keeping Me a fool.”

When the neighbors heard about these activities, they approached Śacīmātā and said, “What kind of enemy of yours has advised you to do something like this to your son? All of us have to struggle to get our children to study. Your son loves to study, and you have told Him not to. Why are you doing this to Him?”

When Jagannātha Miśra heard all of these appeals from the neighbors, and also considering all the mischievous activities of his son, he decided that he better let Him continue His studies. At that point he gave permission to his son: “Alright, You can continue Your studies.” Nimai was very happy. He took His books and went to study. He was completely absorbed in His studies.

Nimai was a very great scholar. He surpassed all the other children in Navadvīpa in their studies, and gradually He became renowned as a great *paṇḍita.*

One night, Jagannātha Miśra had a dream in which he saw his son Nimai with His head shaven and wearing the dress of a *sannyāsī*. He was surrounded by thousands and thousands of followers. When he had this dream, his heart became very disturbed. When he awoke, he revealed to Śacīmātā what he had seen in his dream. He could understand that someday his son Nimai was going to accept the renounced order like His brother Viśvarūpa. Very shortly after he had that dream, Jagannātha Miśra departed from this world. He left this world because he could not bear the thought of his son accepting the renounced order of life, and he left Śacīmātā alone with young Nimai. At that point, Nimai became everything to Śacīmātā. She would do anything within her ability to fulfill all of His needs.

She was also very fearful that some day her son would accept the renounced order of life. You can imagine what happened to her when she heard that Nimai took *sannyāsa*. It is described in *Caitanya-bhāgavata*, that when Śacīmātā heard that Nimai had accepted the renounced order of life, her body became completely lifeless. She completely stared off in space, absorbed in thoughts of the Lord. For many days she did not eat, drink, or even move.

All the residents of Navadvīpa were also absorbed in similar thoughts. It was very difficult for Advaita Ācārya and all the other associates of the Lord from Navadvīpa and Śāntipura.

One day, shortly after the Lord accepted the renounced order, all the devotees heard a voice in the sky that said, “Please do not worry. Although I have begun My travels, very soon I will return to Navadvīpa. I will come, and I will be with all of you. I will satisfy your desires.” Upon hearing this voice, they came back to life. They were all anxiously awaiting the arrival of Lord Caitanya.

This pastime is describing the period shortly after Lord Caitanya’s acceptance of the renounced order. It is at this time that He wants to go and preach in South India, but He is going there on the pretext of looking for His brother, Viśvarūpa, because He does not want to reveal His real plan. He knew that Viśvarūpa had already left the planet. It is described that although Viśvarūpa had left the planet, he actually merged into the body of Lord Nityānanda. Lord Nityānanda is therefore described as the brother of Lord Caitanya, although the form of Viśvarūpa had passed away. Therefore, it is said here (*CC. Madhya* 7.13–18):

**Knowing everything, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu was aware that Viśvarūpa had already passed away. A pretense of ignorance was necessary, however, so that He could go to South India and liberate the people there. Upon hearing this message from Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, all the devotees became very unhappy and remained silent with sullen faces.**

**Nityānanda Prabhu then said, “How is it possible for You to go alone? Who can tolerate this? Let one or two of us go with You; otherwise You may fall into the clutches of thieves and rogues along the way. They may be whomever You like, but two persons should go with You. Indeed, I know all the paths to the different places of pilgrimage in South India. Just order Me, and I shall go with You.”**

**The Lord replied, “I am simply a dancer, and You are the wire-puller. However You pull the wires to make Me dance, I shall dance in that way.”**

All the devotees were anxious because they did not want Lord Caitanya to travel alone. Lord Caitanya did not want to select one or two to go with Him, because if He did, then the others would be in complete anxiety, thinking, “Why did You select them and not us?” The Lord is thus presenting Himself in a humble way, saying, “I am the dancer, you are the wire puller. You make Me dance however you want.” He will now begin speaking about all the different devotees that are present.

**After accepting the** *sannyāsa* **order, I decided to go to Vṛndāvana, but You took Me instead to the house of Advaita Prabhu. (***Cc. Madhya* **7.19)**

This is a wonderful pastime also. Lord Caitanya is complaining to Lord Nityānanda, because immediately after Lord Caitanya took *sannyāsa,* He wanted to go alone to Vṛndāvana. It was the first place He wanted to go—to a solitary place where He could absorb Himself in meditation on topics of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

One might ask, “How can Lord Caitanya absorb His thoughts in Lord Kṛṣṇa? Lord Caitanya is Kṛṣṇa.” Lord Caitanya was very much absorbed in the mood of a devotee, and He accepted the role of a devotee. As a devotee, He was always glorifying the names and pastimes of Kṛṣṇa. When Lord Caitanya is with His intimate associates, they are always speaking about the pastimes of Lord Kṛṣṇa. This was His internal mood, being in the mood of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, who was always absorbed in thoughts of separation from Kṛṣṇa.

Lord Caitanya wanted to go to Vṛndāvana, and He made a plan immediately to leave. However, Lord Nityānanda also made a plan, knowing that Lord Caitanya is always absorbed in ecstasy. Often, when Lord Caitanya is completely absorbed in ecstasy, He loses all external consciousness. In that state of absorption, He wanders throughout Rāḍhadeśa, sometimes appearing aimless. Knowing this, Lord Nityānanda told some cowherd boys, “When Caitanya Mahāprabhu comes this way and asks which way it is to the Yamunā River, you point in that direction.” The cowherd boys accepted the instruction of Lord Nityānanda.

When Lord Caitanya was wandering in ecstasy, He thought He was approaching Vṛndāvana. He saw these cowherd boys and asked them, “Can you tell Me which way it is to the river Yamunā?”

The cowherd boys pointed in the direction of the Ganges, saying, “Yes, it is this way.” In His ecstasy, Lord Caitanya went to the bank of the Ganges, thinking that it was the Yamunā and that He was in Vṛndāvana. As soon as He arrived at the banks of the Ganges, there was a boat waiting for Him. Advaita Ācārya was waiting in the boat, and He had some dry clothing for the Lord.

Lord Nityānanda had told Advaita Ācārya that He should wait for Lord Caitanya on the banks of the Ganges with some dry clothing, because He knew that Lord Caitanya would take a bath as soon as He saw what He thought was the Yamunā. Lord Caitanya entered into the Ganges, and thinking that it was the Yamunā, He began to take His bath. He saw Advaita Ācārya standing there in the boat.

He said, “Advaita, what are you doing here? What has brought you here to Vṛndāvana?”

Advaita Ācārya said to Lord Caitanya, “Wherever You are, there is Vṛndāvana!” When Lord Caitanya came to external consciousness, He realized, “Wait, this is not the Yamunā. This is not Vṛndāvana. Lord Nityānanda has cheated Me! He brought Me to the banks of the Ganges and told Me it was Vṛndāvana!”

Lord Nityānanda said, “No, actually I have not done that. Just see, here at this place the river Yamunā and the river Ganges flow together. I did not lie. This is the Yamunā.” However, the whole thing was a plot by Lord Nityānanda, who wanted to take Lord Caitanya to the house of Advaita Ācārya.

Because of this, Lord Caitanya is complaining here to Lord Nityānanda, saying, “After accepting the *sannyāsa* order, I decided to go to Vṛndāvana, but You took Me instead to the house of Advaita Prabhu.” (*Cc. Madhya* 7.19) The Lord continued:

**While on the way to Jagannātha Purī, You broke My** *sannyāsa* **staff. I know that all of you have great affection for Me, but such things disturb My activities. (***Cc. Madhya* **7.20)**

This is another pastime called *daṇḍa-bhaṅga-līlā,* where Lord Nityānanda broke the *sannyāsa daṇḍa* of Lord Caitanya. In His heart, Lord Nityānanda was thinking, “That same Lord whom I hold in My heart is holding this *daṇḍa*. Lord Caitanya is not bound by any external designations. Why is He carrying this *daṇḍa*?” He then broke the *daṇḍa* into three parts.

When Jagadānanda came back and saw the broken *daṇḍa*, he was shocked: “What am I going to tell Lord Caitanya?” He approached Lord Nityānanda and said, “Who has done this? Who has broken this *daṇḍa*?”

Lord Nityānanda said, “He who carries this *daṇḍa* has broken it.” In other words, He was saying that it was Lord Caitanya’s will that His *daṇḍa* be broken. Lord Nityānanda never acts independently from the will of Lord Caitanya. He was saying that that was actually the will of the Lord.

Jagadānanda Paṇḍita, though, could not understand the real meaning behind the breaking of the *daṇḍa*. He picked up the pieces of the *daṇḍa* and walked to Lord Caitanya. When he showed Lord Caitanya the broken *daṇḍa,* the Lord angrily said, “Who has done this?” Jagadānanda Paṇḍita told Him that Lord Nityānanda was responsible for the breaking of the *daṇḍa*. Externally the Lord was very angry, but internally He was not because Nityānanda broke the *daṇḍa* out of intense love for His worshipful Lord. Sometimes the Lord’s external mood may be different from His actual feelings. There are other examples of this, as is explained in the next verse.

**Jagadānanda wants Me to enjoy bodily sense gratification, and out of fear I do whatever he tells Me. (***Cc. Madhya* **7.21)**

Jagadānanda Paṇḍita and Lord Caitanya had a very special relationship. Jagadānanda Paṇḍita could not tolerate to see the austerities Lord Caitanya performed. It was very painful for him to see Lord Caitanya lying on the hard ground to rest. Thus, he took some cloth and dyed it, stuffed cotton inside it, and gave it to Govinda, Lord Caitanya’s servant. He told Govinda, “When it is time for Lord Caitanya to take rest, please place this mattress for the Lord to lie on.” When Lord Caitanya saw this mattress, He became very disturbed, because He wanted to perform austerities. He had accepted the renounced order of life, and He wanted to display the renunciation of that *āśrama.*

He asked, “Who made this mattress?” Govinda told Him that Jagadānanda had made it. As soon as he said that, fear entered Lord Caitanya’s heart. “Oh no, Jagadānanda made this mattress. I know what it will mean if I do not use it.” Not wanting to lie on the mattress, Lord Caitanya told Govinda, “Please take it away.”

Svarūpa Dāmodara, however, knew that Jagadānanda was pained to see Lord Caitanya lying on the hard ground, so he decided that he would try something else. He took the leaves from a tree, and began breaking the leaves into very small strips with his fingernails. He took the outer cloth of the Lord and put the leaves inside. In this way, he made a mattress for the Lord to lie on. The Lord agreed to accept it, which satisfied Jagadānanda Paṇḍita.

There is another pastime with Jagadānanda Paṇḍita. Lord Caitanya became very angry when Jagadānanda Paṇḍita brought Him sandalwood oil. Jagadānanda Paṇḍita was at the house of Sivanandā Sena, who had a very large quantity of sandalwood oil that he wanted the Lord to use on His body. Jagadānanda brought this sandalwood oil in a clay pot and gave it to Govinda to deliver to Lord Caitanya.

When Lord Caitanya saw this oil, He was thinking, “What does Jagadānanda want Me to become? A sense gratifier? I am a *sannyāsī*. What business do I have smearing sandalwood oil all over My body? Not only that, but if I smear this sandalwood oil on My body and then walk in the streets, people will think that I am associating with women and wearing this oil to attract women. My reputation will be spoiled. However, in order that Jagadānanda’s efforts do not go in vain, take this oil to be burned in the lamps at the Jagannātha temple. In this way, at least the oil will be used in the service of the Lord.”

Govinda took the oil and bundled it up, but he approached the Lord a second time, appealing to Him on Jagadānanda’s behalf. Again Lord Caitanya rejected it.

One day, Jagadānanda came to Lord Caitanya’s house. He already knew that Lord Caitanya had rejected the oil. When he walked into Lord Caitanya’s house, Lord Caitanya said, “Do you understand that I have given instructions that your oil be used in the lamps of the Jagannātha temple?”

Jagadānanda picked up the clay pot and said, “What oil?! I never brought any oil for You! Who told You such a story?” He walked out of the house, took the pot, and threw it on the ground and smashed it. In anger, he went back to his residence. He closed the door, lay down, and fasted for three days.

After three days, Lord Caitanya, understanding the heart of Jagadānanda Paṇḍita, went to Jagadānanda Paṇḍita’s house. He knocked on the door and said, “Jagadānanda, are you in there? I am hungry. Can you cook something for Me to eat?” As soon as Jagadānanda heard Lord Caitanya make this request, he immediately got up, very anxious to render some service to his Lord. Even though he had been fasting for three days, he immediately ran out and gathered ingredients to make a very nice, opulent feast for the Lord. After he cooked and offered the feast, Lord Caitanya came to take *prasāda.*

When Lord Caitanya was eating the rice preparations, He was thinking, “This is such wonderful food; such a wonderful taste. Jagadānanda, you cook such wonderful food! Even when you are angry, still it tastes so good. I can understand that Kṛṣṇa must have personally eaten this food.”

Jagadānanda said, “I did not cook this food. He who is eating this food is responsible for these preparations. I am simply His instrument.” Jagadānanda began putting more and more food on the Lord’s plate. Lord Caitanya was becoming a little fearful because He had already eaten enough *prasāda,* but Jagadānanda kept piling it on. Lord Caitanya was afraid that if He did not eat it, Jagadānanda would become angry with Him, so He continued eating.

Lord Caitanya finally stopped and said, “I have eaten enough for ten people! How can you expect Me to eat any more? I am a *sannyāsī*! I am not supposed to be eating such opulent food!” However, Jagadānanda convinced the Lord by his love to eat more and more. Although Lord Caitanya did not want to eat so much, He was afraid to say, “No, I will not take anymore.” He did not want to see the anger of Jagadānanda. He now wanted Jagadānanda to break his fast and told him, “Please sit down and take some *prasāda*.”

Jagadānanda said, “No My Lord. Many others have helped cook this *prasāda* too. First let me give them some remnants of Your foodstuff and then I will eat.”

When Lord Caitanya left, He told Govinda, “You go back and make sure that Jagadānanda is eating some *prasāda*.” He did not want Jagadānanda to continue his fast any longer. That is why He accepted the foodstuff. He was afraid that Jagadānanda would continue fasting indefinitely. These are the kind of loving exchanges that exist between the Lord and Jagadānanda Paṇḍita. That is why He says, “Jagadānanda wants Me to enjoy bodily sense gratification, and out of fear I do whatever he tells Me.” (*Cc. Madhya* 7.21)

Lord Caitanya is actually describing the qualities of His devotee. He is speaking as if He was complaining about His devotee, but on the contrary, He is actually glorifying him.

There are many other examples given, such as that of Mukunda and Dāmodara Paṇḍita. Dāmodara Paṇḍita also had a very unique relationship with Lord Caitanya. Dāmodara Paṇḍita is famous for his chastisement of Lord Caitanya, and the Lord considers him expert in chastising.

One time Lord Caitanya was going to send Dāmodara Paṇḍita away to Navadvīpa to protect Mother Śacī. He was thinking that Dāmodara Paṇḍita is expert in correcting everybody; Śacīmātā will certainly be saved by Dāmodara Paṇḍita’s association. Lord Caitanya externally appeared angry, but internally He was very pleased. The Lord may sometimes appear angry externally when the devotees want Him to do something that goes against His principles of renunciation, because He wants to establish the principles of renunciation as a *sannyāsī*. Internally, however, He is very pleased by their love. Sometimes, out of their intense love, they would even force Lord Caitanya to do something contrary to His principles of renunciation.

**Madhya-līlā: 7.121–129**

**Sochi—October 28, 1996**

**St. Petersburg—June 3, 1994**

Further in the Seventh Chapter of *Madhya-līlā* are descriptions of how the Lord delivered everyone all over South India by inducing them to chant the holy name of the Lord. In whichever village the Lord entered, all the residents, upon seeing Him, would become absorbed in ecstatic love of Godhead. Simply by seeing Him, they became devotees of the Lord. Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī describes that Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mood of preaching in this particular tour had already been explained, and therefore he does not repeat the description in this chapter.

**In one village there was a Vedic** *brāhmaṇa* **named Kūrma. He invited Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu to his home with great respect and devotion. This** *brāhmaṇa* **brought Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu to his home, washed His lotus feet and, with his family members, drank that water. With great affection and respect, that Kūrma** *brāhmaṇa* **made Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu eat all kinds of food. After that, the remnants were shared by all the members of the family. The** *brāhmaṇa* **then began to pray, “O my Lord, Your lotus feet are meditated upon by Lord Brahmā, and these very lotus feet have come into my home. My dear Lord, there is no limit to my great fortune. It cannot be described. Today my family, birth and riches have all been glorified.” The** *brāhmaṇa* **begged Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, “My dear Lord, kindly show me favor and let me go with You. I can no longer tolerate the waves of misery caused by materialistic life.” (***Cc. Madhya* **7.121–126)**

**Purport**

This statement is applicable for everyone, regardless of how rich or prosperous one may be. Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura has confirmed this statement: *saṁsāra-viṣānale, divā-niśi hiyā jvale*. He states that the materialistic way of life causes a burning in the heart. One cannot make any provisions for the troublesome life of the material world. It is a fact that one may be very happy as far as riches are concerned and one may be very opulent in every respect, yet one has to manage the *viṣayas* to meet the demands of the body and of so many family members and subordinates. One has to take so much trouble to minister to others. Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura therefore prays: *viṣaya chāḍiyā kabe śuddha ha’be mana*. Thus one must become freed from the materialistic way of life. One has to merge himself in the ocean of transcendental bliss. In other words, one cannot relish transcendental bliss without being freed from the materialistic way of life. It appears that the *brāhmaṇa* named Kūrma was materially very happy, for he expressed his family tradition as *janma-kula-dhana*. Now, being glorious, he wanted to leave all these material opulences. He wanted to travel with Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. According to the Vedic way of civilization, one should leave his family after attaining fifty years of age and go to the forest of Vṛndāvana to devote the rest of his life to the service of the Lord.

Since the lotus feet of the Lord had entered into his home, Kūrma *brāhmaṇa* understood that all auspiciousness had come into his life. The *brāhmaṇa* was described as having *janma*, *kula*, and *dhana*, which means birth, family and wealth. He said that because of the Lord’s visit, all of these things had become glorified, having now obtained meaning in his life. Yet, although the *brāhmaṇa* said that his birth, family, and riches were glorified, he thought, “I want to give these things up. Prior to Your arrival in my home, these things had no value; now that You are here, they have value.”

One might ask, “Now that they have value, why is he asking to give them up?” Now his home is glorified because the Lord has entered it; his family is glorified because they have received the lotus feet of the Lord in their home; and his wealth is glorified because now he can employ it in the service of the Lord. Yet, he is saying, “I can no longer tolerate the miseries caused by materialistic life, therefore please let me go with You and leave all this.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda explains in the purport that one must become free from the materialistic way of life; otherwise, it is not possible to relish transcendental bliss. This *brāhmaṇa* was thus thinking, “Let me get rid of it all. I want to experience transcendental bliss,” and he begged Lord Caitanya to be allowed to travel with Him. He wanted to renounce his materialistic way of life, saying that he could no longer tolerate the waves of misery. Prabhupāda explains that this statement is applicable for everyone. Everyone has to give up the materialistic way of life, which causes a burning in the heart. This burning of the heart is our suffering in material existence.

Similarly, in the *Gurv-aṣṭaka* prayers it is described that the spiritual master is like a rain cloud, which pours water on the fire of material existence. This fire is also compared to lust, which is all-consuming. The more fuel that is added to the fire, the more it consumes. When a living entity has many desires that he wants to fulfill, it is like being caught in a blazing fire. No matter how much fuel is added to the fire, it is consumed. In the same way, the living entity is never satisfied, no matter how much sense gratification he engages in.

Prabhupāda explains that in materialistic life one has to meet the demands of the body, of many family members, and subordinates. One has to take much trouble to administer to others. One must thus become freed from the materialistic way of life and submerge himself in the ocean of transcendental bliss. However, as also described here, “One cannot experience this ocean of transcendental bliss until he gives up the materialistic way of life.” This is of course very difficult unless one is introduced to the higher pleasures of renunciation by the mercy of the devotees of the Lord.

This point is also made in an example from *ŚrīmadBhāgavatam* wherein a discussion takes place between Nārada Muni and Prajāpati Dakṣa, who were sons of Brahmā. It is described that in the beginning of creation, Brahmā first produced the four Kumāras and requested them to increase progeny. However, the Kumāras were not interested in this way of life and refused. Lord Brahmā became angry, and from his forehead appeared Lord Śiva. Brahmā was hoping that Lord Śiva would help to expand the creation. But instead of expanding the creation of this universe, Lord Śiva further expanded into different forms, which all began consuming what was already created. Thus, Lord Śiva’s disposition was immediately revealed, and Brahmā could see that he was not going to further expand through Lord Śiva.

Brahmā then ordered Lord Śiva to sit down and meditate so that the *rudras* would not consume the whole creation. Lord Brahmā then produced ten sons, nine of which (Marīci, Atri, Aṅgirā, Pulastya, Pulaha, Kratu, Bhṛgu, Vasiṣṭha, and Prajāpati Dakṣa) were inclined towards fruitive activities. Prajāpati Dakṣa was inclined to perform Vedic sacrifices, but he also wanted to enjoy the fruits of his sacrifices, being somewhat attached to the materialistic way of life.

By Brahmā’s deliberation, Nārada was the tenth son produced. It is described in the First Canto of *ŚrīmadBhāgavatam* that Nārada Muni appeared in a previous life as the son of a maidservant. Then at the time of the next creation, he entered into and appeared from the body of Lord Brahmā. Prabhupāda explains that Nārada Muni appeared in a transcendental body. He preached the path of devotional service—the path of renunciation. However, the nine other sons of Brahmā, although good, had an inclination towards worldly, fruitive activities.

Dakṣa’s expertise was sex life. “Dakṣa” means “expert,” and he was very expert in producing children. He had produced the Haryaśvas and the Savalāśvas. First there were

10,000 sons and then there were the 1,000 Savalāśvas. After Nārada Muni preached to them about the path of renunciation, Daksa produced sixty daughters who married with different progenitors, and they created all the different species of life on the earth.

Dakṣa had sent his 10,000 sons to perform austerities. Nārada Muni saw the severe austerities performed by these sons of Dakṣa and he considered them to be like ripe fruits on a tree, which should be picked. Nārada Muni wanted to convince them of the path of renunciation (*nivṛtti-mārga*). He thought, “Why perform all these austerities simply to become entangled in household life?” Thus, he convinced them all to give up worldly pursuits and go back to Godhead, which they did. The same thing happened with the Savalāśvas, and Prajāpati Dakṣa became very disturbed by the behavior of Nārada Muni.

He said, “It is persons like you who give *sādhus* a bad reputation.” He thought that Nārada Muni had prematurely convinced his sons to take to the path of renunciation. He thus explained to Nārada Muni, “I can understand that this material world is a place of unhappiness, but the only way that one can be genuinely unhappy is by experiencing material life. Let them become married and experience for themselves the path of enjoyment and the frustrations that will become manifest by that enjoyment. A true spirit of renunciation will then become manifest in their hearts. However, you have prematurely convinced them about the path of renunciation.”

Of course, it was not premature because they all went back to Godhead. Nevertheless, Prajāpati Dakṣa was expressing his philosophy to Nārada and chastising him for what he did. He said, “It is not appropriate behavior of a *sādhu* to convince others to prematurely take to the path of renunciation when there is not sufficient frustration with materialistic life.” Prajāpati Dakṣa was thinking that their renunciation was incomplete.

Prabhupāda gives the example of a saying in Bengali, which states that a woman cannot understand the pain of childbirth until she actually has a child, and after experiencing the pain of childbirth, she will not be inclined to have another child. Yet, we know that this feeling often changes. Even though a woman may experience great pain in having to bear the child, still we see that there is an inclination to bear another child. Thus, it is not necessarily a fact that frustration with materialistic life will produce the spirit of renunciation. We may feel a sense of renunciation in the midst of frustration. We may seek some relief from that frustration, and after finding relief, we forget just how much suffering existed previously. Therefore, it is not simply that when we become frustrated with material life that we can actually experience genuine renunciation within the heart.

This was the argument that Dakṣa was trying to use with Nārada Muni. Nevertheless, we know that Nārada Muni, being the great saintly personality that he is, was able to inject into the hearts of the sons of Dakṣa the spirit of renunciation. He awakened it within their hearts by introducing the superior path of renunciation through devotional service, without them having to directly experience material frustration.

An intelligent person learns by hearing. When Parīkṣit Mahārāja inquired from Śukadeva Gosvāmī about how persons can avoid the hellish planets, an example was given that an intelligent person, after hearing, can avoid having to undergo all of these sufferings. Similarly, if a person hears that one will be punished for stealing, he will not steal. Somebody less intelligent may steal and get punished before he learns that stealing is not proper. Finally, the least intelligent person will steal, get punished, and go out and steal again. Thus, the intelligent person should learn by hearing and not have to get punished. He should not have to undergo the materialistic way of life. By hearing about the true path of renunciation, he can be sufficiently educated about the materialistic way of life, and he can learn to avoid it without having to experience it himself.

Prabhupāda says that one cannot relish transcendental bliss without being freed from the materialistic way of life. It appears that Kūrma *brāhmaṇa* became materially very happy, because he had expressed his family tradition as *janma*, *kula*, and *dhana.*

This is a very instructive section of the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* because herein Lord Caitanya instructed Kūrma the *brāhmaṇa* with very famous verses. These verses, which emphasize the mission of the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, are possibly the ones Śrīla Prabhupāda quoted most often.

**Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu replied, “Don’t speak like that again. Better to remain at home and chant the holy name of Kṛṣṇa always.” (***Cc. Madhya* **7.127)**

**Purport**

It is not advisable in this age of Kali to leave one’s family suddenly, for people are not trained as proper *brahmācārīs* and *gṛhasthas*. Therefore Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu advised the *brāhmaṇa* not to be too eager to give up family life. It would be better to remain with his family and try to become purified by chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* regularly under the direction of a spiritual master. This is the instruction of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. If this principle is followed by everyone, there is no need to accept *sannyāsa*. In the next verse Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu advises everyone to become an ideal householder by offenselessly chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* and teaching the same principle to everyone he meets.

**Instruct everyone to follow the orders of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa as they are given in the** *Bhagavad-gītā* **and** *ŚrīmadBhāgavatam***. In this way become a spiritual master and try to liberate everyone in this land. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu further advised the** *brāhmaṇa* **Kūrma, “If you follow this instruction, your materialistic life at home will not obstruct your spiritual advancement. Indeed, if you follow these regulative principles, we will again meet here, or, rather, you will never lose My company. (***Cc. Madhya* **7.128–129)**

**Purport**

This is an opportunity for everyone. If one simply follows the instructions of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, under the guidance of His representative, and chants the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra*, teaching everyone as far as possible the same principle, the contamination of the materialistic way of life will not even touch him. It does not matter whether one lives in a holy place like Vṛndāvana, Navadvīpa or Jagannātha Purī or in the midst of European cities, where the materialistic way of life is very prominent. If a devotee follows the instructions of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, he lives in the company of the Lord. Wherever he lives, he converts that place into Vṛndāvana and Navadvīpa. This means that materialism cannot touch him. This is the secret of success for one advancing in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

In these particular instructions delivered by Lord Caitanya, the necessity of always chanting the holy names of the Lord and always glorifying the Lord in all conditions of life is being emphasized. In other words, Lord Caitanya was instructing Kūrma the *brāhmaṇa* that the materialistic way of life can be relieved. He was teaching the real path of renunciation rather than the false path.

*anāsaktasya viṣayān yathārham upayuñjataḥ*  
*nirbandhaḥ kṛṣṇa-sambandhe yuktaṁ vairāgyam ucyate*

(BRS 1.2.255)

Renunciation of things without understanding their relationship with Kṛṣṇa is not as complete as renunciation when one understands everything’s relationship with Kṛṣṇa and therefore uses it in His service. Lord Caitanya is establishing what that service is. The most important service that one can do in whatever position in life he may be in—*brahmacārī, gṛhastha, vānaprastha*, or *sannyāsa*—is to use everything to glorify the Lord.

Śrīla Prabhupāda translates this verse as: “Tell everybody you meet about the teachings of Lord Kṛṣṇa as given in *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*.” Lord Caitanya uses the words *kṛṣṇa-upadeśa,* which means “instructions of Kṛṣṇa.” However, Śrīla Prabhupāda has translated *kṛṣṇaupadeśa* as “giving orders of Lord Kṛṣṇa as they are given in the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.”*

Of course, it is Śrīla Prabhupāda’s prerogative to translate it in that way. The Vaiṣṇava *ācāryas,* when translating the text of the Vedic literatures, understand the essence of the texts, because they understand the *siddhānta*. One who realizes the *siddhānta* of the Vedic scriptures can translate the texts in such a way that the *siddhānta* is never lost. *Siddhānta* means the ultimate end or the goal of Vedic wisdom.

Kṛṣṇa says (*Bg.* 15.15):

*vedaiś ca sarvair aham eva vedyo*  
*vedānta-kṛd veda-vid eva cāham*

“By all the *Vedas*, I am to be known. Indeed, I am the compiler of *Vedānta*, and I am the knower of the *Vedas*.”

*vāsudeva-parā vedā*  
*vāsudeva-parā makhāḥ*  
*vāsudeva-parā yogā*  
*vāsudeva-parāḥ kriyāḥ*  
*vāsudeva-paraṁ jñānaṁ*  
*vāsudeva-paraṁ tapaḥ*  
*vāsudeva-paro dharmo*  
*vāsudeva-parā gatiḥ*

(SB 1.2.28–29)

“In the revealed scriptures, the ultimate object of knowledge is Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Personality of Godhead. The purpose of performing sacrifice is to please Him. Yoga is for realizing Him. All fruitive activities are ultimately rewarded by Him only. He is supreme knowledge, and all severe austerities are performed to know Him. Religion \[*dharma*\] is rendering loving service unto Him. He is the supreme goal of life.”

In other words, the Vedic literatures are always aimed at the ultimate goal, which is the Supreme Lord Vāsudeva. One who knows the ultimate goal of the *Vedas* knows that all Vedic wisdom is meant to ultimately lead one to rendering transcendental loving service to the Lord. The realized devotee therefore presents the teachings of the *Vedas* in such a way that the ultimate goal is never lost.

Similarly, in the *Bhagavad-gītā* there are different sections, such as *karma-yoga, jñāna-yoga,* and *bhakti-yoga.* Nevertheless, when Prabhupāda gives the purports, he always brings everything to *bhakti*. At whatever platform one may be on, the ultimate goal of the *Vedas* is to take him from that platform and lead him to *bhakti*. He does that because he has realized the ultimate goal of all Vedic knowledge. He knows (*Cc. Madhya* 19.75), *bhagavadbhakti-hīnasya, jātiḥ śāstraṁ japas tapaḥ*: If a person is devoid of devotional service to Kṛṣṇa, then birth in a great family or nation, performing all kinds of austerities and penances, chanting all kinds of Vedic *mantras*, and advanced learning in the Vedic scriptures are all just like ornaments on a dead body. They simply serve the mental concoction of the general population.

Śrīla Prabhupāda understands the importance of devotional service, and that without it, everything is useless. If a person is devoid of devotional service, he is like a dead body, although breathing; devotional service is our life.

Prabhupāda would many times give the example that for a person who is not engaged in devotional service, his body is like the bellows in a blacksmith’s shop, which breathe but have no life. It may look like there is life because they are breathing, but there is no life in the bellows. In the same way, even though the physical body may breathe, if a person is not engaged in devotional service, there is really no life. All his activities are ultimately going to bring him suffering. That is the ultimate result of activities that are not performed in service to Kṛṣṇa. Immediately, we may feel some satisfaction, but ultimately, we will not be satisfied.

*kṛṣṇa-bhakta—niṣkāma, ataeva ‘śānta’*  
*bhukti-mukti-siddhi-kāmī—sakali ‘aśānta’*

(Cc. Madhya 19.149)

A devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa can be satisfied because he has no material desires. Whereas, one who desires material opulence, liberation, or mystic *yoga* perfection can never be satisfied because he is filled with lusty desires. However, a devotee is ultimately satisfied, even though he may not be experiencing full satisfaction now. He understands that the goal of all of his activities is to satisfy the Lord. He is therefore willing to accept inconvenience or difficulties now for the sake of pleasing the Lord.

Śrīla Prabhupāda knows the goal of devotional service and, therefore, when translating these texts of *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and *Bhagavad-gītā,* he is translating them with full knowledge and realization of the *siddhānta*. He therefore translates this as, “Everyone should give the instructions of Lord Kṛṣṇa as given in the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*.” Śrīla Prabhupāda has given us the instructions of Lord Kṛṣṇa in *Bhagavad-gītā* and *ŚrīmadBhāgavatam*. He has given us the *siddhānta*. The goal is devotional service, and because he repeats the teachings of the Lord without any concoction, he takes this one instruction of Caitanya Mahāprabhu and establishes the whole foundation of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement on it. This is the instruction that Lord Caitanya has given to everyone. Someone might say, “This is the instruction that Lord Caitanya gave to Kūrma the *brāhmaṇa,* but it is not His instruction to everyone.” However, we can understand that Śrīla Prabhupāda, being the representative of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, has understood His mission.

Similarly, it is described that the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu was that wherever He went, He induced everyone to chant the holy names of the Lord. He induced them to become Vaiṣṇavas. That was Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* mission—to give Kṛṣṇa to everyone.

Lord Caitanya is Kṛṣṇa Himself, but He appeared as a devotee. He is glorified by Sārvabhauma Bhattācārya in the following verse:

*vairāgya-vidyā-nija-bhakti-yoga*  
*śikṣārtham ekaḥ puruṣaḥ purāṇaḥ*  
*śrī-kṛṣṇa-caitanya-śarīra-dhārī*  
*kṛpāmbudhir yas tam ahaṁ prapadye*

“Let me take shelter of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, who has descended in the form of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu to teach us real knowledge, His devotional service and detachment from whatever does not foster Kṛṣṇa consciousness. He has descended because He is an ocean of transcendental mercy. Let me surrender unto His lotus feet.” (*Cc. Madhya* 6.254)

Sometimes when Lord Caitanya was speaking, He would say, “Oh, you have certainly received the mercy of Kṛṣṇa.” He spoke like this to Sanātana Gosvāmī when he was thinking about giving up his life. Lord Caitanya had instructed him not to. Sanātana Gosvāmī personally accepted many austerities and inconveniences. Lord Caitanya therefore said to him, “Certainly you have purchased Kṛṣṇa.” Similarly, when He spoke to Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmī, He told him, “Certainly Kṛṣṇa will deliver you.”

It is described in the Eleventh Canto of *ŚrīmadBhāgavatam* that Lord Caitanya is Kṛṣṇa, but has appeared in a golden form and has accepted the mood of a devotee. Lord Caitanya was always chanting the names of Kṛṣṇa. Therefore, Lord Caitanya is teaching devotion to Himself, but He is giving the order: “You also should give Kṛṣṇa to everyone.”

Of course, when a devotee of the Lord goes out to give Kṛṣṇa, he is not teaching devotion to “himself.” Lord Caitanya is teaching devotion to Himself because He is Kṛṣṇa. Therefore, Lord Caitanya has instructed, “Give everyone Lord Kṛṣṇa’s instructions.” Prabhupāda has stated in many books, purports, and lectures that this is how one becomes *guru.*

Lord Caitanya says *(Cc. Madhya* 8.128*).*

*kibā vipra, kibā nyāsī, śūdra kene naya yei kṛṣṇa-tattva-vettā, sei ‘guru’ haya*

Whether one is a *brāhmaṇa*, *sannyāsī*, *śūdra*, etc., it makes no difference. As long as he knows the science of Kṛṣṇa, he is *guru*. Therefore, one simply has to know the science of Kṛṣṇa, which means that he has to know that the goal is to render service to Kṛṣṇa. Anyone whose life is dedicated to giving Kṛṣṇa to others has understood the goal.

This is what Lord Caitanya is telling Kūrma the *brāhmaṇa*. Even if one is householder, *brahmācari*, *sannyāsī*, *brāhmaṇa*, or *śūdra*, it does not make any difference. If you want to become freed from all obstacles of life and advance in spiritual life, simply give everybody the teachings of Lord Kṛṣṇa as given in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Bhagavad-gītā*. This was the essence of Lord Caitanya’s teachings, and this was the very basis of the *saṅkīrtana* movement, which was given by Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Thus, it has always been emphasized that if one wants to purify his existence; if he wants to glorify his family, birth, and riches; if he wants to give meaning to this life, he should engage in devotional service to the Lord.

Śrīla Prabhupāda would often quote Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta who said, “One who has life, preaches.” Preaching is our life. If one is not preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness, speaking about Kṛṣṇa, and hearing about Kṛṣṇa, he does not feel like he has any life.

Sometimes devotees ask themselves the question: “Why do I feel like I do not have any life? Why am I losing enthusiasm? I feel like my consciousness is becoming drained.” We often have the conception that preaching means that everyone has to go out the door and completely overpower all the conditioned souls with Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Actually, this is true. Preaching also has to start here. Kṛṣṇa says (*Bg.* 18.68):

*ya idaṁ paramaṁ guhyaṁ*  
*mad-bhakteṣv abhidhāsyati*  
*bhaktiṁ mayi parāṁ kṛtvā*  
*mām evaiṣyaty asaṁśayaḥ*

For one who explains the supreme secret to the devotees, devotional service is automatically guaranteed. Thus, Lord Caitanya tells Kūrma the *brāhmaṇa*, “In your materialistic life, there will be no impediments whatsoever if you simply speak about Kṛṣṇa. Just give the instructions that Lord Kṛṣṇa has given. Tell others about Kṛṣṇa.” This is *kṛṣṇa-upadeśa*. This is what elevates one’s consciousness. This is what enables one to lose the materialistic way of life. Kṛṣṇa is allattractive. The topics about Lord Kṛṣṇa are very satisfying to the heart, and the more we speak about Kṛṣṇa, the more the attraction will increase; and the more our attraction increases, the more we lose attraction for materialistic activities.

It is not that we simply go out and tell everybody to give up their materialistic way of life. A lot of people would say, “Alright, I will give up my materialistic way of life, but what am I supposed to do with my life instead?” In the West, they ask, “What do you people do for pleasure? You don’t go to movies; you don’t take intoxication; you don’t have illicit sex. What do you do?!” This is a very common question, especially from college students. They do not know anything different, and this is inconceivable to them.

Similarly, it is described in the *Mahābhārata* (*Bhīṣmaparva* 5.22): *acintyāḥ khalu ye bhāvā na tāṁs tarkeṇa yojayet.* Anything that is transcendental to the material nature is inconceivable, whereas arguments are all mundane. Since mundane arguments cannot touch transcendental subject matters, one should not try to approach transcendental subject matters through mundane arguments. Yet, people are still trying to approach transcendental subject matters by their materialistic concoction, thinking that it is inconceivable that one can actually have pleasure by denying “pleasure.” To them it is a paradox—a complete contradiction: “You are telling me to deny pleasure, and that will be pleasurable. You are crazy!” However, it is because they have no conception of spiritual pleasure. Thus, we understand that to simply give up the materialistic way of life is not possible unless one experiences real pleasure.

If we want to find out the secret of what the pleasurable activity of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is, we have to study Lord Kṛṣṇa’s teachings. Kṛṣṇa says (*Bg.* 10.9):

*mac-cittā mad-gata-prāṇā*  
*bodhayantaḥ parasparam*  
*kathayantaś ca māṁ nityaṁ*  
*tuṣyanti ca ramanti ca*

“The thoughts of My pure devotees dwell in Me, their lives are fully devoted to My service, and they derive great satisfaction and bliss from always enlightening one another and conversing about Me.”

This is how a devotee derives pleasure. Although inconceivable for materialistic persons, for a devotee it is accessible since he is interested in hearing and speaking about Kṛṣṇa.

Thus, it is not that *kṛṣṇa-upadeśa* means to burst out the doors, capture everybody, tackle them to the ground, and tell them to give up their materialistic way of life. We actually have to give them Kṛṣṇa. That is the instruction of the Lord. How can we expect them to give up their materialistic way of life if we do not give them Kṛṣṇa? That is first and foremost. If we do not give Kṛṣṇa, then people will not be able to understand this pleasure that we are talking about.

Unless we give them a superior pleasure, they will ask the same question, “How can you tell me about experiencing pleasure and tell me to give up pleasure at the same time?” Superior pleasure is found in Kṛṣṇa, the reservoir of all pleasure. Lord Caitanya’s instruction is to go out and give Kṛṣṇa. “Giving Kṛṣṇa” means to tell others about Him. Not only will others benefit, but we will also benefit. This is the all-auspiciousness of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement. We benefit because we are speaking about Kṛṣṇa. The more we speak about Kṛṣṇa, the more we remember Him. That is our activity in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Śrīla Prabhupāda said that everything is centered on that activity. It is our life. That is how we have our pleasure.

Only devotees can understand this, because the only thing a true devotee wants to hear about is Kṛṣṇa. We should always want to speak about Kṛṣṇa. This is life in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. The life of all transcendental knowledge, as Lord Caitanya describes in *Śikṣāṣṭaka,* is that it increases the ocean of transcendental bliss and enables us to taste the nectar for which we are always anxious.

The verses in this section of *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* are giving us the essence of the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya, and from this very instructive verse we can understand the basis for Śrīla Prabhupāda’s teachings.

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu advised the *brāhmaṇa* not to give up family life, but better to remain with his family and become purified by always glorifying Kṛṣṇa. This is real renunciation. One may be a renounced *gṛhastha*, *brahmācari*, *sannyāsī*, or *vānaprastha*. Renunciation, in whatever position we are in, is always hearing and chanting about Kṛṣṇa. This is how we become purified in this life so that we may ultimately achieve the perfection of life.

The spirit of renunciation means to give up the enjoying spirit. Without giving up the enjoying spirit, it is not possible to be renounced. We may renounce all family life without understanding the real spirit of renunciation. We may think, “Yes, I am renouncing so that I can experience enjoyment.” However, if we cannot understand real enjoyment, our renunciation will be incomplete.

*\* Is it ever premature to surrender to Kṛṣṇa?*

It is never premature to surrender to Kṛṣṇa, because the moment we surrender to Kṛṣṇa, we feel the protection of the Lord. Sometimes renunciation may be premature. Sometimes somebody may prematurely give something up, but he still does not surrender to Kṛṣṇa. That is a false kind of renunciation. Kṛṣṇa says, “Surrender unto Me; *mā śucaḥ,* do not fear.” Once we surrender to Krsna, we will be delivered from all sinful reactions. Thus, there is no question of “premature surrender” if one is actually surrendered.

Sometimes one renounces but does not actually surrender. He may give up some attachments, but he still does not take shelter of the lotus feet of the Lord. What really has to be awakened within the heart is the spirit of renunciation, the real and complete path of renunciation. This is *yuktavairāgya,* engaging everything in the service of the Lord.

The enjoying spirit is what actually has to be uprooted from the heart. Lord Caitanya said to Kūrma the *brāhmaṇa*, “If you always chant the holy name of the Lord and tell everyone you meet about the teachings of Lord Kṛṣṇa as given in *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, then there will be no obstructions, whatsoever, to your spiritual advancement—even if you remain situated in your householder life.” By engaging in this preaching mission of Lord Caitanya and constantly chanting the holy name of the Lord, the heart gradually becomes purified and one loses his attachment to the materialistic way of life. Yet, he must stay with his family.

He still has to fulfill his responsibilities, but he no longer thinks, “Let me enjoy. Let me try to find comfort and happiness in this situation.” He knows it is all temporary. One who is absorbed in preaching the message of Caitanya Mahāprabhu no longer tries to take shelter in these affairs, because he knows that the pleasure derived from such experience is temporary.

The example is given that material happiness is like a drop of water in the desert. If a person in the desert has one drop of water, how much can that relieve his thirst? It is insignificant. In the same way, the pleasure in family and society are all temporary enjoyment. We have to become genuinely renounced within our heart before artificially renouncing worldly attachments. Lord Caitanya therefore instructs Kūrma the *brāhmaṇa* to remain in his position, follow His instructions, and follow the regulative principles. By so doing, Lord Caitanya promises that he will never lose His company. One who actually experiences the constant presence of the Lord will be able to overcome the pains of materialistic life. He will see his family and wealth in relationship to his service to the Lord. He will not become illusioned, thinking that these are the sources of pleasure in life. Rather, he will see that they all belong to the Supreme Lord, Kṛṣṇa.

In the same way, Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura prays, “My life, family, wealth—everything—belongs to You, Kṛṣṇa.” This is the true spirit of renunciation. One who is situated in such a state of renunciation finds no obstacles in householder life. This point teaches one how to actually follow the proper path of renunciation and not to prematurely renounce when the desire remains within the heart. The following verse describes this (*Cc. Madhya* 7.130):

**At whosever house Śrī Caitanya accepted His alms by taking** *prasādam***, He would convert the dwellers to His** *saṅkīrtana* **movement and advise them just as He advised the** *brāhmaṇa* **named Kūrma.**

Thus, wherever Lord Caitanya went, the householders were inviting Him to come to their homes and accept some *prasāda*. He gave the same instruction to all of these householders. He did not say to them, “Come with Me and leave this place. Forget your wife and kids. They are just a burden around your neck.” Rather, He told them how they could become purified.

**Purport**

The cult of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu is explained here very nicely. One who surrenders to Him and is ready to follow Him with heart and soul does not need to change his location. Nor is it necessary for one to change his status. One may remain a householder, a medical practitioner, an engineer or whatever. It doesn’t matter. One only has to follow the instruction of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* and instruct relatives and friends in the teachings of the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *ŚrīmadBhāgavatam*. One has to learn humility and meekness at home, following the instructions of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and in that way one’s life will be spiritually successful. One should not try to be an artificially advanced devotee, thinking, “I am a firstclass devotee.” Such thinking should be avoided. It is best not to accept any disciples. One has to become purified at home by chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* and preaching the principles enunciated by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Thus one can become a spiritual master and be freed from the contamination of material life. (…) (*Cc. Madhya* 7.130)

One who always tells everyone about Kṛṣṇa and expands His glories in this way has actually understood the mission of Lord Caitanya. Nevertheless, he should not prematurely accept the position of spiritual master.

Lord Caitanya said to Kūrma the *brāhmaṇa*, “Instruct everyone you meet about the teachings of Lord Kṛṣṇa in *Bhagavad-gītā,* and in this way become a spiritual master and deliver everyone in the land.”

One can take up the mission or the work of the spiritual master, but he has to understand the real mission of the spiritual master. The real mission of the spiritual master is to relieve persons from the materialistic way of life. That is the mission of the spiritual master, just as it is the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Lord Caitanya has given this instruction because He wanted to relieve those in a suffering condition of life by inducing them to take up the chanting of the holy names of the Lord.

Thus, it is not that one prematurely becomes a spiritual master and thinks, “Now I will tell everybody about Kṛṣṇa. I will organize everyone myself, and I will arrange everything independent from the society of Vaiṣṇavas. In this way I will become a self-proclaimed *guru*.” Rather, one actually has to become submissive to *guru*. It is not that he simply follows his instructions and then acts independent of the guidance of the Lord’s representative. He must expand the mission of Lord Caitanya.

Prabhupāda therefore explained that one can remain in whatever position he is in, but he has to increase his responsibility in life beyond fulfilling his householder obligations by expanding the glories of the Lord.

This is the way that Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura expanded Kṛṣṇa consciousness—by engaging the householders. The process of Nāma Haṭṭa preaching was actually the vision of Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura. He devised a whole scheme by which the householders could become preachers. Everyone can preach. That is the real mission of Lord Caitanya. By artificially renouncing one’s worldly attachments, the desires for material enjoyment remain strong within the heart. However, we can purify our heart by chanting the holy names constantly.

Wherever we go, we should tell others about Kṛṣṇa. In the workplace, we should tell others about Kṛṣṇa and give them *Bhagavad-gītā*. We should not go anywhere without *Bhagavad-gītā*. We should be thinking, “This is how I can actually fulfill my responsibilities. I am entangled in householder life, but let me take *Bhagavad-gītā,* give it to others, and somehow try to enthuse them to hear about the message of Lord Kṛṣṇa.” We should invite others to come and hear about Kṛṣṇa in the association of the Vaiṣṇavas. Or we can invite them to our home to preliminarily introduce them to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In whatever way, we should bring them into the association of Vaiṣṇavas and thus expand Lord Caitanya’s preaching mission. We can all take part if we have the proper understanding of the vision of the Lord.

In *A Transcendental Diary,* Volume 2, Hari-śauri Prabhu writes about how Prabhupāda was giving instructions about “*yāre dekha, tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa-upadeśa’*.” There were some very important instructions that Hari-śauri Prabhu had recorded in this particular discussion, which are connected to the subject matter of this part of *Caitanya-caritāmṛta.*

Hari-śauri Prabhu took all the notes from his diary and has expanded upon them in his book, *A Transcendental* *Diary*. In this section, he is describing one particular conversation that took place in Prabhupāda’s room with some of His disciples (Room conversation—May 2, 1976, Fiji). One devotee asked Prabhupāda about other devotees who have initiated disciples and give the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* to those who are not in ISKCON.

Prabhupāda gave us a frank, unbiased analysis from his own broad perspective and successful experience: “The thing is the spirit, real service of preaching, stopped. Formality is going on, but the real business…

“Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s movement means *āmāra ājñāya guru hañā tāra ei deśa* (*Cc. Madhya* 7.128). That is stopped. Do you follow? The formalities are there, but the real life of Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s movement is preaching. Otherwise, why does Caitanya Mahāprabhu say, *āmāra ājñāya guru hañā tāra ei deśa*. *Tāra* means preaching. *Yāre dekha, tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa-upadeśa’*: Instruct everyone to follow the orders of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa as they are given in the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (*Cc. Madhya* 7.128). And that is stopped. They are satisfied if they could construct one temple and beg some rice from the neighborhood: ‘Sir, we have got some temple,’ that’s all. They are satisfied. The spirit of preaching forward—*pāpī tāpī jata chilo, harināme uddhārila* \[The holy name delivers those who are lowly and wretched – *Iṣṭa-deve vijñapti* by Narottama dasa Thakur\]—that is stopped.

“So by *hari-nāma*, by chanting, by this way, to live little peacefully in the temple and eat and sleep, that much they have got. If that is the success, that success they have got. And this was condemned by my Guru Mahārāja, that ‘To earn some money by showing Deity in the temple and eat and sleep. Better you become a sweeper in the street and earn your honest livelihood and live.’”

Many temples in Vṛndāvana have the same mentality, he said. They think of the operation of the temple as a business; that if people are coming and they give something, that is their good income. “According to the temple’s popularity, they think, ‘This is our success.’ Therefore, they are deteriorating. So that is not success. Success is his who is pushing forward the preaching method. That is his success. And if we think that ‘By showing the temple Deity we get some money and rice and cloth, and just peacefully live here; don’t bother about going to Fiji and all over…’ \[Prabhupāda was in Fiji at that time, preaching.\] That much success they have got. But that is not Caitanya Mahāprabhu. *Pṛthivīte āche yata nagarādi grāma, sarvatra pracāra*— What they are doing for that? That is point.”

“What if sometimes a disciple, he may go to preach, by he’s not preaching in the exact same spirit of his spiritual master?” Gurukṛpa wanted to know.

“Therefore he is not to preach,” Śrīla Prabhupāda said. “Caitanya Mahāprabhu says, *janma sārthaka kari’ kara para-upakāra*: (*Cc. Ādi* 9.41) ‘Go to do good to others. First of all you do good to yourself.’ First of all you become really preacher. Then go to preach. Caitanya Mahāprabhu never sent neophyte to go to preach. For neophyte the preaching is not their business. For neophyte, one should stick to the worship of Deity in the temple.

And those who have understood the philosophy, applied the philosophy in his life, he should go for preaching.’” (*A Transcendental Diary*, Page 67–68)

Thus, one could actually be in householder life and have much less of a spirit of enjoyment than one who lives in the temple and simply eats and sleeps. In other words, he is taking responsibility of householder life. He is giving up the enjoying spirit. He is fulfilling his responsibilities, but beyond that he is preaching the mission of the Lord. However, if one’s life in the temple is simply eating and sleeping, and all the preaching stops, then this is a very dangerous situation.

In connection with this, Śrīla Prabhupāda is specifically speaking about the success of his godbrothers who have initiated many disciples simply so they can maintain one temple.

Prabhupāda again repeated that to simply achieve a large following is not success. There are many who claim a following, but their followers do not abide by any philosophy. Christ said not to kill, but the Christians are all meat-eaters, so where are the true Christians, Prabhupāda asked. The real thing, he said, is quality, not quantity. It was the concern of his own spiritual master, and it was his \[Śrīla Prabhupāda’s\] also. “My Guru Mahārāja used to say like that. ‘If I get at least one quality disciple then all my labor will be successful.’ From the very beginning my strictures are there, that ‘You have to follow this’—quality. If I were to say, ‘No, you can do like Vivekananda.’ \[Vivekananda’s philosophy was *yato mat tato pat*, which means ‘I’m ok; you’re ok. I’m God conscious; you’re God conscious; he’s God conscious. Whatever you do to become God conscious is okay. I won’t bother you, you don’t bother me. This was his philosophy\]. Yes, you can do whatever you like, \[but\] then I think the quantity would have to be very, very big. But I don’t say. I make him promise before the fire, before the Deity, before guru.’”

Prabhupāda is quite sure of what he wants in his disciples, and he used one of his favorite examples to impress upon us what he is looking for. “One moon is taken into account. Who takes account of million of stars? *Ekaś candras tamo hanti na ca tārā sahasraśaḥ.* Quality. So we should be quality devotee, not quantity devotee.” (*A Transcendental Diary,* Page 69)

Śrīla Prabhupāda was expanding the preaching all over the world. Of course, temples had to be established. Prabhupāda says that neophyte devotees must first become trained and stick to the worship of the Deity in the temple, but he still has to give up the enjoying spirit by worshipping the Deity in the temple.

In other words, we should not use the temple as a place for eating and sleeping. In fact, when Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta saw that the temples were simply filling up with neophyte devotees, who were eating and sleeping, he left this world. He emphasized the point that real renunciation can take place whether one is *brahmācari*, *gṛhasta*, *vānaprastha*, or *sannyāsī* if one actually understands the mission of the Lord. Prabhupāda goes on to explain this:

And those who have understood the philosophy, applied the philosophy in his life, he should go for preaching. Otherwise he’ll preach wrongly, like Caraṇa dāsa Bābājī. And it will stop. (*A Transcendental Diary,* Page 68)

Caraṇa dāsa Bābājī was a *brijbasi*. He also induced others to chant, but they were chanting a bogus *mantra*: *nitāi gaura rādhe śyāma hare kṛṣṇa hare rāma*. Prabhupāda explained that they got some benefit because they were chanting “Nitāi Gaura,” but he called it *siddhānta-virudha,* which means that it will deteriorate or diminish. The proper *siddhānta* was not there. It was a concoction. Prabhupāda thus says that such preaching or distribution will not endure.

He wanted to preach but he did not know how to preach, and therefore after his life, it is finished. So Caitanya Mahāprabhu does not say like that, that ‘You remain a rascal and go to preach.’ No. *Janma sārthaka kari*. ‘Your first business is that you make your life perfect. Then go to preach. Perfect means you learn how to obey My orders.’ That is perfection. *Āmāra ājñāya*. So if you are actually, perfectly, carrying out the orders of Caitanya Mahāprabhu, then you are preaching. Otherwise you will do wrongly, mislead. Don’t do that. *Andhā yathāndhair upanīyamānāḥ.* If you remain blind, don’t try to lead other blind men. That is misleading. First of all open your eyes. Everything is there. Nobody can do anything whimsically. If you do whimsically, concoctedly, that will be failure. It will not be effective. (*A Transcendental Diary,* Page 68-69)

The emphasis is always there that if we are perfectly obeying Lord Caitanya’s orders, then our Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement will endure. Prabhupāda always explained that it would expand unlimitedly because it is spiritual. It is the spiritual dynamics, which come from surrendering to and obeying the orders of the Lord. The Lord can do anything, and certainly we can become His instruments if we obey His instructions. We have to be perfect examples ourselves. This type of exemplary behavior was what Śrīla Prabhupāda was insisting upon—to give up the spirit of enjoyment. We must become genuinely renounced by accepting the order of the Lord and the spiritual master through disciplic succession. In this way, the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement will continuously expand. We will not simply think about having a nice place for eating and sleeping. A Vaiṣṇava never thinks this way. He is always thinking of how to relieve the suffering of others. He is never satisfied to simply become comfortable himself. This preaching spirit must never stop. Always trying to give Kṛṣṇa to everyone should be our primary motivation in all our efforts. Giving Kṛṣṇa to others is a natural reciprocation that takes place.

Prabhupāda always used to emphasize that if you preach, everything else will come. In other words, if you are giving Kṛṣṇa to others, if you are actually concerned about their spiritual welfare and want to see them advance in spiritual life, you do not have to worry because when you give Kṛṣṇa to others, reciprocation naturally takes place. When you have given them the most valuable thing, they will naturally want to reciprocate. Śrīla Prabhupāda showed this by his example. He perfectly executed the order of Lord Caitanya. Wherever he went, he was simply giving Kṛṣṇa and people were coming forward and giving Prabhupāda everything—giving their lives.

This conversation with Śrīla Prabhupāda actually took place in Fiji where one householder man had given everything to Prabhupāda. He was in the process of building a temple. He had given his life’s earnings and went out and collected from 125 other people. He raised donations to build this particular temple, and when Prabhupāda visited, the man gave everything—“Prabhupāda, this is your temple.” He did it because Prabhupāda was giving Kṛṣṇa. When one receives Kṛṣṇa, he naturally wants to reciprocate.

Prabhupada said that our temples are bases where devotees go out to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness and distribute books. This is the purpose of all our temples. It is a place where people can come and hear about Kṛṣṇa and get Kṛṣṇa. Let the temples expand. Let the devotees move in. But let them take up the mission of Lord Caitanya, give their lives, and become perfect. Let us not forget the order that has been given to all of us—to relieve the sufferings of the conditioned souls and distribute the holy name of the Lord.

It is described that if one is looking for the solid truth in religious principles, it is found in the unadulterated heart of the self-realized soul. Therefore, one has to accept whatever path is advocated by the *mahājanas*. This is the way. We may think that we are engaged in so much mental speculation, or we may have so many concoctions about the purpose of our service. We may be chanting Vedic *mantras,* thinking, “People are seeing me chanting Vedic *mantras,*” but we are living in that concoction. We may be trying to display humility, thinking, “I hope people are noticing how humble I am.” There are many concoctions in which we can be living. If we want to break free from concoctions, we have to do everything on a superior order. That is our basis for becoming free from these concoctions.

Lord Caitanya says, “On My order, become *guru.*” Someone may think, “Oh! Become *guru*! That sounds pretty good! I can become a *guru*! I can get fancy seats; I can get fancy garlands; I can get opulent *prasādam* offered to me; I can get so many people worshiping me; I can list the glorifications of myself.” He says he is doing it because Lord Caitanya said so. However, in his mind, he is thinking of the garlands, the seat, the *prasādam,* and the comfort. He is living in a concoction. He is not *guru*. He is *goru*, as Prabhupāda would say.

He often tells the story of *punar mūṣiko bhava.* If one is *guru* but thinks in this way, again he becomes a mouse. The point is that one has to do it on the order. He is not thinking about how he will benefit. That is the concoction.

It is described that the solid truth of religious principles is kept in the heart of an unadulterated soul *(Cc. Madhya*

17.186*)*: *mahājano yena gataḥ sa panthāḥ.* If we want to give up all of these concoctions, we should accept the path that has been given by the *mahājanas*. That is the way out. There is no other way out. We must not imitate them; rather, we must follow in their footsteps. They are giving us the path, just as Śrīla Prabhupāda has given us the order and path to follow by giving us the teachings of Lord Kṛṣṇa from the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. We must then give it to others and always be conscious of the order. If we are conscious of the order, we will be protected from the materialistic way of life. However, as soon as we forget, we have to live in this hallucination.

## THE LORD’S RETURN TO JAGANNĀTHA PURĪ

**Madhya-līlā: 10.143–162**

**Moscow—May 22, 2000**

This pastime from the chapter entitled *The Lord’s Return to Jagannātha Purī* takes place right after the Lord’s travels to South India. Many of the Vaiṣṇavas were very anxious and felt intense separation from the Lord while He was traveling. Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī begins this chapter with the following verse (*Cc. Madhya* 10.1):

*taṁ vande gaura-jaladaṁ*  
*svasya yo darśanāmṛtaiḥ*  
*vicchedāvagraha-mlāna*  
*bhakta-śasyāny ajīvayat*

“I offer my respectful obeisances unto Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, who is compared to a cloud that pours water on fields of grain, which are like devotees suffering due to a shortage of rain. Separation from Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu is like a drought, but when the Lord returns, His presence is like a nectarean rain that falls on all the grains and saves them from perishing.”

Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī begins this chapter by highlighting its main feature with this particular prayer in glorification of the Lord: “He is just like water that has been poured on fields of grain.” All the devotees in Jagannātha Purī were experiencing great, intense pains of separation from the Lord, and the Lord’s return was like a great downpour from a rain cloud, saving all the devotees who were drying up in His absence from perishing and giving up their bodies.

It is often described that that is how intense feelings of separation are for one who has deep attachment for the Lord. Devotees do not actually give up their body; however, they feel that they may give up their body since the separation is so intense.

At this particular time, Sārvabhauma Bhattācārya, who had been waiting very eagerly for Lord Caitanya to come, personally introduced many devotees to Him who had not personally had His *darśana*. After Sārvabhauma Bhattācārya introduced each devotee by name, Lord Caitanya personally embraced each one of them.

Prabhupāda explains that when one is introduced to the Lord by a great Vaiṣṇava, the Lord immediately accepts him as His associate. Sārvabhauma Bhattācārya was therefore very eager to introduce each of these devotees to Lord Caitanya, who immediately accepted them by His embrace.

It was also at this time that Govinda became the servant of Lord Caitanya. Govinda was previously a very intimate servant of his spiritual master, Īśvara Purī, and is therefore a Godbrother of Lord Caitanya. Upon his departure from this planet, Īśvara Purī instructed Govinda to become the personal servant of Lord Caitanya. After receiving that instruction, and after the departure of Īśvara Purī, Govinda immediately went to Lord Caitanya. However, Lord Caitanya was not very much inclined to accept service from a Godbrother. Therefore, some discussion took place. Lord Caitanya asked what He should do. On one hand, He was being requested to accept service from a Godbrother, but on the other hand, the order was given by His spiritual master. The Lord spoke with Sārvabhauma Bhattācārya:

**As such, it is not befitting that the** *guru***’s servant should engage in My personal service. Yet My spiritual master has given this order. What shall I do?**

**Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya said, “The order of the spiritual master is very strong and cannot be disobeyed. That is the injunction of the** *śāstras***, the revealed scriptures. ‘Being ordered by his father, Paraśurāma killed his mother, Reṇukā, just as if she were an enemy. When Lakṣmaṇa, the younger brother of Lord Rāmacandra, heard of this, He immediately engaged Himself in the service of His elder brother and accepted His orders. The order of the spiritual master must be obeyed without consideration.’ The order of a great personality like** **a father must be executed without consideration because there is good fortune in such an order for both of us. In particular, there is good fortune for Me.”**

**After Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya said this, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu embraced Govinda and engaged him in the service of His personal body.**

**Everyone respected Govinda as the dearest servant of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and Govinda served all the Vaiṣṇavas and saw to their needs.**

**Both Haridāsa senior and Haridāsa junior, who were musicians, as well as Rāmāi and Nandāi, used to stay with Govinda. They all remained with Govinda to serve Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu; therefore no one could estimate the good fortune of Govinda.**

**The next day Mukunda Datta informed Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, “Brahmānanda Bhāratī has come to see You.” Mukunda Datta then asked the Lord, “Shall I bring him here?”**

**Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu said, “Brahmānanda Bhāratī is like My spiritual master. It is better that I go to him.” (***Cc. Madhya* **10.143–152)**

Lord Caitanya was always establishing proper behavior by His example. It is described here that He considered Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī His superior because of his association with Keśava Bhāratī, who was Lord Caitanya’s *sannyāsa-guru*. Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī had also taken *sannyāsa* from the same *sampradāya* as Keśava Bhāratī. In His dealings with others, Lord Caitanya would always show proper respect, even when His superiors were offensive, as in the case of Rāmacandra Purī. Rāmacandra Purī was a disciple of Mādhavendra Purī, and therefore he was a Godbrother of Lord Caitanya’s spiritual master, Īśvara Purī. Thus, Lord Caitanya always treated Rāmacandra Purī with respect, even though he had even offended his own spiritual master, Mādhavendra Purī.

Mādhavendra Purī was the first spiritual master of our *sampradāya* to manifest feelings of separation as those experienced by the residents of Vraja. It is described in the beginning of *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* that Lord Caitanya had come to distribute love of Godhead in the mood of the residents of Vraja. He distributed the seeds of love of Godhead, specifically in the mood of the residents of Vraja, and the first place the seed fructified was in Mādhavendra Purī.

Lord Caitanya considered Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī His superior because Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī had association with Keśava Bhāratī. In this pastime, Lord Caitanya is going to indirectly chastise Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī, but then He is going to be defeated by him.

Mukunda Datta said to Lord Caitanya, “Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī has come to see You. Shall I bring him here?”

Lord Caitanya replied, “No, Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī is like my spiritual master. It is better that I go to him.”

**After saying this, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and His devotees came into the presence of Brahmānanda Bhāratī. When Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and His devotees approached him, they saw that he was covered with a deerskin. Seeing this, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu became very unhappy. (***Cc. Madhya* **10.153-154)**

Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī belonged to the Bhāratī-sampradāya, which was a Śaṅkara-sampradāya. It is customary for persons in that *sampradāya* to wear either deerskin or tree-bark as a symbol of their renunciation. Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī had adopted traditional dress. Nevertheless, it is also a tradition that these *sannyāsīs* from the Śaṅkara-sampradāya adopt the dress of deerskin or bark of a tree for a position of prestige. They want to gain respect for being very renounced. Therefore, when Lord Caitanya came before Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī, He saw him in this deerskin and became very unhappy.

**Seeing Brahmānanda Bhāratī wearing the deerskin, Caitanya Mahāprabhu pretended not to see him. Instead, He asked Mukunda Datta, “Where is Brahmānanda Bhāratī, My spiritual master?”**

**Mukunda Datta replied, “Here is Brahmānanda** **Bhāratī, in Your presence.”**

**The Lord replied, “You are incorrect. This is not Brahmānanda Bhāratī. You must be talking of someone else, for this is surely not Brahmānanda Bhāratī. You have no knowledge. Why should Brahmānanda Bhāratī wear a deerskin?”**

**When Brahmānanda Bhāratī heard this, he thought, “My deerskin is not approved by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.” (***Cc. Madhya* **10.155–158)**

Lord Caitanya is very expert. He knows how to get His point across. Sometimes the Lord would chastise His devotees, and by so doing, would glorify them. He was so expert that He knew how to elicit the right response from His devotees. Therefore, instead of chastising Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī, He said, “This is not Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī. How could it be Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī? Why would Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī wear a deerskin?”
