Similarly, in the West, it is very common that when you go into a taxicab, there will be a sign saying, “Thank you for not smoking.” It does not say, “Do not smoke.” Therefore, something may be inferred by saying it indirectly, rather than directly. Thus, Lord Caitanya was saying, “This could not be Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī. Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī would never wear a deerskin.”

**Thus admitting his mistake, Brahmānanda Bhāratī thought, “He spoke well. I put on this deerskin only for prestige. I cannot cross over the ocean of nescience simply by wearing a deerskin. (***Cc. Madhya* **10.159)**

Such is the potency of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu that even by inference, His devotees gained immediate realization about their pride. Sometimes our head is so thick that we have to be beaten again and again, but still we cannot hear.

One of the questions that I am most often asked is: “How do I become free from my pride?” Here, simply by inference, Lord Caitanya has defeated Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī, who immediately began to think, “I am wearing this deerskin only out of prestige. I cannot cross over the ocean of nescience simply by wearing a deerskin.”

Similarly, Lord Caitanya also chastised a *brahmacārī* for his renunciation who only ate fruit and milk. He said, “Nobody can become qualified to see My *kīrtana* by simply drinking milk.” In that particular case, Lord Caitanya gave chastisement. However, He could not do that with Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī since he was a superior. Therefore, He had instead spoken in this way.

**“From today on I shall not wear this deerskin.” As soon as Brahmānanda Bhāratī decided this, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, understanding his mind, immediately sent for the robes of a** *sannyāsī***. As soon as Brahmānanda Bhāratī gave up his deerskin and covered himself with** *sannyāsī* **robes, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu came and offered His respects at his lotus feet. (***Cc. Madhya* **10.160–161)**

The Lord was thinking, “Ah, here is the person who is as good as my spiritual master!”

**Brahmānanda Bhāratī said, “You instruct the general populace by Your behavior. I will not do anything against Your wishes; otherwise You will not offer me respects but will neglect me. I am afraid of this.” (***Cc. Madhya* **10.162)**

A very interesting argument is about to begin between Lord Caitanya and Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī in this chapter.

**Madhya-līlā: 10.162–175**

**Moscow—May 24, 2000**

It has just been explained that although Lord Caitanya directly chastised Brahmānanda Bhāratī, He was actually giving respect to him. Brahmānanda Bhāratī was in a superior position with his godbrother, Keśava Bhāratī, who was Lord Caitanya’s *sannyāsa-guru*. Brahmānanda Bhāratī had taken *sannyāsa* in the Śaṅkara-sampradāya—the same *sampradāya* as Keśava Bhāratī—which, as we know, is an impersonal *sampradāya*. These Māyāvādīs ultimately worship the Supreme Lord as impersonal.

Many of the *sannyāsīs* in this Śaṅkara-sampradāya*,* following the teachings of Śaṅkarācārya, worship what is called *pañcopāsanā*. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta describes that both the followers of Madhva and Rāmānuja were able to keep themselves distant from the *pañcopāsanā*. However, practically all others who had taken *sannyāsa* in the *sampradāya* were worshipers of the *pañcopāsanā*—five imaginary forms. They say, “Ultimately, the Lord is impersonal, but if you want to worship Him, you can imagine one of five forms of worship: Viṣṇu, Sūrya, Gaṇeśa, Durgā, and Śiva.”

Although Brahmānanda Bhāratī was previously an impersonalist, in this particular pastime, we will see how, by Lord Caitanya’s influence, Brahmānanda Bhāratī became a personalist. There are many different examples of when Lord Caitanya converted impersonalists to personalists. For example, He delivered Prakāśānanda Sarasvatī and the 60,000 Māyāvādī *sannyāsīs* of Benares. This is described both in *Adi-līlā* (Chapter Seven) and *Madhya-līlā* of the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta.*

It is described in *Caitanya-maṅgala* that when Lord Caitanya first approached Keśava Bhāratī for *sannyāsa,* Keśava Bhāratī strongly resisted. He thought Nimai Paṇḍita was very young and had family dependent upon Him, and many people were very attached to Him. He felt that it was not possible to give Him *sannyāsa*. Nevertheless, Lord Caitanya was persistent. He approached Keśava Bhāratī and told him that He had had a dream. He dreamt that He had received a particular *mantra* upon accepting *sannyāsa.* He told Keśava Bhāratī what that *mantra* was that He had heard in His dream. Thus, when He told that *mantra* to Keśava Bhāratī, Keśava Bhāratī was given initiation by Lord Caitanya. Keśava Bhāratī said, “Yes, that is the *mantra* that I am going to give You!” Such are the ways of Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Another example from *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* of the Lord delivering impersonalists is when Lord Caitanya was traveling throughout South India and had come to a place where there were Buddhists. The Buddhists have their nine philosophical arguments (thesis) by which they defend their impersonal worship. However, when Lord Caitanya came to that place, He sat and spoke with all of them and defeated them soundly. These Buddhists were there with their *guru,* and they became very disturbed that they had been so soundly defeated by Caitanya Mahāprabhu. They plotted to give Him some food that was considered “untouchable.” They brought a large tray of untouchable food and gave it to the Lord. However, just as they were about to hand it to the Lord, a huge bird came and picked up the tray with its beak, and all the untouchable food fell on the Buddhists. The bird continued to fly and dropped the huge metal tray on the head of the Buddhists’ *guru,* causing blood to pour from his head and making him fall unconscious to the ground. The disciples saw their *guru* lying unconscious, and they became very disturbed: “What should we do?!” They ran back to Lord Caitanya and said, “Please help us! Do something! Do something!”

Lord Caitanya said, “Just chant the names of Kṛṣṇa and Hari in the ears of your *guru* and he will become conscious again.”

All the Buddhist disciples, who were in great anxiety, immediately ran to their *guru* and started chanting, “Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hari Hari,” in his ears.

When the Buddhist *guru* heard the disciples chanting these names, he came to consciousness and, raising his hands, continuously chanted, “Hari Hari! Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa! Hari! Kṛṣṇa!” Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta explained that in this way the disciples were initiated into the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahāmantra* by Lord Caitanya. When they chanted in the ears of their *guru,* they actually became their *guru’s guru,* initiating him into the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*. Having been initiated and empowered by the Lord, their chanting into the ears of their spiritual master made him become a devotee.

In this particular pastime, Lord Caitanya is dealing with the impersonalist Brahmānanda Bhāratī, who, by the mercy of Caitanya Mahāprabhu, is going to become a very great associate of the Lord. It is described here that Brahmānanda Bhāratī became purified just by receiving this correction from the Lord. Simply by taking away his deerskin, the Lord purified Brahmānanda Bhāratī. Of course, as we explained, the Lord did it very indirectly. Simply because he did something to please the Lord, Brahmānanda Bhāratī was delivered from his impersonalism.

**Text 162**

*bhāratī kahe,—tomāra ācāra loka śikhāite*  
*punaḥ nā karibe nati, bhaya pāṅa citte*

**Brahmānanda Bhāratī said, “You instruct the general populace by Your behavior. I will not do anything against Your wishes; otherwise You will not offer me respects but will neglect me. I am afraid of this.**

**Text 163**

*sāmpratika ‘dui brahma’ ihāṅ ‘calācala’*  
*jagannātha—acala brahma, tumi ta’ sacala*

**At the present moment I see two Brahmans. One Brahman is Lord Jagannātha, who does not move, and the other Brahman, who is moving, is You. Lord Jagannātha is the** *arcā-vigraha***, the worshipable Deity, and it is He who is the nonmoving Brahman. But You are Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and You are moving here and there. The two of You are the same Brahman, master of the material nature, but You are playing two parts—one moving and one not moving. In this way two Brahmans are now residing at Jagannātha Purī, Puruṣottama.**

**Of the two Brahmans, You are fair-complexioned, and the other, Lord Jagannātha, is blackish. Both of You are delivering the whole world.**

**Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu replied, “Actually, to tell you the truth, due to your presence there are now two Brahmans at Jagannātha Purī. Both Brahmānanda and Gaurahari are moving, whereas the blackish Lord Jagannātha is sitting tight and immobile.” (***Cc. Madhya* **10.162–166)**

**Purport**

Brahmānanda Bhāratī wanted to prove that there is no difference between the Supreme Lord and the jīva, whereas Caitanya Mahāprabhu wanted to prove that He and Brahmānanda Bhāratī were *jīvas*. (…)

Brahmānanda Bhāratī was saying that there is Parabrahman and Brahman. However, because He was playing the role of a devotee, Lord Caitanya wanted to prove that He is *jīva,* not Parabrahman. He wanted to show that both He and Brahmānanda Bhāratī were Brahman *jīvas*. Of course, ultimately they are both correct. Nevertheless, Lord Caitanya, assuming the role of a devotee, was turning around his argument and presenting it a different way: “Yes, you are right. There are two different Brahmans: Brahmānanda Bhāratī and Gaurahari. Now that you have come, there are two Brahmans.”

**(PURPORT continued)**

Although the *jīvas* are Brahman, they are many but the Supreme Lord, the Supreme Brahman, is one. On the other hand, Brahmānanda Bhāratī also wanted to prove that Jagannātha and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu are one, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, but that to fulfill His mission Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu appeared to be moving whereas Lord Jagannātha appeared to be inert. Thus this jolly argument was going on. Finally, Brahmānanda Bhāratī referred the whole matter to Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya for a final decision. (*Cc. Madhya* 10.166)

**Brahmānanda Bhāratī said, “My dear Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya, please become the mediator in this logical argument between Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and me.” Brahmānanda Bhāratī continued, “The living entity is localized, whereas the Supreme Brahman is all-pervading. That is the verdict of the revealed scriptures.” (***Cc. Madhya* **10.167–168)**

**Purport**

Brahmānanda Bhāratī drew Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya’s attention because he wanted him to judge the argument. He then stated that Brahman, the Supreme Lord, is all-pervading. This is confirmed by Lord Kṛṣṇa in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (13.3):

*kṣetra-jñaṁ cāpi māṁ viddhi sarva-kṣetreṣu bhārata*  
*kṣetra-kṣetrajñayor jñānaṁ yat taj jñānaṁ mataṁ mama*

**“O scion of Bharata, you should understand that I am also the knower in all bodies, and to understand this body and its knower is called knowledge. That is My opinion.”**

The Supreme Personality of Godhead in His Paramātmā feature is expanded everywhere. The *Brahma-saṁhitā* says, *aṇḍāntara-stha-paramāṇucayāntara-stham*: (*Bs*. 5.35) by virtue of His allpervasive nature, the Supreme Lord is within the universe as well as within all elements of the universe. He is even within the atom. In this way the Supreme Lord Govinda is all-pervasive. On the other hand, the living entities are very, very small. It is said that the living entity is one ten-thousandth of the tip of a hair. Therefore the living entity is localized. Living entities rest on the Brahman effulgence, the bodily rays of the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

Thus, Brahmānanda Bhāratī is explaining his position: There are two Brahmans, and the living entity is the localized Brahman, whereas the Supreme Brahman is all-pervading.

Prabhupāda quotes the verse in the *Bhagavad-gītā* that explains how Paramātmā is all-pervading, being present in the hearts of all living beings. He further explains that there is always a difference between the Paramātmā and the *ātmā*. Paramātmā is all-pervasive and all-knowing, whereas the *ātmā,* the living entity, is only the knower of his own field. The Paramātmā knows all fields of activities.

**Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu purified me by taking away my deerskin. This is proof that He is all-pervasive and all-powerful and that I am subordinate to Him. (***Cc. Madhya* **10.169)**

Brahmānanda Bhāratī is trying to prove that Caitanya Mahāprabhu is Parabrahman, the Supreme Lord. This is the realization he has achieved by the mercy of the Lord. In the coming verses, he will describe how he is able to achieve this realization by the mercy of the Lord. In this argument, he is trying to establish this as the ultimate fact. Lord Caitanya is resisting this argument because He never wants to hear that He is the Supreme Lord. Anytime someone would suggest that He is the Supreme Lord, He would cover His ears. He thinks, “This is blasphemy!” and He starts chanting, “Viṣṇu! Viṣṇu! Viṣṇu!” to protect Himself from such blasphemy.

The position He takes is, “How can you say that I am the Supreme Lord?” He is trying to counter the argument that Brahmānanda Bhāratī is giving, by asking, “How is this statement true?” Both Brahmānanda Bhāratī and He are *jīvas*: “Yes, we are both Brahman, and there is Jagannātha, who is Parabrahman. Thus, now there are two Brahmans: Brahmānanda Bhāratī and Gaurahari. So, you are right. Your statement is absolutely correct.”

However, Brahmānanda Bhāratī did not want to accept this. Now he is trying to establish his conclusion—the realization he has gotten by the mercy of the Lord, which is indisputable. He wants to establish the point that Lord Caitanya is Parabrahman. He will quote a verse from *Mahābhārata*: *Viṣṇu-sahasra-nāma-stotra*. This verse is often quoted to establish the divinity of Caitanya Mahāprabhu. There are many verses in various scriptures, which describe the divinity of Lord Caitanya—His appearance and His characteristics in this age. Brahmānanda Bhāratī quotes this verse:

**Text 170**

*suvarṇa-varṇo hemāṅgo*  
*varāṅgaś candanāṅgadī*  
*sannyāsa-kṛc chamaḥ śānto*  
*niṣṭhā-śānti-parāyaṇaḥ*

**His bodily hue is golden, and His whole body is like molten gold. Every part of His body is very beautifully constructed and smeared with sandalwood pulp. Accepting the renounced order, the Lord is always equipoised. He is firmly fixed in His mission of chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra, and He is firmly situated in His dualistic conclusion and in His peace. (***Cc. Madhya* **10.170)**

What is the dualistic conclusion of Caitanya Mahāprabhu? It is *acintya-bhedābheda-tattva—*inconceivable simultaneous oneness and difference. Lord Caitanya was always preaching this inconceivable simultaneous oneness and difference. He preached it to Prakāśānanda Sarasvatī and Sārvabhauma Bhattācārya, who were also impersonalists.

Here, Sārvabhauma Bhattācārya is involved in this argument, and Brahmānanda Bhāratī is engaging him, saying, “Alright, I want you to mediate this argument. Listen to His argument and listen to my argument, then say who is right. Whatever you decide will be accepted.” At this particular point, Sārvabhauma Bhattācārya was a great Vaiṣṇava, and we will hear what he has to say about this whole affair. But first, Brahmānanda Bhāratī gives his evidence, which is the verse (170) that he has quoted from *Mahābhārata.*

**Text 171**

*ei saba nāmera iṅha haya nijāspada*  
*candanākta prasāda-ḍora—śrī-bhuje aṅgada*

**All the symptoms mentioned in the verse from the** *Viṣṇu-sahasra-nāma-stotra* **are visible in the body of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. His arms are decorated with sandalwood pulp and the thread received from the Śrī Jagannātha Deity, and these are His ornamental bangles.**

**After hearing this, Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya rendered his judgment, saying, “Brahmānanda Bhāratī, I see that you are victorious.”**

**Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu immediately said, “I accept whatever Brahmānanda Bhāratī has said. It is quite all right with Me.” Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu thus posed Himself as a disciple and accepted Brahmānanda Bhāratī as His spiritual master. He then said, “The disciple is certainly defeated in an argument with the spiritual master.” Brahmānanda Bhāratī immediately countered these words, saying, “This is not the cause of Your defeat. There is another cause.” (***Cc. Madhya* **10.171–173)**

Brahmānanda Bhāratī previously said to Lord Caitanya, “You are always showing the right behavior.” Lord Caitanya was establishing the right behavior by accepting Brahmānanda Bhāratī’s conclusion, since Brahmānanda Bhāratī was in a superior position.

The *Mahābhārata* describes that respect for the superior is always upheld very strictly. For example, Bhīma, Nakula, Sahadeva, and Arjuna were all very disturbed when Yudhiṣṭhira Mahārāja gambled away their wife. They would often say to him in the course of events, “All this is happening because you have gambled away our wife!” Nevertheless, they always accepted him as superior, accepting whatever decision he made as an order. He was the elder Pāṇḍava. One who is in the superior position should be worshipped.

Lord Caitanya is showing that by His behavior. He completely accepts Brahmānanda Bhāratī’s statements because Brahmānanda Bhāratī is in the position of His spiritual master. Prabhupāda will go on to explain that even if one has another opinion, he still accepts the decision of the superior.

Lord Caitanya explains, “The disciple is certainly defeated in an argument with the spiritual master.” Now, Brahmānanda Bhāratī is countering this statement. This is one of the most profound features of the dealings between the Lord and His devotees. The Lord is trying to give credit to His devotee, and the devotee is always trying to give credit to the Lord. There is a competition that takes place between them. This is the nature of loving, reciprocal relationships.

Although living beings are expansions of the Supreme Lord, we should not foolishly think that the Lord is doing everything, and we have surrendered our will. Ultimately, the Lord is the supreme controller. He is the supreme sanctioner, but He sanctions the desires of His devotees. By so doing, the devotees can appear to do something very wonderful—so wonderful that they appear to be independent in their activities.

Similarly, when Nārada was speaking to Brahmā, he was trying to understand: “Who are you meditating on? You are supreme; you created this entire universe. Just as a spider creates a web from within itself, you create everything in this universe.” Lord Brahmā is a great devotee, and he appears to be independent. Because the Lord empowers His devotee, he can do something very wonderful. He can even create a universe by the empowerment of the Lord.

Prabhupāda explains that sometimes it may appear that a devotee does something even greater than the Lord. He gives the example of when Lord Nityānanda was delivering Jagāi and Mādhāi. Even Lord Caitanya would not deliver them, but Nityānanda delivered them. Prabhupāda says, “By the mercy of the Lord, a devotee can do something even greater than the Lord.” Prabhupāda calls the argument which is taking place between Lord Caitanya and Brahmānanda Bhāratī “a jolly argument.” Lord Caitanya is accepting Brahmānanda Bhāratī’s decision as final. He says, “I have been defeated by you because you are in the position of My spiritual master.” However, Brahmānanda Bhāratī is immediately going to counter these words of the Lord: “This is not the cause of Your defeat. There is another cause.”

**It is Your natural characteristic to accept defeat at the hands of Your devotee. There is also another glory of Yours, which I ask You to hear attentively. I have been meditating on the impersonal Brahman since my birth, but since I have seen You, I have fully experienced Kṛṣṇa. (***Cc. Madhya* **10.174–175)**

Brahmānanda Bhāratī is now explaining his history. He said that he took initiation from the Śaṅkara-sampradāya; he is a worshiper of *nirākāra,* the impersonal aspect of the Lord. Brahmānanda Bhāratī says, “You are saying that I have defeated You, but now I have been defeated by You, because upon seeing You, I have seen Kṛṣṇa. Even though I was attached to the impersonal conception of Brahman before, now, having seen You, I have fully experienced Kṛṣṇa.”

Brahmānanda Bhāratī will say, “Alright, I am in a superior position. I have defeated You, and I know the reason why I defeated You. The reason is because it is Your nature to let Yourself be defeated by Your devotees. You are the Supreme Lord, and You like to be defeated by Your devotees.”

**Purport**

(…) Concerning this, the words of Bhīṣma in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (1.9.37) are important:

*sva-nigamam apahāya mat-pratijñām*  
*ṛtam adhikartum avapluto ratha-sthaḥ*  
*dhṛta-ratha-caraṇo ’bhyayāc calad-gur*  
*harir iva hantum ibhaṁ gatottarīyaḥ*

“Fulfilling my desire and sacrificing His own promise, He got down from the chariot, took up its wheel and ran toward me hurriedly, just as a lion goes to kill an elephant. He even dropped His outer garment on the way.”

Kṛṣṇa promised not to fight in the Battle of Kurukṣetra, but Bhīṣma, in order to break Kṛṣṇa’s promise, attacked Arjuna in such a vigorous way that Kṛṣṇa was obliged to take up a chariot wheel and attack Bhīṣma. The Lord did this to show that His devotee was being maintained at the sacrifice of His own promise. Brahmānanda Bhāratī said, “Since the beginning of my life I was attached to impersonal Brahman realization, but as soon as I saw You, I became very much attached to the Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa.” Therefore Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu is Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself, and thus Brahmānanda Bhāratī became His devotee. (*Cc. Madhya* 10.175)

*\* We should not see the defects in the spiritual master, so how is it possible that a disciple can argue with his spiritual master?*

The point that Prabhupāda is making is that a person may make a strong point, and it may not necessarily be a philosophical point, but rather with regards to some other particular point. He may present it in a strong way, but ultimately it is not that he is seeing a defect in the spiritual master if he accepts the final decision of the spiritual master. Prabhupāda says, “The spiritual master is naturally victorious even though the disciple may put forward a strong argument.” Also in this particular case, the disciple is putting forward an argument to establish the glory of his spiritual master. This is the purpose of Lord Caitanya’s argument with Brahmānanda Bhāratī. The words of the spiritual master are considered to be always victorious. To show this, the Lord establishes the words of Brahmānanda Bhāratī, allowing him to be victorious even though the Lord is obviously in a superior position.

**Madhya-līlā: 10.176-177**

**Moscow—May 25, 2000**

Brāhmaṇanda Bharati is about to describe another glory, leading up to the point that Lord Caitanya is Kṛṣṇa. However, Lord Caitanya does not like to hear that. Whenever somebody says that He is Kṛṣṇa, He covers His ears, saying, “Viṣṇu! Viṣṇu!” to protect Himself from blasphemy.

Brāhmaṇanda Bharati is now building up his final conclusive argument, which is coming right back to the same point that he started with in the beginning. However, he wants to establish it conclusively, and by so doing, defeat the Lord.

As Brāhmaṇanda Bharati explained, the Lord is allowing him to defeat Him. However, it is a completely different mood of argument and defeat than that which takes place between materialistic people. When the Lord and His devotees engage in argument, there is a completely different mentality and objective. The Lord tries to defeat His devotee. He explains that the devotee is in a higher position, and a devotee is arguing with the Lord to establish the higher position of the Lord, which is completely different than the argument between materialists.

Of course, when Nimāi Paṇḍita was arguing in the mood of an arrogant scholar, it appeared to be very different. In those arguments that Lord Caitanya had in His youth, He was always trying to defeat someone to display that His knowledge of *śāstra*, logic, and argument was superior. Nonetheless, He had a higher purpose for that too, which was to establish Himself as the indisputable logician, thereby attracting many followers whom He would eventually bring to the lotus-feet of Kṛṣṇa.

At that time, scholars and students were very attracted to logic and argument. When they saw somebody who was expert in logic and argument, they were very much attracted to him because they were all very proud. They were thinking: “I want to be his student so that I can become just as expert as he is and defeat everybody that I speak to.”

The Vaiṣṇavas were thinking, “If only Nimāi would become a devotee.” They were running away, trying to avoid Him, because He would never talk about Kṛṣṇa. He would only talk about argument, logic, and grammar. All the Bhaṭṭācāryas and scholars in Navadvīpa at the time were very much attracted to becoming His student. So, Lord Caitanya had a very expert scheme: “The Vaiṣṇavas are already Vaiṣṇavas, but how can we get all of these materialists and bring them into our Vaiṣṇava camp?” This was one of the purposes for His descent. In His youth, His argument appeared to be highly motivated to defeat everybody. He had His purpose, but He did not want that purpose revealed to anybody.

The Vaiṣṇavas would offer blessings: “May You someday become a devotee. If someday You become a devotee, then I will also follow You.”

Lord Caitanya told them, “By your blessings, someday you will see that I will become a devotee, but not yet.”

We see the characteristics of the Lord when He engages in an argument with His devotees. He is establishing the glory of His devotee. However, the devotee does not want to be glorified. He does not worship the Lord so that the Lord will glorify him. A devotee worships the Lord because he wants to please the Lord, and thus, the Lord glorifies him.

Brāhmaṇanda Bharati continues glorifying the Lord, saying, “There is one other glory of Yours that I want You to please hear very attentively. Ever since my birth, I have been meditating on the impersonal Brahman, but having seen You, I have fully experienced Kṛṣṇa.”

**Text 176**

*kṛṣṇa-nāma sphure mukhe, mane netre kṛṣṇa*  
*tomāke tad-rūpa dekhi’ hṛdaya—satṛṣṇa*

**Brahmānanda Bhāratī continued, “Since I have seen You, I have been feeling Lord Kṛṣṇa’s presence in my mind and have been seeing Him before my eyes. I now want to chant the holy name of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Over and above this, within my heart I consider You to be Kṛṣṇa, and I am therefore very eager to serve You.**

**Text 177**

*bilvamaṅgala kaila yaiche daśā āpanāra*  
*ihāṅ dekhi’ sei daśā ha-ila āmāra*

**Bilvamaṅgala Ṭhākura abandoned his impersonal realization for the realization of the Personality of Godhead. I now see that my condition is similar to his, for it has already changed.**

He is now giving the example of how Bilvamaṅgala Ṭhākura was also an impersonalist, but he abandoned that for realization of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. There are some famous examples in the *Bhāgavatam* of other impersonalists who later became devotees. For instance, the four Kumāras had also previously been attracted to the impersonalist conception. However, when the Lord approached them, along with the Goddess of Fortune, and they saw the lotus feet of the Lord and smelled the *tulasī* flowers emanating from them, they became attracted to the Lord’s personal feature. They became attracted to hearing the pastimes of the Lord.

Another example of an impersonalist who became attracted to hearing the pastimes of the Lord is Śukadeva Gosvāmī. He heard the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* both while he was in the womb and also when he came out of the womb. In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, it explains that he became so attracted to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* while he was in the womb that he took up the study of it after coming out also.

There is a very famous verse that Lord Caitanya gave Sārvabhauma Bhattācārya, which explains how the Lord is so attractive that He can attract even the impersonalists (*Cc. Madhya* 6.186):

*ātmārāmāś ca munayo*  
*nirgranthā apy urukrame*  
*kurvanty ahaitukīṁ bhaktim*  
*ittham-bhūta-guṇo hariḥ*

**All different types of** ***ātmārāmas*****, especially those who are established on the path of self-realization, even though they are free from all material bondage, are very eager to render service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead.**

This means that the Lord possesses transcendental qualities and He can attract anyone, including the liberated souls. This verse is therefore spoken to establish the Lord as the supreme transcendental object, having all-attractive qualities. How is it that those who are already self-realized and self-satisfied could be attracted to something else? Thus, all these different types of transcendentalists, by hearing the pastimes of the Lord and associating with the devotees of the Lord, can become attracted to serving the Lord. This means that the Lord can attract anyone, even liberated souls. Examples that I have already given of persons who were previously attracted to the impersonalist conception are the four Kumāras, Sārvabhauma Bhattācārya, and Śukadeva Gosvāmī.

Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī states in verses 175 and 176 that by seeing Lord Caitanya, he sees Lord Kṛṣṇa. He said, “Now I want to chant the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. Over and above this, within my heart, I consider You to be Kṛṣṇa, and I am therefore very eager to serve You.” That is the transformation that takes place. When one develops an attraction to the Lord, the desire to serve the Lord simultaneously develops in his heart.

There are nine processes of devotional service. The first is hearing about the Lord**.** Rūpa Gosvāmī describes that first there is *śraddhā*—one has to hear with faith.

In this particular case, Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī saw the Lord and developed the ability to recognize Him. However, not everyone who sees the Lord recognizes Him.

**Purport**

In his early life, Bilvamaṅgala Ṭhākura was an impersonalistic monist, and he used to meditate upon the impersonal Brahman effulgence. Later he became a devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa, and his explanation for this change is given in a verse (text 178) that is quoted in the *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu*. Sometimes a devotee gradually comes to the stage of Bhagavān realization, realization of the Supreme Person, after having attained the lower stages of realization— impersonal Brahman realization and localized Paramātmā realization. The condition of such a devotee is described in the *Caitanya-candrāmṛta* (5), by Prabodhānanda Sarasvatī:

*kaivalyaṁ narakāyate tri-daśa-pūr ākāśa-puṣpāyate*  
*durdāntendriya-kāla-sarpa-paṭalī protkhāta-daṁṣṭrāyate*  
*viśvaṁ pūrṇa-sukhāyate vidhi-mahendrādiś ca kīṭāyate*  
*yat-kāruṇya-kaṭākṣa-vaibhava-vatāṁ taṁ*  
*gauram eva stumaḥ*

*Kaivalya*, oneness in the effulgence of Brahman, appears hellish to the devotee. The heavenly planets, the abodes of the demigods, appear to a devotee like phantasmagoria. The *yogīs* meditate for sense control, but for the devotee the senses appear like serpents with broken teeth. The devotee doesn’t have to control his senses, for his senses are already engaged in the Lord’s service. Consequently there is no possibility that the senses will act like serpents. In the material condition, the senses are as strong as poisonous snakes. But when the senses are engaged in the Lord’s service, they are like poisonous snakes with their fangs removed, and so they are no longer dangerous. The entire world is a replica of Vaikuṇṭha for the devotee because he has no anxiety. He sees that everything belongs to Kṛṣṇa, and he does not want to enjoy anything for himself. He does not even aspire for the position of Lord Brahmā or Indra. He simply wants to engage everything in the service of the Lord; therefore he has no problem. He stands in his original constitutional position. All this is possible when one receives Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s merciful glance.

In the *Caitanya-candrāmṛta* there are many more verses illustrating this same principle. (…)

A discussion of the impersonal Brahman is not very palatable to a devotee. The so-called regulations of the *śāstras* also appear null and void to him. There are many people who argue over the *śāstras*, but for a devotee such discussions are but tumultuous roaring. By the influence of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, all these problems disappear. (*Cc. Madhya* 10.177)

**Text 178**

*advaita-vīthī-pathikair upāsyāḥ*  
*svānanda-siṁhāsana-labdha-dīkṣāḥ*  
*śaṭhena kenāpi vayaṁ haṭhena*  
*dāsī-kṛtā gopa-vadhū-viṭena*

**Brahmānanda Bhāratī concluded, “Although I was worshiped by those on the path of monism and initiated into self-realization through the yoga system, I have nonetheless been forcibly turned into a maidservant by some cunning boy who is always joking with the** *gopīs***.” (***Cc. Madhya* **10.178)**

This verse was written by Bilvamaṅgala Ṭhākura, and Brāhmaṇanda Bharati is using it as evidence to explain what has happened to himself. In the verse, Bilvamaṅgala Ṭhākura is saying that Kṛṣṇa is so attractive, His dealings are so attractive, and everything about Kṛṣṇa is so attractive that even those who are attached to the impersonal conception become attracted to the Lord.

Prabhupāda quotes this verse from Prabodhānanda Sarasvatī in the previous purport, where he says that *kaivalyaṁ narakāyate—*the impersonal monism or impersonal liberation—is like hell for a devotee. The impersonalists want to give up all kinds of personal relationships. They are logicians, attracted to logic and argument.

In the *Vedās* it says:

*apāṇi-pādo javano grahītā*  
*paśyaty acakṣuḥ sa śṛṇoty akarṇaḥ*

(Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 3.18)

This verse describes that the Supreme Personality of Godhead has no hands and no legs, but still He accepts all sacrificial offerings and He walks. He has no ears, but He hears everything. He has no eyes, but He sees everything.

Thus, when the impersonalists read this statement in the scriptures, they conclude that it means that the Lord really has no eyes and ears. However, wherever verses of this nature exist within the scriptures, it is meant to show that the Lord has no material qualities. Although He is without material qualities, He is *saguṇa—*full of spiritual qualities. He has no eyes, but He sees everything. How does He see everything? Brahma describes in his *Brahma-saṁhitā* that because the Lord’s body is completely transcendental, any of His senses can fulfill the function of any other sense. The material body has certain limitations. However, the Lord’s body has no limitations since it is completely spiritual.

The impersonalists have no conception of spiritual variety. They think that ultimately everything is one, or that everything is null and void. They think that when the Supreme Lord appears, there is variety, and when He appears as a person, He accepts a material body. They think that ultimately the impersonal absolute appears in a material form. Thus, their whole conception of “spiritual” is simply the negation of that which is material. They have no conception of the varieties of spiritual life, spiritual form, or the Lord’s pastimes. They think that when the Lord comes, His activities are fruitive. Having no proper conception of devotional service, they think that devotional acts are merely flattering the Lord for material gain. Lord Brahmā describes that this is because they have not taken shelter of the lotus feet of the Lord.

Lord Caitanya is so merciful that in His dealings with Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī He completely transformed him so that he became very attracted to Him. Lord Caitanya did so simply by saying, “Where is Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī? I do not see Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī here. Where is Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī? Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī would never wear a deerskin, so that is not Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī.”

Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī was thinking, “Caitanya Mahāprabhu is not pleased. He thinks that I am wearing this deerskin for some prestige, and He is right.”

When we surrender to the Lord, the Lord is very merciful and gives us devotional service. For example, there is a pastime of the *brahmacārī* who was attached to performing austerities by eating only fruits and drinking milk. He accepted the Lord’s chastisement and surrendered to the Lord in his mind. Because the Lord is omniscient, He knows what is going on in one’s mind.

There are three Sanskrit words from a verse that says that the Lord takes the essence of a devotee’s attitude: *bhāva-grāhī janārdana.* This means that the Lord sees the best of what a devotee offers, and disregards the rest. “Because this person has accepted Me and surrendered unto Me, I will accept him as My devotee.” Such is the mercy of the Lord. That is how one becomes a devotee. When a devotee thinks, “From this day on, I surrender myself to the Lord,” then the Lord accepts the attitude of that devotee and creates a situation by which he can come to devotional service, and ultimately come to Him.

Of course, to get that opportunity, one has to come in contact with the Lord. How does one come in contact with the Lord? How did the four Kumāras come in contact with the Lord? It was by smelling the aroma of *tulasī* at the feet of the Lord. Śukadeva Gosvāmī came in contact with the Lord by hearing *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* from Vyāsadeva. Thus, one can come in contact with the Lord by hearing from a devotee of the Lord.

There is the very famous verse spoken by Yudhiṣṭhira Mahārāja to Vidura: “Saints of your caliber are verily holy places personified. Because you carry the Supreme Personality of Godhead within your heart, wherever you go, you immediately make that place a place of pilgrimage.” (*Paraphrasing SB* 1.13.10)

Another very famous verse is:

*nāhaṁ tiṣṭhāmi vaikuṇṭhe*  
*yogināṁ hṛdayeṣu vā*  
*tatra tiṣṭhāmi nārada*  
*yatra gāyanti mad-bhaktāḥ*

(Padma Purāṇa)

Kṛṣṇa is saying to Nārada: “O Nārada, you will not find Me in the Vaikuṇṭha planets, nor will you find Me in the hearts of *yogīs*, but you will find Me in that place where My devotees are chanting My glories. That is where I go.” Thus, the Lord is establishing that He is personally very much attracted to go to that place where His devotees are chanting His glories.

We say that the Lord is all-attractive because He can attract everyone by His qualities. However, devotional service is even more powerful than the Lord because it is the devotional service rendered by the devotees which attracts the Lord. Therefore, the Lord says, “When My devotees are chanting My glories, I will leave Vaikuṇṭha. I will not stay where the *yogīs* are meditating. I will go where My devotees are chanting My glories.” That is what attracts Him the most—not the meditation of the *yogīs* or the worship of the Lord in awe and veneration in Vaikuṇṭha. What attracts Him the most is the unalloyed devotional service rendered by His devotees. Thus, the Lord is conquered by devotional service.

In the First Canto, Prabhupāda says that he thanks Nārada Muni for appearing in the pages of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* Vyāsadeva said to Nārada, “Nārada you are like the all-pervasive Supersoul because you know what is going on within the heart.” The point is that the Lord agrees to appear. In the age of Kali, the Lord agrees to appear in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and in its recitation. Therefore, when one hears *ŚrīmadBhāgavatam* with submission, what happens? That is explained in the very beginning of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (*SB* 1.2.17):

*śṛṇvatāṁ sva-kathāḥ kṛṣṇaḥ*  
*puṇya-śravaṇa-kīrtanaḥ*  
*hṛdy antaḥ stho hy abhadrāṇi*  
*vidhunoti suhṛt satām*

Śrī Kṛṣṇa, who is the Supersoul in the heart of every living being, cleanses the heart of desires for material enjoyment in the devotee who develops the urge to hear His message, which itself is virtuous when properly heard and chanted. Devotional service begins when there is submissive oral reception of the message of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. The Lord accepts the attitude of that person who hears submissively the message of the Lord.

Lord Caitanya understood Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī’s submission to the Lord. Similarly, the Lord in the heart understands the submission of that person who desires to hear His pastimes. Therefore, the Lord acts in such a way to bring that devotee to Him. One has to come in contact with the Lord. When one comes in contact and submits himself to the Lord, then he can develop attraction to Him—even for one who is attracted to impersonal liberation.

Lord Caitanya now says something very interesting. Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī has given this whole argument about how he had previously been attracted to the material conception, but now he is directly seeing Kṛṣṇa.

**Text 179**

*prabhu kahe,—kṛṣṇe tomāra gāḍha premā haya*  
*yāhāṅ netra paḍe, tāhāṅ śrī-kṛṣṇa sphuraya*

**Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu replied, “You have a deep ecstatic love for Kṛṣṇa; therefore wherever you turn your eyes, you simply heighten your Kṛṣṇa consciousness.” (***Cc. Madhya* **10.179)**

Lord Caitanya is saying that since Brāhmaṇanda Bhāratī sees Kṛṣṇa everywhere because of his ecstatic love for Kṛṣṇa, then obviously when he looks at Him he sees Kṛṣṇa. Such are the discussions between the Lord and His devotee.

**Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya said, “The statements of both of you are correct. Kṛṣṇa gives direct audience through His mercy. Without having ecstatic love for Kṛṣṇa, one cannot see Him directly. Therefore through the mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, Brahmānanda Bhāratī has acquired direct vision of the Lord.”**

**Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu said, “Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya, what are you saying? Lord Viṣṇu, save Me! Such glorification is simply another form of blasphemy.” (***Cc. Madhya* **10.180–182)**

**Purport**

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu was a little embarrassed by the Bhaṭṭācārya’s statement; therefore He uttered the name Viṣṇu to save Himself. The Lord herein confirms that if one is overestimated, glorification is just another form of blasphemy. In this way He protests this so-called offensive statement.

**After saying this, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu took Brahmānanda Bhāratī with Him to His residence. From that time on, Brahmānanda Bhāratī remained with Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. (***Cc. Madhya* **10.183)**

In this pastime, Lord Caitanya allows His devotee to defeat Him because of his love. In the same way, Īśvara Purī defeated Lord Caitanya when the Lord found fault in his writing, and Īśvara Purī corrected Him saying that there was no fault. Similarly, Bhīṣma defeated Kṛṣṇa by forcing Him to break His vow.

Brahmānanda Bhāratī makes the point that there is another cause for the Lord’s defeat. It is the natural characteristic for Him to accept defeat at the hands of His devotee.

*\* Brahmānanda Bhāratī was in the Śaṅkara-sampradāya, but at the same time, he was a disciple of Mādhavendra Purī. How is it possible?*

Brahmānanda Bhāratī took *sannyāsa* from the Śaṅkarasampradāya in the same way that Lord Caitanya took *sannyāsa* from Keśava Bhāratī, although a disciple of Īśvara Purī. Thus, sometimes a devotee may take *sannyāsa* from a different *sannyāsī.*

In Lord Caitanya’s case, He accepted *sannyāsa* from Keśava Bhāratī because at that particular time, the Bhāratīsampradāya was considered to be a well-known and prestigious *sampradāya*. It mostly consisted of impersonlist *sannyāsīs;* there were not many Vaiṣṇava *sannyāsīs.*

Brahmānanda Bhāratī said, “Previously I was attracted to the impersonal conception of Brahman, but now having seen You, I am seeing Kṛṣṇa, and I know who Kṛṣṇa is.”

Thus, it appears that he had previously taken *sannyāsa* from the Śaṅkara-sampradāya, but then later became a disciple of Mādhavendra Purī.

## THE LORD ACCEPTS PRASĀDAM AT THE HOUSE OF SĀRVABHAUMA BHAṬṬĀCĀRYA

**Madhya-līlā: 15.67–83**

**Donetsk—June 16, 2000**

It is common in the pastimes of the Lord that He, being so pleased with the devotional service rendered by His devotees, wants to glorify them. It is unimaginable how the devotees feel in the presence of the Lord under those circumstances. One of the primary characteristics of a Vaiṣṇava is that he does not want to be glorified. A devotee is one whose motivation is to please the Lord. Therefore, devotees do not take pleasure in hearing their own glorification. Another characteristic of a devotee is that he always feels himself to be very fallen and insignificant.

An example of such a devotee is Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī, who was feeling very embarrassed about the fact that he was writing *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta*. He did not feel inclined to do it on his own, not feeling qualified. Nevertheless, he felt he had to do it because the devotees and the Lord wanted him to, yet, he thought, “Who am I to expand the glories of the Lord?” Whenever a devotee is asked to glorify the Lord, he thinks himself unqualified and feels very small because the Lord is so great and unlimited.

The devotee thinks, “How can I adequately glorify the Lord?” He would never think, “I have covered everything. I have completed the task of glorifying the Lord.” A Vaiṣṇava thinks it is such an inconceivable and insurmountable task to glorify God, because the Lord’s pastimes, His associates, and the spiritual world are unlimited (eternal).

The material world is incredibly expansive, and the Lord has created this material world without even having to get involved in it. There are unlimited universes throughout the entire cosmic manifestation, and the entire material world is only a spark of the splendor of the Supreme Lord. In the unlimited universes, there are unlimited planets and unlimited Lord Brahmās (creators of universes). All of these universes are manifested from one breath of Lord Viṣṇu, who is an expansion of Kṛṣṇa. It is inconceivable how one can cover everything when trying to glorify the Lord, and therefore a devotee always feels very small in this endeavor.

The First Canto of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* gives the example that, just as different birds can fly only so high in the sky, similarly, the learned sages can speak about the Lord according to their own realizations. There is always a greater height above the birds. No matter how high they go, there is unlimited space above them, but they can only go so high. In the same way, when the devotee glorifies the Supreme Lord, he can only go as high as his realizations will allow him to speak. Sometimes, however, the Lord empowers His devotee to speak beyond his realizations, and then the devotee gets more realization—even while he is speaking and glorifying the Lord. Therefore, as the Lord’s glories increase, the devotee’s realizations increase.

Similarly, when Rāmānanda Rāya was speaking to Lord Caitanya, he said, “I have gone as far as I can speak. My intelligence cannot bring me any further than the subject matter I have just described to you.”

Lord Caitanya said, “Yes, but I think there is more that you can say.” Rāmānanda Rāya had already explained *varṇāśrama,* the Vaikuṇṭha regions of the spiritual world, and the characteristics of Goloka Vṛndāvana. He spoke of each of the five primary *rasas*: neutrality, friendship, servitorship, parental affection, and conjugal love. He spoke about Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa and the intimacies of Their relationship. Each time that he went to a higher level, he was quoting from *śāstra*. He was supporting all his conclusive statements on the strength of scripture.

Lord Caitanya was assuming the role of a student, learning from Rāmānanda Rāya. He asked Rāmānanda Rāya to enlighten Him—to speak about the transcendental glories of the spiritual world. Rāmānanda Rāya said, “I have spoken to the height of my ability. I cannot speak any further.”

However, Lord Caitanya said, “I think there is more that you could speak about,” and asked him to please speak higher.

Rāmānanda Rāya said, “I can only speak something higher if You give me the words to speak, otherwise there is nothing else I can say. I have something that I wish to speak, but I am afraid You will not be very pleased to hear it because it is coming from my heart and there is no scripture to support it.” Lord Caitanya said, “Yes, please speak.” The statement of a devotee who has such realizations of the Lord is accepted to be as good as scripture.

Rāmānanda Rāya said, “I am seeing before me the combined form of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa in the form of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.” He began glorifying Lord Caitanya from his heart, expressing his realization of the glories of the Lord. Everything was coming from his heart, but it was Lord Caitanya who had entered into it.

Since Rāmānanda Rāya was thinking, “I can only speak if You give me the words,” the Lord therefore entered into his heart and gave him the words to speak. Rāmānanda Rāya was thinking, “I am afraid that You will not be very pleased to hear this,” because Lord Caitanya does not want to hear that He is the Supreme Lord. He is always absorbed in the mood of being a devotee of the Lord; however, He allowed Rāmānanda Rāya to speak in this way. He was very pleased to accept his words of glorification.

Thus, the devotee can speak according to his realization. Actual realization is when the Lord is revealing Himself in the heart of the devotee. That is reality for the devotee. The Lord is always reciprocating with the devotee. A devotee feels that he can never adequately glorify the Lord because the Lord’s glories are unlimited.

We see here that although the Lord is very much inclined to glorify His devotee, the devotee does not want to hear such glorification. But who is doing it? The Supreme Lord is doing it. How can you stop Him? You can say, “Stop. Please do not glorify me,” but the Lord cannot stop glorifying His devotee. The devotee then thinks, “I have to hear it because my life exists for the pleasure of the Lord. The Lord is pleased to glorify His devotees. How can I stop Him?” This is the kind of reciprocation that takes place between the Lord and His devotee.

The Lord is now going to speak about a devotee named Rāghava Paṇḍita. In the earlier verses of the fifteenth chapter of *Madhya-līlā,* Lord Caitanya spoke about Śacīmātā.

**Text 67**

*eteka kahite prabhu vihvala ha-ilā*  
*loka vidāya karite prabhu dhairya dharilā*

**While describing all this, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu became a little overwhelmed, but just to finish bidding farewell to the devotees, He remained patient.**

When Lord Caitanya was describing the glories of Śacīmātā, He was overwhelmed. Sometimes the Lord becomes moved to tears and begins crying. Nevertheless, because He wanted to finish glorifying all the devotees, He gathered His composure.

**Text 68**

*rāghava paṇḍite kahena vacana sarasa*  
*‘tomāra śuddha preme āmi ha-i’ tomāra vaśa’*

**Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu next spoke some relishable words to Rāghava Paṇḍita. He said, “I am obliged to you due to your pure love for Me.”**

The words used here are *śuddha preme,* which means the purest form of love for the Lord. Lord Caitanya is describing how He is obliged to the devotee for his pure love. It is often said that this is the strength of pure devotional service—it binds Kṛṣṇa. Therefore, pure devotional service is even more powerful than Kṛṣṇa Himself, although the Lord is the supreme powerful personality. There is nobody superior or equal to Him; He is the topmost personality. Yet, devotional service is even more powerful than the Lord. By the strength of devotional service, the Lord comes under control. The Lord feels so obliged to His devotee that sometimes He will even break His own vow to satisfy His devotee. He may appear to externally act in many contradictory ways, but there is one way that He is never contradictory—He is always controlled by the love of His devotees. The Lord always reciprocates with the pure love of His devotees. But sometimes persons who see with external vision perceive it as contradictory behavior. Similarly, materialistic people think that Kṛṣṇa is some licentious or sensual person.

Prabhupāda wrote a paper in the 1950’s about the discussion of Rāmānanda Rāya and Lord Caitanya. He explained how materialistic persons depict Lord Kṛṣṇa in many sensual ways. They cannot understand Kṛṣṇa. They think that Kṛṣṇa was an immoral person. He was meeting with others’ wives in the middle of the night in the forest. Such materialistic persons cannot understand how the internal potency of the Lord acts. They are seeing everything from an external point of view, and therefore their vision is imperfect. Only a devotee of the Lord can understand the purpose and pastimes of the Lord. They can understand how the Lord’s internal potency acts to enhance the pastimes of the Lord. Prabhupāda explains this point nicely in the *Ādi-līlā* of *Caitanya-caritāmṛta.*

In Goloka Vṛndāvana there is an exchange of love known as *parakīya-rasa*. It is something like the attraction of a married woman for a man other than her husband. In the material world this sort of relationship is most abominable because it is a perverted reflection of the *parakīya-rasa* in the spiritual world, where it is the highest kind of loving affair. Such feelings between the devotee and the Lord are presented by the influence of *yogamāyā*. The *Bhagavad-gītā* states that devotees of the highest grade are under the care of *daiva-māyā*, or *yogamāyā*: *mahātmānas tu māṁ pārtha daivīṁ prakṛtim āśritāḥ* (*Bg*. 9.13). Those who are actually great souls (*mahātmās*) are fully absorbed in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, always engaged in the service of the Lord. They are under the care of *daivī-prakṛti*, or *yogamāyā*. *Yogamāyā* creates a situation in which the devotee is prepared to transgress all regulative principles simply to love Kṛṣṇa. A devotee naturally does not like to transgress the laws of reverence for the Supreme Personality of Godhead, but by the influence of *yogamāyā* he is prepared to do anything to love the Supreme Lord better.

Those under the spell of the material energy cannot at all appreciate the activities of *yogamāyā*, for a conditioned soul can hardly understand the pure reciprocation between the Lord and His devotee. But by executing devotional service under the regulative principles, one can become very highly elevated and then begin to appreciate the dealings of pure love under the management of *yogamāyā*. (*Cc. Ādi* 4.30, Purport)

The Lord’s internal potency acts to create a situation by which the devotee can love the Lord better. *Yogamāyā* enhances the Lord’s pastimes, and therefore she creates a situation in order to intensify the attachment of the devotee to the Lord.

Imagine young cowherd girls who are married and thus unable to speak openly about their attachment to Kṛṣṇa. If they speak openly, they will be condemned. They cannot be seen going to see this young cowherd boy because chastity is very important to their culture. When Kṛṣṇa goes out to the forest or to the pasturing grounds with His cowherd boyfriends and cows, the *gopīs* are somewhere in the distance, talking amongst themselves. They want to see Kṛṣṇa; their hearts are running to see Kṛṣṇa, but they cannot go because they cannot be seen. They have to see Kṛṣṇa by some other way.

Even when they speak about Kṛṣṇa, they have to speak about Him in a covered way. If they speak about Kṛṣṇa directly, others will hear their conversation. Therefore, they speak about Kṛṣṇa by talking about the dark clouds in the sky. They are actually speaking about Kṛṣṇa’s body; that is their meditation. *Yogamāyā* creates this situation to intensify the devotees’ attachment to the Lord. *Yogamāyā* is assisting the Lord with His pastimes, increasing the attachment and love between the Lord and His devotees. He becomes completely controlled by their love. That is the highest platform of love.

Prabhupāda says that devotees naturally do not want to transcend regulative principles. However, by the influence of *Yogamāyā,* they are prepared to do anything to increase their love for the Supreme Lord. That is the highest platform. There is no superior platform of love of God than that love which was manifested by the *gopīs* of Vṛndāvana. Lord Caitanya, a *sannyāsī,* said that the *gopīs’* love for Kṛṣṇa is the highest love because it is completely unmotivated and selfless.

To one who has external vision and cannot understand the nature of the Lord’s internal potency, it may appear that the Lord is an immoral person. However, the internal potency obliges the Lord to His devotees. The internal potency is the pleasure potency of the Lord; it is the potency by which the Lord loves His devotee and the devotees love the Lord.

Thus, Lord Caitanya is speaking some relishable words directly to Rāghava Paṇḍita: “I am so obliged to you because of your pure love for Me.” Now, Lord Caitanya will describe some of the characteristics of Rāghava Paṇḍita. For those devotees who worship the Deity, this is a very nice pastime. It is a very wonderful example.

**Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu then informed everyone, “Just hear about the pure devotional service rendered to Kṛṣṇa by Rāghava Paṇḍita. Indeed, Rāghava Paṇḍita’s service is supremely pure and highly accomplished. Apart from other commodities, just hear about his offering of coconuts. A coconut is sold at the rate of five** *gaṇḍās* **each. (***Cc. Madhya* **15.69–70)**

Now Lord Caitanya will describe how Rāghava Paṇḍita offers coconuts to Kṛṣṇa. Something as simple as coconuts binds the Lord. In India, coconuts are very abundant; it is not a very big endeavor to get a coconut. However, because of the way that Rāghava Paṇḍita got coconuts, Lord Caitanya is saying, “I am obliged to Rāghava Paṇḍita.” What did Rāghava Paṇḍita do? How did he get these coconuts?

**Although he already has hundreds of trees and millions of fruits, he is still very eager to hear about the place where sweet coconuts are available. He collects coconuts** **with great endeavor from a place twenty miles away, and he pays four** *paṇas* **each for them. (***Cc. Madhya* **15.71–72)**

He travels thirty kilometers. It is not as though he jumped into the car and drove to get some coconuts at the local supermarket. He walked thirty kilometers every day, because he wanted to find out where the sweetest coconuts grow. With great endeavor, he found out where the sweetest ones were, and although normally a coconut is sold for five *gaṇḍās*, he paid four *paṇas,* which is much more.

**Every day five to seven coconuts are clipped and put into water to keep cool. At the time of offering** *bhoga***, the coconuts are again clipped and cleansed. After holes are made in them at the top, they are offered to Lord Kṛṣṇa. Lord Kṛṣṇa drinks the juice from these coconuts, and sometimes the coconuts are left drained of juice. At other times the coconuts remain filled with juice. When Rāghava Paṇḍita sees that the juice has been drunk from the coconuts, he is very pleased. He then breaks the coconuts, takes out the pulp and puts it on another plate. After offering the pulp, he meditates outside the temple door. In the meantime, Lord Kṛṣṇa, having eaten the pulp, leaves the plate empty. Sometimes, after eating the pulp, Kṛṣṇa fills the plate again with new pulp. In this way, the faith of Rāghava Paṇḍita increases, and he floats in an ocean of love. One day it so happened that about ten coconuts were properly clipped and brought by a servant to offer to the Deity. (***Cc. Madhya* **15.73–79)**

On this particular day, a servant went and got the coconuts, and they were all clipped. They used to be clipped once, and then just before offering, Raghava Pandita would clip them again so that they would be fresh.

**When the coconuts were brought, there was little time to offer them because it was already late. The servant, holding the container of coconuts, remained standing at the door. Rāghava Paṇḍita then saw that the servant touched the ceiling above the door and then touched the coconuts with the same hand.**

**Rāghava Paṇḍita then said, ‘People are always coming and going through that door. The dust from their feet blows up and touches the ceiling. After touching the ceiling above the door, you have touched the coconuts. Now they are no longer fit to be offered to Kṛṣṇa because they are contaminated.’ (***Cc. Madhya* **15.80–83)**

Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta says that Rāghava Paṇḍita is not a crazy man with a phobia of uncleanliness. He said that Rāghava Paṇḍita did not belong to this mundane world. His meditation was always on what was most pleasing to the Supreme Lord, and therefore everything he did was completely spiritual.

Prabhupāda explains in his purport that in lower consciousness, accepting something to be spiritual, when it is actually material, is called *bhauma-ijya-dhīḥ*. *Bhauma-ijya-dhīḥ* is from the verse in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* that describes that a person who thinks that his place of birth is worshipable is no better than a cow or an ass. Rather than go to hear from the saintly persons, he thinks his place of birth is worshipable or he goes to places of pilgrimage simply to take bath.

In other words, he sees something material to be spiritual. He thinks that his place of birth is something that is worthy of worship, and instead of seeing the actual benefit of the places of pilgrimage, he sees them as places to take a quick bath or go swimming. Therefore, he is in material consciousness. Such persons cannot understand the behavior of Rāghava Paṇḍita. Rāghava Paṇḍita’s behavior was completely spiritual. He saw everything related to the service of the Lord, and he was always absorbed in thoughts of how he could serve the Lord better.

Hearing this pastime is certainly beneficial, and we should be meditating on the glories of the Lord. The Lord is very much inclined to reciprocate with the devotees’ loving attitude. The symptom of the lover is that he wants to please the beloved. This is the motivation. Whatever service is rendered with love, Kṛṣṇa says, “I accept that.” That is the perfection of reciprocation. If there is no love in the offering, the Lord is not inclined to accept it. One can offer great wealth and many thousands of offerings, but if there is no love, the Lord is not inclined to accept it. The Lord, being *bhāva-grāhī janārdana,* is looking at the attitude of the devotee.

Therefore, even though an act of devotion may not be performed perfectly, if it is done with a loving attitude to please the Lord, the Lord is very much inclined to accept the offering of the devotee.

An example that is often given is that of when Lord Caitanya was very much inclined to take rice directly from the pouch of Śuklāmbara Brahmacārī even before he offered it to the Lord. Ordinarily, to offer something to the Lord, you have to prepare a nice plate and chant *mantras* and *mudrās*. For the offering to be accepted, these things have to be done. Nevertheless, Lord Caitanya took Śuklāmbara’s offering before it was offered to Him, and He completely relished it. The Lord is very much inclined to take something from His devotee when His devotee has love. When the Lord comes to the doorstep of His devotee who has love, He smashes all these *mudrās* into powder. It is the loving attitude that the Lord desires.

Of course, when worshipping the Deity, we cannot go beyond the rules and regulations and think, “Well that means that I should just be like Sanātana Gosvāmī.”

Sanātana Gosvāmī would take some dough, throw it in the fire, and offer it to the Deities. Kṛṣṇa was very pleased to accept this dough that was thrown in the fire because Sanātana Gosvāmī possessed pure love. However, for those who are performing devotional service according to rules and regulations, in the beginning stages, the way to express one’s love is by strictly following. Prabhupāda says that by strictly following rules and regulations, gradually one can come to the transcendental platform. This is the symptom of one’s love. He always tries to do it for the purpose of pleasing the Deity.

Rāghava Paṇḍita is very strict—so strict that even though someone just touched the ceiling and then touched the coconuts, he said, “Forget it. They cannot be offered.” That meant he had to again travel thirty kilometers to get fresh coconuts.

One might say, “If he had so much love, why did he not just offer the coconuts with love?” It is because he was teaching by example. The symptom of his love was that he would refuse to offer anything that was contaminated, and that is what endeared him so much to Lord Caitanya. Therefore, in the execution of our duties, we should be meditating on the pleasure of the Lord. Whatever small or seemingly insignificant service one does for the pleasure of the Lord with love is magnified by the Lord. Such is the nature of the Lord.

The way Rāghava Paṇḍita offered coconuts to Kṛṣṇa was so pleasing to Lord Caitanya that He magnified it and had to tell everyone. He was glorifying Rāghava Paṇḍita in front of all the devotees. Not only then, but also right now, Raghava Pandita is being glorified for his offerings of coconuts. That is how the Lord magnifies anything done with love. It is not a small thing when one offers something with love, because the Lord considers it so great. How can it be small when the Supreme Lord considers it great? Therefore, whatever service we render with love, it is that love which is attracting the attention of the Lord. That attitude is what He wants more than anything else. As *bhāva-grāhī janārdana*, He accepts the essence of the devotee’s attitude.

**Madhya-līlā: 15.270–277**

**Unknown date and place**

**Text 270**

*āyuḥ śriyaṁ yaśo dharmaṁ*  
*lokān āśiṣa eva ca*  
*hanti śreyāṁsi sarvāṇi*  
*puṁso mahad-atikramaḥ*

**When a person mistreats great souls, his life span, opulence, reputation, religion, possessions and good fortune are all destroyed.**

**Purport**

(…) The word *mahad-atikrama*, meaning “envy of Lord Viṣṇu and His devotees,” is significant in this verse. The word *mahat* indicates a great personality, a devotee or the Supreme Personality of Godhead Himself. Being always engaged in the Lord’s service, the devotees themselves are as great as the Supreme Personality of Godhead. The word *mahat* is also explained by Lord Kṛṣṇa in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (9.13):

*mahātmānas tu māṁ pārtha daivīṁ prakṛtim āśritāḥ*  
*bhajanty ananya-manaso jñātvā bhūtādim avyayam*

**“O son of Pṛthā, those who are not deluded, the great souls, are under the protection of the divine nature. They are fully engaged in devotional service because they know Me as the Supreme Personality of Godhead, original and inexhaustible.”**

Being envious of the Lord and His devotees is not at all auspicious for a demon. By such envy, a demon loses everything considered beneficial.

Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya is speaking, and in this verse, he is referring to the offense that Amogha committed to Lord Caitanya. Amogha had criticized Lord Caitanya for the quantity of food that He was apparently consuming at the house of Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya. Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya was of course very disturbed that Amogha had committed this offense to the Lord. He was thinking that he had invited the Lord to come to his home so that he and his wife could both render some service to Him, and instead he has brought the Lord to his home to be criticized. Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya was therefore certainly lamenting that the Lord was criticized under such conditions and circumstances.

When he heard that Amogha had contracted cholera, he thought, “This is the favor of providence. Now I do not have to punish him.” When both he and his wife were terribly disturbed by the offense committed by Amogha, the wife of Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya said, “Let our daughter become a widow,” and Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya was thinking that the only way this offense can be rectified is if Amogha is killed. However, then he thought, “It is also a great offense to kill a *brāhmaṇa*; therefore I cannot kill him for this offense.” He then thought that he would take his own life, but then he said, “Oh, but I cannot kill myself either, because I am also a *brāhmaṇa*.” Nevertheless, he wanted in some way for Amogha to be punished. Thus, when he heard that Amogha had contracted cholera, he thought, “Yes, this is the favor of providence. This is what I wanted to do, but I could not do it; yet, providence has arranged it.” This verse describes that when one offends the Supreme Personality of Godhead,  *karma* immediately takes effect.

In the purport, Prabhupāda refers to great souls as *mahātmās*. Thus, the great souls are those who are not deluded, and such great souls are under the protection of the divine nature (the *daivīṁ prakṛti)* of the Lord. They are protected because they are fully engaged in devotional service. When a person mistreats such great souls who are under the protection of the Lord’s internal potency, then all these things (life span, opulence, reputation, religion, possessions and good fortune) are ultimately destroyed in the offender’s short duration of life.

Regarding life span, we understand that seven things will diminish as Kali-yuga progresses. One of them is the duration of life. (Memory, truthfulness, and cleanliness also decrease as Kaliyuga progresses.) So, although the life span is diminishing as Kaliyuga progresses, one who offends another devotee may have to accept the reaction that his life span will be decreased even more.

The offender will also lose his opulence, reputation, religion, possessions, and good fortune, because the Lord cannot tolerate offenses committed to one who is completely surrendered to Him. When a great soul is offended by anyone, whether by a demon, as described in this verse, or even by a devotee, the offender loses his reputation. He loses everything he acquired by the grace of the Lord.

Envy is spoken of here. Prabhupāda explains that the word “*mahad-atikramaḥ,*” meaning envy of Lord Viṣṇu and His devotees, is significant in this verse. As was described, a devotee is one who exclusively thinks of serving the Supreme Lord. Such devotees have no personal interest. Everything they do is directed toward the satisfaction of Kṛṣṇa. The envious person is very disturbed by the preaching, actions, and behavior of a person who is fixed in service to the Lord.

Sometimes it may appear that the devotee’s actions or words come into conflict with persons who have strong material attachment. A symptom of an envious person is intolerance, which is especially due to envy of the Lord. Because of his personal or vested interest, such a person is also envious of a devotee who is fully surrendered to the Lord.

Therefore, we see that there are different examples in *śāstra* of persons who are envious. Hiraṇyakaśipu was envious of Prahlāda Mahārāja. Due to his envy, he wished to destroy Prahlāda Mahārāja. He considered Prahlāda Mahārāja to be friendly with his enemy. Thus, Hiraṇyakaśipu was ultimately envious of Lord Viṣṇu. Because Prahlāda Mahārāja was fixed on the *mahā-bhāgavata* platform, he saw everything in relationship to the Lord and did not distinguish between friend and enemy. Hiraṇyakaśipu was intolerant to Prahlāda Mahārāja’s attraction to chanting the glories of the holy names of the Lord. Therefore, when Prahlāda Mahārāja would speak about Kṛṣṇa, it would incite anger in Hiraṇyakaśipu: “How can you speak of the enemy!? Where have you learned such things?” He would speak in this way. This is the nature of a person who is envious of the Lord—he is envious of the fully surrendered devotee of the Lord. Thus, one who is envious in this way will certainly bring about his own ruination, especially if he happens to offend a great devotee.

Similarly, it is described in the *Caitanya-bhāgavata* that although Jagāi and Mādhāi committed many sinful activities, they had not committed blasphemy of a devotee of Lord Viṣṇu. This was their only saving grace since they had committed every other sinful activity, such as many murders, rapes, thefts, and inflicting great pain on others.

When Jagāi and Mādhāi offended Lord Nityānanda by striking Him in the head with an earthen pot, Lord Nityānanda did not take offense. But when that offense had been committed, Lord Caitanya wanted to immediately destroy Jagāi and Mādhāi. He appeared on the scene and invoked His Sudarśana *cakra,* and He was prepared to kill them for having offended Lord Nityānanda. He said to Jagāi and Mādhāi, “Lord Nityānanda’s body is non-different from My own.” Anyone who commits an act against Lord Nityānanda, such as that which was committed by Jagāi and Mādhāi, ultimately must be destroyed. However, Lord Nityānanda was determined to deliver them. Thus, He prevented Lord Caitanya from destroying them, and they were delivered by His grace.

As imperceivable as it may seem, if one offends a great devotee of the Lord, ultimately it will bring about his complete destruction.

The example is given of Gopāla Cakravartī, whose criticism of Haridāsa Ṭhākura brought about his ruination. Rāmacandra Khān had also criticized Haridāsa Ṭhākura. What did he lose? He lost his home and his entire village was destroyed. Meat-eaters came to his property and slaughtered and butchered the animals. Everything was destroyed for Rāmacandra Khān because of his offensive attitude towards Haridāsa Ṭhākura. It was due to his envy of Haridāsa Ṭhākura’s reputation, which was so great that his glories were being sung by many people throughout the land. Rāmacandra Khān thought, “Why is Haridāsa Ṭhākura receiving such recognition and such a reputation? I want to destroy his reputation.” Again, this attitude of enviousness causes one to commit an act against such a surrendered devotee as Haridāsa Ṭhākura, and the result is that everything was destroyed for him.

When one offends the Supreme Personality of Godhead, *karma* may either take effect after some time, or it may take effect immediately. Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya said that it may not be very long before one has to accept the reaction for one’s offense to a devotee of the Lord. Therefore, one must always be very careful about this envious attitude within his heart.

We may not direct our envious attitude towards such greatly surrendered souls or pure devotees of the Lord, but if we are not careful and if we do not check our envious nature, then certainly we risk offending someone who is very dear to Kṛṣṇa. The reaction to our envious attitude, as described here, is that everything will be taken away by the Lord, and the offender will be destroyed because the Lord is so disturbed to see His devotee offended.

Similarly, Rāmacandra Purī was condemned and destroyed for his offense committed to his own spiritual master. His reputation was ruined, and he became infamous as a great offender. He even lost his hope by being condemned to mental speculation birth after birth after birth for his offense to his spiritual master, Mādhavendra Purī.

**Gopīnātha Ācārya informed the Lord that both the husband and the wife were fasting and that their son-inlaw, Amogha, was dying of cholera.**

**As soon as Caitanya Mahāprabhu heard that Amogha was going to die, He immediately ran to him in great haste. Placing His hand on Amogha’s chest, He spoke as follows. “The heart of a** *brāhmaṇa* **is by nature very clean; therefore it is a proper place for Kṛṣṇa to sit. Why have you allowed the** *caṇḍāla* **of jealousy to sit here also? In this way you have contaminated a most purified place, your heart. However, due to the association of** **Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya, all your contamination is now vanquished. When a person’s heart is cleansed of all contamination, he is able to chant the** *mahā-mantra***, Hare Kṛṣṇa.” (***Cc. Madhya* **15.272–276)**

Lord Caitanya is telling Amogha that His inclination to destroy the contamination and envy within Amogha’s heart is due to his association with Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya. This particular pastime illustrates just how great and powerful a pure devotee of the Lord is. It is said that when one becomes a pure devotee of the Lord, many of his past and future generations can derive benefit and achieve liberation. Elsewhere, Prabhupāda explains that they achieve the opportunity to get the association of a Vaiṣṇava. Thus, Amogha’s good fortune is due to being related to Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya. Lord Caitanya explains that everyone who is related to Sārvabhauma immediately becomes very dear to Him. Later in this chapter, He describes that even a dog in the house of Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya is very dear to Him.

Lord Caitanya explains that when one harbors jealousy against a devotee within his heart, he becomes like a *caṇḍāla*. It is described in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* that one who is envious of devotional service to the Lord, even if he is a *brāhmaṇa,* is not considered to be as great as one who is born in a family of *caṇḍālas* but is devoted to the Lord with his body, mind, and words. A *caṇḍāla* is considered to be a very low class person— a social outcast. If one maintains this jealousy within his heart, it means that he has the qualities of a *caṇḍāla*. One must therefore be very careful to not be envious of somebody because of his or her advancement in devotional service.

Similarly, in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, Prabhupāda describes the example of Rūpa and Sanātana Gosvāmī receiving initiation from Lord Caitanya. All the Vaiṣṇava*s* who were present immediately became very pleased and satisfied. They were very joyous to see how somebody had received the mercy of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Prabhupāda explains in the purport that this is the characteristic of a devotee. A Vaiṣṇava is very pleased when he sees another devotee receiving mercy. He is so satisfied that he thinks himself to be unfortunate that he has not been able to receive the same mercy that another Vaiṣṇava has received from the Lord. Prabhupāda explains that if one is jealous or envious when he sees another devotee receiving the mercy of the Lord, this is *avaiṣṇava.* It is a demoniac quality to think, “Somebody has received some favor; why did I not receive such favor.” He looks upon that favor in a negative way and instead becomes disturbed. The disturbance may often increase to wanting to destroy the reputation of somebody who has received that favor. This is an *avaiṣṇava* characteristic, and maintaining such jealousy is considered to be of very low character. Thus, devotees should be very pleased when they see others receive the mercy.

For example, Sanātana Gosvāmī was very pleased when he saw how Jagadānanda Paṇḍita received mercy from Lord Caitanya in the form of being chastised. Sanātana Gosvāmī was thinking that that was a great favor. He was thinking that Jagadānanda Paṇḍita must be such a great, intimate associate of the Supreme Lord: “How fortunate he is to be so intimate an associate of the Lord and be chastised in this way.”

Sanātana Gosvāmī was then thinking himself to be not as fortunate: “I am an outcast. I cannot be as fortunate as Jagadānanda Paṇḍita because he is getting such mercy, but I am eating the bitter fruit of being recognized and glorified by the Supreme Lord.” He was finding it to be distasteful to be recognized by the Lord in this way. He was appreciating just how intimate Jagadānanda Paṇḍita’s relationship was with Lord Caitanya, and was very pleased to see such mercy. He was thinking himself increasingly insignificant and unworthy of such mercy. These are the characteristics of such an elevated Vaiṣṇava.
