Instead of pushing himself forward, a Vaiṣṇava is very anxious to see others go forward in their relationship with the Lord. When he sees others rendering service to the Lord very nicely and being recognized in some way, he is pleased. He derives much satisfaction from this.

A non-Vaiṣṇava, however, becomes disturbed when he sees that someone has received some mercy: “I should get this mercy. Therefore, I have to try to arrange things in such a way that I may also get it, even if it means I have to destroy the reputation of that person who has received it.” That is exactly what is being described here about the symptom of jealousy— in order to get some recognition, one is prepared to do anything to destroy another’s reputation or to diminish the mercy that others have received by the Lord or His devotee.

Thus, Lord Caitanya states:

**\[Despite the fact that you have such jealousy within your heart, because of your association with\] Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya, all your contamination is vanquished. When a person’s heart is cleansed of all contamination, he is able to chant the** *mahā-mantra***, Hare Kṛṣṇa. Therefore, Amogha, get up and chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa** *mahā-mantra***! If you do so, Krsna will unfailingly bestow mercy upon you. (***Cc. Madhya* **15.276–277)**

**Purport:**

The Absolute Truth is realized in three phases—impersonal Brahman, Paramātmā and the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Bhagavān. All of these are one and the same truth, but Brahman, Paramātmā and Bhagavān constitute three different features. Whoever understands Brahman is called a *brāhmaṇa*, and when a *brāhmaṇa* engages in the Lord’s devotional service, he is called a Vaiṣṇava. Unless one comes to understand the Supreme Personality of Godhead, his realization of impersonal Brahman is imperfect. A *brāhmaṇa* can chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* on the platform of *nāmābhāsa*, but not on the platform of pure vibration. When a *brāhmaṇa* engages in the Lord’s service, fully understanding his eternal relationship, his devotional service is called *abhidheya*. When one attains that stage, he is called a *bhāgavata*, or Vaiṣṇava. This indicates that he is free from contamination and material attachment. Lord Kṛṣṇa confirms this in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (7.28):

*yeṣāṁ tv anta-gataṁ pāpaṁ janānāṁ puṇya-karmaṇām*  
*te dvandva-moha-nirmuktā bhajante māṁ dṛḍha-vratāḥ*

Persons who have acted piously in previous lives and in this life and whose sinful actions are completely eradicated are freed from the duality of delusion, and they engage themselves in My service with determination.

Prabhupāda later explains in the purport that if one has been properly initiated and trained by a bona fide spiritual master, he can immediately become a *brāhmaṇa*. However, even beyond that, he can also come to the platform of becoming a Vaiṣṇava. Prabhupāda explains that one must never be envious of Vaiṣṇavas. If one is envious of Vaiṣṇavas, he cannot be a *brāhmaṇa*, what to speak of a Vaiṣṇava. If one does not become a fully qualified *brāhmaṇa*, he cannot advance in the spiritual science. If he is envious of Vaiṣṇavas, he is considered to be a very imperfect neophyte.

Prabhupāda explains that such persons who are envious of Vaiṣṇavas do not know the goal of life: *na te viduḥ svārthagatiṁ hi viṣṇuṁ* (*SB* 7.5.31). This verse describes that persons who accept a materially attached person as their spiritual master (or leader) cannot know that the goal of life is to go to the spiritual world to engage in the service of the Supreme Lord. Persons who accept a materially attached person as a leader or a spiritual master are envious of the Vaiṣṇavas, who are always speaking out and establishing that the goal of life is to satisfy the Supreme Lord Viṣṇu.

Prabhupāda is establishing the point that one cannot become a qualified *brāhmaṇa* if he is envious of Vaiṣṇavas, because many so-called *brāhmaṇas* were envious of the fact that he was going to the West and was creating *brāhmaṇas* out of Europeans and Americans. Meanwhile, the envious so-called *brāhmaṇas,* who were trying to maintain a monopoly on brahminical culture, were saying that one is only considered to be a qualified *brāhmaṇa* if he has taken his birth in a family of *brāhmaṇas.*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhanta, and the other Vaiṣṇava *acāryas* would always preach boldly to expose such a mentality. One cannot immediately be designated as a *brāhmaṇa* or Vaiṣṇava simply because he thinks he is qualified. He may be born in a particular position, or he may have a particular title, but that does not necessarily mean that all the qualifications are there.

Prabhupāda would often state that it is not simply wearing the thread around one’s neck that gives one the qualification to be a *brāhmaṇa.* A person is recognized by the characteristics that he displays. Therefore, one should never be deluded to think that because he has a particular designation, that that is the symptom of his qualification. One should be very careful not to think that the position or designation itself is the symptom of his advancement in Kṛṣṇa consciousness; it is not. Action and behavior are the real symptoms.

Śrīla Prabhupāda would say that persons deluded in this way are envious that those who are Vaiṣṇavas of the Lord can go and preach the principles of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* very strongly (*SB* 2.4.18): *kirāta-hūṇāndhra-pulinda-pulkaśā.* Even those persons who are from the most degraded sections of society can become purified by associating with the devotee of the Lord due to the Lord’s supreme power.

Prabhupāda had this conviction and had the purity, and was therefore able to see beyond the external designations and beyond the designation of *caṇḍāla, mleccha,* and *yavana*. Prabhupāda could see that even one who had a degraded birth can immediately come to a very qualified platform by the association of a pure devotee. Therefore, Prabhupāda preached very, very strongly that nowhere is it stated in *śāstra* that a person can only be a *brāhmaṇa* if he is born in a *brāhmaṇa* family.

Prabhupāda would quote examples from the Seventh Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and other places, which said that a person is recognized by his qualifications, not by his title or his position. This is also a warning to devotees of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. We should not become guilty of the same thing that Prabhupāda was speaking so critically about. We should not think that external designations are the symptom of one who is actually qualified. If one is a *brāhmaṇa,* but is adverse to devotional service or is envious of Vaiṣṇavas, then he cannot be a qualified *brāhmaṇa,* what to speak of a Vaiṣṇava. Therefore, the symptoms enable one to be recognized for a level of advancement in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, not simply the designation.

*sarvopādhi-vinirmuktaṁ*  
*tat-paratvena nirmalam*  
*hṛṣīkeṇa hṛṣīkeśasevanaṁ*  
*bhaktir ucyate*

(Nārada-pañcarātra)

There are two symptoms of one who is fully engaged in devotional service. The first is that he becomes freed from all external designations. Śrīla Prabhupāda would always manifest complete humility, thinking himself to be the servant of everyone. That is the symptom of the highly advanced devotee. A highly advanced devotee never becomes falsely proud of an external designation.

In the Seventh Canto of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* it is described that no one becomes purified by association with persons who are proud and filled with false prestige of their birth in a *brāhmaṇa* family. However, even if a person is born into a family of *caṇḍālas*, if he is a devotee, he is far superior to a falsely proud *brāhmaṇa*. Therefore, it was on that basis that Śrīla Prabhupāda expanded the Kṛṣṇa conscious movement. He was always very, very cautious, warning us not to listen to or accept the statements of envious persons who could not appreciate these *śāstric* conclusions.

Śrīla Prabhupāda often spoke of how such persons cannot appreciate a devotee who is fixed in the service of the Lord. Prabhupāda gives us the conviction to see how clearly dangerous it is to be envious of a pure devotee. There were many persons who were envious of Prabhupāda’s success in expanding Kṛṣṇa consciousness—envious of what they were not capable of doing. They were satisfied of being proud of their position, and they were not capable in any way of going and expanding a movement all over the world and establishing many temples.

Śrīla Prabhupāda often stated that the characteristic of a devotee or a Vaiṣṇava is recognized by how much he can elevate others by his association. Therefore, instead of recognizing it, such envious persons were critical. Of course, some were not; some did recognize what Śrīla Prabhupāda had done. Nevertheless, many of them were unable due to this envious mentality. Prabhupāda therefore warned us that one should not listen to or associate with persons who are envious of Vaiṣṇavas.

## THE LORD’S ATTEMPT TO GO TO VṚNDĀVANA

**Madhya-līlā: 16.63–64**

**Kiev—1992**

The reason why the word “attempt” is used in the title of this chapter is because, although we understand that the Lord can do whatever He desires, sometimes the devotees of the Lord control Him to the point that He acts according to their will. Thus, although the Lord attempted to leave Jagannātha Purī to go to Vṛndāvana, He could not go because His devotees would not give Him permission. This is one example of how He becomes controlled by the love of His devotees.

Lord Caitanya submitted His request to some of His intimate associates like Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya and Rāmānanda Rāya. He told them that He had previously attempted to go to Vṛndāvana, but was tricked by Advaita Ācārya and never made it.

He now wanted to go again, but first they said, “Soon it will be time for Ratha-yātrā. You should stay here for the festival.” The Lord agreed, but after Ratha-yātrā, other events came up. They said, “Now it is time for the Dola-yātrā festival. You should stay. Do not leave yet.” Thus, Lord Caitanya agreed to stay again.

When the Lord came before them again and asked for their permission to leave, they responded, “No, now it is the rainy season. It is not a very good time to travel.” So again Lord Caitanya did not go.

Lord Caitanya asked them many times, but it was not until two years later upon the Lord’s insistence that the devotees finally relented and gave Him permission to go. It was very difficult for the devotees to tolerate separation from the Lord. Although they knew that the Lord was independent and could go at any time, they were afraid to give Him permission because they did not know how they would be able to survive in His absence.

When Lord Caitanya accepted *sannyāsa,* He told His mother, Śacīmātā, that He would make His headquarters in Jagannātha Purī so that she could always be informed of His activities. Jagannātha Miśra, her husband, had departed this world, and Lord Caitanya’s elder brother, Visvarūpa, had taken *sannyāsa.* Therefore, all she had was her young Nimāi.

When the word went out that Nimāi was taking *sannyāsa,* it became intolerable for Śacīmātā. When Śacīmātā first heard this rumour, which was going around Navadvīpa, all she could do was sit in one place and stare off in the distance for fourteen straight days. She could not move because she was completely absorbed in thoughts of young Nimāi. Nevertheless, the inevitable came. When she saw that she was not going to be able to stop her son from accepting the renounced order, she submitted a request to Him to please make His headquarters in Jagannātha Purī so that as the devotees traveled back and forth between Jagannātha Purī and Navadvīpa each year to attend the Ratha-yātrā festival, she could always be kept informed of His activities. Thus, Lord Caitanya fulfilled this request and made His headquarters in Purī.

He was still very determined to visit Vṛndāvana, and He is putting forth His petition: “Please allow Me to go.” He finally pleaded with Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya and Rāmānanda Rāya, “I cannot go without your permission! When will you give Me your permission to go to Vṛndāvana?” They realized that they should give their permission and said, “You are the independent Supreme Personality of Godhead. You can act however You please. We can understand that this is simply part of Your pastimes. Because You are petitioning us in this way, we know we must grant this request.” Thus, He was finally given permission to go.

Every year, the devotees from Navadvīpa and Śāntipura would visit Purī, and they would spend four months of the year there, specifically during the time of the Ratha-yātrā festival. During that time, they would associate with Lord Caitanya Mahaprabhu. They were all His eternal associates, but because Caitanya Mahaprabhu had made His headquarters in Purī and they were residents of Navadvīpa, this large group of devotees would walk every year from Navadvīpa to Purī. Lord Nityānanda, Advaita Ācārya, and certain other devotees who had responsibilities for the journey would also go.

Thus, the following verses are a discussion between Lord Caitanya and Nityānanda, as Nityānanda Prabhu was preparing to leave Purī to return to Bengal, where the Lord had sent Him to preach.

**Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu then told Nityānanda Prabhu, “Please hear Me, O holy man: I now request something of You. Kindly grant My request. Do not come to Jagannātha Purī every year, but stay in Bengal and fulfill My desire.” (***Cc. Madhya* **16.63–64)**

In the purport to this verse, Śrīla Prabhupāda elaborates on Lord Caitanya’s instructions to Lord Nityānanda. He emphasizes that it is a greater fortune to carry out Lord Caitanya’s order than to go to Jagannātha Purī to see Lord Jagannātha. He states that it is more important to preach Lord Caitanya’s message, because when the Lord is satisfied by one’s preaching endeavors, one is associating with the Lord. The devotee who is fixed on this purpose understands his relationship with the Lord in the execution of His order. Śrīla Prabhupāda goes on to explain that just as Lord Caitanya ordered Lord Nityānanda, the same order comes down through the *paramparā* system. The spiritual master presents these orders to the disciple so that he can spread the message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s realization of these principles is very deep. He was the ideal example of one who would always meditate on executing the order of his spiritual master. He would many times refer to the verse in *Bhagavad-gītā* (2.41):

*vyavasāyātmikā buddhir*  
*ekeha kuru-nandana*  
*bahu-śākhā hy anantāś ca*  
*buddhayo ’vyavasāyinām*

**Those who are on this path are resolute in purpose, and their aim is one. O beloved child of the Kurus, the intelligence of those who are irresolute is many-branched.**

He refers to *vyavasāyātmikā buddhir,* which means resolute intelligence, firmly fixed on the order. The example of Prabhupāda’s is that he was completely fixed on the order. From his very first meeting with Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta, Bhaktisiddhānta could see Śrīla Prabhupāda’s potential. Śrīla Prabhupāda had never met Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta before, but his friend had encouraged him: “We should go meet this very nice saintly person.” Śrīla Prabhupāda therefore went to meet him.

Śrīla Prabhupāda was very active in Gandhi’s movement. However, when he heard the message of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta, it entered deep within his heart, and he could understand how Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta was speaking about a far more important mission than the liberation of India from the Western government. He could realize how political liberation was just a temporary adjustment. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta was broadening his field of activities by introducing Lord Caitanya’s mission. His mission was to liberate all living beings from the clutches of birth and death—a far superior form of liberation than a temporary political adjustment.

In his lecture, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta spoke with much enthusiasm, and his message was very clear. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta could see Śrīla Prabhupāda sitting in the audience, and he immediately picked him out. From this first meeting he could tell that Śrīla Prabhupāda was hearing very nicely, and therefore immediately gave Prabhupāda his life’s mission.

Śrīla Prabhupāda was a householder with family responsibilities, active in Gandhi’s movement. Nevertheless, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta perceived that he could hear very nicely, and knowing that hearing nicely is the secret for spiritual advancement, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta planted the seed of his order in the heart of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

He told Śrīla Prabhupāda and his friend, “Both of you look like intelligent men; why don’t you take this order of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and bring this message to the Western world?”

Śrīla Prabhupāda never had any doubt that Kṛṣṇa consciousness would be spread in the Western world. The basis of that freedom from doubt was that it was Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta’s desire that Lord Caitanya’s message would spread throughout the world. Śrīla Prabhupāda knew that Lord Caitanya was going to fulfill the desire of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta, His pure, unalloyed devotee. He simply had to execute the order. From the moment when that seed was planted, he continuously meditated on that order.

It took over thirty years before he was able to fully execute the order. Thus, the first volume of *Śrīla Prabhupāda Līlāmṛta* is entitled *A Lifetime in Preparation.* Prabhupāda had spent his household life in preparation to execute the order of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta. Finally, at the advanced age of seventy, he was able to come to the United States from India.

There is one story of a man who met Śrīla Prabhupāda in 1965. At that time, Śrīla Prabhupāda had no followers. He was trying to find a place where he could deliver lectures. He had his clothing from India and his white pointed shoes, and he had never seen snow before. While he was walking in the streets of New York City, he sat on a park bench, and a gentleman came and sat next to him. Prabhupāda, being a Vaiṣṇava preacher, would take every opportunity to speak to anyone about Kṛṣṇa. He turned to this gentleman and began telling him about his mission.

He said, “There are many temples, many bright-faced followers, and many published books—only they are separated by time.” This was in 1965.

The way we found out about this conversation was in 1978 when one gentleman happened to wander into one of our temples in the Western United States. He walked into the temple and saw many devotees, a big display of books, and this beautiful temple. He then saw a picture of Śrīla Prabhupāda, and he turned to the devotees and said, “Who is this man?”

A devotee said, “That is our spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda.”

The gentleman replied, “I have met this man before! I remember I had a conversation with him in New York City! He was telling me about some temple, about some books, and about some bright-faced followers.” When the man saw everything in the temple, he was overwhelmed. He could understand that Śrīla Prabhupāda had actually executed what he was talking about.

This was Śrīla Prabhupāda’s firm faith. Śrīla Prabhupāda had to undergo many severe difficulties, beginning with two heart attacks aboard the *Jaladuta* steamship on his way from India to the United States, due to the rough ocean waters. He described how he felt that Lord Kṛṣṇa was protecting him and holding him in His arms, massaging his heart. When he arrived in the United States, he had many difficulties: no money, no place to live, and nobody to help him.

As soon as he arrived, he went to Butler, Pennsylvania for a very short time with an Indian man who had offered to help, but he did not stay very long. Since he had a mission to fulfill and an order to carry out, he went to New York City, one of the biggest cities in the Western world.

Just prior to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s passage from India, he was living in Vṛndāvana at the Rādhā Dāmodara temple. In Vṛndāvana, he was writing books and preparing for this trip.

Daily, he would go to the Yamunā River to take his bath. Constantly hearing the glories of the Lord, his life was very peaceful in Vṛndāvana. However, he left that place to go to one of the most degraded places in the world—New York City. Not only did he go to New York City, but Śrīla Prabhupāda entered into the most degraded part of New York City. Anybody who goes into this part of New York—the Bowery— has to admit that it is one of the most degraded places in the whole world. Nevertheless, Śrīla Prabhupāda went there, carrying with him the seed that Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta had planted and his firm faith in his *guru’s* order.

Prabhupāda has very deep realizations when he talks about these things. That is one of the symptoms of the Vaiṣṇava *ācārya—*he has realizations and can speak very convincingly. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s purports are not empty words. He is not giving some instructions that he has not witnessed in his own life. He has full realization of the order that Lord Caitanya gave to Lord Nityānanda. His life was a perfect example. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta had given his order to Śrīla Prabhupāda, and therefore Śrīla Prabhupāda left Vṛndāvana to go to New York City and preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness, firmly fixed on the order.

Śrīla Prabhupāda emphasizes that this same order comes down through disciplic succession. The spiritual master presents these orders to the disciple so that he can spread the message of Śrī Caitanya Mahaprabhu. Any disciple who takes up these orders to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness and who is prepared to undergo any difficulty and austerity he confronts, becomes recognized by Kṛṣṇa.

Kṛṣṇa explains in the *Bhagavad-gītā: ya idaṁ paramaṁ guhyaṁ mad-bhakteṣv abhidhāsyati.* “For one who explains this supreme secret to the devotees, pure devotional service is guaranteed, and at the end he will come back to Me.” (*Bg.*18.68) “There is no servant in this world more dear to Me than he, nor will there ever be one more dear.” (*Bg.* 18.69) Kṛṣṇa is here explaining whom He considers to be His dearest servant. He says “No one is more dear to Me” than that person who voluntarily accepts some inconvenience for the purpose of telling others about Kṛṣṇa. This is how one becomes recognized by Kṛṣṇa.

Kṛṣṇa recognizes one who serves His devotees, and His devotee is one who is dedicated to this mission. His devotee is one who knows the desire of the Lord and that the Lord is anxious to reclaim or recover the conditioned souls who are entangled in *māyā’s* network. Because the Lord is very anxious to see the conditioned souls delivered, the devotee is always thinking how he can relieve the Lord’s anxiety. Of course, we understand that the Lord is never really in anxiety. He is self-satisfied, but still He wants to see the conditioned souls become free from their suffering. Therefore the Vaiṣṇava *ācārya*s, understanding the mission of the Lord, know that it is a much higher principle to execute the orders of the Lord, coming down in disciplic succession, than it is to personally go and see the Lord.

It is with these realizations that such exalted devotees like Prahlāda Mahārāja and Vāsudeva Datta would offer their prayers to the Lord, saying, “I do not want to go back home, back to Godhead, unless I can bring everyone with me.” It is with such feelings that a Vaiṣṇava *ācārya* is more satisfied by serving the Lord than to be in even the most intimate association and personal presence of the Lord. What is that satisfaction? It is a superior satisfaction because one who is always meditating on the desire of the Lord is associating with the Lord. Such devotees are prepared to stay in the material world, birth after birth, because their only desire is to please Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa therefore says, “These are very confidential associates.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda was once meeting with some of his disciples and was telling them a story. He said that he was with Kṛṣṇa before he appeared in this lifetime. He said that Kṛṣṇa had asked him to come to the material world, but he hesitated and Kṛṣṇa could understand. Kṛṣṇa told him, “That is alright. You go and leave behind your books, and then you can come back.” Thus, on the order of Lord Kṛṣṇa, Śrīla Prabhupāda came and walked on this planet. Of course, we know that wherever Śrīla Prabhupāda is, he is preaching Lord Kṛṣṇa’s glories, because he is a very confidential servant of Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa considers such devotees, who voluntarily accept these inconveniences to execute His order, His most confidential devotees.

Therefore, we should understand the Lord’s mission. He appeared in this age of Kali to distribute the most simple and sublime method for developing love of God. Lord Caitanya is freely distributing the fruits of pure love of God, and He is asking everyone: “Please, take these fruits, relish them yourself, and distribute them to others. How many fruits can I alone distribute? There are unlimited fruits.” This is His request, and for anyone who distributes the fruits, the Lord makes it sweeter for those devotees to taste. This is why Lord Caitanya had described the *saṅkīrtana* movement as *ānandāmbudhivardhanam—*the ocean of transcendental bliss.

Thus, Lord Caitanya’s instruction to Lord Nityānanda was to stay in Bengal. Every year, Lord Nityānanda was thinking that He was going to see Lord Caitanya. Lord Caitanya was saying, “No! You cannot come! I do not want to see You here next year! You must stay there and preach to fulfill My order.”

**Madhya-līlā: 16.65**

**Boston—August, 1996**

**Text 65**

*tāhāṅ siddhi kare—hena anye nā dekhiye*  
*āmāra ‘duṣkara’ karma, tomā haite haye*

**Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu continued, “You can perform a task that even I cannot do. But for You, I cannot find anyone in Gauḍa-deśa who can fulfill My mission there.”**

**Purport**

Lord Caitanya’s mission is to deliver the fallen souls of this age. In this Age of Kali, practically cent percent of the population is fallen. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu certainly delivered many fallen souls, but His disciples mainly came from the upper classes. For example, He delivered Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, Sanātana Gosvāmī, Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya and many others who were socially elevated but fallen from the spiritual point of view. Śrīla Rūpa and Sanātana Gosvāmīs were situated in government service, and Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya was the topmost scholar of India. Similarly, Prakāśānanda Sarasvatī was a leader of many thousands of Māyāvādī *sannyāsīs*. It was Śrīla Nityānanda Prabhu, however, who delivered persons like Jagāi and Mādhāi. (…) In the incarnation of Gaura-Nitāi, the Lord is not supposed to kill demons but is supposed to deliver them by preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In the case of Jagāi and Mādhāi, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu was so angry that He would have immediately killed them, but Nityānanda Prabhu was so kind that He not only saved them from death but elevated them to the transcendental position. Thus what was not possible for Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu was carried out by Nityānanda Prabhu.

Similarly, if one is true to Gaura-Nitāi’s service in the disciplic succession, he can even excel Nityānanda Prabhu’s service. This is the process of disciplic succession. Nityānanda Prabhu delivered Jagāi and Mādhāi, but a servant of Nityānanda Prabhu, by His grace, can deliver many thousands of Jagāis and Mādhāis. That is the special benediction of the disciplic succession. One who is situated in the disciplic succession can be understood by the result of his activities. This is always true as far as the activities of the Lord and His devotees are concerned. Therefore Lord Śiva says:

*ārādhanānāṁ sarveṣāṁ viṣṇor ārādhanaṁ param*  
*tasmāt parataraṁ devi tadīyānāṁ samarcanam*

“Of all types of worship, worship of Lord Viṣṇu is best, and better than the worship of Lord Viṣṇu is the worship of His devotee, the Vaiṣṇava.” (*Padma Purāṇa.*

By the grace of Viṣṇu, a Vaiṣṇava can render better service than Viṣṇu; that is the special prerogative of a Vaiṣṇava. The Lord actually wants to see His servants work more gloriously than Himself. For instance, on the Battlefield of Kurukṣetra, Śrī Kṛṣṇa provoked Arjuna to fight because all the warriors on the battlefield were to die by Kṛṣṇa’s plan. Kṛṣṇa Himself did not want to take credit; rather, He wanted Arjuna to take credit. Therefore He asked him to fight and win fame. (...) (*Cc. Madhya* 16.65)

Rūpa and Sanātana Gosvāmīs were sent to Vṛndāvana to write books, Lord Nityānanda was sent to Bengal, and the rest of the world was left for ISKCON to deliver the fallen conditioned souls. Śrīla Prabhupāda is illustrating how the rest of the world can be delivered by one who is true to the service of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi. He can even excel the service of the Supreme Lord, Lord Nityānanda. This is the process of disciplic succession. One can understand by the result. When we look at the results, we do not simply look at the quantity.

The devotees are instruments to the will of the Lord. In proportion to one’s surrender to the Lord, the Lord will use His servant as an appropriate instrument in the execution of His will. The topmost success of life in execution of His service is to always be situated in constant remembrance of the Lord. Yet, in order to come to that stage, one has to be very determined to be engaged in the service of the Lord in this life. It is not possible for one to remember the Supreme Lord throughout this life if he does not practice fulfilling the instructions of the Supreme Lord as delivered through the Lord’s devotees.

Furthermore, in proportion to our surrender to those instructions, we can be empowered by the grace of the Lord to fulfill His desire. The Lord says, “I am not capable of doing it, but you can do it.” The Lord can do it Himself, but He selects those devotees who are fully surrendered to His desires.

Nevertheless, the devotee thinks, “I am not doing it. I am simply an instrument in the hands of the Lord, and it is by the Lord’s desire that any successful result can become manifest.” This is the type of relationship that exists between the pure devotee and the Lord. The pure devotee is never thinking himself the doer of his activities, but simply an instrument.

A competition then begins when the pure devotee thinks himself inadequate to render service to the Lord, and the Lord wants to give credit to the pure devotee. The pure devotee, not interested in recognition, continuously puts it back on the Supreme Lord. Such is the manifestation of reciprocation of love between the Lord and His unalloyed servants. Because the pure devotee does not want glorification, recognition, or followers, the Lord is inclined to give him more and more. The pure devotee does not want it for himself. Rather, his primary concern is, “What does the Lord want?”

Through the principle of disciplic succession, the devotee of the Lord can be seen to be capable of performing incredible tasks. “One who is situated in the disciplic succession can be understood by the result of his activity.”

Being surrendered to the Lord, Śrīla Prabhupāda always saw himself as incapable without the Lord’s mercy. He was not thinking himself to be the doer of his activities. However, we see that a true servant of the Lord can produce wonderful results by His mercy. One cannot be successful in his devotional service without the blessings of the Lord or His pure devotee. The results of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s activities indicate that he received the blessings of the disciplic succession.

The result may not be immediately manifested. However, for one who has firm faith in the disciplic succession and in the order of the Lord and the spiritual master, these results will eventually become manifest. Devotees may sometimes be discouraged, thinking, with a fruitive mentality, that results should be there immediately. When the results are not there, one may lose faith in the process, become discouraged, and think he cannot be successful in his endeavors. However, the Lord sometimes wants to see how much the devotee is painstakingly willing to execute the order. Thus, it may take time before that result is manifest.

Similarly, Śrīla Prabhupāda struggled in the earlier days in establishing the movement. There were no big, immediate results for his efforts, but he had firm faith and a willingness to live in the most austere conditions—the Bowery, for example. He was willing to accept anything for the service of the Lord. It was a test. Thus, a devotee is always being tested to see how much inconvenience he is willing to accept for the service of the Lord—how much he is willing to make personal sacrifices and always be fixed on the mission his *guru* instructed him to carry out. Remembering this, we can understand that a devotee can become successful and can gradually see the Lord’s will manifesting before his eyes.

Thus, we should not immediately judge a person’s success in devotional service by immediate results. Rather, we have to see how much the devotee is willing to painstakingly execute the service given to him and be steady in the execution of that service. In due course of time, we can understand that the Lord may select such a devotee to fulfill His purpose. This is the desire of the Lord—to engage all living entities in His service, which will completely satisfy the self and please the Lord.

Thus, if we accept our service and remain true and steady in the execution of that service, then certainly we can capture the attention of the Lord and invoke His blessings and mercy. Moreover, we will also be able to witness the success of our endeavors by our attempt to please the pure devotee of the Lord. This is the real criterion of success in devotional service, and the Lord is very much inclined to give such success to those devotees who serve Him without any personal motivation. Certainly, one who is always thinking in this way, free from pride and desire for fruitive results, will be seen as very successful in due course of time by the arrangement of the Lord.

*tasmāt tvam uttiṣṭha yaśo labhasva*  
*jitvā śatrūn bhuṅkṣva rājyaṁ samṛddham*  
*mayaivaite nihatāḥ pūrvam eva*  
*nimitta-mātraṁ bhava savya-sācin*

“Therefore get up. Prepare to fight and win glory. Conquer your enemies and enjoy a flourishing kingdom. They are already put to death by My arrangement, and you, O Savyasaci, can be but an instrument in the fight.” (*Bg.* 11.33)

We will get the opportunity to become an instrument in Kṛṣṇa’s plan, but we simply have to become inspired to execute the order, and by that inspiration received through the transcendental disciplic succession, one can enjoy all success both in this life and in the next.

\* The Lord wants all of us to be fully engaged in devotional service cent percent, but why is it that we are not?

It is the Lord’s desire, but it must also be our desire. The devotee should always feel that the responsibility falls on him to cultivate the desire to be fully engaged in devotional service. The desire is lying dormant (covered), therefore one needs to cultivate that desire. It will otherwise never become manifest. When that desire is cultivated through constant chanting, praying, and taking every opportunity to engage in devotional service, then the Lord will naturally reciprocate with the cultivation of that desire by bestowing upon us the opportunity for further service. One has to water the seed by hearing and chanting. The seed of devotional service is planted in the heart by the mercy of a Vaiṣṇava. Although pure love is there within the heart, still it must be awakened by the mercy of a Vaiṣṇava who gives one the desire to serve the Lord. Cultivation then has to take place through the watering process of hearing and chanting as well as uprooting the desires that prevent us from being fully absorbed in devotional service.

## THE LORD TRAVELS TO VṚNDĀVANA

**Madhya-līlā: 17.185**

**Rostov—June 7, 1996**

**Text 185**

*dharma-sthāpana-hetu sādhura vyavahāra*  
*purī-gosāñira ye ācaraṇa, sei dharma sāra*

**\[Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu said,\] “A devotee’s behavior establishes the true purpose of religious principles. The behavior of Mādhavendra Purī Gosvāmī is the essence of such religious principles.”**

**Purport**

(…) It is those who are devoid of devotional service who sometimes mistakenly accept persons with mundane motives as *mahājanas*. The only motive must be *kṛṣṇa-bhakti*, devotional service to the Lord. Sometimes fruitive workers, dry philosophers, nondevotees, mystic *yogīs* and persons attached to material opulence, women and money are considered *mahājanas*.

(…) The conclusion is that all pious activity, fruitive activity, religious principles and renunciation must ultimately lead to devotional service. There are different types of processes for rendering service. One may serve his country, people and society, the *varṇāśrama-dharma* system, the sick, the poor, the rich, women, demigods and so on. All this service comes under the heading of sense gratification, or enjoyment in the material world. It is most unfortunate that people are more or less attracted by such material activity and that the leaders of these activities are accepted as *mahājanas*, great ideal leaders. Actually they are only misleaders, but an ordinary man cannot understand how he is being misled.

Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura says, *sādhu-śāstraguru-vākya, cittete kariyā aikya*: “One should accept as one’s guide the words of the *sādhus*, the *śāstra* and the *guru*.” A sādhu is a great personality like Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the *śāstras* are the injunctions of revealed scriptures, and the guru, or spiritual master, is one who confirms the scriptural injunctions. Accepting the guidance of these three is the actual way of following the great personalities (*mahājanas*) for real advancement in life (*mahājano yena gataḥ sa panthāḥ* \[*Cc. Madhya* 17.186\]). (…)

This verse was spoken by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu specifically in reference to a *brāhmaṇa* who had received the association of Mādhavendra Purī. Because this *brāhmaṇa* had manifested symptoms of ecstatic love for the Lord, Lord Caitanya could immediately understand that he must have had the association of Mādhavendra Purī. The Lord therefore agreed to accept food from him that was offered to the Lord, even though, by external characteristics, it appeared that the *brāhmaṇa* was of a lower caste.

According to the Vedic principles, a *brāhmaṇa* should not accept food from a person of a lower caste. Nevertheless, Lord Caitanya accepted food from the *brāhmaṇa* who was very pleased to offer it. The *brāhmaṇa* said to the Lord, “Foolish people will blaspheme You, but I shall not tolerate the words of such mischievous people.” (*Cc. Madhya* 17.183)

In Lord Caitanya’s response, He is specifically stating that even though there may appear to be contradictions at times in the *Vedās* and *Puranas,* if we want to reconcile them, we must do so by observing the behavior of a Vaiṣṇava.

Mādhavendra Purī was such an ideal Vaiṣṇava, and Lord Caitanya spoke this verse because Mādhavendra Purī accepted this *brāhmaṇa* as his disciple, although, according to social convention, he should not have accepted him. Even though Mādhavendra Purī acted contrary to social convention, Lord Caitanya said, “I am going to follow the example that is set by Mādhavendra Purī.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda explains in the purport that those who follow social customs of behavior forget to follow the path outlined by *mahājanas*. Sometimes they consider such *mahājanas* very conservative or they create their own *mahājanas*. Śrīla Prabhupāda gives a very long purport; I will summarize parts of it. In the beginning of the purport, Śrīla Prabhupāda is describing how everyone establishes some kind of *mahājana* in their life. *\[Paraphrasing purport to verse 185\]*: Just like the conditioned soul who is free from sense gratification, a *mahājana* is recognized according to the proportion of sense gratification that he offers. For instance, a businessman may consider a certain banker to be a *mahājana*. Or *karmīs,* desiring material enjoyment, consider philosophers like Jaimini to be *mahājanas*. For the *jñānīs,* the atheists Kapila and Vasiṣṭha are considered to be *mahājanas*. For the demons, Hiraṇyākṣa, Hiraṇyakaśipu, and Rāvaṇa are accepted as *mahājanas*. For materialistic anthropologists speculating on the evolution of the body, a person like Darwin is *mahājana*. The scientists who are bewildered by Krsna’s external energy have no relationship with the Supreme Lord, yet they are accepted by some as *mahājana.* Similarly, philosophers, historians, literary men, and public speakers are sometimes accepted as *mahājanas*.

In the purport, Śrīla Prabhupāda then quotes a verse from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*:

*śva-viḍ-varāhoṣṭra-kharaiḥ*  
*saṁstutaḥ puruṣaḥ paśuḥ*  
*na yat-karṇa-pathopeto*  
*jātu nāma gadāgrajaḥ*

“Men who are like dogs, hogs, camels and asses praise those men who never listen to the transcendental pastimes of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the deliverer from evils.” (*SB* 2.3.19)

“*Mahājana*” means a great personality, according to one’s conception, who sets an example that we are supposed to follow. Thus, materialistic people create their own *mahājana* based upon their own particular standards. They have a particular ambition in life, and many who share the same ambition may choose a *mahājana* who sets an example they want to follow. This takes place in all circles of society.

What Lord Caitanya is explaining here is that the real *mahājana* is one who is completely surrendered to the will of the Lord. He said that materialistic persons ignore the principles of the *paramparā* system.

Lord Caitanya says, “This is a great misfortune.” If one does not follow in the footsteps of a real *mahājana*, his plans for happiness will be frustrated. So one must know what proper behavior is. In this particular verse, Lord Caitanya specifically emphasizes that great Vaiṣṇavas establish the proper standards of behavior. We have to know what to look for in the behavior of a great Vaiṣṇava so that we can follow in the footsteps and try to adopt the same type of behavior.

Of course, Śrīla Prabhupāda would often state that one should not imitate the activities of highly elevated souls. One must follow in their footsteps: *mahājano yena gataḥ sa panthāḥ* (*Cc. Madhya* 17.186). That means that one has to follow the path which the *mahājanas* have left behind.

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Antya* 4.129–130), Lord Caitanya says to Sanātana Gosvāmī:

“My dear Sanātana, although you are the deliverer of the entire universe and although even the demigods and great saints are purified by touching you, it is the characteristic of a devotee to observe and protect the Vaiṣṇava etiquette. Maintenance of Vaiṣṇava etiquette is the ornament of a devotee.”

Lord Caitanya uses the particular word “ornament.” The ornament of a Vaiṣṇava is not something external, meant to attract attention to the body. When a Vaiṣṇava is ornamented with certain qualities, he attracts the attention of others, but a Vaiṣṇava attracts the attention of others to draw their attention to the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa. The ornaments of a Vaiṣṇava are naturally very attractive, because Kṛṣṇa makes the Vaiṣṇava very attractive. It is not that he has to artificially put on ornaments to attract the minds of other people. A Vaiṣṇava is naturally attractive, being adorned with the saintly qualities of a devotee of the Lord.

Following the *paramparā* system means following the standards Kṛṣṇa, the supreme authority, establishes. The *mahājana* is not established by the mental concoction of masses of people. The minds of people cannot create *mahājanas*. There are many popular people in the world who have thousands and thousands of followers due to having external ornaments that are very attractive to their followers. However, that does not make them *mahājanas*. *Mahājanas* are established by the Lord. The Lord selects a devotee, and empowers that devotee to spread His glories.

For example, Mādhavendra Purī, who manifested the symptoms of ecstatic love of Godhead, had undoubtedly received the mercy of the Lord.

Haridāsa Ṭhākura also displayed symptoms of love of Godhead. Sometimes, Haridāsa Ṭhākura was unable to contain himself and manifested these symptoms in public. He would walk in the streets and experience ecstatic love of the Lord. Tears would fall from his eyes, and he always exhibited great humility. Humility is considered to be one of the most valuable ornaments of a Vaiṣṇava. Although mistreated by others, Haridāsa Ṭhākura was always meditating on the Lord, and was not disturbed by such mistreatment.

There are many examples of different Vaiṣṇavas who manifested profound characteristics. Śrīla Prabhupāda was teaching all of us how to become Vaiṣṇavas. A Vaiṣṇava is one who teaches others how to behave. One of the most powerful ways he teaches others how to behave is by his own example. Śrīla Prabhupāda taught by his example how to behave as an ideal Vaiṣṇava. Therefore, his instructions also had great potency.

Śrīla Prabhupāda said, “We should act in such a way that people will see that we are of ideal character.” When asked how to recognize a Vaiṣṇava, Śrīla Prabhupāda would reply, “He is a perfect gentleman.”

*āpane ācare keha, nā kare pracāra*  
*pracāra karena keha, nā karena ācāra*  
*‘ācāra’, ‘pracāra’,—nāmera karaha ‘dui’ kārya*  
*tumi—sarva-guru, tumi jagatera ārya*

“Some behave very well but do not preach the cult of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, whereas others preach but do not behave properly. You simultaneously perform both duties in relation to the holy name by your personal behavior and by your preaching. Therefore you are the spiritual master of the entire world, for you are the most advanced devotee in the world.” (*Cc. Antya* 4.102–103)

In these verses, Sanātana Gosvāmī is speaking to Haridāsa Ṭhākura.

When we learn how to behave based on the example of Vaiṣṇavas, what is the best way to apply these instructions? Do we apply it to others, or do we apply it to ourselves?

In his purport, Śrīla Prabhupāda said that there might sometimes appear to be contradictions in dealings, or contradictory examples. Sometimes the tendency is to point out misbehavior or to correct others for not following a particular example even though we cannot follow the example ourselves. However, the point that is being made here is that one of the methods for correcting a devotee about his behavior is by one’s personal example. If we do not set the proper example by our own behavior, then who will want to listen to us?

Prabhupāda would often state that example is more important than precept, because if a devotee sets a good example, others will look at that example and respect it. When we get respect from setting a good example, others will accept our corrections and be willing to follow what we say. However, if we do not preach by example and try to correct others, then we will fail. First there must be example.

It is stated here that there is a difference between criticism and correction. Criticism of a devotee is the main cause for falling down from devotional service. Thus, if we want to correct others and not be guilty of criticizing, we have to be non-envious. That characteristic is very important. One of the characteristics of a *brāhmaṇa* is that he is not envious of others. He is always eager to receive the association of a devotee.

There is a verse in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* that explains the point that a devotee is happy to very nicely receive someone who is more advanced than he is. Nārada Muni says to Dhruva Mahārāja (*SB* 4.8.34):

Every man should act like this: when he meets a person more qualified than himself, he should be very pleased; when he meets someone less qualified than himself, he should be compassionate toward him; and when he meets someone equal to himself, he should make friendship with him. In this way one is never affected by the threefold miseries of this material world.

Prabhupāda explains in the purport to this verse, “Instead of being envious of a more qualified man, one should be jolly to receive him.” If one is envious of someone who has more qualifications than himself, or thinks, “He shines more than I do through his greater qualifications. I cannot keep him around me too long because he will outshine me,” then he is not very happy to receive that person. This is one of the symptoms of an envious person. An envious person will find fault, and he will try to point out the fault, even though sometimes there may not be any faults.

For example, Rāmacandra Khān tried to find fault with Haridāsa Ṭhākura, even though there were no faults whatsoever in his behavior. Rāmacandra Khān tried to create a fault because he could not tolerate that Haridāsa Ṭhākura was being glorified. Envy is certainly not a very good quality. If we want to be effective in correcting other devotees, we must first be non-envious. When we find a fault in another devotee, we should point it out for the right purpose. Not wanting to see the fault in himself, an envious person will point out a fault in others to make them look bad. Thus, in the name of helping somebody else, he is actually covering another motivation. He may present himself to be very charitable: “I want to help this person,” but his mind is dwelling on the shortcomings of the other person to make himself look better. We must feel a genuine concern for the person’s Kṛṣṇa consciousness. If our feeling is genuine, then the other devotees will appreciate it.

One of the characteristics of a devotee is that if there is some sincere and honest criticism that is offered to him, he is grateful. When we act negatively to well-intentioned corrections, we manifest false pride. However, an advanced Vaiṣṇava will see every correction offered to him as the mercy of the Lord. If somebody offers a devotee some words of advice, he does not immediately try to defend himself. He is grateful for the advice.

Once, Śrīla Prabhupāda was speaking to a group of people. There was an Indian man who was acting in a very proud way. He was criticizing, saying, “What is the meaning of *sama-darśinaḥ* (that all living entities are equal)?” He was trying to suggest to Prabhupāda that he is not on the platform of *sama-darśinaḥ*. Prabhupāda said, “My *sama-darśinaḥ* is that I simply repeat what Krsna says. I tell everyone who is sinning, without discrimination, that they are sinning.” He spoke very humbly. When he spoke in this way, the Indian man tried to say something else to criticize Prabhupāda about *sama-darśinaḥ,* and Śrīla Prabhupāda said, “Yes, alright. But this is my *sama-darśinaḥ.* I am simply trying to tell you about it.” When the man left the room, he turned to the other devotees and said, “Yes, your Guru Mahārāja is a very humble man.” Śrīla Prabhupāda always displayed complete humility, which is one of the symptoms of a devotee. Prabhupāda did not react negatively with false ego.

It is explained that an advanced Vaiṣṇava accepts everything as the mercy of the Lord. Therefore, when a devotee sincerely wants to assist us in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, then certainly, we should be willing to hear what this devotee has to say. These are some of the characteristics of how a devotee should behave when correcting others.

Harsh words and actions have a negative effect. We want to destroy the ignorance in the heart of a devotee; not destroy the devotee. Thus, when correcting others, we should not speak harsh words.

Of course, there are times when there may be exceptions. Sometimes a spiritual master may have to chastise a disciple very sternly. Sometimes Śrīla Prabhupāda would sharply deal with his disciples. He would then say, in a very humble way, “This is my duty as a spiritual master.”

Therefore, although he may speak very strongly or harshly to correct, it is understood that he has full control of his emotions, including anger. The spiritual master never chastises the disciple out of false ego or anger. Thus, a disciple will certainly willingly accept such chastisement. Nevertheless, in principle, harsh words and harsh actions have no place in dealings between devotees.

## LORD ŚRĪ CAITANYA MAHĀPRABHU INSTRUCTS ŚRĪLA RŪPA GOSVĀMĪ

**Madhya-līlā: 19.149**

**Kiev—July 21, 2001**

**Boston—1994**

**Text 149**

*kṛṣṇa-bhakta—niṣkāma, ataeva ‘śānta’*  
*bhukti-mukti-siddhi-kāmī—sakali ‘aśānta’*

**“Because a devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa is desireless, he is peaceful. Fruitive workers desire material enjoyment,** ***jñānīs*** **desire liberation, and** ***yogīs*** **desire material opulence; therefore they are all lusty and cannot be peaceful.”**

This is Lord Caitanya’s instruction to Rūpa Gosvāmī. He is describing the characteristics of a devotee, using the word *niṣkāma*, desireless. Only a person who is desireless can be peaceful, and peace is something everyone desires. In other words, desires have to be satisfied; otherwise, there is no question of peace. As long as there is desire or hankering within the heart due to feelings of incompleteness, the living entity cannot possibly experience satisfaction, and therefore cannot be peaceful. One of the symptoms of materialistic life is to always be hankering and lamenting.

When Arjuna asks Kṛṣṇa what the symptoms are of a person who is situated in transcendental consciousness, Kṛṣṇa explains that when a person gives up all desires for sense satisfaction, which arise from mental concoction, and when he finds satisfaction in the self alone, he is said to be in pure transcendental consciousness. The mental concoctions are the hankerings and lamentations of materialistic life. Upon experiencing fulfillment of these desires, the living entity falls down into material consciousness. That is the difficulty when we aspire for material fulfillment—when our desires are fulfilled, we do not feel as if they have gone away. Rather, our desires increase.

In the purport to this verse, Śrīla Prabhupāda gives the example of the *karmī*. The *karmī* may work very hard to earn a million dollars, and as soon as he gets the million dollars, he thinks, “This million is so wonderful that I want another million.” He has these desires, and when they are fulfilled, it does not satisfy the self. Therefore, by mental concoction, the living entity thinks, “I know why I am not satisfied. It is because this is not enough.” He thus digs himself deeper into fruitive activity. The *karmī* is always being bitten by material desires. One who tries to satisfy his desires by *karma* is compared to the person who is looking for treasure, but when he tries to find his treasure, he gets bitten by wasps and drones. In other words, he is continuously bitten by his own desires, which do not go away when they are fulfilled; they remain within the heart. How can there be peace for such a person?

The *karmīs* cannot be peaceful due to fruitive desires. The *jñānīs* cannot be peaceful because liberation is not complete and they have not taken shelter of Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet. Similarly, the *yogīs* cannot be peaceful because even though they may get mystic yogic perfections, they use it to attract some cheap adoration and followers. Because they still have lusty desires of wanting to enjoy followers, they also cannot become peaceful since these desires remained unfulfilled.

The point is that these persons cannot become desireless, and therefore there is no question of them ever being peaceful. As long as the symptoms of hankering and lamenting exist, there is no peace for the mind. Only devotees can be peaceful, as described in verse 149, because they are desireless. Śrīla Prabhupāda very nicely describes this in the purport by saying that if a devotee can understand that Kṛṣṇa is the only supreme enjoyer, he will perform sacrifices and austerities, understanding that Kṛṣṇa is the supreme friend of all living entities.

Śrīla Prabhupāda explains that if one actually understands Kṛṣṇa, he immediately becomes desireless, because he knows that Kṛṣṇa will always give protection to His devotee. Since Kṛṣṇa gives this assurance, a devotee lives for Kṛṣṇa and has no desire for personal benefit. The living entity feels complete only when he has re-established his connection with Kṛṣṇa; otherwise, he is always looking for something artificial to make himself feel more complete. He thinks material opulence, power, more followers, or obtaining liberation will make him complete. Even at the platform of liberation, one must fall down. He falls down because he does not find complete shelter. The only complete shelter is in devotional service to Kṛṣṇa.

Desire is the eternal companion of the soul, and we cannot stop it ourselves. Śrīla Prabhupāda gives the example in the *Bhagavad-gītā* of the waves in the river. Trying to stop desires ourselves is like going into the middle of the river holding up our arms and trying to stop the waves of the river. It is not possible to stop the waves of the river by our own efforts. However, he says that when the waves of the river enter the waves of the ocean, the waves of the river can be pushed by the greater strength of the ocean. Thus, we cannot stop the waves of material desires by our own efforts; we have to allow our desires to enter into the ocean of spiritual desires, devotional service. Only then can the desires be pushed back.

Śrīla Prabhupāda explains in relation to this example that the way we put our waves of desires into the ocean is by always making plans for Kṛṣṇa’s service. Lord Kṛṣṇa explains in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (9.13):

*mahātmānas tu māṁ pārtha*  
*daivīṁ prakṛtim āśritāḥ*  
*bhajanty ananya-manaso*  
*jñātvā bhūtādim avyayam*

**“O son of Pṛthā, those who are not deluded, the great souls, are under the protection of the divine nature. They are fully engaged in devotional service because they know Me as the Supreme Personality of Godhead, original and inexhaustible.”**

Kṛṣṇa explains that the *mahātmas,* the great souls, are under the protection of the divine nature and are therefore not deluded. One who comes under the protection of the divine nature of the Lord is always engaged in devotional service.

It is also described in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* that the devotees do not fear any condition of life, whether they are in heaven, in hell, or liberated, because they are always interested in rendering devotional service to the Lord. Therefore they always feel the Lord’s protection. When one experiences this protection in devotional service, he does not look for happiness from anything else.

Similarly, there is the example given of the young child who feels protected by his parents in all circumstances, although it is not that he expects assistance from the parents. In the same way, the devotee does not expect the Lord’s assistance, but he always has firm faith that the Lord will give him protection in all circumstances. This is the symptom of one who has come under the protection of this *daivīṁ prakṛtim,* the internal potency of the Supreme Lord.

It is described in the prayers of one great devotee that when a devotee experiences protection from the Lord and receives the merciful glance of Lord Caitanya, the conception of liberation is as palatable as going to hell, and the heavenly planets become very insignificant. A devotee does not become the least bit interested in these things. Why does he lose interest in these things? He loses his desire because the Lord is fulfilling his desire. One becomes desireless because he is confident that Kṛṣṇa will give him protection in all circumstances. He has complete confidence and faith. He is satisfied to be engaged in devotional service, and he knows he is protected in all circumstances. Kṛṣṇa gives His assurance of this: *kaunteya pratijānīhi, na me bhaktaḥ praṇaśyati*, “O son of Kuntī, declare it boldly that My devotee never perishes” (*Bg.* 9.31).

A devotee feels this protection by being always conscious of the Lord. When one comes under the internal protection of the Lord, feeling such protection, his desires are all fulfilled. How is it that this happens? This, of course, is the unique characteristic of *bhakti* that only a devotee can understand. One who practices devotion to Kṛṣṇa can directly understand the characteristics of devotional service and how it is that the Lord satisfies all of one’s desires. This is a symptom of advancement in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. When Kṛṣṇa fulfills the desires of the devotee, he feels fully satisfied. The Lord knows how to protect the devotee even better than the devotee. He protects His devotee in such a way that the devotee will lose the desire for material enjoyment; that is the protection of the Lord.

For example, when we eat, we know that our hunger is being satisfied. In the same way, when we are engaged in devotional service, we see that our attachments for material things diminish. Similarly, it is described that the nature of *bhakti,* devotional service to the Lord, produces two different byproducts. For one who engages in devotional service to Hṛṣīkeśa, the master of the senses, the result is that his senses become controlled. Kṛṣṇa can satisfy the senses much better than we can. When we try to satisfy our senses, they rage like fire. However, when Kṛṣṇa satisfies the senses, the devotee feels satisfaction, and therefore the devotee always engages his senses in the service of Hṛṣīkeśa. In other words, the Lord is giving the devotee protection by engaging him in devotional service. This is the symptom of peacefulness. Therefore, one must have an understanding of Kṛṣṇa. Without understanding Kṛṣṇa, one is always fearful of this surrender.

We always visualize surrender as something very difficult. Surrender is compared to waving the white flag. There may be two armies fighting, and one army may show superior force. When the superior force becomes overwhelming, the few remaining soldiers of the other army realize that they have no hope for survival unless they surrender. They therefore wave the white flag and say they will surrender. However, that kind of surrender is not very sheltered surrender. When one surrenders to the enemy, the enemy takes them and puts them to work in hard labor, or puts them into prison camps, where they are surrendering themselves to be exploited by the opposing army. Thus, when we think of the word “surrender” we visualize this type of experience—“I just have to surrender myself, because I can see that there is no other choice.”

Surrender to Kṛṣṇa is not that same type of surrender. If one does not know the nature of the Lord, then he does not understand what real surrender is, and he is constantly fearful. The living entity always holds back in fear, thinking, “As long as I maintain these attachments, then at least I will have some protection. But if I surrender everything, I will lose everything.” These attachments are seated deep within the heart.

One of the offenses of the chanting of the holy name of the Lord is to maintain material attachments even after understanding so many instructions on the matter. Kṛṣṇa wants to take everything away because He wants to give Himself. However, He cannot give Himself if we are so overburdened by material desires. Kṛṣṇa only gives Himself to one who has no other desires in this world. Kṛṣṇa feels bound to the devotee who is exclusively dependent upon Him. He is thinking that this devotee is not depending upon anything else.

The Lord is always absorbed in the thoughts of the devotee. However, for the devotee who wishes to serve the Lord, Kṛṣṇa fully satisfies those desires; He is completely prepared to give Himself. Such is the nature of devotional service. The Lord can only be understood by one who is engaged in devotional service.

*nāyaṁ sukhāpo bhagavān*  
*dehināṁ gopikā-sutaḥ*  
*jñānināṁ cātma-bhūtānāṁ*  
*yathā bhaktimatām iha*

“The Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa, the son of mother Yaśodā, is accessible to devotees engaged in spontaneous loving service, but He is not as easily accessible to mental speculators, to those striving for self-realization by severe austerities and penances, or to those who consider the body the same as the self.” (*SB* 10.9.21)

Another name for Kṛṣṇa is Bhakta-vātsalya, which means one who wants to satisfy His *bhakta*. Kṛṣṇa exists to satisfy his *bhakta,* which gives Him His greatest pleasure. When a devotee experiences the higher satisfaction of devotional service, then material pleasures become very insignificant. He sees that this is the supreme protection of the Lord. There is no greater protection than to be engaged in devotional service to the Lord. Therefore, one of the symptoms of a devotee is that he is always anxious to be engaged in devotional service. He cannot tolerate even a moment of not being engaged in devotional service. When the devotee feels dependent in this way, the Lord gives him protection.

We simply have to think how to increase our desire for service. That desire is there within the heart; it just has to be brought out. Although it is there, locked up within the heart, it can be brought out by association with a devotee of the Lord. When we associate with a devotee, our desire to render service to Kṛṣṇa increases.

We therefore offer our prayers to the Vaiṣṇavas, the devotees of the Lord, because they are like wish-fulfilling desire trees, fulfilling the desires of everyone, and they are full of compassion for the fallen conditioned souls. How is it that a Vaiṣṇava fulfills everyone’s desires, when only Kṛṣṇa can fulfill everyone’s desires? We understand that the Vaiṣṇavas fulfill everyone’s desires by giving everyone Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa can only be approached through His devotee; Kṛṣṇa makes Himself accessible in that way. To approach Kṛṣṇa by any means other than by the mercy of His devotees is not possible. By the mercy of a devotee, one can receive a glimpse of Kṛṣṇa. This is causeless mercy. A Vaiṣṇava only gives Kṛṣṇa. This is the value of the association of devotees. Having received this mercy, one’s devotional life can begin.

It is stated by Lord Caitanya (*Cc. Madhya* 19.151):

*brahmāṇḍa bhramite kona bhāgyavān jīva*  
*guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bīja*

“According to their *karma*, all living entities are wandering throughout the entire universe. Some of them are being elevated to the upper planetary systems, and some are going down into the lower planetary systems. Out of many millions of wandering living entities, one who is very fortunate gets an opportunity to associate with a bona fide spiritual master by the grace of Kṛṣṇa. By the mercy of both Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual master, such a person receives the seed of the creeper of devotional service.”

The living entity is wandering throughout the entire *brahmāṇḍa* (universe). When by great fortune he comes in contact with the spiritual master, then the *bhakti-latā bīja,* or the seed of devotional service, is planted in his heart. This seed of devotional service has everything within it to bear fruits of love of God. When this devotional seed is planted in the heart, the disciple waters it with continuous hearing.

By engaging in devotional service, one can begin to understand the nature of devotional service. Otherwise, it will always remain a mystery. The only way one can unlock the mystery is to take the association of the devotee of the Lord and engage in devotional service as distributed by the devotee of the Lord. In this way, one will be able to come to the platform of being completely free from desires. The *karmīs*, *jñānīs,* and *yogīs* cannot have this protection, because Kṛṣṇa will not give protection if they are not interested in it. They want fruitive results, mystic yogic powers, or liberation, thinking that that will give them protection; but only the devotee is fully protected.

Kṛṣṇa explains in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (18.65)*:* “Always think of Me, become My devotee, worship Me and offer your homage unto Me. Thus you will come to Me without fail.” Kṛṣṇa assures that the devotee will come to Him because He is pleased by the devotee’s activities and full dependence upon Him. Nothing will please the Lord more than one feeling himself fully dependent upon the Lord. The Lord feels very obliged to such a devotee.

Prabhupāda explains in the purport (to *Madhya* 19.149) that the devotee does not expect any assistance from Kṛṣṇa. He says that the devotee is desireless because Kṛṣṇa will give him protection in all circumstances. However, he does not expect that Kṛṣṇa is going to be his order supplier. Rather, he expects Kṛṣṇa’s mercy. How will Kṛṣṇa’s mercy become manifest to him? That is according to Kṛṣṇa’s will. A devotee knows that Kṛṣṇa’s will is always the final decision.

Similarly, Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura says, “Whether you decide to kill me or save me, still you are my worshipful Lord. You make the decision.”

The devotee has so much confidence in Kṛṣṇa and knows that if Kṛṣṇa makes a decision, he is safe. The devotee does not want to make the decision himself. Although, for the service of the Lord, the devotee has to make decisions, and he depends on Kṛṣṇa for the result.

Sometimes, however, if we become attached to the result, we want Kṛṣṇa to do it our way. But if Kṛṣṇa chooses something else, He must have some purpose.

*na hy asya karhicid rājan*  
*pumān veda vidhitsitam*  
*yad vijijñāsayā yuktā*  
*muhyanti kavayo ’pi hi*

“(…) No one can know the plan of the Lord \[Śrī Kṛṣṇa\]. Even though great philosophers inquire exhaustively, they are bewildered.” (*SB* 1.9.16)

Sometimes it is very difficult to understand what Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s plan is. We want a result, and therefore we become attached. We then become very disturbed if we do not get the result. Due to not accepting Kṛṣṇa’s decisions, it is very hard to be peaceful. However, such a mentality is no excuse for inactivity.

Sometimes a foolish person will think, “If Kṛṣṇa will make the decisions, then why should I bother to do anything? If He has already made the decision, then I will just let Him do what He wants to do.” However, we will not benefit from that mentality. We will not become Kṛṣṇa conscious; we will not make advancement by that process. We must be active in the service of the Lord. If we are not active in the service of the Lord, if the mind becomes inactive, then *mayā* is always waiting to offer some alternative. No one can be inactive for even a moment. Therefore, a devotee always has to be active for the Lord and always fully dependent upon Him for protection.

Prabhupāda says (*Madhya* 19.149): “It is not that he expects any assistance from Kṛṣṇa; he simply depends on Kṛṣṇa just as a child depends on his parents.” Kṛṣṇa knows how to assist His devotee to come to Him. However, the devotee should never demand Kṛṣṇa to help him like this. The devotee simply prays, “Let me serve You.” When the devotee prays to the Lord, he is not asking for anything for himself.

We often think that praying means asking for something. For example, one may go to the Lord and say, “O Lord, please help my family, help me with this or help me with that…” However, a devotee prays to the Lord, “Lord, let me serve You.” The principle behind real love is that there are no conditions. “Love” means to want to please the beloved. How can we please the beloved unless the beloved indicates what we can do to please him or her?

The desires for material enjoyment (*bhukti*) and liberation from the material world (*mukti*) are considered to be two witches that haunt one like ghosts. As long as these two witches remain in the heart, there is no possibility of experiencing the bliss of transcendental service to Kṛṣṇa.

There is nothing superior to devotional service. Its beauty is how the Lord reciprocates with a devotee. This is the essence of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. When a devotee awakens the desire to please the Lord within his heart, the Lord gives devotional service to him, which is the topmost gift that one can possibly obtain in life. Nevertheless, we know it is not so easy to obtain. There is a verse that says that when Kṛṣṇa gives somebody devotional service, He also gives Himself, and therefore He does not give it easily—even though He may give liberation very easily.

Similarly, if one chants the holy name of the Lord even on the platform of *nāmābhāsa*, he can get liberation. Just a slight glimpse of offenseless chanting of the holy name of the Lord gives liberation.

Gopāla Cakravartī asked Haridāsa Ṭhākura, “How can you say that one can get liberation so easily? You are saying that with only a slight glimpse of offenseless chanting one can get liberation? Do you not know that after millions of births, sages are trying to achieve liberation, but cannot even with the knowledge they have attained? Yet, you say that one can get liberation simply by *nāmābhāsa*?” Therefore, Kṛṣṇa will give liberation easily, but He does not give pure devotional service so easily, because He will have to give Himself also.

When a devotee chants the pure holy name (*śuddhanāma*), he does not simply experience the holy name; he actually experiences everything about Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa personally manifests Himself in His name to the devotee who chants purely. It may take some time, but if you associate with the devotees of the Lord and continue chanting the holy name of the Lord, then the desires for *bhukti* and *mukti* gradually become cleansed from the heart. When these desires are uprooted from the heart, the devotee becomes genuinely peaceful. We become peaceful because we have no other desires that need to be fulfilled.

We can look and see how many times we have had these desires for happiness and how many times we became frustrated. Not only do we become frustrated, but also the pleasure does not last very long. That is the nature of material desire. We think that it is making us happy now, but how long can we continue to enjoy? Therefore, it is described that the desires for happiness and relieving distress are like two witches. We are always being haunted by these desires. As soon as a desire is fulfilled, we are no longer satisfied and we immediately look for some other desire or something else to enjoy. There is no end.

We should therefore strive to become peaceful in the way that Kṛṣṇa recommends in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (5.29)*: bhoktāraṁ yajña-tapasāṁ sarva-loka-maheśvaram.* We should understand that Kṛṣṇa can satisfy all our desires. He is the supreme proprietor, and we should offer everything to Him, trying to please Him. Any service rendered in this consciousness gains permanent result. Just a little service rendered in this consciousness will never be lost. That is what Kṛṣṇa means when He says (*Bg.* 2.40): *nehābhikrama-nāśo ’sti, pratyavāyo na vidyate*.

Sometimes devotees try to do something to please Kṛṣṇa, but the result does not come. They then think, “I did all that, and I did not get any result. I simply wasted my time.” It is a great mistake to think like that. It means that one did not render the service in the right consciousness. The devotee who renders service in the consciousness of trying to please Kṛṣṇa knows that there is never any loss. Kṛṣṇa is looking for how a devotee is becoming increasingly attached to Him. Kṛṣṇa knows how to do that for His devotee, and therefore the external result may not always be there. It may come after a very long time. It is very difficult to know what Kṛṣṇa’s plan is.

Prabhupāda explains that even though a devotee does not know what Kṛṣṇa’s plan is, he is always obedient to Him. The devotee waits for Kṛṣṇa to reveal His plan. We may not fully understand what the ultimate plan is and what we are supposed to accomplish in this life, but we should become obedient to Kṛṣṇa first, and then everything else will become revealed to us. In this way, there is never any loss.

There is a very nice pastime about Śivānanda Sena and how he adopted a dog. When Lord Caitanya’s associates from Bengal were going to Jagannātha Purī, a dog, probably smelling *prasādam,* started to follow them.

When they had to take a boat in Orissa, the boatman did not want to allow the dog onto the boat, but Śivānanda Sena paid a substantial amount of extra money so that the dog could come.

One day, the devotees forgot to feed the dog and the dog went away. Śivānanda Sena immediately began looking for the dog. Not only did he begin looking but he also sent ten devotees to look for the dog.

We can see how thoughtful, kind, and caring a devotee is. Because Śivānanda Sena saw this dog as a devotee, he sent ten other devotees to look for this one missing devotee. Thus, devotees should be very thoughtful of each other and concerned about each other’s devotional life. If a devotee is not coming to the temple, we should be very concerned.

Śivānanda Sena finally fasted because he understood the dog had not eaten. The devotees continued to Jagannātha Purī to meet Lord Caitanya.

When they met Lord Caitanya the following day, they saw the same dog sitting near Lord Caitanya. Lord Caitanya was doing two things in relation to the dog, one of which was feeding the dog coconut. Young coconuts have very little juice inside and are very hearty. Lord Caitanya was throwing the coconut pulp to the dog with His very own hands.

The second thing that Lord Caitanya was doing with the dog was asking him to chant the holy name. The dog was saying, “*hare kṛṣṇa hare kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa hare hare / hare rāma hare rāma rāma rāma hare hare*.”

According to the statements of Haridāsa Ṭhākura, even the birds and beasts can chant the holy name of the Lord.

Śrīla Prabhupāda used to say, “These Americans do not even know the meaning of this Hare Kṛṣṇa, but we are seeing how, by the power of Lord Caitanya and by hearing the holy name of the Lord, they are becoming so attracted. This is all the credit of Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

## THE PROCESS OF DEVOTIONAL SERVICE

**Madhya-līlā: 22.100**

**St. Petersburg—January 2, 1995**

**Text 100**

*ānukūlyasya saṅkalpaḥ*  
*prātikūlyasya varjanam*  
*rakṣiṣyatīti viśvāso*  
*goptṛtve varaṇaṁ tathā*  
*ātma-nikṣepa-kārpaṇye*  
*ṣaḍ-vidhā śaraṇāgatiḥ*

**“The six divisions of surrender are the acceptance of those things favorable to devotional service, the rejection of unfavorable things, the conviction that Kṛṣṇa will give protection, the acceptance of the Lord as one’s guardian or master, full self-surrender, and humility.”**

This is one of the verses of Lord Caitanya’s instructions to Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī. Prior to this verse, Lord Caitanya explains that there are two kinds of devotees: those who are fully satiated and free from all material desires and those who are fully surrendered to the lotus feet of the Lord. Their qualities are the same, but those who are fully surrendered to Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet are qualified with another transcendental quality called *ātmā-samarpana,* which means full surrender without reservation. This full surrender is divided into six different divisions as described in verse 22.

The first two divisions are to accept everything favorable and to reject everything unfavorable for devotional service. This is described as a symptom of surrender. If we are going to analyze this surrender, we have to understand it according to these definitions given here. The word that is used is *śaraṇāgati,* which describes the process of surrender by which one becomes a fully surrendered instrument for the Lord. This verse lists the characteristics of one who is fully surrendered. Therefore, this is the ideal standard that one should come to if he is aspiring to become a surrendered servant of the Lord.
