Nārada Muni is trying to use that same type of approach with Mṛgāri the hunter. Thus, Mṛgāri is asking how he can become freed from the reactions of his sinful activities.

**Text 254**

*ei pāpa yāya mora, kemana upāye?*  
*nistāra karaha more, paḍoṅ tomāra pāye”*

**The hunter continued, ‘My dear sir, please tell me how I can be relieved from the reactions of my sinful life. Now I fully surrender unto you and fall down at your lotus feet. Please deliver me from sinful reactions.’**

Śrīla Prabhupāda further explains surrender in his purport to this verse. To whom should we surrender? We should surrender to the representative of Kṛṣṇa. The representative of Kṛṣṇa does not preach surrender unto himself; rather, he preaches surrender unto Kṛṣṇa.

Prabhupāda says that when a person submits himself in this way, his life can be saved. Of course, it does not necessarily mean that if a person has some terminal illness and he surrenders, his life will be saved. It means that his path is cleared. The path does not necessarily end with becoming happy after this life. It means his path is cleared for going back home, back to Godhead in the future.

He can certainly be freed from the reactions of sinful life and become happy. He becomes happy by being able to tolerate all kinds of adverse conditions, which are due to the *karma* of his past misdeeds. He becomes happy because he sees Kṛṣṇa in everything and feels the protection of Kṛṣṇa despite the difficulties. In this way, his path is cleared.

*tat te ’nukampāṁ su-samīkṣamāṇo*  
*bhuñjāna evātma-kṛtaṁ vipākam*

(SB 10.14.8)

He tolerates the *karma* of his past activities, always seeks the compassion of the Lord, glorifies the Lord constantly, and always engages in service to the Lord. His path then becomes cleared and he becomes a bona fide candidate for liberation. He becomes assured of the protection of the Lord and that his future after this body will also continue to be auspicious. It is described in the Second Canto of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* that the most auspicious path to follow is the path of rendering devotional service to the Lord.

Therefore, this example is very illustrative. It shows how somebody as sinful as a hunter—a hunter who had previously derived great pleasure from half-killing animals—had become relieved from his reactions of sinful life and found himself on the most auspicious path, giving up all sinful activities and achieving perfection.

**Nārada Muni assured the hunter, ‘If you listen to my instructions, I shall find the way you can be liberated.’ (***Cc. Madhya* **24.255)**

The pure devotee’s instruction can relieve one from sinful reaction because he is not giving instructions to surrender to himself—he is giving instructions to surrender to Kṛṣṇa. By the power of his words, surrendering to those instructions is as good as surrendering to Kṛṣṇa.

When the Supreme Lord gives that empowerment to the devotee, it means that the Supreme Lord is accepting this person as His representative. If the Lord accepts someone as His representative, then the process works because the direct connection to the Lord is there. If the Lord does not accept someone as His representative, then surrender unto that person is not recognized by the Lord, and therefore he cannot become relieved from reactions to sinful life.

The spiritual master is referred to as “His Divine Grace.” He is carrying the mercy of the Lord, and therefore through his instructions and example, he is giving others the opportunity to come in contact with the Lord. To make advancement in spiritual life, a disciple must have faith that the spiritual master is the representative of Kṛṣṇa.

Everybody in this material world is very eager to give instruction. But we have to see that instruction which is most conducive to bringing us to the process of surrendering at the lotus feet of the Supreme Lord. Such surrender must be offered to the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa’s representative.

**Text 256**

*vyādha kahe,—‘yei kaha, sei ta’ kariba’*  
*nārada kahe,—‘dhanuka bhāṅga, tabe se kahiba’*

**The hunter then said, ‘My dear sir, whatever you say I shall do.’ Nārada immediately ordered him, ‘First of all, break your bow. Then I shall tell you what is to be done.’**

**Purport**

This is the process of initiation. The disciple must vow that he will no longer commit sinful activity—namely illicit sex, meat-eating, gambling and intoxication. He promises to execute the order of the spiritual master. Then the spiritual master takes care of him and elevates him to spiritual emancipation. (*Cc. Madhya* 24.256)

Prabhupāda is comparing the breaking of Mṛgāri’s bow to initiation. Both initiation and the broken bow mean that previous sinful activities are finished.

Nārada first told Mṛgāri to completely kill the animals; not to half-kill them. But now he is going a little bit further by instructing him to break his bow.

The hunter replied, ‘If I break my bow, how shall I maintain myself?

Nārada Muni replied, ‘Do not worry. I shall supply all your food every day.’

**Purport**

The source of our income is not actually the source of our maintenance. Every living being— from the great Brahmā down to an insignificant ant—is being maintained by the Supreme Personality of Godhead. *Eko bahūnāṁ yo vidadhāti kāmān.* The one Supreme Being, Kṛṣṇa, maintains everyone. Our so-called source of income is our own choice only. If I wish to be a hunter, it will appear that hunting is the source of my maintenance. If I become a *brāhmaṇa* and completely depend on Kṛṣṇa, I do not conduct a business, but nonetheless my maintenance is supplied by Kṛṣṇa. The hunter was disturbed about breaking his bow because he was worried about his income. Nārada Muni assured the hunter because he knew that the hunter was not being maintained by the bow but by Kṛṣṇa. (*Cc. Madhya* 24.257)

*nityo nityānāṁ cetanaś cetanānām e*  
*ko bahūnāṁ yo vidadhāti kāmān*

(Kaṭha Upaniṣad, 2.2.13)

Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Lord and He is maintaining all other eternal living beings. Thus, Nārada Muni’s statement was based on the conviction that Kṛṣṇa will maintain.

The surrendering process means to be surrendered to hearing what is one’s duty in life, and by executing that duty with faith in the spiritual master, which means faith in Kṛṣṇa, then maintenance will come.

**Being thus assured by the great sage Nārada Muni, the hunter broke his bow, immediately fell down at the saint’s lotus feet and fully surrendered. After this, Nārada Muni raised him with his hand and gave him instructions for spiritual advancement.**

**Nārada Muni then advised the hunter, ‘Return home and distribute whatever riches you have to the pure** *brāhmaṇas* **who know the Absolute Truth. After you have distributed all your riches to the** *brāhmaṇas,* **you and your wife should leave home, each of you taking only one cloth to wear.’ (***Cc. Madhya* **24.258-259)**

Nārada Muni’s instructions to Mṛgāri were according to his particular status, which was *vānaprastha*. It is not that this instruction is meant to be given to everyone who surrenders.

**Nārada Muni continued, ‘Leave your home and go to the river. There you should construct a small cottage, and in front of the cottage you should grow a** *tulasī* **plant on a raised platform. After planting the** *tulasī* **tree before your house, you should daily circumambulate that** *tulasī* **plant, serve her by giving her water and other things, and continuously chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa** *mahā-mantra***.’ Nārada Muni continued, ‘Every day I shall send sufficient food to you both. You can take as much food as you want.’**

**The three animals that were half-killed were then brought to their consciousness by the sage Nārada. Indeed, the animals got up and swiftly fled. When the hunter saw the half-killed animals flee, he was certainly struck with wonder. He then offered his respectful obeisances to the sage Nārada and returned home. After all this, Nārada Muni went to his destination. After the hunter returned home, he exactly followed the instructions of his spiritual master, Nārada. The news that the hunter had become a Vaiṣṇava spread all over the village. Indeed, all the villagers brought alms and presented them to the Vaiṣṇava who had formerly been a hunter. (***Cc. Madhya* **24.260–266)**

Mṛgāri the hunter was taking *vānaprastha,* giving up his occupational duties and preparing himself for his future renunciation. Therefore, he was taking the principles of Vaiṣṇava life and renunciation. Vaiṣṇava *brāhmaṇas* who are genuinely following their prescribed duties—they may be worshipping the Deity or they may be preaching—should be given some charity.

It is the duty of the householders to give in charity to the *brāhmaṇas*. By giving in charity to those who are performing brahminical work, they become relieved from the reactions of their own work. Those who are living, working, and maintaining themselves in this way, as honest *gṛhasthas*, can fulfill their duties, either by giving charity for the purpose of assisting others in preaching, or they themselves can go out and preach and use their mind and words for preaching.

However, as Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura always instructs in many of his songs, they should not go out and preach with the motive of collecting rice. They should maintain themselves by some honest work. They should go out and preach and beg charity in the same mood as Nārada Muni, Lord Nityānanda, and Haridāsa Ṭhākura. They should go out to preach to give transcendental knowledge and the holy name to others. Householders can use either their wealth by giving in charity to the *brāhmaṇas,* or they can use their intelligence and words.

*Prāṇair arthair dhiyā vācā* (*SB* 10.22.35). One can use any of these things: life, wealth, words, or intelligence, but it is the duty of every living being to perform welfare work for the benefit of others. Being engaged in one’s own work for the benefit of himself is not a prescribed duty given in *śāstra*. Human life is meant for sacrifice.

**In one day enough food was brought for ten or twenty people, but the hunter and his wife would accept only as much as they could eat.**

**One day, while speaking to his friend Parvata Muni, Nārada Muni requested him to go with him to see his disciple the hunter. As the saintly sages approached the hunter’s place, the hunter could see them from a distance. With great alacrity the hunter began to run toward his spiritual master, but he could not fall down and offer obeisances because ants were running hither and thither around his feet. Seeing the ants, the hunter whisked them away with a piece of cloth. After thus clearing the ants from the ground, he fell down flat to offer his obeisances.**

**Nārada Muni said, ‘My dear hunter, such behavior is not at all astonishing. A man in devotional service is automatically nonviolent. He is the best of gentlemen. O hunter, good qualities like nonviolence, which you have developed, are not very astonishing, for those engaged in the Lord’s devotional service are never inclined to give pain to others because of envy.’**

**The hunter then received the two great sages in the courtyard of his house. He spread out a straw mat for them to sit upon, and with great devotion he begged them to sit down. He then fetched water and washed the sages’ feet with great devotion. Then both husband and wife drank that water and sprinkled it on their heads.**

**When the hunter chanted the Hare Kṛṣṇa** *mahāmantra* **before his spiritual master, his body trembled and tears welled up in his eyes. Filled with ecstatic love, he raised his hands and began to dance, waving his garments up and down.**

**When Parvata Muni saw the ecstatic loving symptoms of the hunter, he told Nārada, ‘Certainly you are a touchstone.’ Parvata Muni continued, ‘My dear friend Nārada Muni, you are glorified as the sage among the demigods. By your mercy, even a lowborn person like this hunter can immediately become attached to Lord Kṛṣṇa.’ Nārada** **Muni then asked the hunter, ‘My dear Vaiṣṇava, do you have some income for your maintenance?’**

**The hunter replied, ‘My dear spiritual master, whoever you send gives me something when he comes to see me. Please do not send so much grain. Only send what is sufficient for two people, no more.’**

**Nārada Muni approved his not wanting more than a daily supply of food, and he blessed him, saying, ‘You are fortunate.’ Nārada Muni and Parvata Muni then disappeared from that place.**

**Thus I have narrated the incident of the hunter. By hearing this narration, one can understand the influence of association with devotees. (***Cc. Madhya* **24.267–282)**

**Purport**

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu wanted to stress that even a hunter, the lowest of men, could become a topmost Vaiṣṇava simply by associating with Nārada Muni or a devotee in his bona fide disciplic succession.

*\* Are there some criteria for determining what is right and what is wrong?*

Certainly there have to be some criteria between what is right and what is wrong. That is why we have intelligence. Intelligence means to be able to discriminate between what is right and what is wrong. Intelligence is supposed to be superior to the mind because the mind is always giving ideas of sense gratification, especially when it is not fully controlled. Intelligence is supposed to discriminate between what is the dictation of the mind and what we are supposed to do. For somebody who does not have very strong intelligence, the mind always seems to be victorious.

Kṛṣṇa therefore says that for one who has conquered his mind, the mind is his best friend; and for one who does not control his mind, it is his worst enemy. It is the worst enemy because the mind will defeat us and we will have to follow its dictations and suffer the consequences for doing so. Therefore, we must have the strength of intelligence in order to discriminate what is the mind and what is the superior dictation. There must be some superior dictation to the dictation of the mind.

Our criteria for discrimination are based upon certain standards for our activities: *guru, sādhu,* and *śāstra*. We have to try to use these things as our criteria and not put so much faith on the mind.

In conditional life, we purify our intelligence with the instructions of the spiritual master. The spiritual master may instruct the disciple to engage in a particular work according to his status. When he accepts a spiritual master, he does not give up his means of maintenance and become a *sannyāsī*. He should perform his prescribed duties according to his particular status.

However, if the disciple happens to be a butcher, then the spiritual master will say to him, “Find some other means of work to maintain yourself. Give up your life as a butcher.” For Mṛgāri, the breaking of his bow meant he was supposed to give up sinful activities. That has to be done externally. Even though there may still be some desire or attachment left within the heart, by continually following the instructions of the spiritual master, which Prabhupāda emphasized means chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa continuously, gradually the disciple will be purified of all these attachments.

The disciple needs to learn how to assimilate the instructions of the spiritual master. The spiritual master may not give specific instructions, but he does say, “Every householder has to earn an honest means of livelihood.” By his means of livelihood, he has to support himself and his family, always depending upon Kṛṣṇa. He has to utilize his wealth, mind, intelligence, or words to somehow assist in the spreading of the holy names of the Lord.

The instructions are already there. A sincere disciple learns how to assimilate that instruction in the association of others who represent the instruction. He takes these instructions to heart and begins to act on them, seeking the association of devotees who will reinforce that instruction.

When a disciple is associating with devotees and hearing and chanting about Kṛṣṇa, he is preparing the soil for the seed to be planted. Therefore, at some later point in his life, the seed may be planted in the form of another instruction which will be the fruit of having followed all previous instructions.

## Part 3 - Antya-līlā

## THE MEETING OF ŚRĪ CAITANYA MAHĀPRABHU AND VALLABHA BHAṬṬA

**Antya-līlā: 7.1–15**

**Moscow—May 29, 2000**

**Text 1**

*caitanya-caraṇāmbhojamakaranda-liho bhaje yeṣāṁ prasāda-mātreṇa pāmaro ’py amaro bhavet*

**Let me offer my respectful obeisances unto the devotees of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Simply by the causeless mercy of the devotees engaged in licking honey from His lotus feet, even a fallen soul becomes eternally liberated.**

The invocation verses for each of the chapters of *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, by Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī, are filled with deep meaning. In this verse, he is offering his obeisances to the devotees of the Lord because they are engaged in licking the honey from the lotus feet of Lord Caitanya and thus liberating fallen souls.

In the *Ādi-līlā* (2.2)*,* Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī writes:

“O my merciful Lord Caitanya, may the nectarean Ganges waters of Your transcendental activities flow on the surface of my desert-like tongue. Beautifying these waters are the lotus flowers of singing, dancing and loud chanting of Kṛṣṇa’s holy name, which are the pleasure abodes of unalloyed devotees. These devotees are compared to swans, ducks and bees. The river’s flowing produces a melodious sound that gladdens their ears.”

Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī, again using metaphors, compares the activities of the Supreme Lord to the flowing of the Ganges across his desert-like tongue. When the cooling, refreshing Ganges water flows across the tongue, it relieves the scorching heat of the desert. Just as lotus flowers beautify the Ganges, the loud chanting, dancing, and singing of Kṛṣṇa’s names enhance the attractiveness of Lord Caitanya’s activities. These lotus flowers are the pleasure abodes of unalloyed devotees, who are compared to bees, ducks, and swans. They linger around the lotus flowers because they are a source of their nourishment. While lingering around the lotus flowers, the swans sometimes make their heads go down and their long necks get stuck in the stems of the lotus flowers.

This metaphor is contained in the verse in the *Mukundamālā-stotra,* where King Kulaśekhara is comparing his mind to be like a swan, which sinks down into the stem of a lotus flower and gets entangled in the network of the lotus flower. He thus prays that his mind may similarly be entangled in thoughts of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

The swans reside around these lotus flowers, and the bees are always buzzing around the lotus flowers, extracting honey. The devotees are thus compared to swans and bees, because they are attracted to the lotus flowers of singing, dancing, and the loud chanting of the holy name.

*mac-cittā mad-gata-prāṇā*  
*bodhayantaḥ parasparam*

(Bg. 10.9)

“The thoughts of My pure devotees dwell in Me, their lives are fully devoted to My service, and they derive great satisfaction and bliss from always enlightening one another and conversing about Me.”

Devotees relish the honey of the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa by discussing the Lord’s glories amongst themselves. When the devotees are thus licking the honey and relishing the sweet taste of Lord Caitanya’s pastimes, then even a fallen soul becomes eternally liberated simply by being in their presence.

Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī sets the mood in the opening verse of each chapter to inspire the devotee to enter and relish the nectar that is coming from the pastimes of the Lord. Every pastime of the Lord is unique. Ananta Śeṣa has been describing the Lord’s pastimes since time immemorial, and He has never repeated one of them. Of course, the more we repeat the Lord’s pastimes, the sweeter they become.

Śrīla Prabhupāda explained this in the Seventh Canto of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* where Nārada Muni instructs Yudhiṣṭhira Mahārāja. Nārada Muni instructs that one of the primary duties of the householder is to always associate with devotees who are expanding on the glories of the pastimes of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Thus, they can gradually awaken from their dream-like condition of life. He describes that material consciousness is like a dream, and the pastimes of the Lord are transcendental reality. However, in our dream-like state, we are engaged in the temporary affairs of the material world, whereas the liberated souls are always absorbed in the Lord’s pastimes.

That is one of the characteristics of liberated souls. When they chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, the Lord’s pastimes are constantly manifesting in their minds and hearts, and the waves of these pastimes are approaching them continuously. They are diving and surfacing in the waves of these pastimes, and sometimes they are absorbed in chanting all night long. The holy name of the Lord is continuously dancing on their tongues because they are actually seeing the Lord’s pastimes manifesting in His holy name.

Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura describes that the holy name of the Lord is like a flower which opens up. When it begins to open and the sweet fragrance begins to manifest, one chants the holy name of the Lord and perceives His form.

First *rūpa* (the form of the Lord) is manifested, then *guṇa* (the qualities of the Lord) are manifested, and finally *līlā* (the pastimes of the Lord) are manifested. The devotees of the Lord are always absorbed in the pastimes of the Lord, continuously diving and surfacing in the waves of these pastimes. When they speak these pastimes, they speak from a level of consciousness that is beyond this temporary material world.

There are five stages of knowledge: *pratyakṣa, parokṣa, aparokṣa, adhokṣaja, and aprākṛta*. *Pratyakṣa* is the stage based on sense perception. *Parokṣa* is knowledge (not necessarily transcendental knowledge) based on hearing from others. *Aparokṣa*, which is like a deep *susupti*, is revealed by the process of *jñāna*. The next stage of knowledge is called *adhokṣaja*, which is the knowledge of Vaikuṇṭha. This knowledge of Vaikuṇṭha is completely transcendental and cannot be achieved by any kind of mental gymnastics. It can only be achieved when, by the mercy of the Lord, He descends in the form of that knowledge and reveals it within the heart.

When *mayā,* or illusion, is dispelled, the Lord reveals His transcendental form. Thus, even the stage of *adhokṣaja* is not the topmost. Beyond that stage is *aprākṛta*, which is the highest platform. *Aprākṛta* is the knowledge by which the residents of the spiritual world, Goloka Vṛndāvana, are always absorbed in their relationships with the Lord. This knowledge is called *kevalā-bhakti* or *jñāna-śūnyā bhakti*.

*Jñāna-śūnyā bhakti* is devotional service rendered without knowledge. It is the highest stage because those rendering service do not have any knowledge of Kṛṣṇa as God. They may have some hint of it at times, but they think that Kṛṣṇa is a young cowherd boy who is their friend, son, or lover. They sometimes speak about God, but they are referring to Nārāyaṇa. They are not absorbed in thoughts of Kṛṣṇa as God. Their minds, hearts, words, and activities are completely absorbed in their relationship with Kṛṣṇa.

They are so exalted that they do not even know of their own exalted position. Such personalities are liberated souls, and sometimes such liberated souls appear in this world and speak about the pastimes of the Lord, revealing the transcendental abode (reality).

Those who are in the material world and absorbed in material consciousness are simply thinking about names, forms, qualities, and pastimes of this material nature. Nārada Muni therefore describes it as a dream-like stage. Everybody is dreaming about how to become happy in this life.

Similarly, there is the example of night dreams and daydreams. At night, people sometimes have very nice dreams, and sometimes they have very terrible dreams. People may also dream during the day; however, their daydreams last a little bit longer than their night dreams. They are dreaming about something that they would like to have, or something they would like to avoid. Regardless, it is a dream. As described in the Eleventh Canto of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, they create a reality that has no existence beyond their imagination.

However, those devotees who are continuously licking the honey from the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa taste a different reality. Nārada Muni thus uses the word *abhīkṣṇam,* which means that in order to awaken from one’s dream-like condition, one has to constantly hear about the pastimes of the Supreme Personality of Godhead in the association of devotees.

What is about to appear in the Seventh Chapter of *Antya-līlā* is a very wonderful pastime where *kevalā-bhakti* is explained by Lord Caitanya in reference to His relationship with Rāmānanda Rāya.

**Text 2**

*jaya jaya śrī-caitanya jaya nityānanda*  
*jayādvaita-candra jaya gaura-bhakta-vṛnda*

**All glories to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu! All glories to Nityānanda Prabhu! All glories to Advaitacandra! And all glories to all the devotees of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu!**

**Text 3**

*varṣāntare yata gauḍera bhakta-gaṇa āilā*  
*pūrvavat mahāprabhu sabāre mililā*

**The next year, all the devotees of Bengal went to visit Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and as previously, the Lord met each and every one of them.**

Similarly, the Sixth Chapter of *Antya-līlā* describes that when Lord Caitanya returned to Jagannātha Purī after traveling in South India, there were many devotees who gathered to meet the Lord. Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya and Rāmānanda Rāya were personally introducing them, one by one, to Lord Caitanya. After they were introduced, Lord Caitanya would embrace each one.

When the devotees would come from Navadvīpa, Lord Caitanya would sometimes personally go and receive them Himself, and sometimes He would send Govinda and other devotees to receive them with a garland for each one. The devotees were then immediately taken to their quarters and given Jagannātha-*prasāda*. Lord Caitanya personally taught how to receive Vaiṣṇavas.

**Text 4**

ei-mata vilāsa prabhura bhakta-gaṇa lañā hena-kāle vallabha-bhaṭṭa milila āsiyā

**Thus Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu performed His pastimes with His devotees. Then a learned scholar named Vallabha Bhaṭṭa went to Jagannātha Purī to meet the Lord.**

Vallabha Bhaṭṭa had a mission. He wanted to come and personally meet the Lord so that He would hear his commentary on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. The first thing Vallabha Bhaṭṭa did when he came before the Lord was glorify Him by speaking about His empowerment of distributing ecstatic love for Kṛṣṇa.

**When Vallabha Bhaṭṭa arrived, he offered his obeisances at the lotus feet of the Lord. Accepting him as a great devotee, the Lord embraced him. With great respect,** **Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu seated Vallabha Bhaṭṭa near** **Him. Then Vallabha Bhaṭṭa very humbly began to speak.**

**For a long time, he said, ‘I have desired to see You, my Lord. Now Lord Jagannātha has fulfilled this desire; therefore I am seeing You. One who receives Your audience is fortunate indeed, for You are the Supreme Personality of Godhead Himself. Since one who remembers You is purified, why should it be astonishing that one becomes purified by seeing You?’ (***Cc***.** *Antya* **7.5–9)**

Vallabha Bhaṭṭa then quotes a verse by Yudhiṣṭhira Mahārāja from the First Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*:

**Text 10**

*yeṣāṁ saṁsmaraṇāt puṁsāṁ*  
*sadyaḥ śudhyanti vai gṛhāḥ*  
*kiṁ punar darśana-sparśa*  
*pāda-śaucāsanādibhiḥ*

**‘One can immediately purify his entire house simply by remembering exalted personalities, to say nothing of directly seeing them, touching their lotus feet, washing their feet or offering them places to sit.’**

This verse supports Vallabha Bhaṭṭa’s statement, describing how one can become purified simply by remembering great personalities; what to speak of directly seeing them, touching or washing their feet, or rendering devotional service to them.

**The fundamental religious system in the Age of Kali is the chanting of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. Unless** **empowered by Kṛṣṇa, one cannot propagate the** *saṅkīrtana* **movement. You have spread the** *saṅkīrtana* **movement of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Therefore it is evident that You have been empowered by Lord Kṛṣṇa. There is no question about it. You have manifested the holy name of Kṛṣṇa throughout the entire world. Anyone who sees You is immediately absorbed in ecstatic love of Kṛṣṇa. Without being especially empowered by Kṛṣṇa, one cannot manifest ecstatic love of Kṛṣṇa, for Kṛṣṇa is the only one who gives ecstatic love. That is the verdict of all revealed scriptures. There may be many all-auspicious incarnations of the Personality of Godhead, but who other than Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa can bestow love of God upon the surrendered souls? (***Cc***.** *Antya* **7.11-15)**

Vallabha Bhaṭṭa is glorifying the Lord with appropriate verses and recognizing how the Lord is distributing love of God to everyone. He also quotes a verse that states that no one “other than Kṛṣṇa can bestow love of Godhead upon the surrendered souls.” (Cc. *Madhya* 7.15)

Another name for Kṛṣṇa is Mukunda, which means the giver of liberation. Only Kṛṣṇa can give liberation to the conditioned souls because He is the supreme controller of all potencies.

It is also described in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* that “if the conditioned soul becomes Kṛṣṇa conscious by the mercy of saintly persons who voluntarily preach scriptural injunctions and help him to become Kṛṣṇa conscious, the conditioned soul is liberated from the clutches of *māyā*, who gives him up.” (Cc. *Madhya* 20.120) Thus, although the Lord is the liberator, it is by the mercy of the devotees of the Lord that He delivers us.

Similarly, in the Eleventh Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, Kṛṣṇa says, “With the dust of My devotees’ lotus feet I desire to purify the material worlds, which are situated within Me.” (*SB* 11.14.16) Only the Lord can deliver, but He engages His devotees in executing His mission. By the mercy of such pure devotees of the Lord who deliver the instructions, pastimes, and holy name of the Lord, anyone who takes shelter of them will be delivered by the Lord.

For example, when Nityānanda delivered Jagāi and Mādhāi, He was thinking, “If these two become delivered, Lord Caitanya will be glorified throughout the three worlds. Lord Caitanya is *patita-pāvana,* the most merciful incarnation. He came to deliver *patita,* the most fallen. Therefore, if we can somehow, by carrying out Lord Caitanya’s order, deliver them, Lord Caitanya will be glorified.”

The servant of the Lord never thinks he can deliver anyone. He is only acting on behalf of the Lord. Ultimately, it is only the Lord who can deliver one.

Advaita Ācārya also said, “It is not possible to deliver these fallen souls. Only if the Lord personally comes can they be delivered. I will invoke the Lord to come. By the strength of My prayers, the Lord will come. If He does not come, then I will have to sever these fallen souls’ heads from their body.” Advaita Ācārya made such a strong vow because He was determined to bring the Lord, and on the strength of His prayer, the Lord indeed descended.

Lord Caitanya would often say to Advaita Ācārya, “Nāḍā \[He called Him Nāḍā\], You brought Me here.” For instance, when Advaita Ācārya was preaching impersonal philosophy, Lord Caitanya said, “You brought Me here to preach devotional service to the Lord and to deliver devotional service to everybody! Now look at what You are doing!” Angered, Lord Caitanya chastised Advaita Ācārya and started to slap him.

Advaita’s wife, Sītā, was crying. She said, “Please do not beat Him. He is an old man. Do not hurt Him!” Thus, Advaita Ācārya caused the Lord to descend to deliver the fallen souls. He was always absorbed in the mood of serving the Lord.

Devotees of the Lord always consider themselves to be servants of the Lord. By the strength of their devotional service, the Lord becomes bound to them. The Lord agrees to go wherever His devotee distributes Him. Therefore, Lord Caitanya would often say to His devotees, “You can sell Me wherever you like.”

Once He said it to Haridāsa Thakura, because of his overwhelming humility towards Him. Haridāsa Thakura was offering very intense prayers of devotion to the Lord, describing himself as someone who had no qualification to see the Lord. Lord Caitanya heard his prayers and said, “O Haridāsa, you have made Me a prisoner in your heart, and now you can sell Me wherever you would like.” Thus, Lord Caitanya describes Himself as being purchased by that strong love that a devotee has for the Lord. Because Haridāsa Thakura was always absorbed in chanting the holy names of the Lord, he had the power and potency to give the Lord to others. The Lord said, “Wherever you want, you can sell Me.” It is thus the Lord who ultimately delivers, and it is the merciful devotees of the Lord who distribute the Lord.

**Antya-līlā: 7.16–28**

**Moscow—June 30, 2000**

**\[In response to Vallabha Bhaṭṭa’s glorification of Him,\] Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu replied, “My dear Vallabha Bhaṭṭa, you are a learned scholar. Kindly listen to Me. I am a** *sannyāsī* **of the Māyāvāda school. Therefore I have no chance of knowing what** *kṛṣṇabhakti* **is. Nevertheless, My mind has become purified because I have associated with Advaita Ācārya, who is directly the Supreme Personality of Godhead. He is unparalleled in His understanding of all the revealed scriptures and the devotional service of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Therefore He is called Advaita Ācārya. He is such a great personality that by His mercy He can convert even the meat-eaters \[***mlecchas***\] to the devotional service of Kṛṣṇa. Who, therefore, can estimate the power of His Vaiṣṇavism?” (Cc.** *Antya* **7.16-19)**

**Purport**

It is extremely difficult to convert a *mleccha*, or meat-eater, into a devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Therefore anyone who can do so is situated on the highest level of Vaiṣṇavism. (Cc. *Antya* 7.19)

Advaita Ācārya is the first person Lord Caitanya is speaking about. However, He is not just going to stop with Advaita Ācārya; He is going to speak about many different *ācāryas* with whom He has intimately associated and from whom He has personally heard the higher topics of devotional service. Both Rāmānanda Rāya and Haridāsa Ṭhākura had instructed Him on topics of devotional service. Of course, it was ultimately the Lord who empowered them to speak. He wanted to teach Vallabha Bhaṭṭa that one cannot be qualified to speak anything about *kṛṣṇa*-*bhakti* unless he has also taken the dust of the lotus feet of such great Vaiṣṇavas.

**Lord Nityānanda Prabhu, the** *avadhūta***, is also directly the Supreme Personality of Godhead. He is always intoxicated with the madness of ecstatic love. Indeed, He is an ocean of love of Kṛṣṇa.**

**Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya perfectly knows the six philosophical theses. He is therefore the spiritual master of the entire world in teaching the six paths of philosophy. He is the best of devotees. Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya has shown Me the limit of devotional service. Only by his mercy have I understood that devotional service to Kṛṣṇa is the essence of all mystic yoga.**

**Śrīla Rāmānanda Rāya is the ultimate knower of the transcendental mellows of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s devotional service. He has instructed Me that Lord Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Through the mercy of Rāmānanda Rāya, I have understood that ecstatic love of Kṛṣṇa is the highest goal of life and that spontaneous love of Kṛṣṇa is the highest perfection.**

**The servant, friend, superior and conjugal lover are the shelters of the transcendental mellows called** *dāsya***,** *sakhya***,** *vātsalya* **and** *śṛṅgāra***. There are two kinds of emotion \[***bhāva***\]. Emotion with an understanding of the Lord’s full opulences is called** *aiśvarya-jñāna-yukta***, and pure, uncontaminated emotion is called** *kevalā***. One** **cannot achieve shelter at the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa, the son of Mahārāja Nanda, simply by knowing His opulences. (Cc.** *Antya* **7.20-26)**

Although the residents of Vṛndāvana are aware of the opulence of Kṛṣṇa, they do not consider it. It is described that there are two types of attraction to Kṛṣṇa—pure attachment that is free from all awe and reverence and attachment with awe and reverence. Pure attachment is found in Goloka Vṛndāvana. Attachment in which awe and reverence are prominent is found in Mathurā, Dvārakā, and Vaikuṇṭha. The difference is that the residents of Goloka Vṛndāvana, although they are aware of, or they experience, the opulence of the Lord, they do not consider it. The most important thing for them is their relationship with Kṛṣṇa. It is therefore described that by knowledge of the opulence of the Supreme Lord, love of Godhead is somewhat crippled.

According to *kevalā* devotion, even though a devotee sees the unlimited potency of Kṛṣṇa, he considers himself to be equal to Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda explains that *yoga-māyā* acts in such a way that the devotee will do anything to serve Kṛṣṇa better, even overstep Vedic injunctions and awe and reverence towards the Lord, because the Lord is most attracted to spontaneous devotional service.

*nāyaṁ sukhāpo bhagavān*  
*dehināṁ gopikā-sutaḥ*  
*jñānināṁ cātma-bhūtānāṁ*  
*yathā bhakti-matām iha*

**‘The Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa, the son of mother Yaśodā, is accessible to those devotees** **engaged in spontaneous loving service, but He is not as easily accessible to mental speculators, to those striving for self-realization by severe austerities and penances, or to those who consider the body the same as the self.’ (***Cc***.** *Antya* **7.27, quoting** *SB* **10.9.21)**

Knowledge of the opulence of the Lord is somewhat prominent in the transcendental relationships of neutrality and servitorship. Whereas, in the transcendental mellows of friendship, parental affection, and conjugal love, the knowledge of the opulence of the Lord is diminished. It is the knowledge of the opulence of the Lord that hampers and cripples one’s spontaneous service and affection to the Lord.

Similarly, an example of Vasudeva and Devakī is given in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta.* When Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma were fighting in the wrestling arena in Mathurā, Kṛṣṇa killed Kaṁsa. Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma came before Vasudeva and Devakī and released them from their shackles. Because Vasudeva and Devakī saw Kṛṣṇa kill Kaṁsa, they were seeing Him and Balarāma as the Supreme Personality of Godhead. When Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma came and bowed to their feet in respect, Vasudeva and Devakī were actually afraid. They did not want Them to offer this respect because they had a sense of awe and reverence, as well as fear in their hearts. Thus, that manifestation was there temporarily since their paternal affection had been crippled by the awe and reverence of seeing Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma as God.

The same thing is also true with Arjuna. Arjuna has a relationship of friendship with Kṛṣṇa, but he became very much afraid after seeing the universal form of Kṛṣṇa on the battlefield of Kurukṣetra. He said, “Please forgive me for my arrogance. In the past, I addressed You, ‘O Kṛṣṇa, O Yādava, O Mādhava, O my friend,’ without knowledge of Your glories. Please forgive me for whatever I have done either out of madness or out of love. I have offended You. Many times, we were sitting together or eating together. We were laying as friends together on the bedstead, alone and sometimes in front of many friends. Please excuse me for all my offenses.” Arjuna was afraid, and therefore there was reverence.

The Pāṇḍavas also had this kind of relationship with Kṛṣṇa. However, Kṛṣṇa behaved differently with each of them. He would treat Yudhiṣṭhira Mahārāja with reverence because he was elder. Kṛṣṇa would have friendly relationships with Arjuna and Bhīma. He would jokingly tap the heads of Nakula and Sahadeva, who would offer respect before His feet because they were younger. Nonetheless, they all had affectionate dealings with Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Thus, although Arjuna previously had this friendly relationship with Lord Kṛṣṇa, after seeing all His opulence, he was filled with fear. All he could say was, “Please forgive me. Whatever I did was out of madness or love. I do not know what I did, but it was wrong and I have offended You.” That is another example of how the opulence of the Lord cripples one’s love for Him; it inhibits what is very naturally spontaneous for a devotee who has an intimate relationship with the Lord in which the devotee thinks of Kṛṣṇa as equal.

In this way, Kṛṣṇa’s cowherd boyfriends think themselves to be equal with Kṛṣṇa. *Nectar of Devotion* describes that when Kṛṣṇa was holding Govardhana Hill and all Kṛṣṇa’s cowherd boyfriends were standing around Him, they were saying, “Kṛṣṇa, how long can You stand there holding up Govardhana Hill. You must be very tired! Why don’t You put the hill on Sudāmā’s hands, so that You can rest?” Thinking that they were equal, they thought that they should try to help their friend. They suggested, “We should put it on Sudāmā’s hand. Let him hold it for some time. Or if You do not do that, then why don’t You at least switch hands so we can massage Your hand that was raised. You must be very tired!” Thus, they are thinking of equal friendship with Kṛṣṇa, and not that He is God.

There are four types of friends that Kṛṣṇa has: well-wishers, friends, confidential friends, and intimate friends. Kṛṣṇa’s well-wishing friends are generally older than Kṛṣṇa, and they have a mood of wanting to protect Him. For example, when the Ariṣṭāsura demon came, Maṇḍalībhadra pulled out a sword and said, “I am going to protect Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa is younger and therefore needs someone to protect and take care of Him.”

*Nectar of Devotion* also describes how some of Kṛṣṇa’s friends who are equal to Him would go to His house in the morning before Kṛṣṇa was out of bed, and they would stand in front of the house waiting for Him to get up. Mother Yaśodā would see them out in front of the house and say, “Viśāla, come in. Come into the house.” While she was dressing Kṛṣṇa, Viśāla would come in and help mother Yaśodā dress Kṛṣṇa by putting on His ankle bells. While Viśāla was putting on His ankle bells, Kṛṣṇa would take His flute and hit him on the head. Mother Yaśodā would say, “Kṛṣṇa, why are You teasing Your friends in this way?!” They would laugh together. These are some of the friendly dealings that Kṛṣṇa would have with His friends.

One of Kṛṣṇa’s confidential friends is Madhumaṅgala. It is described that sometimes the cowherd boyfriends would get together to eat, and Madhumaṅgala would always eat more than everyone else. After eating everything he had, he would ask, “Kṛṣṇa, can You give me one *laḍḍu*? If You give me one of Your *laḍḍus*, I will give a very good recommendation for You to Your friend, Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, so that She will give You Her blessings.” He said, “I will also give You my blessings. I am a *brāhmaṇa;* You are a *vaiśya*. Therefore, I will give You my blessings so that You can get the blessings of Rādhārāṇī.” He would speak this way to Kṛṣṇa, not thinking of Him as God and not being disturbed by the opulence of God.

It is described that although the devotee may experience or may see the unlimited potency of the Lord, he still considers himself to be equal to Kṛṣṇa. This, of course, is all by the influence of *yoga-māyā*. This is the difference between *kevalā*-*bhakti* and *aiśvarya-jñāna-yukta*, as described in this twenty-sixth verse of the Seventh Chapter of *Antya-līlā*. “There are two kinds of emotion \[*bhāva*\]. Emotion with an understanding of the Lord’s full opulences is called *aiśvaryajñāna-yukta*, and pure, uncontaminated emotion is called *kevalā*. One cannot achieve shelter at the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa, the son of Mahārāja Nanda, simply by knowing His opulences.” *Aiśvarya-jñāna-yukta* is manifest in Mathurā, Dvārakā, and Vaikuṇṭha. There are a few nice verses that explain this point (*Cc*. *Madhya* 19.203–5):

In the stage of *kevalā* \[unalloyed devotion\] a devotee does not consider the unlimited opulence of Kṛṣṇa, even though he experiences it. He takes seriously only his own relationship with Kṛṣṇa. ‘When mother Yaśodā saw all the universes within Kṛṣṇa’s mouth, she was astonished for the time being. The Lord is worshiped like Indra and other demigods by the followers of the three *Vedas*, who offer Him sacrifices. He is worshiped as impersonal Brahman by saintly persons who understand His greatness through studying the *Upaniṣads*, as the *Puruṣa* by great philosophers who analytically study the universe, as the all-pervading Supersoul by great *yogīs*, and as the Supreme Personality of Godhead by devotees. Nevertheless, mother Yaśodā considered the Lord her own son. Although Kṛṣṇa is beyond sense perception and is unmanifest to human beings, he takes up the guise of a human being with a material body. Thus mother Yaśodā thought Him to be her son, and she bound Lord Kṛṣṇa with rope to a wooden mortar, as if He were an ordinary child.’

These are three verses on how the devotee takes seriously only his own relationship with the Lord.

‘When Kṛṣṇa was defeated by Śrīdāmā, He had to carry him on His shoulders. Similarly, Bhadrasena carried Vṛṣabha, and Pralamba carried Balarāma, the son of Rohiṇī.’ (*Cc*. *Madhya* 19.206)

How could a cowherd boy get on Kṛṣṇa’s shoulders unless he was thinking of Him as a friend? Who, with the understanding of the opulence of Kṛṣṇa, would jump on His shoulders? It is not possible. The following verses, which describe Rādhārāṇī, are also from the Tenth Canto of the *Bhāgavatam*:

My dearmost Kṛṣṇa, You are worshiping Me and giving up the company of all the other *gopīs*, who wanted to enjoy themselves with You.” Thinking like this, Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī considered Herself Kṛṣṇa’s most beloved *gopī*. She had become proud and had left the *rāsa-līlā* with Kṛṣṇa. In the deep forest She said, “My dear Kṛṣṇa, I cannot walk any more. You can take Me wherever You like.

When Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī petitioned Kṛṣṇa in this way, Kṛṣṇa said, “Just get up on My shoulders.” As soon as Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī began to do so, He disappeared. Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī then began to grieve over Her request and Kṛṣṇa’s disappearance. (*Cc*. *Madhya* 19.207-209)

Thus, Rādhārāṇī was speaking to Kṛṣṇa as His most beloved girlfriend.

“Dear Kṛṣṇa, we *gopīs* have neglected the order of our husbands, sons, family, brothers and friends and have left their company to come to You. You know everything about our desires. We have come only because we are attracted by the supreme music of Your flute. But You are a great cheater, for who else would give up the company of young girls like us in the dead of night?” (*Cc*. *Madhya* 19.210)

On the platform of neutrality and servitorship, knowledge of the opulence of the Supreme Lord is prominent. However, according to the verses of the seventh chapter of *Antya-līlā*, in friendship, parental affection, and conjugal love, knowledge of the opulence of the Lord is diminished. All these verses serve as examples for these three relationships of the Lord. The cowherd boys were getting on Kṛṣṇa’s shoulders; Radharani was requesting Kṛṣṇa to carry Her; the *gopīs* were chastising Kṛṣṇa; and mother Yaśodā was thinking of Kṛṣṇa as her son.

Thus, on the platform of *kevalā-bhakti*, one has feelings of either equality or superiority to Kṛṣṇa. However, when there is knowledge of the opulence of the Lord, there is always a presence of awe and reverence.

Sometimes, fear may even be present, as in the examples of Arjuna and Vasudeva and Devakī. Although Vasudeva and Devakī have a relationship of parental affection with Kṛṣṇa, sometimes they experience the Lord’s opulence. This pastime took place in Mathurā; however, in Vṛndāvana, Kṛṣṇa’s cowherd boyfriends and the *gopīs* do not consider any of the Lord’s opulence.

It is described in *Ādi-līlā* of *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* that Lord Caitanya appeared to distribute the spontaneous devotion of the residents of Vraja. That was explained in the chapter where it describes the causes for the Lord’s descent. Previously, Kṛṣṇa was thinking, “It has been a very, very long time since I have been worshiped in the mood of the residents of Vraja. Everybody is worshipping Me with great awe and reverence. Therefore, I will descend and disseminate the mood of the residents of Vṛndāvana.” That is *kevalā*-*bhakti—*pure, unalloyed, devotional service.

Rūpa Gosvāmī therefore describes that there is no one more merciful than Lord Caitanya, because He appeared to distribute *kevalā-bhakti,* which is rendered by the residents of Vṛndāvana. It is also described in the *Ādi-līlā* that by discussing these pastimes of spontaneous devotional service performed by the intimate associates of the Lord, and by hearing them in the association of devotees, one can awaken ecstatic love for the Lord on the spontaneous platform. Of course, before we can come to the spontaneous platform of devotional service, we must have the qualification (*adhikāra*).

One of the primary qualifications is *lobha,* or intense greed. In other words, one has to have such an intense greed for hearing the pastimes of the Lord. We often think of greed in a negative sense, but from a positive sense, greed is intense attachment to want to hear the pastimes of the Lord—so much so, that one is willing to sacrifice everything in order to hear. That is the manifestation of one’s intense desire to hear these pastimes. Without that *lobha*, it is not possible to come to the platform of spontaneous devotional service. Therefore, one of the primary purposes of the descent of Lord Caitanya is to promote the processes of *śravaṇam kīrtanaṁ,* hearing and chanting about the pastimes of the Lord, which is *saṅkīrtana*. *Saṅkīrtana* means glorification of the holy name, form, and pastimes of the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

Lord Caitanya descended primarily to distribute the holy name of the Lord. The more we chant the holy name of the Lord, according to our degree of faith, the more the holy name opens up like a flower. Within the holy name of the Lord are also the Lord’s quality, form, and pastimes. According to our intensity of faith, when we chant the holy names of the Lord, we can experience the Lord’s pastimes. If the Lord is non-different from His holy name, then certainly the holy name of the Lord has unlimited pastimes.

Lord Caitanya is thus speaking about this characteristic in reference to His relationship with Rāmānanda Rāya. He says to Vallabha Bhaṭṭa (Cc. *Antya* 7.24), “Through the mercy of Rāmānanda Rāya, I have understood that ecstatic love of Kṛṣṇa is the highest goal of life and that spontaneous love of Kṛṣṇa is the highest perfection.”

That spontaneous love is the love that we described of the cowherd boys, the *gopīs*, and those devotees who have parental affection towards Kṛṣṇa. Lord Caitanya said, “I am a Māyāvādī *sannyāsī,* but I learned all about these higher topics from Rāmānanda Rāya and Sarvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya.” Lord Caitanya continues to speak a little more about this.

**‘The Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa, the son of mother Yaśodā, is accessible to those devotees engaged in spontaneous loving service, but He is not as easily accessible to mental speculators, to those striving for self-realization by severe austerities and penances, or to those who consider the body the same as the self.’**

**The word** *‘ātma-bhūta’* **means ‘personal associates.’ Through the understanding of the Lord’s opulence, the goddess of fortune could not receive the shelter of Kṛṣṇa, the son of Nanda Mahārāja.” (Cc.** *Antya* **7.27-28)**

There is a long explanation given about this in the pastime between Lord Caitanya and Veṅkaṭa Bhaṭṭa when the Lord was traveling to South India. Veṅkaṭa Bhaṭṭa was in the Rāmānuja-sampradāya, and he was worshipping the Lord in the form of Lakṣmī-Nārāyaṇa.

Lord Caitanya once went to the house of Veṅkaṭa Bhaṭṭa. The Lord was very satisfied by his attitude, and they became very close during that time. However, Lord Caitanya saw that Veṅkaṭa Bhaṭṭa was worshiping Lakṣmī-Nārāyaṇa and therefore asked him, “Why is it that your worshipable goddess of fortune, Lakṣmīdevī, who resides on the chest of Nārāyaṇa and is the most chaste consort of Nārāyaṇa, would desire to associate with Kṛṣṇa? Being so chaste, why would She want to associate with Kṛṣṇa and not just stay with Nārāyaṇa?” In this joking way, Lord Caitanya spoke to Veṅkaṭa Bhaṭṭa.

Veṅkaṭa Bhaṭṭa explained, “Actually, Lord Nārāyaṇa and Lord Kṛṣṇa are the same person; therefore, it is certainly not unchaste for Lakṣmīdevī to associate with Kṛṣṇa. But Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes are more attractive than Nārāyaṇa’s pastimes because of their sportive nature. Therefore, Lakṣmīdevī wanted to associate with Kṛṣṇa in these sportive pastimes.”

Veṅkaṭa Bhaṭṭa had spoken correctly, and Lord Caitanya accepted it. However, Lord Caitanya said, “Yes, but according to scripture, Lakṣmīdevī was never allowed to associate with Kṛṣṇa in *rāsa-līlā* even though She performed very severe austerities to get that position. Can you explain the reason why?” Veṅkaṭa Bhaṭṭa replied, “I am an ordinary human being.

This subject is too deep for me. Only You can know why this did not happen. Can You please explain it to me?”

Lord Caitanya went on to give a very detailed explanation. He explained that one of the reasons is that although the goddess of fortune worships the Lord in great opulence, She wanted the association of Kṛṣṇa. The *gopīs* are not interested in the association of Nārāyaṇa.

Lord Caitanya gave the example of how Kṛṣṇa was once playing a trick on the *gopīs*. He manifested His four-armed form of Nārāyaṇa, and when the *gopīs* saw this form, their ecstatic love was diminished. They asked Nārāyaṇa, “Where is Kṛṣṇa? Where is Kṛṣṇa?” They were not at all interested in associating with Nārāyaṇa.

Thus, the goddess of fortune, who always resides on the chest of Nārāyaṇa, wanted the association of Kṛṣṇa. Therefore, Kṛṣṇa is certainly in the superior position. Lord Caitanya explained all of this to convince Veṅkaṭa Bhaṭṭa that his worship of Laksmī-Nārāyaṇa, although performed with great devotion, was performed with awe and reverence.

In the purport to this verse, Prabhupāda says:

Lakṣmī, the goddess of fortune, has complete knowledge of Kṛṣṇa’s opulences, but she could not achieve the association of Kṛṣṇa by dint of such knowledge. The devotees in Vṛndāvana, however, actually enjoy the association of Kṛṣṇa. (Cc. *Antya* 7.28)

**Antya-līlā: 7.29-44**

**Moscow—June 2, 2000**

Lord Caitanya continues to speak about what He learned from Rāmānanda Rāya.

**Text 29**

*nāyaṁ śriyo ’ṅga u nitānta-rateḥ prasādaḥ*  
*svar-yoṣitāṁ nalina-gandha-rucāṁ kuto ’nyāḥ*  
*rāsotsave ’sya bhuja-daṇḍa-gṛhīta-kaṇṭha*  
*labdhāśiṣāṁ ya udagād vraja-sundarīṇām*

**‘When Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa was dancing with the** ***gopīs*** **in the** ***rāsa-līlā*****, the** ***gopīs*** **were embraced around the neck by the Lord’s arms. This transcendental favor was never bestowed upon the goddess of fortune or the other consorts in the spiritual world. Nor was such a thing ever imagined by the most beautiful girls in the heavenly planets, girls whose bodily luster and aroma resemble the beauty and fragrance of lotus flowers. And what to speak of worldly women, who may be very, very beautiful according to material estimation?’**

**Text 30**

*śuddha-bhāve sakhā kare skandhe ārohaṇa*  
*śuddha-bhāve vrajeśvarī karena bandhana*

**In pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness, a friend mounts the shoulder of Kṛṣṇa, and mother Yaśodā binds the Lord.**

This verse describes parental affection and friendship, whereas verse twenty-nine described conjugal affection. Lord Caitanya is giving examples of these three particular *rasas* because He is explaining what Rāmānanda Rāya taught Him about the spontaneous loving dealings among the residents of Vṛndāvana and that it is perfection.

**Purport**

*Śuddha-bhāva*, pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness, is not dependent on an understanding of the Lord’s opulences. Even without such opulences, the devotee in *śuddha-bhāva* is inclined to love Kṛṣṇa as a friend or son.

**In pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness, without knowledge of the Lord’s opulences, a devotee considers Kṛṣṇa his friend or son. Therefore this devotional attitude is praised even by Śukadeva Gosvāmī and Vyāsadeva, the supreme authority. Neither those who are engaged in self-realization, appreciating the Brahman effulgence of the Lord, nor those engaged in devotional service while accepting the Supreme Personality of Godhead as master, nor those who are under the clutches of Māyā, thinking the Lord an ordinary person, can understand that certain exalted personalities, after accumulating volumes of pious activities, are now playing with the Lord in friendship as cowherd boys. (***Cc***.** *Antya* **7.31–32)**

These verses are serving as more evidence that the cowherd boyfriends of Kṛṣṇa are in a very exalted position. Moreover, the three types of persons described in the above verse (those who are attached to the worship of the impersonal Brahman; those who serve the Lord in awe and reverence; and those who are under the clutches of *māyā,* thinking that the Lord is an ordinary person) cannot understand the exalted position of the cowherd boys.

Sometimes the cowherd boys’ relationships are mixed with other *rasas* as well. One may have a relationship with Kṛṣṇa as a friend, but sometimes the servant attitude may be manifest. For example, some of the cowherd boyfriends massage Kṛṣṇa’s feet. Although they are friends, they also render some service. Therefore, fraternal affection is not always exclusively that relationship; servitorship is also present.

The example is given of the five elements: earth, air, fire, ether, and water. In each of them, there exists a certain quality. For instance, sound exists in ether. The quality in ether also exists in the other four elements; the qualities in air, sound and touch, also exist in fire, water, and earth. The qualities that exist in the subtler elements also exist in the grosser elements, but not vice versa. Similarly, the different relationships we can have with Kṛṣṇa are all manifest in the conjugal *rasa*.

**When mother Yaśodā saw all the universes within Kṛṣṇa’s mouth, she was astonished for the time being. The Lord is worshiped like Indra and other demigods by the followers of the three** *Vedas***, who offer Him sacrifices. He is worshiped as impersonal Brahman by saintly persons who understand His greatness through studying the Upaniṣads, as the** *puruṣa* **by great philosophers who analytically study the universe, as the all-pervading Supersoul by great** *yogīs***, and as the Supreme Personality of Godhead by devotees. Nevertheless, mother Yaśodā considered the Lord her own son. O** *brāhmaṇa***, what pious activities did Nanda Mahārāja perform to receive the Supreme Personality of Godhead Kṛṣṇa as his son? And what pious activities did mother Yaśodā perform that made the Absolute Supreme Personality of Godhead Kṛṣṇa call her ‘Mother’ and suck her breasts? Even if a pure devotee sees the opulence of Kṛṣṇa, he does not accept it. Therefore pure consciousness is more exalted than consciousness of the Lord’s opulence. Rāmānanda Rāya is extremely aware of transcendental mellows. He is incessantly absorbed in the happiness of ecstatic love of Kṛṣṇa. It is he who has taught Me all this. It is impossible to describe the influence and knowledge of Rāmānanda Rāya, for only by his mercy have I understood the unalloyed love of the residents of Vṛndāvana. (***Cc***.** *Antya* **7.33–37)**

Lord Caitanya has now finished explaining what He has learned from Rāmānanda Rāya. Thus far, He has mentioned Advaita Ācārya, Lord Nityānanda, Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya, and now Rāmānanda Rāya. Next, He will speak about what He has learned from Svarūpa Dāmodara.

From Rāmānanda Rāya, he learned about the spontaneous devotional service rendered by all the residents of Vraja. When Lord Caitanya asked Rāmānanda Rāya to explain to Him what the highest goal of life was, Rāmānanda Rāya went successively through all the stages and explained all the *rasas*. Of course, he culminated in the conjugal love that exists between Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Every time Rāmānanda Rāya explained a different stage, he substantiated it by *śāstra*. Lord Caitanya then asked Rāmānanda Rāya, “Could you please now explain something higher?” Rāmānanda Rāya, at that particular point, said that it was not possible to explain anything higher than that which he had already explained. He said that there was no *śāstric* evidence for anything higher. Lord Caitanya persisted, “You know something higher! Please tell Me.”

Rāmānanda Rāya replied, “If I say it, You will not be pleased. You will only be pleased if You give me the words to say it. Only by Your potency will I be able to say something higher.” Rāmānanda Rāya then speaking completely from the heart, without using *śāstra,* revealed his highest realization: Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa had combined together and had manifested before him in the form of Śrī Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu.

Lord Caitanya fully accepted his statements because they were completely from his realization. Lord Caitanya then revealed His special form, which Rāmānanda Rāya had never seen before. He showed him how Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa were combining in His form. Rāmānanda Rāya then fainted in ecstasy because he had never seen this before. He had seen Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and he had seen Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. (Rāmānanda Rāya is actually Viśākhā-devī, so he had seen Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.) However, he had never seen how Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa had combined into the form of Lord Caitanya.

It is described that there are three types of union: *bhāvimilana*, *bhūta-milana*, and *bhavan-milana*. These words mean acute, more acute, and most acute. *Bhāvi-milana* describes how Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa are in the forest. They are there together, but have not united. *Bhūta-milana* describes how Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa combined in the form of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. In other words, it is the form of Śrī Caitanya that is the united form of Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. However, *bhavan-milana* is actually the union—how Kṛṣṇa entered the heart and manifested the form of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. That is what Rāmānanda Rāya experienced when Lord Caitanya manifested this form before him, causing him to faint in ecstasy.

Just as there are three types of union, there are also three types of separation: *bhāvi-viraha, bhūta-viraha*, and *bhavanviraha*. This is explained by Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī. *Bhāvi-viraha* means the type of separation the *gopīs* are experiencing in anticipation of Kṛṣṇa leaving Vṛndāvana when Akrūra comes to take Him away. At that point, Akrūra is in Vṛndāvana; he has not taken Kṛṣṇa away yet. However, the *gopīs* know why he is there. They know that Kṛṣṇa is going to leave. Just the very thought of Kṛṣṇa leaving, immediately evokes separation in their hearts. That is acute separation.

Then, “more acute separation” is when Akrūra has taken Kṛṣṇa. He has now left, and each day that Kṛṣṇa is away from Vṛndāvana, the *gopīs* are thinking about His absence. Their separation is increasing more and more. That is called *bhūtaviraha*.

The most acute separation is *bhavan-viraha*, which is when Akrūra is there and Kṛṣṇa is in the chariot. The *gopīs* are seeing Kṛṣṇa leaving with Akrūra. This is the most acute feeling of separation that is manifested. What did they do when they saw that? They fell down in front of the cart so that its wheels would have to roll over them. They were feeling such intense anxiety: “No, Kṛṣṇa cannot leave! We will give up our bodies. How is it possible? He is the source of our life and breath.”

These feelings of separation are, of course, the most intense feelings of ecstasy. It is described that the *gopīs* manifest these highest feelings of separation. Śrīla Prabhupāda very nicely explains this (*Cc*. *Ādi* 4.31):

**Purport**

(…) The spontaneous attraction of Śrī Kṛṣṇa for His dearest parts and parcels generates an enthusiasm that obliges Śrī Kṛṣṇa and the *gopīs* to meet together. To celebrate this transcendental enthusiasm, there is need of a sentiment of separation between the lover and beloved. In the condition of material tribulation, no one wants the pangs of separation. But in the transcendental form, the very same separation, being absolute in its nature, strengthens the ties of love and enhances the desire of the lover and beloved to meet. The period of separation, evaluated transcendentally, is more relishable than the actual meeting, which lacks the feelings of increasing anticipation because the lover and beloved are both present.

These points are all relevant because we are approaching the section in *Antya-līlā* where Lord Caitanya is speaking about how Svarūpa Dāmodara taught Him about the feelings of separation manifested by the *gopīs*. Prabhupāda says, “There is a need of a sentiment of separation in order to celebrate this enthusiasm, because it obliges them to meet.” In other words, what is it that increases the need to meet? It is the separation. Without separation, where is the intensity of meeting? Thus, the *gopīs* were feeling intense separation when Kṛṣṇa left Vṛndāvana.

Kṛṣṇa personally sent Uddhava to Vṛndāvana to witness the feelings of love that the residents of Vṛndāvana had for Him. Kṛṣṇa sent Uddhava with a message to bring to the residents of Vṛndāvana. When Uddhava appeared in Vṛndāvana, the cowherd boys first greeted him. Uddhava then went to Nanda Mahārāja and mother Yaśodā, and he saw the intense feelings that they had for Kṛṣṇa. When he came to the *gopīs,* he saw how much intense separation they were feeling. Uddhava offered a prayer, asking that he become a blade of grass in Vṛndāvana so that these devotees could step on him continuously. He had never seen such intense love as the love that was manifest in the *gopīs—*especially in Rādhārani.

When he gave Kṛṣṇa’s message to Rādhārani to read, She was looking at it but could not read it because Her tears were wetting the paper so nothing could be read. Then, when Rādhārani was trying to write a message for Uddhava to bring back to Kṛṣṇa, She could not do it. She could not write. She was blinded by tears. Uddhava was overwhelmed by seeing these feelings that Rādhārani was manifesting. He said, “I have to make Kṛṣṇa come back!” He then vowed, “I promise to bring Kṛṣṇa back to Vṛndāvana.”

Uddhava told Rādhārani, “I will personally ask on Your behalf.” When Rādhārani heard that, She was immediately relieved. She did not even know how She would write anything to Kṛṣṇa.

When Uddhava returned to Kṛṣṇa, he begged, “You have to uphold my promise. I made a promise that You will go back; therefore You must go back.” Of course, as explained previously, Kṛṣṇa will always uphold the promise of His devotees. Thus, Kṛṣṇa went back to Vṛndāvana, and He gave life to the *gopīs*.

Lord Caitanya is going to speak about the transcendental mellow of conjugal love, which He learned from Svarūpa Dāmodara.

**The transcendental mellows of ecstatic love are personified by Svarūpa Dāmodara. By his association I have understood Vṛndāvana’s transcendental mellow of conjugal love. The unalloyed love of the** *gopīs* **and Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī is without any trace of material lust. The criterion of such transcendental love is that its only purpose is to satisfy Kṛṣṇa. ‘O dearly beloved! Your lotus feet are so soft that we place them gently on our breasts, fearing that Your feet will be hurt. Our lives rest only in You. Our minds, therefore, are filled with anxiety that Your tender feet might be wounded by pebbles as You roam about on the forest path.’ Obsessed with pure love, without knowledge of opulences, the** *gopīs* **sometimes chastise Kṛṣṇa. That is a symptom of pure ecstatic love. (***Cc***.** *Antya* **7.38–41)**

There is also a verse wherein the *gopīs* call Kṛṣṇa a cheater. They came to Him in the middle of the night, giving up everything. Some *gopīs* were cooking for their husbands, some feeding their children, some getting dressed, and some putting on their makeup; however, when they heard the sound of Kṛṣṇa’s flute, they all dropped whatever they were doing to go meet Lord Kṛṣṇa. Even when Kṛṣṇa goes to the pasture with the cowherd boys during the day, the *gopīs* are feeling separation from Kṛṣṇa. They always have to try to hide their feelings because they are married. How could married women enter into the forest and leave their families? How could they act contrary to religious principles? Even when the *gopīs* are talking amongst themselves, they have to talk in a very covert way. They cannot even speak about Kṛṣṇa directly, fearing their husbands or somebody else may hear. These thoughts are always in their hearts. That is the characteristic of the *parakīya-bhāva*.

It was earlier explained that *yoga-māyā* creates a situation in which the devotees are prepared to do anything to please Kṛṣṇa more and more. *Yoga-māyā* therefore makes this arrangement where the *gopīs* appear to be married but leave their husbands to go meet Kṛṣṇa in the middle of the night. However, it is explained here that there is no trace of lust in their activities. Their only meditation is pleasing Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa derives great pleasure from this unalloyed devotional service.

Thus, to please the Lord, devotees want to appear before Him so that He can see them. When the *gopīs* heard the sound of Kṛṣṇa’s flute, they were not thinking that they wanted to go and enjoy Kṛṣṇa. They never think that way. They are always wanting to be enjoyed by Kṛṣṇa. Therefore, since everything they do is for the pleasure of the Lord, they sacrificed everything to go to the Lord when He would call them by the transcendental sound of His flute. The *gopīs* heard this transcendental flute entering from the forest and could no longer continue with their household duties. They left and went into the forest, making great personal sacrifices to satisfy the Lord.

**The conjugal love of the** *gopīs* **is the most exalted devotional service, surpassing all other methods of** *bhakti***. Therefore Lord Kṛṣṇa is obliged to say, ‘My dear** *gopīs***, I cannot repay you. Indeed, I am always indebted to you. O** *gopīs***, I am not able to repay My debt for your spotless service, even within a lifetime of Brahmā. Your connection with Me is beyond reproach. You have worshiped Me, cutting off all domestic ties, which are difficult to break. Therefore please let your own glorious deeds be your compensation.’ (***Cc***.** *Antya* **7.43-44)**

When the *gopīs* appeared before Kṛṣṇa in the forest, He first tested them, saying, “What are you doing here in the forest in the dead of night? Do you not know that it is very dangerous? You should not have come into the forest like this.” He then started explaining the duties of a wife, “According to *śāstra*, it is the duty of the wife to serve the husband. She must be very faithful and chaste. She must even serve the family members of the husband, and most importantly, she must serve her child. This is the conclusion of scripture. Why have you come here in the middle of the night? You should go back and serve your family.”

The *gopīs* then became very angry. “O Kṛṣṇa, how can You do this to us?” They became very disturbed and were chastising Him—as in the previous verse (*Cc. Antya* 7.42):

**O dear Kṛṣṇa, we** *gopīs* **have neglected the order of our husbands, sons, family, brothers and friends and have left their company to come to You. You know everything about our desires. We have come only because we are attracted by the supreme music of Your flute. But You are a great cheater, for who else would give up the company of young girls like us in the dead of night?**

Kṛṣṇa was, of course, testing the *gopīs*, and the *gopīs* refused to go back home. They could not go back. They said, “We are *abalā*. We have no strength. Our feet will not take us back.” When Kṛṣṇa saw the intensity of the unrestricted love they had for Him, He became bound by them.

This is also very nicely described by Śrīla Prabhupāda in the *Kṛṣṇa* book when the *gopīs* asked Kṛṣṇa:

In dealings between lovers, we find that there are three classes of men. One class simply receives, another class reciprocates favorably, even if the lover is very contrary, and the third class neither acts contrary nor answers favorably in dealings of love. So out of these three classes, which do You prefer, or which do You call honest? (*KB* 32)

The *gopīs* are trying to find out what Kṛṣṇa is thinking. Thus, they are saying that there are three types of lovers, and the first type is the person who simply takes. Kṛṣṇa says in response, “\[Persons\] who simply reciprocate the loving dealings with the other party are just like merchants.” (*KB* 32) In other words, if somebody does something for them, they do something in return. They give in loving affairs as much as they get from the other party. Practically, there is no question of love. “It is simply self-interested or self-centered business.” (*KB* 32) He says that you cannot even call it love; it is just a business exchange. The symptom of love is that it is unmotivated and uninterrupted. Even if the beloved acts in a contrary way, one still continues to love. However, if somebody serves the beloved based on proportional reciprocation, this is not real love. Kṛṣṇa goes on to explain:

Better than the first class is the second class of men, who love in spite of the opposite party’s contrariness. Such sincere love can be seen when the father and mother love their children in spite of their children’s neglect. The third class neither reciprocates nor neglects. They can be further divided into two classes. One is the self-satisfied, who do not require anyone’s love. They are called *ātmārāma*, which means they are absorbed in the thought of the Supreme Personality of Godhead and so do not care whether one loves them or not. But another class are ungrateful men. They are called callous. The men in this group revolt against superior persons. For instance, a son, in spite of receiving all kinds of things from loving parents, may be callous and not reciprocate. Those in this class are generally known as *guru-druhaḥ*, which means they receive favors from the parents or the spiritual master and yet neglect them.

Kṛṣṇa indirectly answered the questions of the *gopīs*, even those questions which implied that Kṛṣṇa did not properly reciprocate their dealings. (*KB* 32)

The *gopīs* were asking this question because they wanted to know why Kṛṣṇa was not reciprocating. They wanted to hear what Kṛṣṇa had to say about their having come, sacrificing everything, and entering into the forest. In answer, Kṛṣṇa said that He, as the Supreme Personality of Godhead, is selfsatisfied. He does not require anyone’s love, but at the same time He said that He is not ungrateful. (*KB* 32)

Thus, although Kṛṣṇa does not require it, He is never ungrateful for loving service rendered to Him. He continues His explanations:

You might be aggrieved by My words and acts, but you must know that sometimes I do not reciprocate My devotees’ dealings with Me. My devotees are very much attached to Me, but sometimes I do not reciprocate their feelings properly in order to increase their love for Me more and more. If I can very easily be approached by them, they might think, ‘Kṛṣṇa is so easily available.’ So sometimes I do not respond...My dear friends, do not think for a moment that I have been dealing with you as I do with ordinary devotees. I know what you are. You have forsaken all kinds of social and religious obligations; you have given up all connection with your parents. Without caring for social convention and religious obligations, you have come to Me and loved Me, and I am so much obliged to you that I cannot treat you as ordinary devotees. Do not think that I was away from you. I was near to you. I was simply seeing how much you were anxious for Me in My absence. (*KB* 32)

Kṛṣṇa is speaking to the *gopīs,* who had been looking for Him while He had left them for some time. The *gopīs* were feeling such intense feelings of separation that they saw Kṛṣṇa everywhere. Everything reminded them of Kṛṣṇa. When they saw a creeper, plant, or peacock, these things made them think of Kṛṣṇa. Nevertheless, they were looking: “Where is Kṛṣṇa? Why does Kṛṣṇa not reciprocate?”

‘I was near to you. I was simply seeing how much you were anxious for Me in My absence. So please do not try to find fault with Me. Because you consider Me so dear to you, kindly excuse Me if I have done anything wrong. I cannot repay your continuous love for Me, even throughout the lifetimes of the demigods in the heavenly planets. It is impossible to repay you or show enough gratitude for your love; therefore please be satisfied by your own pious activities. You have displayed exemplary attraction for Me, overcoming the greatest difficulties arising from family connections. Please be satisfied with your highly exemplary character, for it is not possible for Me to repay My debt to you.’

The exemplary character of devotional service manifested by the devotees of Vṛndāvana is the purest type of devotion. It is enjoined in authoritative *śāstras* that devotional service must be *ahaitukī* and *apratihatā*. This means that devotional service to Kṛṣṇa cannot be checked by political or religious convention. The stage of devotional service is always transcendental. The *gopīs* particularly showed pure devotional service towards Kṛṣṇa, so much so that Kṛṣṇa Himself remained indebted to them. Lord Caitanya thus said that the devotional service manifested by the *gopīs* in Vṛndāvana excelled all other methods of approaching the Supreme Personality of Godhead. (*KB* 32)

Kṛṣṇa says, “A person who is *ātmārāma*, self-satisfied, does not reciprocate. Nevertheless, I am never ungrateful.”

Never ungrateful” means that Kṛṣṇa feels that He has a debt that He can never repay to those devotees who worship Him with such intense love. What will He give in return? He said, “Sometimes it may appear that I am not reciprocating, but this is increasing your love for Me. You were thinking that I was far away, but I was always near. I was never far away. I did this to increase your love for Me.

Kṛṣṇa can never forget the devotee who renders service to Him with love. The devotee, feeling intense separation, thinks, “Where is Kṛṣṇa?” Prabhupāda explains that separation is there to enhance the union. Without separation, how can there be the celebration of union? What is the meaning of union, without separation? Therefore, Kṛṣṇa creates these feelings of separation. Of course, it is all arranged by His internal potency, which acts very perfectly according to the divine will of the Lord. Thus, the Lord is never ungrateful towards those who sacrifice to render service.

Lord Caitanya describes that the love manifested by the *gopīs* is the highest love, because the *gopīs* will always serve and love the Lord, no matter how the Lord deals with them. Even though the Lord does not reciprocate, it does not make any difference to them.
