Lord Caitanya exemplified this in His *Śikṣāṣṭaka* prayers, “You may handle Me roughly by Your embrace or make Me broken-hearted by not being present before Me, but You are always My worshipful Lord unconditionally.” This is the highest form of love—not the type of love that Kṛṣṇa explains is like a merchant—“Only if I get something back will I act in a favorable way and serve.”

Thus, Kṛṣṇa, in His dealings with His devotees, sometimes acts very mercifully towards them and does not reciprocate with their endeavors. He does so to increase the devotees’ love and to get them off the platform of business. Kṛṣṇa says that if one thinks, “I will only do this if I get this…” it is not love.

He says, “If you render some service, I will never be ungrateful for the service rendered.” How will He reciprocate? That is Kṛṣṇa’s choice. He knows best how to reciprocate because He knows how to increase the love of His devotee. Therefore, Lord Caitanya is pointing to the *gopīs* because the *gopīs* love Kṛṣṇa without conditions. They are ready to sacrifice everything; and, even after giving up everything, Kṛṣṇa tells them to go back home, and He disappears. What do they do? They still love Kṛṣṇa; Kṛṣṇa can never be separated from their hearts. They never reject Him.

That is why Lord Caitanya always speaks of the *gopīs’* love for Kṛṣṇa as the highest platform of devotional service. Here, Lord Caitanya is explaining that that is what He has learned from Svarūpa Dāmodara—the conjugal relationships of the *gopīs*.

Lord Caitanya has now finished His explanation about what He learned from Svarūpa Dāmodara. He is now going to speak about Uddhava, who, as was previously explained, desired to take on his head the dust of the *gopīs’* lotus feet because he saw such intense love manifest in the *gopīs*.

## ŚRĪ CAITANYA MAHĀPRABHU ACCEPTS PRASĀDAM FROM HIS DEVOTEES

**Antya-līlā: 10.1–8**

**Kishinev—June 29, 2000**

**Text 1**

*vande śrī-kṛṣṇa-caitanyaṁ*  
*bhaktānugraha-kātaram*  
*yena kenāpi santuṣṭaṁ*  
*bhakta-dattena śraddhayā*

**Let me offer my respectful obeisances unto Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, who is always pleased to accept anything given with faith and love by His devotees and is always ready to bestow mercy upon them.**

**Text 2**

*jaya jaya gauracandra jaya nityānanda*  
*jayādvaita-candra jaya gaura-bhakta-vṛnda*

**All glories to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu! All glories to Lord Nityānanda Prabhu! All glories to Advaitacandra! And all glories to all the devotees of Lord Caitanya!**

**Text 3**

*varṣāntare saba bhakta prabhure dekhite*  
*parama-ānande sabe nīlācala yāite*

**The next year, all the devotees were very pleased to go to Jagannātha Purī \[Nīlācala\] to see Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.**

Every year, all the devotees from Śāntipura and Navadvīpa would get together and travel to Purī under the arrangements of Śrī Śivānanda Sena. They would go to see Lord Caitanya, whom they were very eager to associate with.

After Lord Caitanya accepted *sannyāsa*, He remained in Jagannātha Purī, because He could not return to Navadvīpa having previously lived there as a householder. By the request of Śacīmātā, Lord Caitanya remained in Purī so that she could regularly hear news of His activities.

Therefore, each year, devotees from Navadvīpa, Śāntipura, and many other places in that area of Bengal would go to see Lord Caitanya. This verse describes how the devotees were very pleased to go to Purī and see the Lord. One year Lord Caitanya had instructed them not to come, but traditionally they would go every year.

**Text 4**

*advaitācārya-gosāñi—sarva-agra-gaṇya*  
*ācāryaratna, ācāryanidhi, śrīvāsa ādi dhanya*

**Advaita Ācārya Gosāñi led the party from Bengal. He was followed by Ācāryaratna, Ācāryanidhi, Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura and other glorious devotees.**

**Text 5**

*yadyapi prabhura ājñā gauḍe rahite*  
*tathāpi nityānanda preme calilā dekhite*

**Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu had ordered Lord Nityānanda to stay in Bengal, but nevertheless, because of ecstatic love, Lord Nityānanda also went to see Him.**

**Text 6**

*anurāgera lakṣaṇa ei,—‘vidhi’ nāhi māne*  
*tāṅra ājñā bhāṅge tāṅra saṅgera kāraṇe*

**Indeed, it is a symptom of real affection that one breaks the order of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, not caring for the regulative principles, to associate with Him.**

Sometimes a devotee who is situated on the platform of ecstatic love of the Lord will behave in a way that is contrary to regulative principles, even sometimes contrary to the direct instruction of the Lord. On the platform of ecstatic love, a devotee is only thinking of the pleasure of the Lord. Sometimes the Lord may give a particular order, but the devotee, understanding the heart of the Lord, may act contrary to fulfill His actual desire.

Lord Caitanya had previously said, “Listen, magnanimous Nityānanda, You should go immediately to Navadvīpa. I promised that I would drown everyone, including fools and sinners, in the ocean of *kṛṣṇa*-*prema*. If You remain here with Me as an ascetic, keeping a vow of silence and concealing Your enthusiasm, who will deliver the fallen souls of this world? You are the bestower of love and devotion. Please tell Me, if You must always check yourself, what is the use of Your incarnation? If You want to make My words true, go to Bengal at once. Deliver everyone by giving *prema*-*bhakti* to all the fallen, foolish, lowcaste, and distressed people. You should not return here to see Me in Purī, but should remain in Bengal. In intervals I will come to see You, although I will remain invisible.”

Lord Caitanya would go to wherever Lord Nityānanda was dancing. There were four places where the Lord would always be present—in the temple room of Śacīmātā, wherever Lord Nityānanda danced, in the house of Rāghava Paṇḍita, and in the *kīrtana* hall of the house of Śrīvasa Paṇḍita.

It is explained in the *Caitanya-bhāgavata* that Lord Nityānanda did not begin His distribution of love of Godhead until He was given the order by Lord Caitanya. Until that time, Lord Nityānanda had not profusely distributed *kṛṣṇa-prema*.

Vṛndāvana dāsa Ṭhākura explains that He did not want to do it independent from the order of the Lord. Therefore, although Lord Nityānanda traveled for twelve years to different places of pilgrimage, He did not distribute love of Godhead. He waited until He met Lord Caitanya in Navadvīpa. After meeting the Lord and receiving the instruction to go out and preach, He began His widespread dissemination of love of Godhead.

It is also described that His widespread distribution began at the house of Rāghava Paṇḍita. During His travels to Bengal, Lord Nityānanda first stopped at Rāghava Paṇḍita’s home. There, Lord Nityānanda would bestow His merciful glance on others, and they would begin to experience ecstatic symptoms of love of God, rolling on the ground in complete ecstatic love.

It was also there that Lord Caitanya first appeared invisibly. All the devotees were engaged in *kīrtana* at the home of Rāghava Paṇḍita, and they smelled a very wonderful fragrance. It was very strong, pervading everywhere. All the devotees were wondering, “What is this fragrance?”

Lord Nityānanda told them, “What you smell is the fragrance of the flowers of the garland around Lord Caitanya, called the *damanaka* flower. It is a very fragrant flower.”

At that moment, Lord Caitanya appeared, sitting in the tree in the courtyard of Rāghava Paṇḍita’s home. Lord Nityānanda pointed to Him, but the devotees could not see Him. However, they could understand His presence by the very fragrant smell of the garland.

Thus, although Lord Nityānanda was given the order to remain in Bengal, He could not contain Himself, and was very eager to go to Purī and associate with Lord Caitanya. Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja describes that this is the symptom of real affection—breaking the order of the Supreme Lord, not caring for the regulative principles, in order to associate with Him.

Another example of this is that of Mahārāja Pratāparudra \[from *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Madhya-līlā*, Chapters Thirteen and Fourteen\]. Mahārāja Pratāparudra was very anxious to associate with Lord Caitanya. He was situated on the platform of deep love for the Lord. Sārvabhauma Bhattācārya petitioned Lord Caitanya on behalf of Mahārāja Pratāparudra, but was very careful because he knew that Lord Caitanya would resist. First he asked Lord Caitanya, “May I ask you a question, and do You promise that You will not overreact to my question?”

Lord Caitanya said, “Okay, you may ask Me a question.” Sārvabhauma Bhattācārya began explaining that there is a great king, Mahārāja Pratāparudra, who is very anxious to have His *darśana*. Lord Caitanya told him, “Please do not ask that question again. If you do, I will leave Jagannātha Purī.” So Sārvabhauma Bhattācārya immediately became very quiet, and he decided that he should not ask anything further of Lord Caitanya.

Lord Caitanya explained His reasons for refusing to externally associate with Mahārāja Pratāparudra. He said, “It is very dangerous for a *sannyāsī* to associate with a king, because the king is always engaged in social affairs and dealing with large quantities of money. Therefore, a *sannyāsī* should not intimately associate with a king. All the people will speak about it, and his reputation will be destroyed. If the *sannyāsī’s* reputation is destroyed, then how can he preach? If the *sannyāsī* cannot preach, then what is the meaning of his existence?”

Lord Caitanya was so strict that He would not associate with Mahārāja Pratāparudra, even though he was a great devotee of the Lord. Because Lord Caitanya took this very strong position, Sārvabhauma Bhattācārya felt that he could no longer request Lord Caitanya to meet the king.

Soon after, Mahārāja Pratāparudra returned to Orissa from South India with Rāmānanda Rāya, his minister. Rāmānanda Rāya went to see Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. He told Lord Caitanya how Mahārāja Pratāparudra immediately felt ecstatic love simply by hearing Lord Caitanya’s name. He described all the characteristics of Mahārāja Pratāparudra’s deep affection for the Lord, but did not ask Him to give *darśana* to Mahārāja Pratāparudra. He just described the king’s qualities.

He then explained that he requested Mahārāja Pratāparudra to relieve him of all his ministerial duties so he could associate with Lord Caitanya. When Mahārāja Pratāparudra heard this request from Rāmānanda Rāya, he immediately granted his wish. Not only did he relieve Rāmānanda Rāya from all his ministerial duties, but he also gave him a full pension for life so he could completely dedicate himself to the lotus feet of Lord Caitanya.

When Lord Caitanya heard this from Rāmānanda Rāya, He was very pleased. He said, “Because Mahārāja Pratāparudra has rendered service to you in this way, certainly he will get the mercy of Lord Viṣṇu.” Although He indicated that Mahārāja Pratāparudra would someday get this mercy, He still maintained a very strict position externally, and whenever asked for a meeting, He would refuse.

Later, several devotees decided to approach the Lord together. They told Lord Caitanya about Mahārāja Pratāparudra’s deep love for Him, and that if he did not get His association, he would give up his life.

Mahārāja Pratāparudra thought, “What is the value of my kingdom? What is the value of all my wealth? What is the value of my body, if I cannot achieve the *darśana* of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu? How is it that I am so unfortunate? Śrī Caitanya has come to deliver everyone in this world, but He is neglecting a king named Mahārāja Pratāparudra? How can I go on living?” Even after hearing all this, Lord Caitanya still refused to give him *darśana*.

Mahārāja Pratāparudra approached Sārvabhauma Bhattācārya and asked him, “Did you speak to Lord Caitanya? What did He say?”

Sārvabhauma Bhattācārya explained, “Yes, we approached Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, but He refused. He will not grant you *darśana*.”

Mahārāja Pratāparudra began speaking about his uselessness and the futility of his existence if he could not get the *darśana* of the Lord. Sārvabhauma Bhattācārya could understand that the king had very deep love for the Lord. He also knew that Lord Caitanya was very merciful. Even though Lord Caitanya was saying, “No, do not ask Me again,” Mahārāja Pratāparudra was always asking.

Sārvabhauma Bhattācārya told Mahārāja Pratāparudra how he could get the association of the Lord, “When Lord Caitanya attends the Ratha-yātrā festival, He will dance in complete ecstasy. After dancing, He will enter the Guṇḍicā gardens in ecstatic love, and there He will lie down to take rest. At that time you should go to the gardens, having removed all your dress of a king, and you should approach His lotus feet. Begin reciting verses from the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* of the prayers by the *gopīs*. When Lord Caitanya hears these verses coming from your mouth, He will immediately recognize you as a great devotee, and certainly He will embrace you.”

Although the Lord had continually been refusing, Sārvabhauma Bhattācārya acted contrary to the Lord’s instructions because he was on the platform of ecstatic love for the Lord. The Lord greatly relishes and derives His greatest pleasure from associating with His devotees who are situated on the platform of ecstatic love.

After the Ratha-yātrā festival, Lord Caitanya went into the Guṇḍicā gardens and was lying there in trance. Mahārāja Pratāparudra abandoned his royal dress and went into the garden. He went before the lotus feet of the Lord and began reciting many verses from the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* ending with this verse spoken by the *gopīs* (*SB* 10.31.9):

*tava kathāmṛtaṁ tapta-jīvanaṁ*  
*kavibhir īḍitaṁ kalmaṣāpaham*  
*śravaṇa-maṅgalaṁ śrīmad ātataṁ*  
*bhuvi gṛṇanti ye bhūri-dā janāḥ*

“The nectar of Your words and the descriptions of Your activities are the life and soul of those suffering in this material world. These narrations, transmitted by learned sages, eradicate one’s sinful reactions and bestow good fortune upon whoever hears them. These narrations are broadcast all over the world and are filled with spiritual power. Certainly those who spread the message of Godhead are most munificent.”

When Lord Caitanya heard this verse, immediately He got up, still not in external consciousness. Compelled by hearing this verse, He said, “You are the most munificent. You are the most munificent. You are the most munificent.” He kept repeating it again and again. Then He embraced Mahārāja Pratāparudra and began crying. Mahārāja Pratāparudra also began to cry, tears profusely flowing from his eyes. In this way, Mahārāja Pratāparudra got the fulfillment of his desire, and Lord Caitanya also was pleased and satisfied to associate with His very dear and intimate devotee.

This thus exhibits how a devotee may act contrary to the order of the Lord to associate with Him.

Another example in *Caitanya*-*caritāmṛta* (*Madhya-līlā*, Chapter Sixteen) is that of Gadādhara Paṇḍita, who had taken a vow of *kṣetra-sannyāsa*, meaning that he had to stay in one place.

There are *sannyāsīs* who are *parivrajakācāryas* and *paramahaṁsas*, who travel and preach, but a *kṣetra sannyāsī* remains in one place, accepting duties in that particular place. Gadādhara Paṇḍita accepted the duty of worshipping the Gopīnātha Deity, but he wanted to give up his duties to travel with Lord Caitanya.

Lord Caitanya was leaving Jagannātha Purī, very anxious to go to Vṛndāvana. He had been trying to leave for two years but was unsuccessful in obtaining the permission of His devotees, such as Rāmānanda Rāya and Sārvabhauma Bhattācārya.

After hearing excuses for two years for Him to remain in Purī, finally Lord Caitanya became quite insistent, and they then considered that what they had been doing was not right, and thus gave Him permission to leave. The following morning, Lord Caitanya left.

The Lord first stopped in the city of Caturdvāra. Many of the devotees accompanied the Lord on His travels to this place because they did not want to give up His association, not being able to bear separation from Him. When the Lord had to cross the river in a boat, Gadādhara Paṇḍita started to go with the Lord.

“\[But\] he was forbidden to \[go\] and was asked not to give up the vow of *kṣetra-sannyāsa*. When he was requested to return to Jagannātha Purī, Gadādhara Paṇḍita told the Lord, “Wherever You are staying is Jagannātha Purī. Let my so-called *kṣetra-sannyāsa* go to hell.” When Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu asked Gadādhara Paṇḍita to remain at Jagannātha Purī and engage in Gopīnātha’s service, Gadādhara Paṇḍita replied, “One renders service to Gopīnātha a million times simply by seeing Your lotus feet.”

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu then said, “If you abandon His service, it will be My fault. It is better that you remain here and render service. That will be My satisfaction.”

The Paṇḍita replied, “Do not worry. All the faults will be on my head. I shall not accompany You but shall go alone. I shall go to see Śacīmātā, but I shall not go for Your sake. I shall be responsible for the abandoning of my vow and service to Gopīnātha.”

Thus Gadādhara Paṇḍita Gosvāmī traveled alone, but when they all arrived at Kaṭaka, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu called him, and he went into the Lord’s company. No one can understand the loving intimacy between Gadādhara Paṇḍita and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Gadādhara Paṇḍita gave up his vow and service to Gopīnātha just as one gives up a piece of straw.” (*Cc. Madhya* 16.130–137)

**Purport**

Just to get Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s association, Gadādhara Paṇḍita gave up his life’s vow to engage in Gopīnātha’s service. This kind of loving affection can be understood only by very confidential devotees. Ordinarily, no one can understand its purport. (*Cc. Madhya* 16.137)

This kind of loving affection is manifest by those intimate devotees who are on the platform of ecstatic love. When one hears these different examples of the pastimes of the Supreme Lord in ecstatic love—how the devotees act in a contrary way—one should never think that such a platform can be imitated. This ecstatic love manifest by the devotees is always in relation to the Supreme Lord’s desires. It is not that we can give up our duty or a superior order for some other purpose and say that it is in the name of love for the Supreme Lord.

There are instances of devotees who neglected the order of their spiritual master. For example, Rāmānujācārya was instructed by Goṣṭhīpūrṇa not to give out the confidential *mantra* that he had given to him. Goṣṭhīpūrṇa did not want to give the *mantra* to just anybody; only to persons who were very highly qualified. When he was requested to give the *mantra* to Rāmānujācārya, he was not inclined to do so. However, once when he went to get the *darśana* of the Raṅganātha Deity, the priest told Goṣṭhīpūrṇa, “You should give this *mantra* to Rāmānuja; he is qualified.” After hearing that, he understood it was the desire of the Lord that he give the *mantra*.

When giving Rāmānuja the *mantra,* Goṣṭhīpūrṇa instructed, “This *mantra* can be given exclusively to persons who are qualified. Do not give it to anyone else.”

Rāmānujācārya accepted the *mantra* from Goṣṭhīpūrṇa. Later, he began to think, “I have received this *mantra* from my spiritual master, and I was told that anyone who chants it would achieve liberation. How can I keep this *mantra* to myself?” He decided to share it with everyone. He stood on the roof of the temple and began calling, “Come, come, I have a wonderful *mantra* to give all of you.” All the townspeople gathered in a great crowd and he told them, “Please listen very carefully, I have a great *mantra* that will give you liberation.” Everyone was very eager, standing with their arms open, “Yes, yes, please give it to us. We want to hear it.” Rāmānujācārya then gave them the *mantra*, *oṁ namo nārāyaṇāya.* When they heard this *mantra*, they immediately became ecstatic.

Word soon got back to Goṣṭhīpūrṇa about his disciple, Rāmānujācārya, and he was greatly angered. When Rāmānujācārya came before Goṣṭhīpūrṇa and explained to him what he had done, Goṣṭhīpūrṇa spoke to him in a very angry tone, “What have you done, you rascal!? You are a sinful wretch, and you will have to go to hell for having done this!”

Rāmānujācārya folded his palms before his spiritual master and said, “Yes, I realize that. But when you gave me this *mantra*, you told me that anyone who chants it will be able to achieve liberation. I was thinking of all these unfortunate people who have to go to hell. I considered that if I broke your order, I would go to hell, but all these unfortunate people who are destined for hell could be liberated. Therefore, I broke your order and gave them this *mantra* because I thought it would be better for me to go to hell instead of all these people.

When Goṣṭhīpūrṇa heard this explanation from Rāmānujācārya, he understood the depth of his realization and compassion. Goṣṭhīpūrṇa prostrated himself before the lotus feet of Rāmānujācārya and said, “From this day forward, I am your disciple, and you are my *guru*. Please give me the shelter of your lotus feet.”

Rāmānujācārya was on a highly realized platform. It is not something that can be imitated; the realization must be there. Otherwise, how can one possibly think of going against the order of the spiritual master?

It is very difficult to understand the loving intimacy between the Lord and His devotees like Gadādhara Paṇḍita. Only a confidential devotee of the Lord can understand their loving affection.

Although Lord Caitanya gave Gadādhara Paṇḍita a particular instruction, due to his ecstatic love, he could not follow the order. Nevertheless, Lord Caitanya was internally very pleased by Gadādhara Paṇḍita’s intense love. Continuing this pastime:

“Gadādhara Paṇḍita’s behavior was very pleasing to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s heart. Nevertheless, the Lord took his hand and spoke to him, displaying the anger of love.” (*Cc. Madhya* 16.138)

Lord Caitanya will now speak in an angry tone externally, but internally He is very pleased. He does this with His intimate associates.

Similarly, Lord Caitanya was externally very angry when Lord Nityānanda broke His *daṇḍa,* but He was very pleased internally because it was His desire and Lord Nityānanda knew His heart.

Thus, Lord Caitanya is chastising Gadādhara Paṇḍita. He took his hand, displaying this anger, and said:

“You have abandoned Gopīnātha’s service and broken your vow to live in Purī. All that is now complete because you have come so far. Your wanting to go with Me is simply a desire for sense gratification. In this way, you are breaking two religious principles, and because of this I am very unhappy.” (*Cc. Madhya* 16.139-140)

Thus, one religious principle is having given up his vow, and the other principle is engaging in sense gratification.

“If you want My happiness, please return to Nīlācala. You will simply condemn Me if you say any more about this matter. Saying this, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu got into a boat, and Gadādhara Paṇḍita immediately fell down unconscious. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu ordered Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya to take Gadādhara Paṇḍita with him.

The Bhaṭṭācārya told Gadādhara Paṇḍita, “Get up! Such are the pastimes of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.” (*Cc. Madhya* 16.141–143)

He then said something very important, explaining why Lord Caitanya was so strict although He very pleased by Gadādhara Paṇḍita’s ecstatic love. The reason is explained by Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya in the following verses.

“You should know that Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself violated His own promise just to keep the promise of Grandfather Bhīṣma.” (*Cc. Madhya* 16.144)

*sva-nigamam apahāya mat-pratijñām*  
*ṛtam adhikartum avapluto rathasthaḥ*  
*dhṛta-ratha-caraṇo ’bhyayāc caladgur*  
*harir iva hantum ibhaṁ gatottarīyaḥ*

“‘Intending to make my promise true, Lord Kṛṣṇa broke His own promise not to take up a weapon at Kurukṣetra. With His outer garment falling off, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa jumped from His chariot, picked up a wheel and came running at me to kill me. Indeed, He rushed at me like a lion going to kill an elephant, and He caused the whole earth to tremble.’ (Quoted from *SB* 1.9.37) Similarly, tolerating separation from you, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu has protected your vow with great endeavor.” (*Cc. Madhya* 16.1145-46)

Thus, Lord Caitanya took a strong and angry position to protect the vow of Gadādhara Paṇḍita in the same way as Lord Kṛṣṇa became very angry towards his devotee, Bhīṣmadeva. Bhīṣmadeva said (*SB* 1.9.16):

*na hy asya karhicid rājan*  
*pumān veda vidhitsitam*  
*yad vijijñāsayā yuktā*  
*muhyanti kavayo ’pi hi*

“King, no one can know the plan of the Lord \[Śrī Kṛṣṇa\]. Even though great philosophers inquire exhaustively, they are bewildered.”

It is very difficult to understand the purpose of the Lord, but His purpose is the highest. It is also very difficult for the Lord to be separated from His devotees, and a devotee is sometimes prepared to break the order of the Lord to be able to associate with Him.

Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī explains:

**Indeed, it is a symptom of real affection that one breaks the order of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, not caring for the regulative principles, to associate with Him. During the** *rāsa* **dance, Kṛṣṇa asked all the** *gopīs* **to return home, but they neglected His order and stayed there for His association. If one carries out Kṛṣṇa’s order, Kṛṣṇa is certainly pleased, but if one sometimes breaks His order due to ecstatic love, that gives Him millions of times greater happiness. (***Cc. Antya* **10.6–8)**

But again, this cannot be imitated. This is certainly not for devotees who are engaged in devotional service on the level of *vaidhī-bhakti* (following the prescribed rules and regulations). Rather, it is the type of service that is rendered by devotees on the level of spontaneous love. This type of service, or *kevalā-bhakti*, is completely spotless; it is pure and uncontaminated. On the level of *kevalā-bhakti*, the devotee does not consider anything except his relationship with the Lord, and will therefore even act in a way that may appear to be completely contrary.

*itthaṁ satāṁ brahma-sukhānubhūtyā*  
*dāsyaṁ gatānāṁ para-daivatena*  
*māyāśritānāṁ nara-dārakeṇa*  
*sākaṁ vijahruḥ kṛta-puṇya-puñjāḥ*

“In this way, all the cowherd boys used to play with Kṛṣṇa, who is the source of the Brahman effulgence for *jñānīs* desiring to merge into that effulgence, who is the Supreme Personality of Godhead for devotees who have accepted eternal servitorship, and who for ordinary persons is but another ordinary child. The cowherd boys, having accumulated the results of pious activities for many lives, were able to associate in this way with the Supreme Personality of Godhead. How can one explain their great fortune?” (*SB* 10.12.11)

The cowherd boys could not consider Him as God. Such is the devotional service rendered by those devotees who are on the topmost platform of love of Godhead.

Ultimately, we must aspire for this level of devotional service. The only means for attaining that platform is strictly following the regulative principles, especially the regulative principles of hearing the pastimes of the Supreme Lord and associating with devotees who are always chanting the glories of the Lord. Hearing the pastimes of the Lord awakens the desire to associate with the Lord and to render service to Him.

It is stated in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Madhya* 22.107): *nitya-siddha kṛṣṇa-prema ‘sādhya’ kabhu naya.* Pure love for Kṛṣṇa is eternally situated in the heart of every living being. It does not come from any other source.

Sometimes foolish persons try to imitate devotees on the level of spontaneous love, but their hearts are filled with many material desires. However, Kṛṣṇa cleanses the heart of one who has an intense desire to hear about Him. At the stage of *anārtha-nivṛitti*, unwanted desires no longer pollute the heart, and intense greed to hear the pastimes of the Lord occupies the heart. One whose heart is fully occupied with this intense greed can become qualified to come to this stage of spontaneous devotional service to the Lord. However, as long as one’s heart is still filled with *anārthas*, one has to develop intense attachment for hearing and associating with devotees. He continues to strictly follow the regulative principles, which include hearing the pastimes of the Lord in the association of devotees.

The purpose for which Lord Caitanya came was to distribute ecstatic love for the Lord. His method to fulfill His purpose is distributing the holy name and pastimes of the Supreme Lord. Thus, hearing these wonderful pastimes of the Lord is meant to awaken within our heart the desire to hear more.

We must very strictly follow the regulative principles of devotional service, not thinking that we can abandon the orders of the Lord. It is explained that Kṛṣṇa is pleased millions of times more if one neglects His order, but as long as these desires are in the heart, one does not have the qualification to neglect the order of the Lord. Only those whose hearts are completely pure can be qualified. To come to that stage, we must strictly follow the order of the Lord, strictly follow the regulative principles, and strictly hear the pastimes of the Lord in the association of the devotees of the Lord.

The *gopīs’* love for Kṛṣṇa is completely free from personal desire for sense gratification. It is so intense that their love for the Lord is described as lust. But it is not material lust, as explained in *Ādi-līlā* in the chapter entitled, *The Confidential Reasons for the Appearance of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu*.

**Antya-līlā, 10.9–21**

**Riga—July 11, 2000**

**Vāsudeva Datta, Murāri Gupta, Gaṅgādāsa, Śrīmān Sena, Śrīmān Paṇḍita, Akiñcana Kṛṣṇadāsa, Murāri, Garuḍa Paṇḍita, Buddhimanta Khān, Sañjaya Puruṣottama, Bhagavān Paṇḍita, Śuklāmbara Brahmacārī, Nṛsiṁhānanda Brahmacārī and many others joined together to go to Jagannātha Purī. It would be impossible to mention the names of them all.**

**The inhabitants of Kulīna-grāma and Khaṇḍa also came and joined. Śivānanda Sena took the leadership and thus started taking care of them all. Rāghava Paṇḍita came with bags full of food prepared very nicely by his sister, Damayantī. Damayantī made varieties of unparalleled food just suitable for Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu to eat. The Lord ate it continually for one year.**

**These are the names of some of the pickles and condiments in the bags of Rāghava Paṇḍita:** *āmra-kāśandi***,** *ādā-kāśandi***,** *jhāla-kāśandi***,** *nembu-ādā***,** *āmra-koli***,** *āmsi***,** *āma-khaṇḍa***,** *tailāmra* **and** *āma-sattā***. With great attention, Damayantī also made dried bitter vegetables into a powder. Do not neglect** *sukutā* **because it is a bitter preparation.**

**Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu derived more happiness from eating this** *sukutā* **than from drinking** *pañcāmṛta* **\[a preparation of milk, sugar, ghee, honey and curd\]. Since Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu is the Supreme Personality of Godhead, He extracts the purpose from everything. Heaccepted Damayantī’s affection for Him, and therefore He derived great pleasure even from the dried bitter leaves of** *sukutā* **and from** *kāśandi* **\[a sour condiment\]. (***Cc. Antya* **10.9-18)**

The words *bhāva-grāhī* are used here to describe the Lord with reference to Damayantī, because He sees the intention and attitude of a person.

The perfect example of this is when Lord Caitanya stood by the *Garuḍa-stambha* while taking *darśana* of Lord Jagannātha in the temple. An elderly woman whose vision of the Deity was blocked by a huge crowd of people, in her enthusiasm to see Lord Jagannātha, climbed up the *Garuḍastambha* and stepped on Lord Caitanya’s shoulder to be able to take *darśana* of Lord Jagannātha. Not only was she offending a great Vaisnava, Garuḍa, but she was also standing on Lord Caitanya’s shoulder. Govinda, who was standing next to Lord Caitanya, as he often does because he is His personal servant, immediately chastised her. “What are you doing?! Can you not see what you are doing?”

Lord Caitanya turned to Govinda and said, “Listen, you *ādi-vasyā* (uncivilized person), why are you chastising this woman?” He became angry at Govinda for chastising the woman. By that time the woman realized what she had done—not only standing on Garuḍa but also standing on Lord Caitanya’s shoulders—and she immediately got down and begged forgiveness from the Lord. Lord Caitanya did not even mention anything about her offenses. He turned to Govinda and the devotees and said, “I wish I could have as much affection to see Lord Jagannātha as she has! This woman has given her body, her mind, and her life for service to Lord Jagannātha. I simply pray to Lord Jagannātha that I can obtain shelter at her feet so that she can teach Me how to have the same affection as she has.” As the perfect example of *bhāva-grāhī,* the Lord considers the purpose and essence of the devotee’s attitude, even though he may commit an offense.

*’īśvara-svabhāva’—bhaktera nā laya aparādha*  
*alpa-sevā bahu māne ātma-paryanta prasāda*

“Characteristically, the Supreme Personality of Godhead does not take seriously an offense committed by a pure devotee. The Lord accepts whatever small service a devotee renders as being such a great service that He is prepared to give even Himself, what to speak of other benedictions.” (*Cc. Antya* 1.107)

*bhṛtyasya paśyati gurūn api nāparādhān*  
*sevāṁ manāg api kṛtāṁ bahudhābhyupaiti*  
*āviṣkaroti piśuneṣv api nābhyasūyāṁ*  
*śīlena nirmala-matiḥ puruṣottamo ’yam*

“The Supreme Personality of Godhead, who is known as Puruṣottama, the greatest of all persons, has a pure mind. He is so gentle that even if His servant is implicated in a great offense, He does not take it very seriously. Indeed, if His servant renders some small service, the Lord accepts it as being very great. Even if an envious person blasphemes the Lord, the Lord never manifests anger against him. Such are His great qualities.” (*Cc. Antya* 1.108, quoted from *BRS* 2.1.138)

In the last part of the Seventh Canto of *ŚrīmadBhāgavatam*, Prabhupāda says that sometimes if a person continuously blasphemes the Lord, but in his blasphemy recites the name of the Lord, the Lord punishes him in his next life but gives him liberation in the following life. Such is *bhāvagrāhī janārdana.* The Lord has such a pure mind that even if His pure devotee commits an offense, the Lord does not take it seriously and instead glorifies his service. Sometimes when the Lord glorifies His devotees, He speaks as if He has five mouths; He cannot stop glorifying them.

It is very significant when the Lord recognizes and glorifies a service attitude. If we do something that someone else recognizes but is not recognized by the Lord, then it is useless. But when the Lord recognizes the service rendered by His devotee, although recognized by no one else, the Lord glorifies it and amplifies it. When the Lord recognizes it, all the devotees glorify the service rendered by that devotee. The Lord understands the motive because He is omniscient.

We must be very careful because since we are not omniscient, how can we say we know somebody’s motive? Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself warns (*Bg.* 9.30): *api cet sudarācāro bhajate mām ananya-bhāk.* “Even if a devotee commits an abominable act, but is rightly situated in his determination, he should not be criticized.” Being rightly situated in determination means that there is no motivation to commit an offense to the Lord. The devotee may have a weakness, but after falling prey to that weakness, he must realize what he has done and repent for his activity. One must be very careful not to unnecessarily criticize devotees.

Of course, if we commit an offense and justify it by this verse, then that is sense gratification. We should not calculate committing sinful activities and then justifying them. One should certainly not commit an offense or abominable act, thinking that the Lord will give His forgiveness because he is “rightly situated in his determination.” As explained by Lord Caitanya, the Lord understands the heart and understands the motivation and intention of a devotee. Therefore, if a devotee sincerely tries to please the Lord, but commits an offense unknowingly, the Lord will extract the essence of his service. Of course, when one comes to realize that he has committed an offense, he should not do it again.

Lord Caitanya thus accepted Damayantī’s affection for Him and thus derived great pleasure even from the dried bitter leaves of *sukutā* and *kāśandi*.

One might think that Damayantī was offensive by thinking of offering something to the Lord that is used for indigestion. But Lord Caitanya, understanding her attitude, knew that she was not thinking in an offensive way, but was rather thinking of the Lord out of affection, and her thinking was on the same platform as the residents of Goloka Vṛndāvana. On this topmost platform, which is *jñāna-śūnyā bhakti*, she had no knowledge of the opulence of the Lord and only thought of Him as an equal.

Similarly, Rādhārāṇī manifests anger before Kṛṣṇa, and sometimes gets into a lovers’ quarrel with Him. But when Kṛṣṇa hears chastisement from His devotee, those are the most relishable words for Him to hear—more so than the chanting of Vedic *mantras* and hymns in great reverence.

Kṛṣṇa always has been and always will be God, but He does not always want to be treated with reverence. He wants to be treated with intimacy all the time, but who can treat Him that way? Only those who are situated on the platform of spontaneous ecstatic love for the Lord can treat Him that way.

**Because of her natural love for Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, Damayantī considered the Lord an ordinary human being. Therefore she thought that He would** **become sick by overeating and there would be mucus within His abdomen. Because of sincere affection, she thought that eating this** *sukutā* **would cure the Lord’s disease. Considering these affectionate thoughts of Damayantī, the Lord was very pleased. (***Cc. Antya* **10.19-20)**

*priyeṇa saṅgrathya vipakṣa-sannidhāv*  
*upāhitāṁ vakṣasi pīvara-stanī*  
*srajaṁ na kācid vijahau jalāvilāṁ*  
*vasanti hi premṇi guṇā na vastuni*

**A dear lover strung a garland and placed it on the shoulder of his beloved in the presence of her co-wives. She had raised breasts and was very beautiful, yet although the garland was tainted with mud, she did not reject it, for its value lay not in material things but in love. (***Cc. Antya* **10.21, quoted from** *Kirātārjunīya***)**

Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī quotes this verse to illustrate that the Lord accepted the bitter *sukutā,* greatly relishing eating it, in the same mood as the wife that accepted the mud-tainted garland.

There are numerous examples of devotees offering something to the Lord, and the Lord being very eager to accept it. Sudāmā Brāhmaṇa offered chipped rice, Śuklāmbara Brahmacārī offered pieces of broken rice, and Lord Caitanya ate and relished the rice that Raghunātha Dāsa Gosvāmī was eating that had been rejected by the cows.

The other end of the spectrum is when Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya cooked hundreds of preparations for Lord Caitanya; Advaita Ācārya fed Lord Caitanya and Lord Nītyānanda very sumptuously at His house; Śacīmātā prepared varieties of opulent dishes for Lord Caitanya and Lord Nītyānanda; and Murāri Gupta offered rice to Lord Caitanya.

Once, Murāri Gupta was experiencing ecstatic love, and he took rice and ghee and mixed them. Then he began throwing it on the floor, saying, “Kṛṣṇa eat! Kṛṣṇa eat!” He threw all the rice on the floor, and when Murāri Gupta’s wife saw his mood and saw the empty plate, she immediately brought more rice and more ghee. Murāri Gupta was in ecstasy and was taking this rice and offering it to Kṛṣṇa, seeing Him everywhere.

The following morning, Lord Caitanya came to Murāri Gupta who is a physician. Murāri Gupta asked Him, “Have You come here for Your problems of indigestion? How did You get indigestion?”

Lord Caitanya said, “You, rascal! You know how I got indigestion. I had to eat all the rice you offered to Me yesterday! The only thing that can cure indigestion is to drink some water from the water pot of a *brāhmaṇa*.” Thus, Lord Caitanya came and drank water from a pot that Murāri Gupta kept on a table.

There are unlimited pastimes of how the Lord relishes accepting food from the hands of His devotees. The devotees relish offering food to the Lord, and then they relish taking the Lord’s remnants. So many wonderful exchanges take place between the Lord and His devotees during these offerings of food.

We should always remember that when we are making an offering, it is our attitude that the Lord is noticing. He therefore says, “*patraṁ puṣpaṁ phalaṁ toyaṁ*.” (*Bg.* 9.26) “When one offers a leaf, fruit, flower or water with love and devotion, I will accept it.”

**Antya-līlā: 10.33-39, 55-56, 107-130**

**Riga—July 10, 2000**

Every year, Rāghava Paṇḍita would bring the big bags of food prepared by Damayantī to Lord Caitanya. Rāghava Paṇḍita’s disciple, Makaradhvaja Kara, would personally take responsibility for carrying these bags, and he would give them to Govinda, the servant of Lord Caitanya. Everyday, Lord Caitanya would take something from the bags of Rāghava Paṇḍita.

Once, Govinda became very disturbed because not only was Rāghava Paṇḍita bringing food to offer to Lord Caitanya, but so were many other devotees. The devotees would ask Govinda, “What did Lord Caitanya say about the *prasādam* I made for him?” But Lord Caitanya had not eaten it and Govinda would wrap it up and put it away. The devotees were continuously asking, “What did He say? What did He think of the food?” This situation became very difficult for Govinda because he did not know what to tell them. He wanted neither to lie, nor tell them that the Lord had not taken anything. Thus, he came before the Lord with a very disturbed mind.

The Lord asked him, “What are you so disturbed about?” Govinda replied, “All the devotees are cooking for You, but You are not eating. They are asking me what You thought of the food that they made for You? I do not know what to tell them. Please tell me, what should I tell them?”

Lord Caitanya said, “Well, I will give you something to tell them. Bring it all here!”

Govinda brought the bags, which were many since he had been storing them for such a long time. One by one, he would take the preparations out and say who had made it. Lord Caitanya would eat the whole preparation, and Govinda would bring another and say who made it. Everything that Govinda brought, Lord Caitanya ate. The Lord then said, “Alright, now go back and tell them.”

The Lord does not like to see His devotees in anxiety. It is intolerable for Him. He therefore always thinks of how to relieve their anxiety. To satisfy His devotees, the Lord ate everything that was offered. It is not difficult for the Lord to eat so much. He is, after all, the Lord of all sacrifice. Every day, He is eating fifty-six offerings at the Jagannātha temple. Everyday, He is eating offerings made by His devotees throughout the entire creation. Therefore, to eat a few bags of food is not very difficult for Him, and thus He did so in order to please His devotees.

Govinda then said, “Now here is the food that Rāghava Paṇḍita brought.” (This was some freshly made *prasādam.*) Lord Caitanya him, “Put that in storage, and I will take a little of it every day.” Thus, Lord Caitanya would eat this food from Rāghava Paṇḍita every day. Moreover, He would even go to his home when he would make his offering, being very eager to accept it from the hands of His devotee.

This pastime further emphasizes the first verse of the Tenth Chapter of *Antya-līlā*:

**Let me offer my respectful obeisances unto Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, who is always pleased to accept anything given with faith and love by His devotees and is always ready to bestow mercy upon them.**
